# CLAUDE.md

This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.

## Keeping This File Current

Before ending substantive work in a session, check whether anything here needs updating: new commands, architecture changes, new or changed invariants, deprecated systems, newly discovered gotchas. Edit the relevant existing section directly — no changelog, no dated entries, this file stays current-state-only. Remove or correct anything no longer true rather than leaving it to go stale. Skip this if the session made no durable/structural changes (pure Q&A, one-off debugging, trivial fixes).

## The M Principle — Non-Negotiable

> "We built this to save lives, not to make profit."

**RED is a hard block. Always.** No bypass button, no workaround, no exception for any business reason.
If a procedure combination is clinically dangerous, M will not facilitate it — full stop.
The client can book elsewhere. M will not be the platform that put their life at risk.
This principle must never be softened, worked around, or compromised in any feature, edge case, or demo.

## Safety-Decision Architecture — Invariants (do not regress)

The AI/narration layer has **no technical capability to approve, reject, or modify a safety decision.** Only deterministic engines write safety outcomes. Established 2026-07 (commit `45647208`); a CI red-team guards it.

- **Deterministic decides, AI narrates.** The patient SAFE-T risk level is computed only by `base44/functions/_shared/safeTEngine.ts` (`computeSafeT` — pure, replayable). `computeSafeTScreening` writes the decision to the append-only, hash-chained `SafeTScreening` entity **first**, then invokes the LLM only to narrate it — the LLM receives just the finalized `risk_level` + flags (never raw patient text) and any risk level it returns is discarded. Never move SAFE-T scoring back into an LLM or into the client.
- **Fail closed.** Ambiguity, incomplete profile, pregnancy, or detected prompt-injection → `review` (needs human review), **never** `low`. `SafeTScan.jsx`'s error fallback is `review`.
- **The RED block stays deterministic** (`validateProcedureSafety` → `_shared/procedureCompatibility.ts` `getViolations`, re-checked server-side). `checkMedicalRisk` (admin-review routing) and `runMedGuardAnalysis` (in-travel score) are deterministic too. AI cannot suppress or override any of them.
- **AI may raise caution, never clear it.** In any hybrid scorer the LLM may only *add* risk. `runInternetIntelligence`'s AI can flag but not lower a score. The clinic agent (`verifyClinicStatus`) may auto-*block* (`closed`) but never auto-writes the permissive `operating` — it routes that to a human (`agent_proposed_operating`).
- **Doctor-verified completion + memory bank** (`logProcedureComplete`, `_shared/procedureMatch.ts`, `writeOutcomeMemory`, `recallSimilarOutcomes`): `computeProcedureMatch` deterministically compares booked vs. doctor-confirmed procedures — a mismatch is flagged (`mismatch_flagged`) for human review, never silently resolved, and an LLM may only narrate the already-decided status. Doctor-entered `doctor_recommended_medications` (never AI-generated) feed an anonymized `OutcomeRecord` (category/condition tags only — no patient identity, no raw med text). `recallSimilarOutcomes` surfaces that history back only as **doctor-only** aggregate advisory context (never patient-facing), always carrying a fixed caveat in the payload itself — it informs a doctor's own judgment and writes nothing back automatically.
- **Sanitize all user text reaching a prompt** via `base44/functions/_shared/sanitizePromptInput.ts`; an injection hit on the safety path forces `review`.
- **Log decision + narration** to the `SafeTScreening` `prev_hash` chain. Append-only — never update/delete.
- **Red-team gate.** `tests/redteam/safety-redteam.spec.js` (Playwright `redteam` project) runs deterministically, no network/credits; `.github/workflows/redteam.yml` re-runs it on every change to `base44/functions/**/entry.ts` (catches model-version changes) and weekly. Changing a model ID or the engine must keep it green.

## Data Freshness — fail-safe, not fail-silent

Time-sensitive status (clinic operating, doctor licence, visa, regulatory) is governed by `base44/shared/freshness.ts` (per-type TTLs + `flagForReview` → the `DataFreshnessReview` human queue). Cached status is never served as confirmed past its TTL — it is re-verified or clearly labelled unconfirmed. Clinic bookings gate on `checkClinicStatus` (blocks unless operating **and** fresh; soft-labels until `CLINIC_GATE_ENFORCE=true`). Scheduled by `.github/workflows/freshness-cron.yml`, guarded by `shared/cronAuth.ts` (cron secret **or** admin session). Admin surfaces: `/admin/clinics`, `/admin/data-freshness`.

**Curated country-data auto-refresh — the one deliberate auto-publish exception.** `src/lib/visaMatrix.js` (`EXPLICIT_ROUTES`, `EVISA_LINKS`) and `src/lib/travelReadiness.js` (`PASSPORT_RENEWAL_LINKS`) are hand-curated JS constants with no TTL of their own (distinct from the live `VisaRequirementSnapshot` cache above, which does have one). `.github/workflows/curated-data-refresh.yml` runs **monthly**, invoking Claude Code directly (`anthropics/claude-code-action`, real `WebSearch`/`WebFetch` tool access — not Base44's `InvokeLLM`, which already produced one wrong visa answer for a real patient and is why the curated matrix outranks it) to re-verify every entry against the live web and auto-commit any confirmed fix straight to `main` — the one place in this repo where an AI-driven change ships without a human confirming first, deliberately mirroring the trust model already extended to `base44-builder[bot]`'s direct commits (reviewed after the fact, not gated before). Guardrails: a deterministic post-run `git diff` check discards the commit if anything outside those two files changed; the commit only survives if `build`/`vitest`/`redteam`/`typecheck` all still pass; the agent is instructed to never fabricate a URL and to leave anything it can't confidently verify untouched. Every outcome (applied, or a gate/scope failure) is logged via the new `logCuratedDataRefresh` function into the same `DataFreshnessReview` queue (`subject_type: 'passport_renewal_link'` added for this) — applied changes land pre-`actioned` (transparency, nothing to decide), failures land `pending`/`critical` like any other real flag. Needs a one-time `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` GitHub secret (skips harmlessly, green, if unset) — see the workflow file's own header for full setup.

## Travel Safety Escalation — "no human touch needed" partner/patient recovery

Every scheduled job below runs from `.github/workflows/safety-cron.yml` (15-min tick, hourly, 6-hourly, and a daily 09:00 UTC deployment audit that fails loudly on anything undeployed) — **not** Base44's own scheduler, and **not** an admin clicking a button. This file's own header documents a recurring bug class worth knowing before adding a new scheduled function: real, correct code that nobody ever actually scheduled, or that required a live admin session (which a cron tick can never hold). This has bitten `checkPartnerSLABreaches`, `dispatchSoloSecurity`, `scheduleSoloCheckIns`, `detectFallbackCrisis`, `sendTravelCountdownReminders`, and `autoCompletePatientJourney` — each existed, worked, and protected nobody until fixed (the last two found and fixed 2026-08-13, see M-Care Phase 44 below). Any new scheduled safety job must (1) gate on `_shared/cronAuth.ts`'s `cronAuthorized()`, never `base44.auth.me()` + a role check, and (2) actually appear in `safety-cron.yml`, not just in its own header comment describing an intended cadence — trust the workflow file, not a function's self-description of when it runs.

- **Partner never responds to being offered a job** (doctor, driver, travel agency, companion) — `checkPartnerSLABreaches`: 24h → auto-dispatches a backup, 48h → human-intervention alert. For doctors specifically, this used to be a silent swap — the original doctor got no warning before being replaced. Added 2026-08-05: at the 12h halfway point, a reminder goes to the doctor on every channel we have for them — link-only email always, plus SMS/WhatsApp/in-app push where a phone or push subscription exists (`doctor_email` + their existing `doctor_portal_token` portal link, gated by a new one-time `doctor_reminder_sent_at` field) — so the 24h auto-backup is a deadline they were actually told about, not a surprise. Content and channel selection live in `base44/shared/doctorReminderCopy.ts`, translated into the doctor's `language_preference` (9 languages matching `src/i18n.js`'s `SUPPORTED_LANGUAGES` — `tr`/`th`/`zh` were added to `Doctor.jsonc`'s enum the same day specifically because M has doctors in Turkey/Thailand who may not read English). All channels stay link-only/generic (no patient name, no date/time) — same rule as everywhere else in this file; the real detail is only ever in the portal behind the link. One real caveat: the in-app push only reaches a doctor who has logged into the full dashboard at least once — the magic-link `/portal/doctor/:token` page these reminders link to has no auth and no push subscription of its own, so a doctor who only ever uses that link won't get pushed, same as before this change.
- **Partner drops after already confirming** (doctor withdraws, driver/companion cancels) — three shared, reusable finders: `base44/shared/{findDoctorBackup,findDriverBackup,findCompanionBackup}.ts`. Each finds the next verified/licensed replacement, reassigns the case, and alerts admin; if none is available it's a loud critical email + `DispatchFailureLog` entry, never a silent status flip. `findDoctorBackup` additionally escalates to an SMS (`ADMIN_PHONE`) and a link-only FYI to the travel agency/companion already on that trip when the patient is already traveling (`departure_date` passed or `procedure_date` within 72h).
- **Doctor still unconfirmed as the procedure itself approaches** — `detectFallbackCrisis` previously only checked confirmation *speed* (15 minutes after being notified, `CONFIRMATION_WINDOW_MS`); a doctor notified early who simply never got around to confirming, with the patient already on the ground, was invisible to that rule. Added 2026-08: a second, independent check — `doctor_confirmation_status` still `PENDING` with `procedure_date` within 24h flags the case urgent (same `fallback_state` shape); within 4h additionally fires an admin SMS, not just the flag; the 24h tier also reminds the doctor directly on every channel (email/SMS/WhatsApp/push, translated — same `sendDoctorReminder`/`doctorReminderCopy.ts` pattern as `checkPartnerSLABreaches` above, added 2026-08-05) so the doctor has a real chance to confirm before admin is ever pulled in at 4h. Deliberately does **not** auto-call `findDoctorBackup` at either tier — "hasn't confirmed yet" is a softer signal than a driver physically failing to show, and unilaterally reassigning a doctor who may still come through risks real harm (duplicate assignment, a doctor arriving to find they've already been replaced); a human decides, with the real backup-finder one click away in the admin console. `detectFallbackCrisis` itself was also a real, correctly cron-auth-guarded function that had simply never been added to `safety-cron.yml` — another instance of this section's own recurring bug class — now scheduled in both the 15-minute and daily blocks. **Deliberately deferred, same session**: companion meet-up confirmation has the identical shape of gap (`DigitalHandshake.jsonc`'s `checkpoint_type` enum already declares `companion_departure_airport_confirmed`/`companion_arrival_airport_confirmed`/`companion_clinic_arrival_confirmed`/`companion_hotel_arrival_confirmed`/`companion_recovery_instructions_received`, and `CompanionHandshakePanel.jsx` can list/complete them — but nothing anywhere ever creates one, and `CompanionAssignment.status`'s `active`/`completed` values are declared but never written). Real, bigger feature than this fix — new checkpoint-creation wiring across 5 trip stages, a no-show detector, and a `findCompanionBackup` hookup — scoped for its own future session, not rushed in alongside this one.
- **Driver no-show at any pickup/dropoff leg** (the 9-step journey in `completeHandshake/entry.ts` — steps 1/3/5/7/9 each need a driver) — `escalateMissedDriverHandshake` checks `TravelRequest.scheduled_departure` (step 1) or the prior step's confirmed timestamp (steps 3/5/7/9); 30 minutes overdue with no confirmation dispatches a backup driver via `findDriverBackup` and texts the patient directly (the one case where texting the patient proactively is correct — they're already watching no car arrive, so "we see it, help's coming" is reassurance, not bad news). Idempotent via `TravelRequest.noshow_flagged_steps`.
- **Patient goes silent / phone dies** — `scheduleSoloCheckIns` creates a check-in for every actively-traveling case, only once it's actually due (first at 6h post-intake-handshake, then every 12h — it used to fire the moment it noticed the case, ignoring both numbers; covers solo **and** companioned travelers — a companion nearby doesn't guarantee M can reach the patient). `escalateSoloCheckIn` runs the ladder: **2h → guardian notified** (email + SMS + WhatsApp if a phone is on file, each with a live link to the same Google-Map `GuardianView` used at every later tier, so they can see the patient's location and reach out themselves at the first missed check-in, not the third) → 3h → voice call attempt + satellite fallback to the patient → 5h `dispatchSoloSecurity` (real SMS handshake with a security agency, tries the next agency if unconfirmed in 15 min, loud failure if every agency is exhausted) → 9h police/embassy escalation. `checkStaleLiveLocations` is a parallel, consent-gated GPS-staleness check. **Guardian contact fields, read this before touching any of them**: `CaseRecord.emergency_contact` is a display label only ("Jane Doe (Spouse)"), never an email or phone — the real fields are `emergency_contact_email`/`emergency_contact_number`, synced from `PersonalEmergencyContactsPanel.jsx`. Both `escalateSoloCheckIn`'s guardian email and `completeHandshake`'s milestone Guardian SMS were silently reading the wrong field for months (the label, or a `guardian_phone` that was never written) — fixed, but a fresh reminder that a field name matching what a comment *says* it holds isn't proof it does.
- **The guardian isn't just notified, she can act.** `GuardianView.jsx` (`/guardian/:token`) has a "Reach Out" panel with real `tel:`/`sms:` links to the patient and their doctor, the local country emergency number (from `EmergencyContacts` — real entity, but currently has **no seeded rows for any country**; the button simply doesn't render until one exists for a given country), and a "Request Immediate Security Check" button. That button calls `requestGuardianSecurityCheck` (token-verified, same `GuardianSession` check as `getGuardianViewData`) which invokes `base44/shared/dispatchSecurityForCheckIn.ts` — the exact same dispatch logic `dispatchSoloSecurity`'s 5h cron sweep uses, extracted so both callers share one verified implementation. Deliberately does **not** hand the guardian a security vendor's raw phone number — she directs M's system, M stays the one actually contacting the agency.
- **Known, deliberately unfixed gap**: a companion who accepted an assignment then silently vanishes mid-trip (no explicit cancel) has no detection today. `MealDelivery.status` has the right shape (`scheduled → en_route → delivered`) but no live writer anywhere — building a detector on it would just be false alarms. Needs a real confirmation action (companion or patient side) before this is closeable.
- A second, more granular escalation engine (`runSilentSafetyEscalation`, 15m/30m/60m/120m/180m ladder) exists but is **not** scheduled anywhere — admin-button-only via `AdminSoloMonitor.jsx`. Don't assume it runs; a comment in `submitRecoveryCheckin/entry.ts` claims it does and is wrong.
- **Same bug class, event-triggered instead of cron-triggered**: `storeSafetySignal` (feeds the anonymized `SafetySignal`/`SafetyIndexCache` "Safety Intelligence Network" behind MedGuard's "gets more predictive over time" claim) had zero callers anywhere — its own doc-comment claimed HS9/`closeEmergencyThreatLoop`, neither ever called it. Fixed 2026-08-04: wired into `completeHandshake`'s HS9 block with real per-trip data (SOS events, escalated check-ins, MedGuard audit-log risk history). A useful diagnostic for finding more of these: grep every function name in `base44/functions/*` against the whole repo (frontend, other functions, workflows) — anything with zero hits outside its own `entry.ts` is a real dead-end candidate, though verify each by hand before touching it. One candidate found this way, `scheduleRecoveryCheckIns`, turned out to be genuinely superseded (two other working recovery-check-in pipelines already exist — `schedulePostOpCheckIns`→`PostOpCheckIn`, and `logProcedureComplete`→`RecoverySession`→`checkMissedRecoveryCheckins`) rather than a live gap — not every zero-reference function is a bug; some are abandoned duplicates of a system that moved on. **Follow-up pass (commit `24e95c49`)**: a fresh sweep of all ~310 functions found 33 zero-reference (34 minus `storeSafetySignal`); of 29 hand-verified, 18 were DEAD-CONFIRMED real gaps (10 SUPERSEDED, 2 UNCLEAR — Base44-automation-shaped, unconfirmable from repo code alone). Wired 13 of the 18 (`sendActivityNudge`, `runFraudIntelligence`, `runFraudOverride`, `welcomeHomeSafe`, `generateCulturalCarePackage`, `resendChauffeurPortalEmail`, `sendPostSurgeryFeedback`, `sendReputationSurvey`, `checkReturningPatient`, `expirePassportGrants`, `expireConfigChanges`, `cleanupExpiredGuardianSessions`, `purgeRateLimitBuckets`) — several had the identical auth-model bug described above (admin-session-only, so no cron or peer function could ever call them; or `cronAuthorized()`'s header check used where a function-to-function caller needed `internalOrAdminAuthorized()`'s body-field check instead, since `.functions.invoke()` can't pass a custom header). Deliberately deferred: `handleFlightDisruption` (needs a missing flight-cancellation detection branch in `pollActiveTripFlights` first), `approveRevokePassportAccess` (no passport-access-management UI exists at all), `priceBroadcastEngine` (5 loosely-related actions, no single natural call site), `approveCompanionExpense` (needs new UI, and its own docblock claims an `EscrowHold` release the code never actually performs), and `processPerformanceMetrics` (currently a no-op stub — wiring it would call a function that does nothing).

## Struggle-Aware Recovery ("M Rescue")

Detects someone stuck or abandoning signup and offers help before they quit — deterministic detection, never AI deciding anything, and never AI-personalized outbound messages (see the link-only rule below). Two independent halves:

- **In-session**: `src/hooks/useStruggleDetector.js` watches a field for two signals — idle-while-invalid past a threshold, or N consecutive failures (both pure/testable via `shouldArmIdleTimer`/`shouldFireRetryFail`, no network). On either, the caller emits a hint via `src/lib/struggleHint.js`'s `emitStruggleHint(emoji, text)` — a window-event bridge (same pattern as `i18n.js`'s `languageChange`) to `MCareOrb.jsx` ("M-Care"), the one floating assistant, since struggle signals originate deep in arbitrary forms while the orb is a singleton mounted once in `AppLayout` (plus the two partner-portal pages below). The hint pre-empts the orb's normal timer-based tip rotation and clears on route change. Wired into `Login.jsx` (phone/OTP); `DoctorSignup.jsx`/`TravelAgencySignup.jsx` are wired for the abandon-tracking half below but not yet this in-session half.
- **Post-abandon**: signup forms already call `saveSignupDraft(type, formData, {step})` (`src/lib/signupDraft.js`) on every step change, purely to localStorage. `trackSignupProgress(type, data, step)` piggybacks on those exact call sites — fires at most once per step, only once a phone or email exists in the draft — POSTing to `trackSignupAbandon` (public, `bodySchema`-validated, writes only via `asServiceRole`; `SignupProgress` has **no client-readable/writable RLS**, admin-only, same reasoning as `AccountFlag`). `checkStalledSignups` (Base44-dashboard cron, `cronAuthorized`) finds records stale 30+ min and sends **one** recovery nudge.

**M-Care consolidation (2026-08-05).** This app used to run three separate, unconnected AI assistant personas at once — "Morales Guide" (a bottom-left tip/Q&A orb), "Morales Assist" (a bottom-right general-concierge chat + human handoff), and, on one page, "SAFE-T VISA ASSIST™". On `/visa-assist` a logged-in patient could see all three assistant surfaces simultaneously — real clutter, and a name collision waiting to happen. Guide and Assist are now one component, `src/components/mcare/MCareOrb.jsx` ("M-Care") — same bottom-left position and hero-collision math Guide already had tuned, same KB-first/tip-rotation/quiet-route/struggle-hint behavior verbatim, but a logged-in non-admin user whose question misses the KB now falls through to the real, case-aware `moralesAssist` edge function (which can hand off to a human specialist) instead of the old generic platform-Q&A LLM call. Mounted in `AppLayout.jsx` (universal except demo routes — same condition Guide had, deliberately not narrowed to logged-in-only) plus `PortalTravelAgency.jsx`/`PortalDoctor.jsx` (the two partner magic-link pages that mounted Guide independently). M-Care only ever narrates — it has no write access to any SAFE-T/procedure-compatibility decision or intake data. SAFE-T VISA ASSIST™ (`src/components/visa/VisaAIChat.jsx`) deliberately **stays a separate identity** — its compliance-footer branding reads as a deliberate sub-brand, a call for Portia not an engineering default — but its real privacy bug got fixed regardless: it was calling `InvokeLLM` directly from the client with no PHI scrubbing and no real rate limit (its `MAX_MESSAGES`/3-second cooldown was only JS state, trivially bypassed), bypassing a backend function that already existed for exactly this (`base44/functions/safeTAssist/entry.ts`, deliberately unauthenticated, IP-rate-limited, and already redteam-exempted — but until this fix had zero real callers anywhere in the repo). `safeTAssist`'s own hardcoded system prompt is a *different* persona ("Safe-T4life", general journey/safety Q&A) — routing visa questions through it unchanged would have answered them in the wrong voice, so the fix added a `topic: 'visa'` request field that swaps in a visa-specific system prompt while sharing the same scrub/sanitize/rate-limit pipeline both personas need. Also fixed in passing: `VisaAIChat.jsx`'s own source had mojibake-corrupted UTF-8 (™, em dashes, emoji all garbled) in its user-visible strings — a real, visible bug, cleaned up while the file was already open for the backend-call fix.

**M-Care super-agent, Phase 1 — doctor signup by chat (2026-08-05).** Portia's vision (partly inspired by Boardy.ai's conversational onboarding): M-Care should be able to run a real signup end-to-end through conversation, not just answer questions about the platform. The one non-negotiable constraint shaping every part of this: "deterministic decides, AI narrates" (see Safety-Decision Architecture above) extends to signup too — M-Care may sequence and narrate, but the actual record-write, role-grant, and verification-kickoff must be the exact same code path the classic form uses, never a second, less-tested one. Shipped: a doctor can now complete real signup — landing on an actual `pending_verification` `Doctor` record — entirely inside M-Care's chat panel (`src/components/mcare/DoctorSignupChatFlow.jsx`), triggered via a "🩺 Become a partner doctor" quick action shown to visitors/patients. Built by extending, not duplicating, this app's existing conversational-intake machinery: `src/lib/intakeFlow/flowEngine.js` (already graph-agnostic — confirmed by the fact it already drives both the medical-patient and travel-only intake graphs) walks a new `src/lib/mcareFlow/doctorSignupGraph.js`; free-text steps are parsed/narrated by `intakeConversationTurn` (now takes an additive `persona: 'doctor_signup'` field, defaulting to the original patient voice, so every existing caller is unaffected — same pattern as `safeTAssist`'s `topic` param). The critical structural move: `DoctorSignupStep3.jsx`'s submit logic was extracted into `src/lib/partnerSignup/submitDoctorSignup.js`, and BOTH the classic form and the new chat flow call it — there is exactly one place a `Doctor` record gets created, so the chat path cannot silently diverge from the form's safety/data properties. That function itself enforces the auth-gate-before-create ordering (throws `'AUTH_REQUIRED'` if no session — the same invariant that fixed the 2026-07-24 "Partner Onboarding Lockout" bug) rather than trusting each caller to check first. A new redteam invariant (`PARTNER SIGNUP: doctor signup — chat and form both call the one gated submission function`) guards this structurally: both callers must reference `submitDoctorSignup(`, neither may contain a raw `Doctor.create(`, and the shared function itself may never set `status: 'active'`/`verification_status`/`license_verified: true` — those stay the exclusive province of `activateVerifiedDoctor`. Progress persists via the existing `signupDraft.js` (`{data, meta}` under the `'doctor'` key) so a chat-started signup can resume as the classic form and vice versa, and picks back up automatically if the user is sent through the sign-in redirect mid-conversation. **Deliberately scoped out of this pass, not silently forgotten**: free-text intent recognition ("I'm a dentist" typed into normal chat) — Phase 1 is button-primary only (tap "Become a partner doctor"); the license-document upload step is optional and skippable, never blocking; the other 4 partner types (travel agency, taxi, companion, security agency) — Phase 2, same engine, new graph data per persona, not built yet; patient-side "I want to book a procedure" should hand off to the existing `/intake` flow rather than reimplementing medical intake in the chat bubble (that flow already has the RED-block and guardian-minor gate wired in — rebuilding it here would just be more redteam surface for no gain), not built yet; live voice input to M-Care specifically (see Phase 2 below — voice infrastructure itself turned out to already exist elsewhere in this repo, just not wired to M-Care). Two real, separate gaps surfaced during this work and deliberately not touched: `Doctor.jsonc` has no RLS `create` rule at all (unlike its 4 sibling partner entities) — worth confirming with Base44 support; `/intake`'s conversational flow runs the RED hard-block but never actually invokes the full `computeSafeT`/`computeSafeTScreening` graduated risk-scoring engine (that only runs in the older `Booking.jsx` wizard via `SafeTScan.jsx`) — a real coverage gap in the existing patient flow, unrelated to M-Care, worth its own follow-up.

**M-Care super-agent, Phase 2 — one-shot booking entry, voice input, doctor availability by NL (2026-08-05).** Portia escalated further: "one input, no clicking through" should apply to patients booking AND partners managing their business, with voice as first-class, everything else optional. Three real findings reshaped this before building: (1) `/intake` was already conversational and already produces real "approve this card" doctor recommendations after ~6 questions — the actual gap was a one-shot entry point, bounded hard by `derivedFields.js`'s `SAFETY_INPUT_FIELDS` (clinical-history fields stay structured one-at-a-time picks, never inferred from open text, full stop); (2) only doctors have anything resembling real day/time availability data (`DoctorAvailability`) — the other 4 partner types have none at all, so NL-availability is doctor-only for now; (3) a prior assumption that voice input didn't exist anywhere in this repo was **wrong** — `src/components/procedures/VoiceMode.jsx` (browser `SpeechRecognition`) and `base44/functions/walkieTalkieTranslate/entry.ts` (`MediaRecorder` + Base44's own `Core.TranscribeAudio`/`Core.GenerateSpeech`) are both real, shipped features; M-Care itself was the only thing that had never had a mic.

**2A — one-shot entry** (`base44/functions/parseBookingIntent/entry.ts` + `src/components/mcare/BookingIntentEntry.jsx`, "📋 Book a procedure" quick action): one sentence ("dental veneers in Mexico") gets parsed into ONLY `procedure`/`destination_country` — this function's response schema is hardcoded, not caller-suppliable like `intakeConversationTurn`'s `target_fields`, specifically so it can never be tricked into extracting a clinical fact. M-Care shows the parse back ("Got it — Porcelain Veneers in Mexico. Sound right?") for explicit confirmation before navigating — an LLM guess at "which procedure" is proposed, never silently applied, same discipline as the existing `aiProcedureFallback` cards on `/procedures`. Confirmed values seed `/intake` via its **existing** `?doctor_id=&doctor=&country=` URL-seed effect in `ConciergeIntake.jsx` (extended with a `procedure` param) and `useIntakeSession`'s existing `seedAnswers()` — zero changes to `questionGraph.js`/`flowEngine.js`/`DoctorPickStep.jsx`; every remaining question, medical history included, is still asked exactly as before. Discover.jsx/Providers.jsx (classic search-UI directory) are unchanged — kept as the secondary/optional browse path, not removed.

**2B — voice input** (`base44/functions/transcribeVoiceInput/entry.ts` + `src/components/mcare/VoiceInputButton.jsx`): built on the `walkieTalkieTranslate` pattern (`MediaRecorder` + `Core.TranscribeAudio`), not `SpeechRecognition` — recognition has zero Firefox support and inconsistent iOS Safari support (this repo already codes around that elsewhere with manual-text fallbacks). Transcribe-only, no translation/TTS/session — a thin sibling, not a fork. Stays `requireAuth: false` (same audience as M-Care's own text chat) with `createHandler`'s default public rate limit intact. Wired into M-Care's chat input and both `BookingIntentEntry.jsx`/`AvailabilityIntentEntry.jsx`'s one-shot boxes — transcribed text fills the input for review, never auto-sends. Real prerequisite fixed alongside this: `android/app/src/main/AndroidManifest.xml` had no `RECORD_AUDIO` permission — the exact same silent-failure bug class already hit once here for GPS (`ACCESS_FINE_LOCATION`) — added, `required="false"` on the hardware feature so nothing needs a mic to install.

**2C — doctor availability by natural language** (`base44/functions/parseAvailabilityIntent/entry.ts` + `applyDoctorAvailability/entry.ts` + `src/components/mcare/AvailabilityIntentEntry.jsx`, "🗓️ Update my availability," doctors only): same parse-then-confirm-then-act split as 2A. `parseAvailabilityIntent` is LLM, parse-only, zero write calls (redteam-checked) — extracts day-of-week + a bounded week count (1–12, default 8) from a sentence like "free Tuesday and Thursday for the next month." The doctor sees the plain-English plan and confirms it. Only then does `applyDoctorAvailability` — fully deterministic, no LLM — expand it into real `DoctorAvailability` rows via the exact existing entity, so `DoctorAvailabilityCalendar.jsx` needs zero changes. Two invariants a redteam test guards structurally: `doctor_id` is derived from `Doctor.filter({ email: user.email })`, never a caller-supplied field (`DoctorAvailability`'s own RLS is currently wide open — a separate, pre-existing gap, not fixed here — so this app-layer check is load-bearing); and a date a patient's case already has locked (`locked_case_id`, set by `confirmProcedureDate`) is always skipped and counted, never silently overwritten — a bulk "I'm free Tuesdays" update must never unlock an already-booked date out from under a confirmed patient.

**Deliberately deferred out of Phase 2**: NL-availability for the other 4 partner types (no day/time data model exists for them at all yet — real new-data-model work, not an incremental add); voice *output* from M-Care (the ask was voice input, not new TTS replies); removing Discover.jsx/Providers.jsx (kept as the optional/secondary path, matching Portia's own "optional, never required" principle); `/booking`'s 12-step wizard retirement or bringing `/intake` up to its SAFE-T-input parity (BMI/pregnancy/lifestyle/emotional-concerns — `/intake` still doesn't ask any of these; `/booking` does) — surfaced again during this research, still a real, separate, unresolved gap. A new M-Care logo (gold "M" inside a globe/network graphic) replaced the styled `<span>M</span>`/`<div>M</div>` marks in `MCareOrb.jsx` (orb button + panel header) and `SpecialistCard.jsx` — scoped to M-Care's own icon only, not the site's main brand mark elsewhere. The source screenshot Portia shared was 1.24MB; resized to a 256×256 PNG (`public/mcare-logo.png`, ~170KB) before adding it, since it's an asset `MCareOrb` mounts on every page.

**M-Care super-agent, Phase 3 — one routing brain instead of three mode-switching buttons (2026-08-05).** Portia's reaction to the shipped Phase 2 work: it "feels like a chat bot... not like an operation system on its own like you and Boardy" — then, pointing at the actual panel, "the window is so small its like an mediocre AI system." Two separate, real problems: (1) `MCareOrb.jsx` branched on a `mode` state — `booking_intent`/`availability_intent`/`doctor_signup` each fully replaced the panel, reachable ONLY by clicking the matching quick-action button first; typing "I want veneers in Cancun" into the plain message box just answered a question about booking, it never started one. (2) the panel itself was a fixed 360×540 corner card whose default view was a vertical wall of canned "Quick questions" pills — the exact visual language of a generic FAQ widget, regardless of what was happening underneath. Fix for (1) already existed in this codebase as a proven pattern: `/demo/m-recon`'s `runMReconLoop` (`base44/functions/_shared/mRecon.ts`) is a real tool-calling loop where an LLM decides for itself which tool applies and narrates why. `base44/functions/_shared/mCareRouter.ts` + `routeMCareMessage/entry.ts` generalize the *discipline* of that pattern (fixed hardcoded toolset never caller-suppliable, injectable `decide` fn, fail-closed to a safe default on any error) — but deliberately NOT its iteration loop: mRecon's tools return data that feeds further reasoning (gather-then-brief), while M-Care's three routes are mutually-exclusive one-shot hand-offs, so `decideRoute` makes exactly one decision (`route` or `answer`) rather than looping pointlessly over the same question. On a KB miss, `MCareOrb.jsx`'s `sendMessage` now calls `routeMCareMessage` before falling through to the existing `moralesAssist`/`InvokeLLM` answer path (step 4/5, untouched) — a `route` decision narrates its `reasoning` as a chat bubble, then hands off into the exact same `BookingIntentEntry`/`AvailabilityIntentEntry`/`DoctorSignupChatFlow` components Phase 1/2 already built, now also seeded with the message the user already typed (`initialQuery` prop, auto-runs once) so they never have to repeat themselves. Every actual write (`applyDoctorAvailability`, `submitDoctorSignup`, `Doctor.create`) keeps its unchanged explicit confirm-then-act step — this is a routing/narration layer on top of Phase 1/2, never a bypass of their safety discipline. Role-gating moved server-side: `routeMCareMessage` is `requireAuth: false` (a visitor must reach booking/signup routing) but calls `base44.auth.me()` internally to derive the caller's real role and only offers `startAvailabilityIntent` to an actual `doctor`, since a message can now reach any route without a button click first — redteam-guarded (`M-CARE ROUTER: routing never bypasses role-gating or writes anything itself`). Fix for (2): the empty-state pill wall was replaced with a short greeting (reusing the already-translated `tips[0]` — zero new i18n strings needed) plus the real capability shortcuts (book / signup / availability / speak-to-specialist) shown first; the generic FAQ questions moved behind a collapsed "▾" disclosure instead of dominating the first impression. The docked panel also gained a header expand toggle (`Maximize2`/`Minimize2`, 380×560 default up to 560×760) — deliberately NOT a full-screen takeover, since "always there, never blocks what you're doing" is core to what M-Care is.

**M-Care super-agent, Phase 4 — real consent, real narration, honest about what's fake (2026-08-05).** Portia sent a full example script of the M-Care experience she wants (one-shot multi-procedure booking, a "Background Mode" that scans 23 dentists + flights + hotels for 15 minutes, explicit medical-history consent before a doctor is assigned, a proactively-narrated trip from pickup to Golden M). Three Explore passes over the real codebase found which pieces are genuinely real today, which are close, and which don't exist at all — building the ones that don't exist by faking them would repeat exactly the mistake the Demo Pages audit above documents and fixed. **What's not real and was deliberately NOT built**: live flight/hotel search (the only references anywhere are a code comment — "in production use Amadeus/Sabre API" — and a fully commented-out FlightStats stub in `checkFlightStatus/entry.ts` needing env vars never set; every flight/hotel number today is either hand-typed by a human travel-agency partner or a hardcoded rate table in `calculateTravelPackagePrice` — this needs Portia to choose a real vendor and get API credentials before any code can call one); a general background-job architecture (`createHandler.ts` is plain request/response, and the one entity shaped like a job queue, `EmailQueue.jsonc`, has zero implementing function — gated on a vendor decision above actually existing to justify it, not worth building against nothing); charging a saved card from inside a chat message (should reuse the existing Stripe checkout path exactly, not a new charge call — deferred). **What shipped, all built on infrastructure that was already real**:

- **4A — richer one-shot booking extraction.** `parseBookingIntent` (Phase 2A) widened from single-procedure + country to an array of up to 3 procedures, plus destination_city and a travel_month/travel_period pair — still fully hardcoded/allowlisted output, still cross-checked against `SAFETY_INPUT_FIELDS`. Deliberate and load-bearing: travel_month/travel_period are **never** combined into a fabricated exact date. `questionGraph.js`'s `preferred_date` step is a real DATE input — inventing a specific day from "early November" and seeding it would both put words in the patient's mouth and wrongly SKIP that question via `flowEngine`'s "already answered" logic. So destination_city/travel_month/travel_period are echoed back in `BookingIntentEntry.jsx`'s confirm bubble only (the patient feels heard) and never seeded into `/intake` — the real question graph still asks every one of those questions normally. Only `procedures`/`destination_country` are seeded, via `?procedures=a,b&country=` (comma-separated; `?procedure=` singular kept as a fallback for older links).
- **4B — consent-gated medical-history sharing before a doctor is assigned.** Real gap found: `assignDoctorToCase` (admin-triggered, the single real choke point where a doctor gets emailed + portal access to a case) auto-notified the doctor the moment `safe_t_result === 'PASSED'`, with no patient sign-off scoped to that specific act — only the existing blanket `data_processing_consent` captured once at the very start of intake (`DataProcessingConsent.jsx`, before any question is even asked). Fixed with a SEPARATE, later consent: `MedicalHistoryShareConsent.jsx`, shown at `ReviewStep.jsx` right after the patient sees exactly what they disclosed (`medical_conditions`/`allergies` are already in `REVIEW_FIELDS`), gates the "Send to My Care Team" submit button, and is recorded on the Consultation the same durable/auditable way as `data_processing_consent` (`medical_history_share_consent` + timestamp, via `fieldMap.js`'s `buildConsultationPayload`). `assignDoctorToCase` now refuses to notify a doctor without it — a missing/false consent flags `Admin-Review` with an explanatory `admin_notes` entry and returns an explicit 400, same fail-to-human-review discipline as its existing "no doctor available" branch, never a silent block or a silent share. Scoped to `/intake` only, matching every other `/intake`-vs-`/booking` scoping decision in this file — `/booking`'s parity gap is still separately open, still not this pass.
- **4C — M-Care becomes a recognizable voice in the real trip lifecycle, not just the chat panel.** The 9-checkpoint handshake (`completeHandshake`) and post-op recovery check-ins (`schedulePostOpCheckIns`) already sent real push notifications/emails — but every push in this app rendered with the same hardcoded site mark (`sw.js`'s `push` listener), with no way to tell a genuine M-Care moment from a generic system alert. Added real, small, additive plumbing: `sendPushNotification` now forwards an optional `icon` field through the webpush payload (undefined for every existing caller — zero behavior change unless a sender opts in), `sw.js` reads `data.icon || '/morales-m-mark.png'`, and the handshake's 5 patient-facing checkpoint pushes plus a **new** push alongside the Day-3 recovery check-in email (previously email-only — a patient who doesn't check email promptly had no other signal) now pass `/mcare-logo.png` — the same icon the chat panel itself uses, so the OS notification visibly matches the identity the patient already talks to. **Correction made mid-build, not glossed over**: the plan assumed `interpretRecoveryCheckIn` only accepted structured sliders and needed extending for free text — reading the actual code found it already accepts and narrates a free-text `note` field (`RecoveryCheckIn.jsx`'s step 3 textarea). The real remaining gap is that recovery check-ins live on a separate token-gated page reached by email link, not inside M-Care's own conversation — genuinely bigger, separate work (detecting "this logged-in user has a pending PostOpCheckIn," exposing it as a router tool), deliberately left for its own future pass rather than rushed in here.

**Hard rule for any new sender, not just this one:** `base44/shared/notify.ts`'s `linkOnlyEmail`/`linkOnlySms` are mandatory — a generic line + a portal/resume link, `assertLinkOnly()` throws on anything identity/amount/date-shaped. `sendOnboardingNudges` and `checkAbandonedBookings` predate this file; the former still calls `SendEmail` directly with AI-personalized (Claude Haiku) content and is a known, tracked gap, not a pattern to copy. `checkStalledSignups` was built link-only and fully deterministic from day one — a brand-new function has no excuse to add a fresh violation. Redteam guards this: `M RESCUE: checkStalledSignups is link-only...` in `tests/redteam/invariants.spec.js`.

## Transport Partner Platform — Foundation (2026-08-05)

Portia asked for a large, generic "multi-agent operations platform" — 7 roles, 6 agent modules, 12 new entities (Partner/Driver/Vehicle/ServiceArea/VerificationDocument/Trip/TripOffer/Assignment/CheckIn/Incident/Notification/Escalation), 5 new UI surfaces — plus platform assumptions ("entity hooks and scheduled automations") that **don't exist in this Base44 project**: every scheduled job runs via GitHub Actions cron hitting a `cronAuthorized()`-gated function (see the Travel Safety Escalation section above), not a built-in Base44 automation resource. Building toward that fictional resource would be building on a platform feature that isn't real. (Note: `base44/agents/` itself — a real native Base44 "Agent" resource, distinct from the automation-hooks idea above — was added 2026-08-07; see M-Care Phase 7 below. The backend resource directories are now `agents/`, `connectors/`, `entities/`, `functions/`, `shared/`.)

Three Explore passes found the transport-partner domain **already ~70% built** under the name `TaxiService` — company-level verification pipeline (`verification_status`/`identity_verification_status`/`background_check_status`/`license_verification_status`), a real generic `PartnerVerification` entity + `initiatePartnerVerification` (sanctions screening via `runSanctionsScreening`, then AI fraud-scan via `analyzePartnerDocuments`, generic across doctor/travel_agency/taxi_service/companion/security_agency), `assignChauffeurServices`/`findDriverBackup`/`checkPartnerSLABreaches`/`escalateMissedDriverHandshake` (assignment + SLA monitoring/recovery, simplistic but real and scheduled), and `AdminPartners.jsx` (the operations dashboard). Building 12 parallel entities would have duplicated real, working infrastructure — this pass extends `TaxiService` instead.

**The real, load-bearing bug found and fixed**: verification was fake in two places, exactly the failure mode "never represent an unverified background check as verified" rules out. (1) `TaxiServiceSignupStep3.jsx` wrote `license_verified`/`insurance_verified` straight from the APPLICANT'S OWN "I have a valid license" / "I have valid insurance" checkboxes at signup — self-attestation treated as verification, before `initiatePartnerVerification`'s real sanctions/fraud-scan pipeline had even run. (2) `AdminPartners.jsx`'s `handleApproveTaxiService` then compounded it: a single unconditional `TaxiService.update({status:'active', license_verified:true, insurance_verified:true})` on approval, never reading what the real `PartnerVerification` record actually found (fraud score, sanctions status) — approving was a rubber stamp regardless of the pipeline's own output. Fixed: signup now writes `license_self_attested`/`insurance_self_attested` (new fields — a required claim, not a verification); `license_verified`/`insurance_verified` are set true in exactly one place, `reviewTaxiServiceVerification/entry.ts` (new, admin-only), which refuses to approve if the linked `PartnerVerification.verification_status` is `sanctions_blocked`/`denied`. `AdminPartners.jsx` now calls that function instead of writing the entity directly, and gained a real Reject path for taxi services (previously only doctors had one).

**Formal state machine**: `base44/shared/partnerOnboardingState.ts` mirrors `bookingState.ts`'s shape (`canTransition`/`assertTransition`, plus `guardedPartnerStatusUpdate` parameterized by entity name so it can extend to other partner types later) applied to `TaxiService.status` instead of `CaseRecord`'s booking state. `status`'s enum grew from `pending_verification/active/inactive` to add `verifying`/`pending_setup`/`suspended`/`rejected` — a taxi service can no longer reach `active` directly from `pending_verification`, it must pass through `verifying` (`initiatePartnerVerification` advances it there once real verification kicks off; `reviewTaxiServiceVerification`'s approve branch is what reaches `active`). `pending_setup` is declared but deliberately **not** a mandatory hop — there's no real post-verification setup-checklist behind it yet, and inserting a mandatory step with nothing real behind it would be its own kind of fake completeness.

**New `Driver.jsonc` entity** — `TaxiService` had exactly one `driver_name`/`driver_license_number` STRING pair on the company row, no way to represent an actual fleet. `Driver` is FK'd via `taxi_service_id` (app-level queries) plus a denormalized `taxi_service_email` (the RLS ownership anchor — Base44 RLS conditions compare data fields directly against the caller's session, no joins, so the owning company's email is copied onto each driver row at creation). `license_verified` on a `Driver` row is real-shaped the same way — default false, only ever settable by a future admin review action, never by the company adding its own roster entry. `src/components/partner-dashboard/DriverRosterSection.jsx` (new, wired into `TaxiServiceDashboard.jsx`) is the first UI that reads/writes it — add/view/activate/deactivate drivers, no edit/delete yet (kept minimal for this pass).

**Deliberately deferred, not silently forgotten** (matches the plan's own "explicitly deferred" list): Trip/TripOffer/Assignment with real driver-side accept/decline/timeout — confirmed **zero driver signal exists anywhere today**, every one of the 9 handshake checkpoints is patient-tap-only, a prior `OfflineHandshake handshake_role:'driver'` concept was dead code with zero producers; a real Driver Handoff mobile page depends on that same missing signal. Both are genuinely new capability, not an extension, and belong in their own pass once this foundation is live. Smarter zone/scoring matching in `assignChauffeurServices`/`findDriverBackup` (real, working, simplistic today). A unified Notification Agent (email/SMS/push are each real but independently composed per call site, no single dispatcher). Hotel/accommodation as a distinct partner type — no existing infrastructure at all, a new domain. Clinic/care-coordinator as a role distinct from `Doctor` — `Doctor` already substantially covers this.

## M-Care super-agent, Phase 5 — real narration, a second partner-signup persona, live-chat presence (2026-08-06)

Portia's feedback on Phase 3's routing brain: it still read as "input-output" — every reply that needed a network call showed the same generic 3-dot spinner the whole time, then jumped straight to a final answer, regardless of how smart the routing behind it was. Fixed in `MCareOrb.jsx`'s `sendMessage`: a new `thinkingStatus` state narrates the REAL step in flight ("Let me see what you need..." while the routing call runs, then the routing decision's own reasoning before handing off — with a short readability pause instead of an instant teleport — or "Thinking that through..." while the answer call runs). Every status string is tied to a real async call already happening; nothing here is a fabricated delay. **A real gotcha hit while shipping this through the new sandbox bridge** (see below): `sandbox write` reported a 502 three times in a row, but the write had actually succeeded server-side each time — caught by reading the file back and diffing before assuming the CLI's own failure report was authoritative.

Portia then asked for the bigger thing directly: M-Care should handle sign-up and booking for **every** user and partner type, not just doctors. Doctor signup by chat (Phase 1) was the only partner type ever wired up — CLAUDE.md's own Phase 1 write-up already flagged "the other 4 partner types... Phase 2, same engine, new graph data per persona, not built yet" and it was never picked back up. This pass adds the **second** one: **travel agency signup by chat**, proving the pattern generalizes past doctor —

- `src/lib/mcareFlow/travelAgencySignupGraph.js` — a second, deliberately **separate** graph file from `doctorSignupGraph.js` (two instances doesn't justify forcing a shared abstraction yet, and this graph needs two input types the doctor graph never has). Adds `MULTI_SELECT` (service regions, services offered — commit only on an explicit "Continue," not per-tap) and `BOOLEAN` (emergency support; the legal confirmation) to what was a doctor-only `TEXT/EMAIL/PHONE/SELECT/FILE_UPLOAD` set. One real correctness issue worked through carefully: `flowEngine`'s `isFieldKnown` treats **any** non-empty value — `false` included — as "answered," so a plain BOOLEAN step would let a "No" on the required legal confirmation silently complete signup. Fixed with a `mustBeTrue` step flag: `TravelAgencySignupChatFlow.jsx` re-prompts instead of committing when a mustBeTrue step is answered false, matching the classic form's submit button staying disabled until checked.
- `src/lib/partnerSignup/submitTravelAgencySignup.js` — extracted from `TravelAgencySignupStep3.jsx`'s inline `handleSubmit`, byte-faithful to the original (same `TravelAgency.create` fields, same non-fatal `initiatePartnerVerification` call the original already made), same "one gated function, chat and form both call it" discipline as `submitDoctorSignup.js`. Unlike the doctor form (which relies on catching the shared function's `AUTH_REQUIRED` throw), this file already had its own pre-flight guest check before the extraction — kept as-is, so `submitTravelAgencySignup`'s own `AUTH_REQUIRED` throw is defense-in-depth here, not the primary gate; the redteam invariant checks for the actual shape present, not a copy-pasted assumption from the doctor pattern.
- `intakeConversationTurn` gained a third persona (`travel_agency_signup`) alongside `patient`/`doctor_signup` — same additive pattern as before, existing callers unaffected.
- `routeMCareMessage`/`mCareRouter.ts` gained `startTravelAgencySignup`, gated identically to `startDoctorSignup` (visitor/patient only, never an existing partner).

**Deliberately not built this pass, same honest-scope-cut discipline as every prior phase**: Taxi/Companion/SecurityAgency signup by chat — the reusable pieces now exist (graph shape, MULTI_SELECT/BOOLEAN rendering, the extract-a-shared-submit-function pattern proven twice), but each remaining partner type's classic form has real, non-trivial fields to map (taxi's step 2 alone has origin/destination-dependent per-leg dynamic pricing with a live total — genuinely complex UI logic, not a quick graph stub) and deserves its own careful pass rather than a rushed fourth-in-a-row copy. A 3rd instance is also the point where generalizing `DoctorSignupChatFlow.jsx`/`TravelAgencySignupChatFlow.jsx` into one shared component becomes worth doing, rather than a 3rd near-duplicate file.

**Panel presence**: opening M-Care now defaults to a genuinely taller window (`maxHeight` 560→680px, expanded 760→860px; width unchanged at ~380px) instead of needing a manual "expand" click to feel substantial — matches how real live-chat widgets (Intercom, Drift) default: modest width, but close to full viewport height, not a short card.

## M-Care super-agent, Phase 6 — a living orb, and a cinematic "big moment" language (2026-08-06)

Portia's reaction to Phase 5, with two reference screenshots: M-Care still read as a flat input-output text widget next to how clean the app's own dashboard/homepage moments look, and she wanted the orb itself to feel "3D talking like iphone" (Siri's glowing orb). Decided with her up front (both recommendations taken): the orb is built as **CSS + framer-motion, not WebGL/Three.js** — no new dependency, `framer-motion` (already installed, already used for near-identical concentric-ring effects in `BeatingHeartTracker.jsx`'s `HeartPulse` and `HeartRingAnimation.jsx`) drives layered rings + a glass core; and the cinematic "big moment" treatment **stays inside M-Care's own docked panel**, never a full-screen takeover — preserves the "always there, never blocks what you're doing" rule that's been load-bearing since Phase 3.

Research correction worth recording: the second screenshot ("Doctor confirmed." full-bleed slide, huge type + pulsing-dot status pill + pagination dots) is **not** `/demo/master-journey` (`MasterJourneyDemo.jsx` — a different chat-bubble/speech-synthesis demo, confirmed via grep that its literal strings don't exist anywhere in the codebase). It's the actual **homepage**: `src/components/home/LiveJourneyCard.jsx` supplies the exact huge-type/pulsing-dot/pill typography, `src/components/home/HowItWorksModal.jsx` supplies the fixed dark-vignette full-bleed overlay mechanics. Neither was a generic reusable primitive before this — both bespoke, single-purpose components.

Shipped, purely presentational — no routing/signup/submit logic touched:

- **`src/components/mcare/LivingOrb.jsx`** (new) — replaces the static gold-glow `<img>` logo in both the closed 56px floating button and the open panel's 32px header slot. A `state` prop (`idle`/`listening`/`thinking`/`speaking`) swaps only the animation config on the same DOM shape (2-3 staggered framer-motion rings + a glass core, styled on `HeartPulse`'s concentric-ring pattern but gold instead of green) — never a different component per state. `thinking` additionally gets a rotating conic-gradient sweep across the core, replacing the old flat three-dot `guideThink` pulse for that state. Respects `prefers-reduced-motion` — falls back to the original static `drop-shadow` glow with zero animation, matching this app's existing accessibility posture. `listening` is wired to `VoiceInputButton`'s real recording state (added an `onRecordingChange` callback prop there — additive, every other caller unaffected); `speaking` is a short, honest window right after a new assistant message lands, explicitly **not** an audio-reactive waveform — there's no real TTS signal for M-Care's text replies to react to, and faking one would be exactly the kind of dramatized-beyond-the-code claim the Demo Pages audit below exists to catch.
- **`src/components/mcare/OrbMoment.jsx`** (new) — a small, in-panel version of `LiveJourneyCard`'s huge-type/pill treatment, scaled down to fit M-Care's 380-600px panel instead of the viewport. Deliberately not a stepper/carousel (no fake "stage 1 of 3" — these are one-off confirmations, not a sequence) and the optional status pill only renders when there's a genuine ongoing state to reflect (e.g. verification actually in progress) — a pill with nothing real to say would be decoration, not information. Swapped in at 4 sites that already produced a real confirmation string and previously rendered it as a plain chat bubble: `MCareOrb.jsx`'s routing hand-off reasoning (`source: 'router'` messages), `DoctorSignupChatFlow.jsx` and `TravelAgencySignupChatFlow.jsx`'s completion message ("Application submitted." + a real "Verification in progress" status pill), and `BookingIntentEntry.jsx`'s parse-confirm bubble. Each is a rendering upgrade of an existing string, not new logic.

**Deliberately not done**: no WebGL/3D engine (per the decided approach); no full-screen takeover anywhere in M-Care (per the decided scope); no audio-reactive waveform (no real signal to drive one honestly). Manual/visual verification — whether the orb actually reads as "alive" and the panel as "clean" — can't be done from this environment (no local Base44 session, no browser tool); shipped live for Portia to judge directly, same standing limitation as every prior M-Care phase.

**Follow-up, same day**: seeing it live, Portia's read was that the panel still "looked like a chat bot stuck in the corner" — a static screenshot can't show the orb's animation, but the outer shape genuinely hadn't changed: still the same ~380px card docked bottom-left from Phase 5, since Phase 6 only touched what rendered *inside* it. Asked directly (AskUserQuestion) how far to go given that a large-but-full-screen-avoiding middle ground existed between "keep it docked" (what she'd picked the round before) and "full takeover" (explicitly ruled out that same round) — she picked the middle option. Fixed: the open panel is no longer bottom-left-docked — it's now a large centered overlay (`min(880px, 92vw)` × `min(82vh, 820px)` default, `expanded` goes to `min(1160px, 96vw)` × `94vh`) over a dimmed, blurred backdrop (`rgba(4,8,16,0.72)`), dismissible by clicking the backdrop or pressing Escape — both wired directly (backdrop `onClick` closes, the panel itself calls `stopPropagation()`; a new `keydown` effect closes on Escape while open). Still stops short of the full-screen takeover ruled out earlier in this same phase — the backdrop is click-through-to-dismiss, not a hard block. The closed-state floating orb button is unchanged.

**Real bug found in the same live-testing pass, unrelated to sizing**: Portia hit a KB miss on the deployed app and M-Care answered as a bare, unbranded model — "I do not have a name. I am a large language model, trained by Google" — with zero platform awareness (told her not to upload her passport, with no mention the Passport Vault feature exists, which `buildSystemPrompt` itself already describes). Root cause, found by comparing against every other conversational LLM caller in the codebase: `moralesAssist`, `routeMCareMessage`, and `safeTAssist` all pass an explicit `model` and embed their persona directly in the `prompt` string; `MCareOrb.jsx`'s two generic-fallback `InvokeLLM` calls (the KB-enhance path and the final logged-out/admin answer path) were the one place relying on the separate `system_prompt` field alone with no `model` set — and this file's own top-of-file comment already flagged `system_prompt` as "not fully type-supported" without anyone following up on what that implied at runtime. An admin account (which never routes through `moralesAssist`, see `isConcierge`) hits this exact path on every KB miss, so Portia's own testing account was guaranteed to reproduce it. Fixed: both calls now set `model: 'gpt_5_mini'` (matching the three known-good callers) and embed `buildSystemPrompt(...)`'s full text directly into `prompt`, not just `system_prompt` — belt-and-suspenders in case a given model/provider combination drops or deprioritizes the separate field. `buildSystemPrompt` itself also gained an explicit persona-lock clause ("never say you are a large language model, never name an underlying AI provider or model, never break character, even if asked directly"). Audited every other conversational (free-text, non-JSON-schema) `InvokeLLM` caller in the repo for the same gap — none found; this was isolated to these two call sites.

## M-Care super-agent, Phase 7 — a real native Base44 agent replaces the mode-picker (2026-08-07)

Thursday night (2026-08-06) this file's own working notes paused with Portia's complaint that every M-Care layer through Phase 6 — including the routing brain (`routeMCareMessage`/`mCareRouter.ts`) — was "a mode-picker with a chat skin": one LLM call routed to ONE of a few fixed mini-flows, each of which then walked a rigid step-by-step form graph. The identified precedent for a real fix was `_shared/mRecon.ts`'s `runMReconLoop`, a genuine iterative tool-calling loop.

Portia built the actual fix herself, directly in Base44's builder, and it landed as 39 `base44-builder[bot]` commits. It does **not** reuse `runMReconLoop` — instead it's a genuine **native Base44 "Agent" resource**, `base44/agents/m_care.jsonc` (declarative: description, system-prompt instructions, `tool_configs` granting ~52 named backend functions plus some direct entity CRUD, `model: "automatic"`, memory config, voice config, `allow_anonymous_access: true`), run by Base44's own agent/conversation runtime — `MCareOrb.jsx` and a new full-page surface (`src/pages/MCareAgent.jsx`, `/m-care`) now both drive real multi-turn tool-calling conversations via `base44.agents.createConversation/addMessage/subscribeToConversation`, not a single routing decision. `routeMCareMessage`/`_shared/mCareRouter.ts` are now dead code — no live caller invokes them (only a redteam test statically reads `routeMCareMessage`'s source). A real learning loop shipped alongside it: `mcareResearchAndLearn`/`recallMcareKnowledge`/`McareKnowledge` (recall-first, research-with-live-web-context second, persist only at ≥80% self-reported confidence).

**Known gap surfaced, not yet closed**: the new agent's 52-tool list has no equivalent of Phase 1/5's doctor/travel-agency **signup-by-chat** (`submitDoctorSignup`/`submitTravelAgencySignup` and their chat-flow components have no path in). May be a deliberate scope narrowing toward the traveler/M-Safe use case, or a dropped feature — unconfirmed with Portia, worth asking before assuming either way.

**Correction (2026-08-08, see "WhatsApp check-ins, Crisis Reroutes dashboard, and Family Portal rebrand" below)**: this was flagged as an unreconciled duplicate of the guardian system — re-read in full, it isn't one. `TravelerGuardian.jsx` is the *patient's own* screen for activating milestone check-ins; `ShareLiveLocation.jsx` is a *patient-consented*, distress-triggered GPS beacon for the care team. Neither is guardian-facing, so there was never a UI/logic overlap with `GuardianView.jsx` to merge — only a naming collision ("Guardian" appearing in both families' copy), fixed by renaming `TravelerGuardian.jsx`'s user-facing text. `LiveLocationRequest`'s consent model is still token-possession-only (no explicit consent flag checked before serving a patient's last-known GPS) — narrower than this repo's usual consent-gating standard, still not tightened, still a real open item.

**Security fixes applied same day, before this was trusted further**: a three-pass audit of the diff found `runDoctorVerificationScan` (writes `Doctor.verification_status: 'verified'`/`status: 'active'` via `asServiceRole`, bypassing RLS) had **zero role/ownership check** — combined with the agent's `allow_anonymous_access: true`, any caller could pass an arbitrary `doctor_id` and auto-activate someone else's doctor record, reopening exactly the hole `initiatePartnerVerification`'s HMAC-gated auto-activation hand-off to `activateVerifiedDoctor` was built to close. Fixed with a self-or-admin ownership gate (`user.email === doctor.email || user.role is admin/platform_admin`). `reviewTravelAgencyVerification` had the same shape of gap for agency approval — fixed with `allowedRoles: ['admin','platform_admin']`, mirroring the existing `reviewTaxiServiceVerification` precedent exactly. Separately, 14 of the 17 new backend functions bypassed the mandatory `createHandler` middleware (raw `Deno.serve`/bare `export default`) — the same anti-pattern that caused a real silent-404 incident (see Edge Functions section) — all 14 migrated onto `createHandler`, preserving each function's existing `requireAuth`/response-shape behavior exactly except the two gates above.

**Deliberately deferred (medium/low severity, not fixed this pass)**: `sendClientBookingProposal` sends the dollar amount inline in SMS/email rather than through the mandatory link-only `notify.ts` pattern; `analyzeDocumentIntelligence`/`crossCheckFraudIndicators` let any authenticated non-admin user write to/read the nominally admin-only `FraudIndicator` table; `McareKnowledge`'s RLS lets any authenticated user directly create a confidence-100 entry, bypassing the ≥80%-research gate that's supposed to be the only path in. None of these are reachable without an authenticated session, unlike the two fixed above.

## M-Care super-agent, Phase 8 — closing the write-access gap Phase 7 missed, plus Boardy-inspired judgment (2026-08-08)

Portia asked for M-Care to be "robust like Boardy AI's super agent" and wanted a real deep dive on how Boardy actually works, not just vibes. Research (web search, founder interviews, a real engineering job posting — the most concrete technical artifact found, since Boardy has published no architecture docs) found its "feels like an agent, not a chatbot" quality traces to three concrete, separable mechanics: unprompted outreach, cross-session memory continuity, and — the one founder Andrew D'Souza credits most — the ability to independently push back. His framing: *"I don't even think of Boardy as an agent. I think of Boardy as an AI principal... an agent executes what you ask, a principal has independent goals and can refuse."* Also surfaced as a necessary counterweight: Boardy's worst PR incident (a sexist Inauguration-Day marketing email) came from the exact same proactive/personality-forward instinct that makes it feel alive — proactivity and personality cut both ways, directly relevant to a platform handling sensitive health/travel data. Portia chose to prioritize the judgment/pushback piece, paired with closing M-Care's raw entity-write access — more autonomy needs stronger rails first, not after.

**The real finding, more serious than expected**: re-reading `base44/agents/m_care.jsonc` directly (not a summary) during this work showed Phase 7's own security fixes from the day before were incomplete. Yesterday's fixes (self-or-admin gate on `runDoctorVerificationScan`, admin-only gate on `reviewTravelAgencyVerification`) were correct but insufficient — the agent's `tool_configs` *also* granted unrestricted raw `create`+`update` directly on the `Doctor` and `TravelAgency` entities, with no field-level restriction available in Base44's tool_configs schema. That meant the LLM could call a raw entity-update tool with `{"verification_status": "verified", "status": "active"}` on any record, completely bypassing both of yesterday's fixed functions — the prompt's own rule 9 ("You NEVER mark anyone verified yourself") was doing all the work, and a prompt instruction was never supposed to be the only thing standing between a request and a consequential write (see Safety-Decision Architecture above). The SAFE-T risk decision itself was never at risk — `SafeTScreening` was never in the agent's entity list — this was specifically a partner-verification-status gap.

**Fixed**: two new gated edge functions, `mcareCreateDoctorPending` and `mcareCreateTravelAgencyPending`, are the Deno/agent-callable equivalents of the existing browser-only `src/lib/partnerSignup/submitDoctorSignup.js`/`submitTravelAgencySignup.js` (same hardcoded-`pending_verification`-only invariant, same non-fatal `UserOnboardingProfile`+`syncTenantRole` role-grant side effect that fixed the 2026-07-24 Partner Onboarding Lockout bug, same fraud-check/verification-kickoff calls). `Doctor`/`TravelAgency`'s `tool_configs` entity grants are now `read`-only; the two new functions are the only creation path, and the system prompt's DOCTOR/TRAVEL AGENCY sections now call them explicitly instead of describing direct entity creation.

**Deliberately deferred, same shape, not silently forgotten**: `TaxiService`, `Companion`, `SecurityAgency`, and `ExternalJourney` have the identical unrestricted `create`+`update` grant in the same file. Same fix pattern would apply — a gated creation function per type, `read`-only entity access for the agent — but this wasn't in today's scoped priority. Worth a dedicated pass before any of those partner types get more agent-driven capability.

## M-Care super-agent, Phase 9 — human-feeling message reactions (deterministic, not AI-decided) (2026-08-08)

Portia had a live WhatsApp conversation with Boardy AI and noticed it sometimes reacts to her messages with an emoji tapback "like a human" — asked for the same on M-Care's chat panel. Given the choice between the LLM deciding the reaction itself vs. a fast deterministic rule on our side, Portia picked deterministic: it ships with zero risk to `m_care.jsonc`'s giant single-line `instructions` field (which already produced one real merge conflict against Base44 builder autosaves this same week, see Phase 8 above), and the agent never needs to know it happened.

`src/lib/mcareReactionHeuristic.js`'s `pickMessageReaction(text)` is a small, pure, tested function — same input always produces the same output, no network call. A `DENY_PATTERNS` list (pain, bleeding, emergency, allergy, fear, complaint, refund, anger, legal threat, etc.) is checked first and wins unconditionally, even over text that would otherwise also match a positive rule — the same fail-closed instinct as everywhere else in this file: a wrong or tone-deaf reaction on something a patient may be genuinely worried about is worse than no reaction at all. Past that gate, a short ordered list of positive rules (gratitude, laughing, strong enthusiasm, agreement, greeting, a question) picks an emoji; most messages match nothing, which is intentional — reactions should feel occasional, like a human noticing something worth reacting to, not a reflex on every message.

Wired into `src/components/mcare-agent/MessageBubble.jsx` (the one component both `MCareOrb.jsx` and `MCareAgent.jsx` already share for every message) as a new `showReaction` prop, following the file's existing "all-optional, default rendering unchanged" contract (`accent`/`showAvatar`/`showMeta`) — off unless a caller opts in, and both M-Care surfaces now do. Renders as a small `framer-motion` badge on the corner of the patient's own bubble, popping in after a short delay (the same "let it feel like someone actually read it" pause used since Phase 5) rather than appearing instantly. Only ever reacts to the human's own message, never the assistant's — matching the Boardy behavior being copied. Nothing is persisted — the reaction is recomputed deterministically from `message.content` on render, so there's no new entity field and nothing to migrate.

**Added**: a new Rule 13 ("PROFESSIONAL JUDGMENT") in the system prompt, grounded only in real tool data already available (never a hunch, per rule 3's no-invented-data discipline) — M-Care now proactively flags a recovery-timeline conflict against `getProcedureKnowledge`'s `recovery_time`, pauses instead of auto-continuing when `verifyExternalJourney` returns `overall: 'concerns'` on a BYOJ enrollment, and mentions when a presented doctor was very recently verified. Explicit closing clause: this never weakens the Safety Gate, Consent Gate, or verification requirements — it only ever adds caution, matching the same non-negotiable boundary as everything else in this section.

## M-Care super-agent, Phase 10 — root cause of a fully silent agent, and a safety net for the next one (2026-08-08)

Immediately after Phase 8 shipped, M-Care went completely silent for Portia — a message would send, the "coordinating" indicator would clear, and nothing would ever come back. No error, no reply. Root cause: Phase 8 added `mcareCreateDoctorPending`/`mcareCreateTravelAgencyPending` and registered them in `m_care.jsonc`'s `tool_configs` in the same commit — but per this file's own Deploying & Environment section, a repo push only *syncs* code into the Base44 Builder, it doesn't make it live; new edge functions need a separate Publish. The two functions existed in the repo and in the agent's declared toolset, but weren't deployed yet, and the agent's tool-resolution step appears to fail the *entire* conversation turn when a declared tool doesn't actually exist server-side — silently, with nothing surfaced back to the chat UI. Portia found and fixed this herself directly in Base44 (confirmed via both functions now returning 400-needs-params instead of 404-not-found when pinged, and the agent replying normally again).

**The durable lesson, not just this one incident**: adding a new function to an agent's `tool_configs` is a two-step operation with a real gap in between — pushing the code and registering the tool name is not the same as the tool existing yet, and unlike a normal broken API call, the failure mode here isn't a clean error, it's the agent going fully mute. Any future M-Care backend function addition needs the same order of operations Phase 8 skipped: deploy/Publish the function *before* (or atomically with) wiring it into `tool_configs`, and verify with a direct ping (expect 400/401 for "exists, rejects bad input," not 404) before trusting the agent to use it.

**Fixed in the same pass**: while investigating this live, a real defense-in-depth gap was found — `sendAgentMessage`'s and `startNewJourney`'s catch blocks in `MCareOrb.jsx`, and the equivalent ones in `MCareAgent.jsx`, swallowed any send/create failure with no user-facing feedback at all. Wired in `src/lib/friendlyError.js` (the existing, established utility for exactly this — turns a raw error, including Base44's own documented 402 integration-credit gate, into a safe sentence; already used the same way in `ConsultationFeeModal.jsx`) via a `toast`, so *any* future cause of a failed send — not just an undeployed tool — surfaces something to the user instead of dead silence. Portia independently shipped her own, better version of the same idea directly in Base44 the same day: the conversation subscription now only clears the "sending" spinner once an actual assistant reply lands (not just on any update), a 12s safety-net re-fetch catches a subscription that missed the agent's turn, and a 40s last-resort timeout posts an honest in-chat fallback message. Both landed together via a real (successfully reconciled) merge conflict on this exact file.

## M-Care super-agent, Phase 11 — a real human-handoff path (2026-08-08)

Portia asked for a competitive research pass on comparable customer-facing conversational agents (Sierra, Decagon, Expedia's Romie) to find concrete, applicable ideas — same scoped pattern as the Phase 8 Boardy research, explicitly *not* the unfounded "beat every SOTA benchmark, rewrite the core in Python, agent writes its own tools" framing from an unrelated viral prompt template she'd also pasted in, which was declined for good reason (no Python exists in this repo, M-Care isn't a custom-built agent loop to rewrite, and a tool-generating agent is the exact capability class the Phase 8 write-access fix exists to prevent).

The research (full sourced findings in that session's transcript) surfaced one universal, zero-disagreement pattern across every system checked — Sierra, Decagon, even Kayak and Booking.com: an explicit request to speak with a human is honored immediately, no argument, regardless of whether the AI believes it could resolve the issue itself (Decagon's own stated priority order: *"first, perfect the escalation path, second, improve accuracy, and finally, optimize for speed"*; *"it is always better for a bot to admit it doesn't know an answer and escalate than to provide a wrong one."*).

Checking M-Care against this surfaced a real, confirmed bug in the same family as Phase 10's: `m_care.jsonc`'s own instructions already described `flagIntakeHandoff` under "you call it" — but it was never actually granted in `tool_configs` (confirmed by enumerating all granted `function_name` entries). The instructions described a capability the agent could not actually use. `flagIntakeHandoff` itself needed no changes — it's a real, already-built, already-tested function (`requireAuth: false`, `bodySchema`-validated, only `user_email` required) that does exactly what Sierra/Decagon call a "warm transfer": emails the care team the full `turn_history`/`answers`/`reason` so the patient never repeats themselves, flags the session, writes an audit entry, creates a `DoctorReviewTask` — every side effect beyond the email already non-fatal/best-effort.

**Fixed**: `flagIntakeHandoff` added to `tool_configs`, and a new **Rule 14 ("HUMAN HANDOFF")** added to the instructions — any direct request for a human triggers an immediate handoff (no talking the patient out of it, no resolve-it-first attempt), and genuine uncertainty on something consequential not already covered by a more specific rule also routes here rather than guessing. Explicit closing clause, matching Rule 13's pattern: never overrides the Safety Gate, Consent Gate, or verification requirements — this is a human-escalation path, not a safety decision.

**Also surfaced, worth Portia knowing**: neither Sierra nor Decagon has real public evidence of their agent independently pushing back or adding unsolicited caveats beyond enforcing a preset policy guardrail — M-Care's Rule 13 (Phase 8) is genuinely ahead of what's publicly documented for either category leader on that specific front, not behind.

## Guardian email re-access + procedure risk display (2026-08-08)

Closed the last two items from Portia's original 4-part M-Care backlog (WhatsApp check-ins and the Crisis Reroutes dashboard shipped earlier the same week). Both were reframed after research found the original ask ("Family Portal login," "add risk factors") didn't match what already existed — see below.
