**Guardian access has no login precedent anywhere in this app, so it doesn't get one here.** The existing system (`GuardianView.jsx` / `GuardianSession` / `getGuardianViewData`) is 100% opaque-token-based by design — a patient mints a link, the guardian opens it, no account is ever created or checked, and `GuardianSession` carries no field that could tie a session to a `User`. Every other "magic link ↔ real account" pair in this repo (doctor, travel agency, taxi portals) turned out on inspection to be two parallel, never-bridged systems — not a real precedent for graduating a token into a login — so building true account-based login here would have been genuinely new auth surface with real deadline risk. Decided with Portia: ship the lighter option instead. `requestGuardianAccess` (`base44/functions/requestGuardianAccess/entry.ts`, `requireAuth: false`, `bodySchema`-validated) lets a guardian who lost their link re-enter the email a patient already shared their journey with; if it matches one or more active `GuardianSession` rows, each gets a fresh `/guardian/{token}` link emailed via `linkOnlyEmail` (a brand-new sender, so it follows the mandatory link-only rule from day one — unlike the older `generateGuardianLink`, which predates that module and still emails patient name + link directly; not touched here, not a pattern to copy going forward). Anti-enumeration: the response is identical whether or not the email matched anything, and a per-email `RateLimitBucket` cap (5/hour) sits alongside `createHandler`'s own per-IP default. New page `src/pages/GuardianAccess.jsx` at `/guardian-access` (registered in `tokenRoutes.jsx`, deliberately **not** named "Family Portal" — that name already refers to the notification/messaging thread inside `GuardianView.jsx` itself, per `GuardianMessage`'s own entity description). `GuardianView.jsx`'s expired-link state now points here. Nothing about `GuardianSession`, `getGuardianViewData`, `getGuardianFeed`, `sendGuardianMessage`, or `requestGuardianSecurityCheck` changed — this is a second way in to the exact same token-gated system, not a new one. Known gap, same shape as `generateGuardianLink`'s own free-text `guardian_email` field: the lookup is an exact match on the caller's lowercased email, so an unusual casing on the patient's side when they originally shared it is the one way a real guardian could still come up empty.

**Procedure risk display is plumbing only — no risk content was authored.** The right field already existed (`ProcedureKnowledge` entity: `risk_level`, `risk_factors`, `complication_rate`, `common_complications`, fully schema-complete) with a working admin CRUD page (`AdminProcedureKnowledge.jsx`, `/admin/procedure-knowledge`), but zero rows are seeded anywhere in the repo, and the real patient-facing catalog (`src/components/procedures/ProcedureData.jsx`, 69 procedures) is a completely separate hardcoded file with no risk fields at all — four disconnected procedure data sources exist in this app total (`ProcedureData.jsx`, `ProcedureKnowledge`, `ProcedurePricing`, and `TravelTimelineCard.jsx`'s inline map), none sharing a foreign key. Inventing risk/complication content for 69 procedures ourselves was ruled out — that's exactly the kind of ungrounded clinical claim this app's own principles (Rule 13, SAFE-T) rule out elsewhere. What shipped is matching + display only: the fuzzy name/alias scorer that used to live inline in `getProcedureKnowledge/entry.ts` was extracted to `base44/shared/procedureKnowledgeMatch.ts` (`scoreProcedureMatch`/`findBestMatches`, unit-tested in `tests/procedureKnowledgeMatch.test.js`) so it has exactly one implementation; `getProcedureKnowledge` itself is behavior-unchanged. A new sibling, `getProcedureRiskSummaries/entry.ts`, takes the catalog's title list and returns a title→risk-summary map using the same scorer, but at a deliberately higher match threshold (0.5 vs. the chat lookup's 0.2) — a chat answer can hedge, a silent pill on a browsing card cannot, so this only surfaces near-exact name/alias matches and simply omits anything looser, never a fabricated default. `Procedures.jsx` (`ProcedureCard`) and `ProcedureModal.jsx` render a risk pill/detail row only when a match exists — with zero `ProcedureKnowledge` rows populated (today's real state), both render identically to before this change. Portia populates real rows herself, procedure by procedure, through the admin page that already exists, at her own clinical judgment and whatever pace she chooses — this was a deliberate scope boundary, not an oversight.

## Signature retention — "permanently archived" is a deliberate legal-retention choice, now stated honestly everywhere (2026-08-08)

Portia flagged a live M-Care answer claiming "Every signature is archived permanently" and asked whether that's backed by a real policy. It wasn't scripted verbatim — M-Care paraphrased real-but-narrower source language: literal "Permanently Archived Digital Agreement" / "immutable legal archive" copy in `base44/shared/informedConsentArchive.ts`'s email template, which is unenforceable text on a plain, mutable `CaseRecord.informed_consent_email_html` field, not a technical guarantee. Digging further surfaced something bigger: account deletion (`deleteMyAccount` → `base44/shared/anonymizePatientRecords.ts`) scrubs `CaseRecord.client_name`/`client_email`/`client_phone`/`emergency_contact` to redacted placeholders, but never touches `signature_data` (the patient's own typed full legal name from a signed Safe-T4life HIGH-risk waiver, or the `processInformedConsentAndEmail` informed-consent/payment archive), `signature_ip_address`, or the archive HTML itself — all three survive account deletion completely untouched, sitting on the very same row whose name field now reads `'DELETED'`. The in-app deletion confirmation (`src/components/dashboard/modules/SettingsModule.jsx`'s `DeleteMyAccountCard`) claimed these records are "retained but anonymized" — true for identity fields, false for the signature itself. `WaiverRequest` (a *different* waiver entity, for companion-refusal/clinical-risk-override/age-threshold cases — not the Safe-T4life HIGH-risk gate) is likewise never referenced by the anonymizer at all; its schema's `expires_at`/`status:'expired'` are declared but dead — nothing in the codebase ever reads or writes them.

**Asked Portia directly, decided: keep the retention, make every claim about it honest.** Retaining a signed legal document (name, IP, timestamp) after account deletion is standard, defensible practice — GDPR's right to erasure has an explicit exception for data needed for legal claims/compliance, and this app already treats the surrounding case/payment record the same way (identity redacted, substance kept) for the same reason. So this was a copy/prompt-accuracy pass, not a data-model change — nothing about what's retained or deleted changed. Fixed in three places: `SettingsModule.jsx`'s deletion confirmation now says plainly that a signed agreement is kept with the name on it, for legal/audit compliance, distinct from the anonymized case/payment record around it. `informedConsentArchive.ts`'s footer dropped "immutable" (false — it's a normal DB field with ordinary admin RLS) and kept "retained... for legal and audit compliance" (true). `m_care.jsonc` gained **RULE 15 — SIGNATURES & RETENTION**, plus a correction to the CONSENT-stage scripted confirmation line (which — found while making this edit — appeared twice, identically, in both the MEDICAL and LEISURE flows, and directly contradicted the new rule by telling the traveler "you can revoke this at any time" immediately after calling `processInformedConsentAndEmail`, the very function that signs and permanently archives that consent): it now distinguishes a revocable checkbox consent (data-processing, medical-history-share — withdrawing stops *future* sharing) from a signed document (retained permanently, never framed as revocable or deletable). Same safe-edit discipline as every prior `m_care.jsonc` change this session — extract/verify via structural diff before writing.

**Deliberately not touched, same session**: `WaiverRequest`'s dead `expires_at`/`status` fields (real but separate gap, not this pass); no code change to `anonymizePatientRecords.ts` itself (the decision was to keep retention, not narrow it) — if that decision ever changes, the fields to touch on `CaseRecord` are `signature_data`, `signature_ip_address`, and `informed_consent_email_html`.

## M-Safe Travel Pass — QR navigate + emergency-contact card (2026-08-08)

Portia shared an AI-generated pitch for an "Offline QR Travel Kit" — three QR codes after booking: navigate-to-hotel/clinic, emergency contact, and a full medical summary for first responders. Research found two real, reusable pieces already in this app (`qrcode.react` is an installed dependency with a working render→PNG-download pattern in `src/components/baggage/QRCodeDisplay.jsx`, and `src/lib/mapLinks.js`'s `generateMapLink()` already builds the exact Waze/Google/Apple deep links M-Care's chat maps buttons use) — but the third piece, a no-login/no-PIN medical-history QR, would have been a real security regression: this app already exposes the exact payload the pitch wants (`verifyEmergencyPIN`'s `get_manifest` action — blood type, allergies, medications, conditions, doctor contact) but keeps it deliberately PIN-gated, with code comments showing real thought went into preventing an attacker from pre-provisioning a PIN for a victim. A QR that skips the PIN turns a lost or photographed physical card into permanent, unrevoked, unaudited access to someone's full medical history — the opposite of `getCaseTrackerPublic`'s own established norm for public/no-login links ("no medical data exposed"). Asked Portia, confirmed: ship the safe half (navigate + contact), leave the medical-ID QR as its own future, focused decision — not a rushed add-on this close to the buildathon deadline.

**Shipped**: `src/components/dashboard/TravelPassCard.jsx`, mounted in `DashboardHome` (`src/pages/Dashboard.jsx`) right after the existing `JourneyMap` block. Reads the real, correct `CaseRecord` via `useActiveCaseRecord` (`src/hooks/useActiveCaseRecord.js` — the same hook `EmergencyHub`/`EmergencyMedCard` already share one cached read through), **not** `Dashboard.jsx`'s own `latestConsultation`/`Consultation`-sourced state that `JourneyMap` uses a few lines above it — a real, pre-existing, unrelated bug found during this work: `JourneyMap` is fed `hotel_coords`/`clinic_address`/etc. from `Consultation`, which has no such fields declared anywhere, so it most likely never renders a real pin in production. Not fixed here (out of scope, flagged so it isn't "rediscovered" as a surprise later) — `TravelPassCard` deliberately sources from `CaseRecord` correctly instead of copying that pattern.

Renders nothing until the active case has a real `hotel_address` or `clinic_address` (no placeholder QR codes). Two QR types, both 100% client-side, no new backend function: a Google Maps universal link (resolves natively on both iOS and Android, unlike an Apple Maps link — the right single choice for a QR a driver might scan on any phone) per address that exists, and a hand-built vCard (`BEGIN:VCARD...END:VCARD`, same "no library, hand-roll simple structured text" convention this repo already uses for `.ics` files in `generateItineraryCalendar`) carrying the patient's `emergency_contact`/`emergency_contact_number` plus the assigned doctor's name/phone (looked up client-side from the public `Doctor` entity by `doctor_email`, same lookup `getGuardianViewData` already does server-side) — contact info only, never medical data. Download/print follows the two zero-dependency patterns already established in this app: `QRCodeDisplay.jsx`'s SVG→canvas→PNG per-code download, and `GoldenMCertificate.jsx`/`EmergencyMedCard.jsx`'s `@media print` scoped-`id` pattern for the combined printable pass — confirmed no PDF-generation library exists anywhere in this codebase, so nothing here promises real PDF output beyond the browser's own Save-as-PDF print dialog.

**Also surfaced, unrelated, not fixed in this pass**: `blood_type` is collected in `MedicalIntakeForm.jsx`'s UI but explicitly destructured out and never persisted to any entity — every existing reader of it (`EmergencyMedCard`, the PIN manifest) already renders blank for every real patient today.

**Deliberately deferred**: the medical-ID QR itself. If pursued, it needs a genuinely minimal (not the full manifest — no passport, no insurance, no full case linkage), revocable/expiring, audit-logged token flow, closer to `GuardianSession`'s shape than either of the two existing PIN-gated or full-RLS-gated systems — a real medical-alert-bracelet-style tradeoff (curated minimal summary, not a full chart), and its own session's worth of decisions, not squeezed in here.

## M-Care super-agent, Phase 12 — a real QR code inside the chat itself (2026-08-08)

Portia tested M-Care live right after the Travel Pass card above shipped and asked it for a QR code a driver could scan to get back to her hotel. M-Care answered honestly that it had no such feature — accurate at that moment, but stale: the Dashboard already had one, M-Care's own instructions just never mentioned it. Asked whether to fix M-Care's knowledge only (point to the Dashboard card) or have it produce a QR directly in the chat, Portia chose the latter.

The build reused an existing, proven mechanism rather than inventing a new one: `MessageBubble.jsx` (the one message-rendering component both `MCareOrb.jsx` and `MCareAgent.jsx` already share) already parses a `{{maps:LABEL|DESTINATION}}` token M-Care emits into three tappable Waze/Google/Apple buttons, built via `mapLinks.js`'s `generateMapLink()` — a token the agent emits itself, with **no backend tool call involved**. A QR code is just another rendering of the same destination string, so a new `{{qr:LABEL|DESTINATION}}` token reuses that entire pipeline — same DESTINATION format, same "must come from a real tool result, never invented" rule (Rule 3), same shared component. This was the deliberate reason to build it this way: it needed **zero new backend function and zero new `tool_configs` entry** — the exact two-step pattern (new function + wiring it into the agent's tool list before the function is Published) that made M-Care go completely silent once before (Phase 10 above). A token the agent emits as plain text carries none of that risk.

**Shipped**: `extractQr()` in `MessageBubble.jsx` (mirrors `extractMaps()` exactly), rendering a real `QRCodeSVG` (`qrcode.react`, already a dependency) inline in the chat bubble, encoding the same Google Maps universal link `generateMapLink(dest, 'google_maps')` already builds for the maps-token buttons. A "Save" button downloads it as a PNG via the same SVG→canvas→PNG mechanic used twice already in this app (`QRCodeDisplay.jsx`, `TravelPassCard.jsx`) — pulled into a small shared `src/lib/qrDownload.js` helper for the third use rather than a third copy-paste (the two existing components were left untouched, not retrofitted). New **RULE 16 ("PRINTABLE / SHAREABLE QR CODE")** added to `m_care.jsonc`'s instructions, right after Rule 15: distinguishes `{{qr:...}}` (a code for someone else — a driver, family, hotel staff — to scan on their own phone, or to save/print) from Rule 12's `{{maps:...}}` (opens navigation on the traveler's own device, right now); same DESTINATION rules as Rule 12; explicitly tells the agent to say so plainly and offer to send it once travel logistics are confirmed if no real address exists yet, never fabricate a placeholder; also mentions the Dashboard's M-Safe Travel Pass card as the always-available equivalent (plus its Emergency Contact QR, which stays Dashboard-only).

**Deliberately not built this round**: an Emergency Contact vCard token in chat (e.g. `{{qrcontact:...}}`). The reported gap was specifically navigation-back-to-hotel; the Dashboard card already covers the contact QR, and the same token pattern makes a contact-QR chat token a cheap follow-on later if full parity is ever wanted.

## M-Care super-agent, Phase 13 — closing the last 3 partner-type write-access gaps, and making verification fair across countries (2026-08-08)

A buildathon framing conversation with Portia surfaced a real, still-open security gap from Phase 8: `Doctor`/`TravelAgency` were fixed to be read-only for the agent, but `TaxiService`/`Companion`/`SecurityAgency`/`ExternalJourney` still granted the agent unrestricted `create`+`update` in `tool_configs` — confirmed directly against the live file, not from memory. Portia then asked for M-Care to complete partner approval "end-to-end, no human touch" at scale (millions of users, one founder). Investigated before building anything: doctors don't actually get zero-human-touch today either — `initiatePartnerVerification`'s auto-clear path deliberately stopped stamping `background_check_status: 'passed'` off an AI document scan (a real criminal background check needs a vendor this app doesn't have; faking that check was a past bug already found and removed), so `activateVerifiedDoctor` always falls back to requiring one human click (`verifyDoctorBackground`) regardless. Then Portia asked the sharper, better question: is verification actually fair across different countries' documentation norms (naming Thailand, Haiti, Venezuela) — are good partners lost to a screening process that doesn't understand their country? That investigation found two real, live bugs, not hypothetical ones.

**Closed the write-access gap (matches the Doctor/TravelAgency Phase 8 fix exactly)**: three new gated creation functions — `mcareCreateTaxiServicePending`, `mcareCreateCompanionPending`, `mcareCreateSecurityAgencyPending` — mirror `mcareCreateDoctorPending`/`mcareCreateTravelAgencyPending` byte-for-byte in spirit: hardcode `status: 'pending_verification'` (never caller-suppliable), narrow field allowlist per entity schema, same non-fatal `UserOnboardingProfile` + `syncTenantRole` role-grant side effect that fixed the 2026-07-24 Partner Onboarding Lockout bug, same non-fatal `initiatePartnerVerification` kickoff. `tool_configs` for `TaxiService`/`Companion`/`SecurityAgency` downgraded to `read`-only; `m_care.jsonc`'s TAXI/COMPANION/SECURITY AGENCY instruction sections rewritten to call the gated function first, matching TRAVEL AGENCY's existing wording exactly. `ExternalJourney` was left unchanged — out of scope for this pass, same as it was for Phase 8.

**Considered and explicitly declined: auto-activation for TaxiService/Companion/SecurityAgency.** `activatePartner/entry.ts` already exists, already covers all three, already checks identity/background/license (+insurance for taxi) — but its own header comment already reasons through exactly this question and answers it: a companion/driver/security escort is alone with a vulnerable, possibly-sedated patient (a hotel room at 2am, an unfamiliar car, by definition alone), a categorically higher-risk scenario than a doctor in a clinic with staff present, and "no automated signal available today is strong enough" to skip a human there — deliberately, permanently. Respected that decision rather than building around it; no new auto-activation path was added for these three partner types.

**Fixed instead — the real fairness bugs, live today, most consequential for doctors (the one partner type with an autonomous decision path):**
- `analyzePartnerDocuments/entry.ts`'s fraud/forgery vision-AI prompt had zero country or partner-type context — every document from every country was judged against the same generic notion of "normal," a real risk of flagging a legitimate document as suspicious purely for looking unfamiliar rather than being fraudulent. Now accepts `partner_type`/`country` (threaded through from `initiatePartnerVerification`) and instructs the model explicitly not to penalize script, formatting, or production conventions merely typical of that country's official documents. Stated honestly as a mitigation, not a guarantee — no vision model can perfectly know every country's document-issuance conventions, especially in lower-infrastructure states.
- `initiatePartnerVerification/entry.ts` used to auto-DENY a doctor outright (`fraud_score > 70`) with **no human ever reviewing the application** — just an email telling the applicant to contact support if it was wrong. Removed. A high score now routes to manual review flagged urgent (`verification_notes` marker, no new schema field) — AI can escalate urgency, it can never resolve an adverse outcome by itself. The existing `<= CLEAR_THRESHOLD` auto-clear path is unchanged (still requires the one human background-check click either way, per above — already safe).
- `runSanctionsScreening/entry.ts` used to write `status: 'blocked'` (an invalid value outside every partner entity's own `status` enum, on top of being premature) the instant a fuzzy name-match fired — before any human confirmed the match was real. Fuzzy sanctions/PEP/adverse-media matching is well-documented to produce more false positives for non-Western naming conventions, which lands disproportionately on exactly the countries this conversation was about. Fixed: a flag now sets `sanctions_review_needed: true` + a `verification_notes` marker, leaves `status` untouched (the applicant was already non-bookable — that gate doesn't move either way), and the existing admin alert email was reworded to say plainly the applicant has NOT been rejected. The parallel `KYPVerification.overall_status` write (`'rejected'` on a mere flag) was the same bug in a second place — changed to `'in_progress'`, a valid enum value, accurate either way.
- `SecurityAgency.jsonc` had no `status` field at all, even though `activatePartner` unconditionally wrote one to it — a latent bug (the one partner type that puts someone physically alongside a sedated patient was the only one with nothing for an activation gate to read). Added a simple 3-value `status` enum matching `Companion`'s (not `TaxiService`'s fuller state machine — `SecurityAgency` doesn't participate in `partnerOnboardingState.ts` and doesn't need to for this fix).

**Also closed the same session**: `EmergencyContacts` had zero seeded rows for any country — `getGuardianViewData`'s own code comment already flagged this ("that table needs seeding"), and `GuardianView.jsx`'s "Local Emergency" button simply never rendered as a result, for any country, undercutting the "anywhere in the world" claim specifically. New admin-gated, idempotent `seedEmergencyContacts/entry.ts` (same `filter-before-create` pattern as `seedDemoJourney`) seeds 26 countries: the 20 already curated in `src/components/emergency/NaturalDisasterPanel.jsx`'s `COUNTRY_DATA` (reused verbatim — zero invented numbers, and consistent with what this app already shows elsewhere) plus 6 more this app already references (`visaMatrix.js`, `TaxiServiceSignupStep2.jsx`'s operating countries) that weren't covered, looked up live via WebSearch against official sources this session: Jamaica, Barbados, Trinidad and Tobago, Belize, Malaysia, South Korea. Needs Portia to actually invoke the function once (admin session) for the rows to exist — writing the function doesn't seed the table by itself.

**Deliberately deferred, same shape as Phase 8's own deferred list, not silently forgotten**: two separate raw, ungated `base44.entities[...].update(...)` bypass surfaces still exist in the admin UI itself — `AdminPartners.jsx`'s `handleApproveDoctor`/`handleApproveTravelAgency`/`handleApproveCompanion` and `PartnerCommandCenter.jsx`'s `handleApprove` (covers doctor/travel_agency/taxi/security) — meaning a **human** admin clicking the wrong button can still skip every check, even for partner types whose AI-agent path is now fully closed. Real, found this session, not fixed — rewiring two live admin approval UIs carries real risk of breaking approval entirely if done under deadline pressure, and Portia's own call was to close the AI-agent gap and the fairness bugs this round, not rebuild admin UX. `activatePartner` remains uncalled by any UI. Worth its own dedicated pass.

## M-Care super-agent, Phase 14 — real web research on any country's verification landscape (2026-08-08)

Same day, Portia pushed further on Phase 13's fairness fixes: she wants M-Care to actually research any country's real partner-verification norms — e.g. know that a Trinidadian doctor is checked against the real Medical Board of Trinidad and Tobago, not judged by a generic yardstick — general to any country, not a hand-maintained list. Investigated honestly before proposing anything: **there is no mechanism anywhere in this repo where a backend function gets real, live, independently-sourced web search results at runtime.** Every "research" path in this app (`mcareResearchAndLearn`, `clinicVerify.ts`, `visaLookup.ts`, `runInternetIntelligence`, etc.) uses Base44's own `InvokeLLM({ add_context_from_internet: true })` — a black-box grounding flag, not a controllable search API, no inspectable source list, confidence self-reported by the same call. The only place with confirmed *real*, independently-verified live web access is `.github/workflows/curated-data-refresh.yml` (full Claude Code with real WebSearch/WebFetch, but scheduled/manual, structurally scoped to exactly two files, CI-gated) — not invocable mid-conversation. And the only genuinely independent per-country registry checks (`base44/shared/registryLookup.ts`) cover exactly US/CO/MX — Trinidad, Haiti, Venezuela, and Thailand are all hardcoded to the manual-review fallback. Building a live "M-Care browses the web" claim on top of the black-box grounding flag alone would have repeated the exact mistake the Demo Pages audit above exists to catch — so this pass is honest about which of those two things it actually is.

First proposal (a new curated `RegulatoryBody` reference entity, hand-seeded for 4 named countries with a seed function) was explicitly rejected by Portia as over-engineered — "dont complex the system." Rebuilt simpler: this app already has exactly one *working* internet-grounded research mechanism, `mcareResearchAndLearn` (recall-first from `McareKnowledge`, `InvokeLLM({ add_context_from_internet: true })` on a cache miss, persists only at ≥80% self-reported confidence, already supports a `journey_context: 'verification'` tag) — already built, already live in M-Care's chat. The fix was simply to call it from the verification pipeline too, for every partner, generalizing to any country by construction rather than a maintained list.

**Shipped**: `initiatePartnerVerification/entry.ts` now calls `mcareResearchAndLearn` right after gate 1 (sanctions) clears, asking what the real regulatory/licensing body for this `partner_type`/country is and what a legitimate credential from them looks like. Best-effort and non-fatal — a failed or low-confidence (`<80`, the same bar `mcareResearchAndLearn` itself uses to decide whether an answer is "reliable enough to memorize") lookup just leaves verification running exactly as it did before this change. A confident result flows two places: into `analyzePartnerDocuments`'s existing fairness `contextLine` (upgrading Phase 13's generic "be fair about country" nudge into a concrete fact about the real regulator when one is known) and onto a new `PartnerVerification.regulatory_context` field, so a human reviewer sees it plainly rather than it being baked silently into a prompt. Same non-negotiable guardrail as every AI-touches-verification change in this file: advisory only, never changes the auto-clear/manual-review thresholds, never resolves an outcome by itself. Because `mcareResearchAndLearn` self-caches into `McareKnowledge`, the first partner from any given country pays the live-research cost and every partner after them (same country, same partner type) gets an instant recall — the system gets faster and more complete the more countries it actually sees, with zero hardcoded list to maintain.

**Deliberately not built this pass**: no new entity, no seed function, no curated-country allowlist — the point was generality, and Portia explicitly asked to keep this simple. Not wired into M-Care's chat `tool_configs` — pipeline-side only, avoiding a fresh tool-registration Publish-order risk (the Phase 10 lesson) on top of this change; M-Care proactively mentioning a country's real regulator in conversation would reuse this exact same call and is a cheap, low-risk follow-on once this is confirmed live. Not a real government-registry API integration — `registryLookup.ts`'s adapter model still only covers US/CO/MX; Thailand is worth flagging as a strong future candidate since both its Medical Council (tmc.or.th) and Dental Council (dentalcouncil.or.th) run real public online license-lookup portals, found while researching this feature but not built into anything this pass.

## M-Care super-agent, Phase 15 — a "trust me" example answer that oversold itself (2026-08-08)

Portia asked M-Care directly to prove itself: "give me an example please so i trust you." Its answer read well — a full Maria-from-Trinidad walkthrough — and she was genuinely impressed. Checked it against the real code before agreeing, because that's exactly the wrong moment for anything in this app to be less honest than it sounds. Verdict was mixed, not a clean pass:

**Real, confirmed against actual code**: the SAFE-T MEDIUM-risk example matched `computeSafeTScreening`'s real behavior; consent-before-sharing is a real, audited gate; the nutrition plan, the Golden M, and the 9-handshake completion are all real, wired tools. The one-tap payment link across email/SMS/WhatsApp is genuinely real — `sendClientBookingProposal` does exactly that, fault-tolerant per channel, logged to `NotificationLog`.

**Overstated — three specific claims, all first-person, all implying something already happened that hadn't**: "I coordinate her flights... book a recovery-friendly hotel" — `assignTravelAgency` (read in full) only emails a real travel agency partner asking for a quote within 24h; there is no live flight/hotel booking anywhere in this codebase, confirmed again here (see Phase 4's original finding, still true). "I... dispatch a verified driver" — `assignChauffeurServices` (read in full) similarly only emails a real taxi company a quote request via a portal link; nothing is confirmed until that company responds, and no live driver-acceptance signal exists anywhere (matches the Transport Partner Platform section's finding). "Dr. López in Cancun, Dr. Rivera in Tijuana, Dr. Mendez in Los Algodones" — specific named doctors with specific cities, almost certainly invented on the spot to make the example vivid rather than pulled from a real `matchDoctorsForProcedure` call in that turn. All three are exactly the kind of confident-but-untrue claim Rule 3 (NO INVENTED DATA) already exists to prevent elsewhere — the gap was that Rule 3 never explicitly covered "explain yourself" / illustrative example answers, so nothing stopped the model from treating a hypothetical walkthrough differently than a live tool-backed claim.

**Fixed, `m_care.jsonc` instructions only — no `tool_configs` change, so no Publish-order risk**: Rule 3 gained a closing sentence requiring example/explanatory answers to stay honest the same way live answers already have to — describe the real steps and real tools, never invent a specific outcome (a named doctor, city, confirmation number, or completed booking) that didn't actually happen, and say plainly when something is a hypothetical illustration rather than a real case. The BOOKING-stage paragraph was rewritten to state plainly that `assignTravelAgency`/`assignChauffeurServices` send real quote *requests* to real human partners (typically a 24h response window), and to explicitly forbid telling a traveler their flights are booked, a hotel is reserved, or a driver is dispatched/confirmed before that partner's quote actually comes back.

**Also surfaced, not fixed this pass**: `assignTravelAgency/entry.ts` and `assignChauffeurServices/entry.ts` both bypass `createHandler` (raw `Deno.serve`/no wrapper) — the same anti-pattern flagged in the Edge Functions section of this file as a real cause of silent Cloudflare-worker 404s elsewhere. Worth its own migration pass, not bundled into a wording fix under time pressure.

## M-Care super-agent, Phase 16 — trip-readiness guidance, and a real "checklist exists but chat can't say it" bug (2026-08-08)

Portia's next ask, prompted by the Phase 15 finding: M-Care should proactively tell a traveler how to get ready once a booking is confirmed — hydration, sleep, packing, the kind of thing before a flight — for medical trips AND pure leisure trips alike. Investigated before building: there's already a real, good, deterministic pre-op checklist system (`_shared/preOpChecklist.ts`, universal prep + fasting/medication items that always defer to the doctor, never invented) that `sendPreOpInstructions` builds and stores on the case once the doctor confirms — genuinely solid, not a gap. But its actual bug, found by reading the function rather than assuming: it only ever returned a count (`items: 5`) or a bare `already_sent: true` boolean to its caller — never the checklist content itself. M-Care had this tool the whole time and still had nothing to say if a patient asked "how do I get ready?" `sendPostOpInstructions` had the identical shape of bug (`standard_rules_count` instead of the actual `STANDARD_CARE` array or the doctor's own outcome notes). Both fixed — both now return their real content in every response branch, including the idempotent "already sent" path, which previously returned nothing usable at all.

Confirmed separately: neither tool is available for LEISURE trips (by design — they're procedure-specific), so Portia's "leisure travelers should get this too" ask was a real, complete gap, not an oversight in an existing tool.

**Shipped, deliberately split into two sourcing tiers rather than routing everything through a live LLM call**: `base44/shared/tripReadinessTips.ts` (`getUniversalTravelTips`) is fixed, curated content — hydration, sleep, movement/circulation, comfort, jet lag, motion sickness, documents, packing — the same discipline as `preOpChecklist.ts`: none of this is time-sensitive or destination-specific, so a live model call to regenerate it every time would be slower and no more reliable than writing it once. New `base44/functions/getTripReadinessTips/entry.ts` pairs that with a second, genuinely destination-specific layer — a best-effort call to the existing `mcareResearchAndLearn` (the same recall-then-research-then-cache mechanism wired into partner verification in Phase 14) asking what a traveler should know about climate/packing/local customs for this specific origin→destination route. Same `>=80` confidence bar, same discipline: omitted entirely, never fabricated, if research fails or isn't confident. Deliberately does **not** claim an exact flight duration ("your flight is 3 hours") — no real flight-duration data exists anywhere in this app until a specific flight is actually booked (see Phase 15's finding that flight/hotel arrangement is a human-partner quote request, not a live booking) — inventing one to sound precise would repeat exactly the Phase 15 mistake. Available to ALL journey types, medical and leisure alike; wired into the CONFIRMED stage instructions in `m_care.jsonc` so M-Care offers it proactively once a booking is confirmed, the same way the countdown reminders already work — not just on request.

## M-Care super-agent, Phase 17 — voice output, and a real platform finding worth revisiting (2026-08-08)

Portia's next ask was the biggest yet: a genuinely frictionless, "no clicking anywhere" M-Care reachable on WhatsApp/Telegram/SMS as well as in-app, plus voice output so it can talk back to someone who can't type. Investigated before proposing anything, and found real, already-built pieces along the way worth crediting: no-forms chat-driven flows already cover nearly everything in-app (88 real tools); document-vault upload already happens through M-Care's own chat (`MCareVaultUpload.jsx`); voice **input** already works (`VoiceInputButton.jsx` → `transcribeVoiceInput`, Phase 2B) — "if they can't type" was already solved for typing *in*, just not for M-Care talking back.

**A genuinely significant finding, worth its own note**: reading `base44/agents/m_care.jsonc`'s own top-level fields directly (not just `tool_configs`/`instructions`, which is all any prior M-Care session ever touched) surfaced fields no one had looked at before — `whatsapp_greeting`, `telegram_greeting`, `line_greeting` (all currently `null`), and `voice_config: { voice_id: null }`. This strongly suggests Base44's native Agent platform has built-in multi-channel deployment and native voice as platform features, configured through the Base44 Builder dashboard (the same place Publish and `base44-builder[bot]`'s direct commits already live) — not something this repo would need to hand-build via custom webhook receivers or a bespoke TTS pipeline. **Unconfirmed and deliberately not acted on this pass** — Portia has a screenshot of the relevant Base44 screen that would show exactly what's configurable, but redirected before it was shared: "we dont need to build the whatapp and telegram we can build everything else." Worth revisiting with that screenshot before ever hand-building a channel integration — if the platform already does this natively, custom webhook engineering would be duplicate work solving an already-solved problem.

**Shipped this pass — voice output**, scoped down from the full omnichannel ask per Portia's own redirect. New `base44/functions/speakMcareText/entry.ts` is a thin sibling of `transcribeVoiceInput` (Phase 2B) and reuses `Core.GenerateSpeech`, the exact TTS primitive `walkieTalkieTranslate` already proved out in production — no new integration, no new vendor. `requireAuth: false`, matching voice input's own reasoning: it should work for a logged-out visitor exactly like typing and speaking-in already do. A new `SpeakButton` in `src/components/mcare-agent/MessageBubble.jsx` (the one message component both `MCareOrb.jsx` and `MCareAgent.jsx` already share) adds an opt-in "Listen" button on M-Care's own replies — deliberately **not** auto-play-every-reply: unsolicited audio in a quiet or public setting would be the opposite of frictionless, so the user asks for it the same way they'd tap a link. Speaks the current app language (`i18n.language` from `src/i18n.js`'s `useTranslation()`), not hardcoded English. The `voice_config.voice_id` platform field was deliberately left untouched — building on the proven, controllable `Core.GenerateSpeech` path rather than an unconfirmed native behavior neither of us has actually seen configured yet.

**Deliberately not built this pass, not silently dropped**: WhatsApp/Telegram/SMS bidirectional messaging — explicitly redirected away from by Portia this round, and pending that Base44 screenshot regardless. The `LivingOrb.jsx` "speaking" animation still runs on a fixed timer, not real audio-driven timing — now that real TTS audio exists (when a user taps Listen), syncing the orb's speaking state to actual playback duration is a small, honest, low-risk follow-on, not done here to keep this pass scoped to what was asked.

## M-Care super-agent, Phase 18 — Talk Mode: auto-speak, word-sync, voice toggle for the buildathon demo (2026-08-08)

Portia sent a detailed 3-part spec for a full demo-day voice experience: M-Care auto-speaks every reply, text streams word-by-word in sync with the speech, and the whole thing is toggleable by natural voice/text commands ("toggle talk mode", "speak to me", "stop talking"). Explicit, repeated ask: **do not break M-Care** — reasonable, given the false alarm two turns earlier (`/msafe`, a disconnected mockup, got mistaken for a live regression). Given how tightly coupled toggle state, speech playback, and word-reveal have to be across files, and given the diagnostic workflow from that same false alarm had just failed outright from hitting a session usage limit, this was built directly and incrementally rather than delegated to parallel agents — a demo-critical feature isn't the place to risk fragmented, inconsistent implementations across files that need to agree with each other.

**Deliberately built on `window.speechSynthesis` (Web Speech API), not a server call** — matches Portia's own explicit preference ("Recommended for reliability... no API keys needed"), and is the right call independent of that: zero network dependency once the page has loaded, so a dropped WiFi connection on stage can't kill the demo. This is a second, separate system from Phase 17's per-message "Listen" button (which still uses server-side `Core.GenerateSpeech` and was left completely untouched) — Talk Mode auto-speaks; Listen stays a manual, opt-in replay.

**New `src/lib/talkMode.js`** — pure, DOM-light functions, unit-tested (`tests/talkMode.test.js`, 13 cases): `detectTalkModeCommand` (exact-phrase match only against Portia's specified trigger list, never fuzzy/substring — so a real question containing "talk" or "silent" is never mistaken for a mode command; verified the overlapping `"toggle talk mode"` / `"toggle talk mode off"` pair disambiguates correctly), `extractSpeakableText` (strips `{{choices/maps/qr:...}}` tokens and markdown before speaking — a deliberately separate, simplified copy of `MessageBubble.jsx`'s own stripping regexes rather than a shared import, so this new file never touches that already-shipped hot path), and `speakText` (wraps `SpeechSynthesisUtterance`, always cancels any in-progress utterance first so overlapping speech can never happen, reports word-reveal progress via the browser's own `onboundary` word events, and — since Safari doesn't reliably fire those — arms a timed ~170wpm fallback reveal after 600ms of silence so the demo never gets stuck showing zero words while audio is clearly playing).

**`MessageBubble.jsx`** gained one new optional prop, `revealUpTo` (a word count). When absent (every existing caller, and every message except the one currently being spoken), rendering is byte-identical to before — verified by inspection and by the full test suite staying green. When present, only the paragraph text truncates to that many words; the Listen button hides itself for that one message while it's mid-reveal (avoiding a duplicate/overlapping trigger), and reappears once the reveal state clears.

**`MCareOrb.jsx`** is where it all comes together: a new Talk Mode toggle button in the header (a speaker icon, only rendered when `speechSynthesis` exists in the browser at all); the existing "honest speaking pulse" effect — previously a fixed 1800ms fake timer, explicitly documented elsewhere in this file as "not an audio-reactive waveform... there's no real TTS signal to react to" — now branches: **that honest fallback is completely unchanged when Talk Mode is off**, and only becomes real, audio-driven `speaking` state plus a live word-reveal when Talk Mode is on. Sending any new message unconditionally stops in-progress speech first (the required interruption behavior); the toggle commands are intercepted before that same send path ever reaches the real agent conversation — a purely client-side, zero-risk decision, deliberately mirroring `mcareReactionHeuristic.js`'s reasoning from Phase 9 ("ships with zero risk to `m_care.jsonc`'s giant instructions field... the agent never needs to know it happened"). A separate effect stops any Talk Mode speech the instant the microphone starts listening, preventing a feedback loop. Turning Talk Mode off mid-speech stops audio and snaps the in-progress message to full text immediately, never leaving it half-spoken.

**Deliberately scoped down from Portia's full spec, not silently dropped**: only wired into `MCareOrb.jsx` (the floating "M-Safe" panel she's actually been testing and screenshotting) — `MCareAgent.jsx`'s full-page `/m-care` surface shares the now-safe `MessageBubble.jsx` change but not the toggle/orchestration layer yet, a real follow-on if full parity is wanted. No separate "Demo Mode" flag — Talk Mode's rate is hardcoded to the same 0.9x her demo-clarity spec asked for regardless, since there's no real distinction between "the demo" and "someone actually wants to hear replies out loud." Talk Mode state is in-memory only, resets on page reload — not persisted to a user profile. "Mute while typing" was narrowed to "mute while the microphone is listening" (the actual feedback-loop risk); a broad is-the-user-typing heuristic was judged not worth the added complexity for a real gap that doesn't otherwise exist. Compound commands ("turn on talk mode and book me a flight") fall through to the normal agent turn unmodified rather than attempting to split intent — out of scope for what the actual demo test plan exercises, and a fuzzy NLU split would have been real new risk under time pressure for a case not in the plan.

**Merge with a parallel `base44-builder[bot]` autosync, and a real security gap found in it.** While this was being built, Portia was independently working in the Base44 Builder dashboard — 7 bot commits landed on `origin/main` mid-session, adding a new `McareAvatar` component (replacing the plain "M" icon everywhere), `SmartInputSuggestions` (predictive/spelling-correction chips above the input), and clipboard-paste support (`src/lib/chatPaste.js`) — none of it textually overlapping with the Talk Mode edits, confirmed by diffing the two change sets independently before merging, then merged and re-verified in full rather than assumed compatible. Two real issues surfaced by that re-verification, both in the bot's own new code, neither related to Talk Mode:

- **`base44/functions/smartMcareInput/entry.ts`** (the backend for the new predictive-text chips) was a raw `export default async function(req)` with no `createHandler`, no auth gate, no rate limit at all — a genuinely unguarded, world-reachable endpoint that could drive unlimited `InvokeLLM` calls at Portia's expense. Caught by the redteam suite's own `AUTH: no edge function is reachable without SOME guard` check going red — exactly the invariant it exists for. Rewrote it onto `createHandler` (`requireAuth: false`, matching its own stated intent that anonymous visitors get the same help), preserving its predict/correct logic exactly — now gets the default rate limit, request tracing, and generic error response every other function in this repo has.
- Two unused-import lint errors (`Shield` in `MCareOrb.jsx`, `Heart` in `MCareAgent.jsx` — both leftover after the bot swapped them for `McareAvatar`) and two `chatPaste.js` type-strictness issues (an overly-strict required-field JSDoc type, and a non-iterable-per-TS `DataTransferItemList` loop) — all four fixed, none behavior-changing.

Full suite re-run after the merge and these fixes: lint clean, `npm run build` exit 0, 524/524 vitest, 222/222 redteam (the one that had gone red is back to green). One remaining, pre-existing-pattern-consistent typecheck note left alone: a React `SyntheticEvent<HTMLInputElement>` vs native `ClipboardEvent` structural mismatch in `MCareAgent.jsx`'s paste wiring — type-only, doesn't affect the successful build, not introduced by this session's own work.

## M-Care super-agent, Phase 19 — Conversational Mode: always-listening, barge-in, honest interruption handling (2026-08-08)

Portia sent a detailed spec for "real-time conversational voice with barge-in" — an always-listening mic, the ability to talk over M-Care mid-reply and have it stop immediately, spoken "let me look that up" acknowledgments while a tool runs, gentle silence prompts, and "context-preserving interruption" (M-Care voicing both the new answer and a finished version of the interrupted one, unprompted, in a single woven reply). Explicit closing instruction, matching Phase 18's: don't break anything.

Went through Plan Mode for this one rather than building directly — the spec was written without checking it against this app's actual architecture (an AI-generated pitch, the same pattern as several prior asks in this file), and before proposing anything it was worth confirming what the platform can actually do. Read `node_modules/@base44/sdk/dist/modules/agents.js` directly — Base44's real `subscribeToConversation` is a WebSocket room that delivers one full message at a time (matched and replaced by id), not a token stream. **That one fact reshapes the whole feature**: by the time any text exists to speak, the agent has already finished generating the full reply — there is no way to stop the AI "mid-thought," only to stop the client-side playback of an already-finished reply. Everything below is built honest to that constraint rather than pretending otherwise.

**Shipped**: `src/lib/conversationalMode.js` (new, mirrors `talkMode.js`'s pure-helpers-plus-one-isolated-lifecycle-function shape) — `isConversationalModeSupported()` gates the whole feature behind both `SpeechRecognition` (input) and `speechSynthesis` (output) being present, which excludes Firefox entirely (zero `SpeechRecognition` support) — an accepted gap, not a bug, matching the same tradeoff `VoiceInputButton.jsx`'s own header already documents for push-to-talk. `startContinuousRecognition()` wraps `SpeechRecognition` with `continuous:true`, auto-restarting (capped, with backoff) since browsers stop continuous recognition on their own silence timeout despite the flag. `createBargeInDetector()` requires several consecutive, non-shrinking interim results before confirming a real interruption — standard `SpeechRecognition` gives no volume/energy signal, only recognized text, so a single stray syllable bleeding through the speaker (imperfect echo cancellation without headphones is a real risk) could otherwise look identical to a real barge-in; the thresholds are deliberately named/exported constants Portia will need to retune after testing on real hardware, not tuned values presented as final. `classifyInterruptionUtterance()` recognizes "never mind" (drops every pending topic) and an anchored-prefix "no wait, I meant..." correction (drops just the topic this exact interruption pushed) versus the default new-topic case — exact/anchored matching only, so "I mean, is that safe?" mid-sentence is never mistaken for a correction, the same discipline `talkMode.js`'s phrase matching already uses.

**Wired into `MCareOrb.jsx`** (the floating "M-Safe" panel, same scope Talk Mode shipped to — not yet `MCareAgent.jsx`'s full-page surface): a new phone-icon toggle in the header, off by default, additive to (never replacing) existing typed input and push-to-talk. Turning it on also turns Talk Mode on (a silent phone call makes no sense); turning it off leaves Talk Mode as the user left it. Mic ownership is resolved by simply not rendering `VoiceInputButton` while Conversational Mode is on, rather than trying to make `MediaRecorder` and `SpeechRecognition` coexist — there's no real scenario needing both, and whether they can safely share a mic isn't verifiable from this checkout anyway. Barge-in gets its own effect, completely separate from the existing push-to-talk anti-feedback effect (`if (listening) stopSpeaking()`), so neither was touched. Interim results live-fill the existing input box during normal turn-taking (satisfies "streamed interim results" with zero new UI); a confirmed barge-in stops audio, snapshots what M-Care was cut off saying, and flashes the orb (`LivingOrb.jsx` gained an optional one-shot `flashToken` ring pulse, replayed via a `key` remount — additive to its existing four-state `state` prop, not a new looping state). The header avatar swaps from the static `McareAvatar` to the live `LivingOrb` only while Conversational Mode is on, so its listening/thinking/speaking/flash states are actually visible during a call — the closed floating button already showed `LivingOrb`, but the open panel's header never had before now.

**The one honest reframe, called out explicitly rather than shipped as a silent downgrade**: the spec's "context-preserving interruption" has M-Care voicing both topics unprompted in one woven reply. Building that would mean either injecting hidden text into what gets sent to the agent, or literal words into the user's own chat bubble that they never said — and it would only work if `m_care.jsonc`'s giant instructions field reliably learned a brand-new convention with no way to verify it before Publish. That file has already caused one real outage this project (Phase 10's undeployed-tool silence), and no `m_care.jsonc` change of any kind was needed for anything else in this feature — spoken tool-call acknowledgments reuse the `display_projection.active_label` field already driving `MessageBubble.jsx`'s visual spinner, nothing new was added to `tool_configs`. Adding one unverifiable convention here to chase the scripted example wasn't worth that risk. Instead: `src/components/mcare/InterruptedIntentChip.jsx` (new) surfaces each interrupted topic as a small, dismissible, opt-in chip — *"You also asked about '[topic]' — want me to finish that?"* — tap to actually ask it as a real follow-up message, capped at 3 pending topics (oldest evicted first).

**A real gap found and closed along the way, not part of the original ask**: Base44's socket delivers message updates by id-replace, not just by array growth. The existing Talk Mode speaking effect only triggers on array-length growth — meaning if an assistant message first arrives as an empty placeholder (a tool call running) and its final content fills in later via an in-place update to the *same* id, the length-based effect would never re-fire and Talk Mode would silently never speak the answer. Added a second, Conversational-Mode-gated effect that catches exactly that transition, coordinated with the original effect via one shared `spokenAssistantIdsRef` set so a message is never spoken twice regardless of which path sees it first. Left the original effect's structure otherwise untouched (only a two-line dedup guard added) — safer than refactoring proven, already-shipped code under the same "don't break anything" instruction.

**Real bug caught mid-implementation, not shipped**: the new `agentMessagesRef` sync effect was first written up near Conversational Mode's other new state, above where `const [agentMessages, setAgentMessages] = useState([])` is actually declared further down the component — its dependency array (`[agentMessages]`) would have referenced the variable before initialization, a genuine temporal-dead-zone crash on every render. Caught by re-reading the full file before running anything, not by the test suite (this is a render-time crash, not something a unit test over pure functions would catch) — moved the sync effect to right after the real declaration.

**Verification**: `tests/conversationalMode.test.js` (new, 18 tests, same style as `tests/talkMode.test.js`) covers every pure function — support detection, interruption classification (including the "I mean" mid-sentence non-match case), the capped intent stack, and the barge-in debounce logic including its shrink-resets-the-count and stays-confirmed-until-reset behavior. Full suite green after these changes: lint clean, typecheck at the same 5-error pre-existing baseline (zero regression — the only new errors introduced, both from `window.SpeechRecognition`/`webkitSpeechRecognition` not being in this project's TS lib types, were fixed with the same `/** @type {any} */(window)` cast `VoiceMode.jsx` already established elsewhere in this repo), `npm run build` exit 0, 541/541 vitest, 222/222 redteam — no new redteam invariant was needed, confirmed by running the suite rather than assumed, since nothing here touches `tool_configs` or entity write permissions.

**Deliberately out of scope this pass, stated plainly rather than silently dropped**: true interruption of the AI's own generation (architecturally not possible, per the finding above); guaranteed clean echo cancellation (relies on the browser/OS's own handling plus the debounce heuristic — the toggle's own tooltip says "best with headphones," never "flawless"); the unprompted single-reply topic-weaving behavior (replaced by the dismissible chip); any `m_care.jsonc` edit of any kind; simultaneous push-to-talk *and* always-listening (mutually exclusive by design); native Capacitor app support (same Firefox/inconsistent-iOS-Safari gap `VoiceInputButton.jsx` already carries); Conversational Mode on `MCareAgent.jsx`'s full-page surface (Talk Mode itself hasn't been extended there yet either — a real follow-on if full parity across both surfaces is ever wanted); persisting interrupted topics or the mode toggle across a reload — both reset the same way `revealState` already does, and closing the panel now also turns Conversational Mode off (the mic shouldn't keep listening in the background once the chat isn't visible).

## M-Care super-agent, Phase 20 — a human-sounding voice for the stage demo (2026-08-08)

Right after Phase 19 shipped, Portia asked how Boardy AI (a conversational "matchmaking" agent she wants M-Care to feel as impressive as) actually holds a full conversation, framed around making an impact at the buildathon stage demo. Went through Plan Mode for this rather than guessing — did real, sourced web research (founder interviews, an engineering job posting, independent user reviews) rather than trusting Boardy's own marketing copy. Two findings reshaped the ask: **no source, marketing or independent, names a specific telephony or voice-AI vendor behind Boardy** (the founder's own soundbite is "half OpenAI, half Pixar" — not a confirmed stack), and **nothing found confirms whether Boardy even supports real interruption/barge-in** — meaning Phase 19's barge-in, verified working today, may already be ahead of what's publicly known about Boardy, not behind it. The one thing every independent account agreed on: Boardy's voice sounds human, with natural minor imperfections, not robotic — and that's the one gap actually worth closing before a stage demo, since it's the single most perceptible difference to a live audience and something this app already has a proven answer for.

**Shipped**: `src/lib/neuralSpeech.js` (new) — `speakTextNeural(text, options)`, same call contract as `talkMode.js`'s `speakText` (a cancel function, `onWordBoundary`/`onEnd`/`onError` callbacks), but speaks through `speakMcareText` (`Core.GenerateSpeech`, Phase 17's own primitive, already proven in production via the per-message "Listen" button and `walkieTalkieTranslate`) instead of the browser's built-in `speechSynthesis`. On any network error, timeout (4s), empty response, or playback failure, it falls back to the exact same `speakText` browser-TTS path Talk Mode always used — a flaky venue connection degrades to the familiar robotic voice instead of going silent mid-demo, the same fail-open discipline as everywhere else in this file. Deliberately kept out of `talkMode.js` itself, which stays intentionally pure/network-free; this file owns the async call and `Audio()` element playback, isolated the same way `conversationalMode.js`'s `startContinuousRecognition` is — and, like that function, has no dedicated unit tests (impure, DOM/network-coupled logic with no clean pure function to extract without a risky refactor under deadline pressure); covered instead by the full standing verification gate. No native word-boundary event exists for an `<audio>` element the way `SpeechSynthesisUtterance.onboundary` gives Talk Mode's browser path — word-reveal here is an honest approximation, pacing words evenly once the real audio duration is known via `onloadedmetadata`, not a claim of exact sync.

**Wired into `MCareOrb.jsx`**: every place that used to call the bare `speakText`/`stopSpeaking` (the main speaking effect, Conversational Mode's ack-and-content-fill effect, the Talk Mode toggle voice confirmation, and all four interrupt points — sending a new message, a confirmed barge-in, turning Talk Mode off mid-speech, and the mic-starts-listening anti-feedback guard) now calls `speakTextNeural`/`stopAllSpeech` (a new `neuralSpeech.js` export that stops both the neural and browser-TTS paths in one call, so every existing interrupt point needed exactly one line changed, not new bookkeeping). This means Conversational Mode's silence nudge and spoken tool-call acknowledgments — both of which just append a message and let the shared speaking effect pick it up — automatically get the human voice too, with no special-casing needed.

**Verification**: lint clean, typecheck at the same 5-error pre-existing baseline (one genuinely new error surfaced and was fixed along the way — writing the literal path alias `@/api` inside a `/** */` JSDoc comment triggers TypeScript's JSDoc tag parser, which reads `@/api` as a malformed tag name and throws `TS1003`; reworded the comment to avoid an `@`-prefixed path reference inside doc comments, a reusable gotcha for any future JSDoc block in this repo), `npm run build` exit 0, 541/541 vitest (unchanged — no new test file, per the reasoning above), 222/222 redteam.

**Deliberately out of scope this pass**: real outbound telephony / M-Care proactively calling someone, Boardy-style — no confirmed vendor even on Boardy's own side, and this app has no outbound voice infrastructure today; explicitly declined as too large and unproven this close to a deadline (Portia's own choice, offered alongside the shipped option and rehearsal-only as alternatives). Cross-session/cross-channel memory unification — a real, much bigger feature, and Boardy's own reported friction (having to admit it once failed to clearly explain how it stores users' data) is a caution against rushing this, not a reason to chase it. No change to the barge-in/interruption logic itself, which was already real and working. Real neural-voice latency over an actual network connection, and how convincing the word-reveal timing feels without a native boundary event, can't be verified from this checkout (no local Base44 session, the same standing limitation as every prior phase) — Portia needs to test this herself on the actual venue connection before trusting it live, not assume it from a green test suite.

## M-Care super-agent, Phase 21 — grounding the stage script in what's actually true (2026-08-08)

Portia sent a full Apple-keynote-style stage script for the buildathon — Theon cues M-Care live with spoken lines ("M, introduce yourself, and tell them why you were created"), and M-Care is scripted to recite a specific origin story (a Costa Rica tragedy, an NYPD officer's death) and to flatly refuse a grandmother/liposuction case with specific medical reasoning. Went through Plan Mode rather than treating this as a copy/rehearsal problem, because this platform's entire brand promise is that its safety claims are real and never invented — and a stage demo in front of judges is exactly where a gap between "what we say M does" and "what M actually does" would be most damaging.

Checked both load-bearing claims against the real code before touching anything:

- **The origin story wasn't grounded anywhere.** Searched `m_care.jsonc`'s full instructions, the `McareKnowledge` knowledge base, and the whole repo — zero hits. The two tragedy stories exist only as static marketing copy on two unrelated demo pages (`DemoShowcase.jsx`'s Costa Rica procedure-stacking story, `RecoveryTrackerDemo.jsx`'s Cali-Colombia "Det. Alicia Stone" story), never wired to the agent, and the two pages' details don't even agree with each other or with the founder's script (different countries, a named detective vs. an unnamed mother/teacher). Asked live, M-Care would have either dodged the question or improvised — a real risk of it saying something inconsistent or simply wrong, live, on the one subject (honesty, safety) the whole platform is built on.
- **The grandmother scenario doesn't produce a refusal.** Ran age 82 + diabetes + hypertension + stroke history + liposuction through the real deterministic engines (`_shared/safeTEngine.ts`'s `computeSafeT`, and the separate `safeT4LifeScan` tier logic). Result: `review`/`HIGH` — a flagged case requiring a signed waiver before the booking can continue, not an outright block. Neither engine generates "anesthesia risk, bleeding risk, stroke recurrence risk" either — that phrasing was written for the script, not produced by the system.

Asked Portia directly rather than guessing which way to resolve each gap. She chose to ground the story in real content (not script around the AI or accept the risk of live improvisation) and to match the grandmother line to what the system actually does (not build toward a fabricated hard-refuse, not ship the mismatch as-is). For the origin story's source, she picked the existing Costa Rica/unnamed-patient version over the Colombia/Det. Alicia Stone one. One thing surfaced and flagged before grounding anything: even her chosen source doesn't contain the script's specific embellishments (a 45-year-old, "a teacher," the family's quotes) — those aren't sourced anywhere in this codebase, so grounding M-Care's *knowledge* in them would just move the fabrication from live improvisation into permanent instructions, not fix it. The fix uses only the real, verified facts already in `DemoShowcase.jsx`'s own copy.

**Shipped**: a new `RULE 17 — IDENTITY & ORIGIN` appended to `m_care.jsonc`'s instructions (text-only addition, no `tool_configs` change — zero Publish-order risk, the same anchor-verified, no-restructuring edit discipline used for every prior instructions change this session, confirmed by a 1-line git diff on a 90KB field). It gives the agent the real mission framing (medical tourism safety layer, protects end-to-end) plus the actual Costa Rica incident stated exactly as `DemoShowcase.jsx` already states it — a multi-procedure-stacking death, no name/age/quote — followed by the real, working follow-through (`getViolations`'s procedure-stacking check blocks that exact combination before payment). The rule explicitly cross-references Rule 3 (NO INVENTED DATA) and instructs the agent never to add a name, age, occupation, or quote on top of the real facts, and never to invent a second incident.

**Not a code change, a corrected line for Portia's own script** — the grandmother beat, rewritten to match what `computeSafeT`/`safeT4LifeScan` actually do: *"I flagged it as high-risk. Before we go any further, a licensed physician has to review her case and she has to sign a waiver acknowledging the added risk — I don't let it proceed on trust alone."* Same dramatic weight, defensible if a judge tests the exact scenario afterward.

**Also flagged, not acted on (Portia's call, not a blocking item)**: the script's "it remembers, it adapts" framing around live interruption is very close to true — barge-in is real and verified working — but M-Care does not automatically weave an interrupted topic back into a later reply; Phase 19 deliberately built a dismissible "want me to finish that?" chip instead, for the same honesty reasons as this phase. If the live interrupt moment is just "ask something, cut it off, ask something else," that works exactly as scripted; if it implies M-Care will unprompted circle back mid-reply, that doesn't happen. Also flagged as a non-code, operational risk: stage audio (distance from mic, crowd noise, echo) is a genuinely hard environment for browser speech recognition — recommended Portia rehearse the exact cue phrases with Conversational Mode on the actual venue mic setup beforehand, the same "test on real conditions before trusting it live" caveat as every prior voice feature this session.

**Verification**: lint clean, typecheck unchanged at the same 5-error pre-existing baseline (a `.jsonc` file isn't in TypeScript's checked scope), `npm run build` exit 0, 541/541 vitest, 222/222 redteam — no new invariant needed, confirmed by running the suite, since this touches only instructions text, no entity/tool/permission surface. Live-only, cannot be confirmed from this checkout (no local Base44 session, the same standing limitation as every prior phase): whether M-Care, asked the exact cue phrase on stage, reliably recites the grounded facts without drifting back into inventing extra color — Portia should test the exact phrasing live, more than once, before trusting it in front of judges.

## WhatsApp check-ins, Crisis Reroutes dashboard, and Family Portal rebrand (2026-08-08)

Portia asked for four things in one message: automated WhatsApp check-ins on travel milestones, an admin dashboard showing when M-Care had to intervene in a booking, a guardian-facing "Family Portal" with login, and recovery-time/risk-factor detail on procedures ("if this is already wired in dont make changes"). Explored all four before writing any code, since this repo's whole history is full of "the gap was wiring, not a missing feature" — three of the four turned out to already substantially exist.

**Procedure risk display — confirmed already fully wired, zero changes made.** Verified fresh (not just from memory): `ProcedureKnowledge`'s `risk_level`/`risk_factors`/`complication_rate`/`recovery_time` fields, `getProcedureRiskSummaries` (never fabricates, 0.5 match threshold), and the risk pills on `Procedures.jsx`/`ProcedureModal.jsx` are all real and working exactly as documented in the "Guardian email re-access + procedure risk display" section above. Still zero `ProcedureKnowledge` rows populated — by design, Portia's own future task via `/admin/procedure-knowledge`.

**WhatsApp check-ins — a real, live, cron-scheduled system already existed** (`sendMilestoneWhatsAppCheckIns`, every 15 min, `.github/workflows/safety-cron.yml`) covering exactly the two examples Portia gave (hotel arrival, procedure completion) plus 4 more `SoloCheckIn` milestones. Asked her directly (AskUserQuestion) whether this task meant fixing/finishing that system vs. also connecting M-Care's chat agent to WhatsApp (Base44's own agent platform has real, unused `getWhatsAppConnectURL`/`getTelegramConnectURL` SDK support — confirmed live in `node_modules/@base44/sdk`, never invoked anywhere in this repo, `whatsapp_greeting`/`telegram_greeting` still `null` on `m_care.jsonc`) — she chose fix/finish. The real bug: both `sendMilestoneWhatsAppCheckIns` and a second, never-scheduled sibling (`sendWhatsAppCaseUpdate`) called Twilio directly with hand-composed bodies containing the patient's name and per-milestone medical/status text ("Your procedure is complete," "Hi *Maria*") — bypassing `notify.ts`'s mandatory link-only discipline the same way `checkStalledSignups`'s own redteam-guarded precedent exists to prevent. Fixed: both now route through `linkOnlySms`/`linkOnlyEmail`, one generic milestone-agnostic line per channel (the real per-stage detail still lives in-platform on `SoloCheckIn.milestone_type` and behind the confirm link — nothing lost, just not in the outbound text); both migrated onto `createHandler` (were raw `Deno.serve`, this repo's standing anti-pattern). `sendWhatsAppCaseUpdate` was confirmed never called from any live path (its mention inside `m_care.jsonc` is only in the free-text `instructions` prose, not an actual `tool_configs` grant — checked directly, zero real risk) — its body contract was simplified to `{case_id}` (re-reads the real case fresh, never trusts a caller-supplied name/status) but it was deliberately **not** wired into any new call site: `CaseRecord.status` is written directly in 72 different functions, and the one "canonical" chokepoint (`bookingState.ts`'s `guardedStatusUpdate`) is actually used by only 4 of them — wiring there would give partial, misleading coverage for real risk to a shared file, and isn't what was actually asked (the two given examples are already covered by the milestone system). Left as a safe, standalone, correctly-built function for a future, deliberate wiring pass. `sendMilestoneWhatsAppCheckIns` added to the redteam `MIGRATED` link-only-senders list. **Not verifiable from this checkout**: whether Twilio's WhatsApp channel is actually connected/approved on Portia's account — that's a Twilio-console fact, not a code fact.

**Crisis Reroutes dashboard — the page was already real and fully built** (`src/pages/AdminCrisisReroutes.jsx`, `/admin/crisis-reroutes`, `CrisisReroute` entity) but nothing populated it from the automated safety net — its only writer, `handleCrisisReroute`, is only ever invoked by M-Care's chat agent, never by the 5 real cron functions that already perform automated interventions (`checkPartnerSLABreaches`, `detectFallbackCrisis`, `escalateMissedDriverHandshake`, `dispatchSecurityForCheckIn.ts` — shared by `dispatchSoloSecurity`, `escalateSoloCheckIn`'s 5h tier, and the guardian-triggered security request — and `escalateSoloCheckIn`'s own 9h tier). `handleCrisisReroute` itself couldn't just be called from those 5: it hard-requires a user session (no cron path in) and re-runs its own backup search + notifications, which would have double-dispatched backups and double-notified people on top of what each function already does. Fixed: new `base44/shared/logCrisisReroute.ts`, a small, genuinely-never-throwing helper (verified by inspection — every await sits inside a try/catch, the outer catch only logs) that each of the 5 functions now calls right after its own existing action, reusing the outcome it already computed — no re-search, no duplicate notification, and a logging failure can never block the real safety action. Added one new `crisis_type` enum value, `PARTNER_UNRESPONSIVE` (a single-line, git-diff-confirmed additive edit to `CrisisReroute.jsonc`; `AdminCrisisReroutes.jsx` renders `crisis_type` as plain text with no hardcoded badge map, so this needed zero UI change). `checkPartnerSLABreaches`'s 48-hour human-escalation branch has no natural one-time gate (it re-fires every 15-minute tick) — given an explicit dedupe check (skip if an unresolved row already exists for the case) so it doesn't spam a new row every tick. The dashboard should now populate from real interventions going forward — worth Portia checking `/admin/crisis-reroutes` live after the next real SLA breach or no-show fires.

**Family Portal — no new login, per Portia's own choice** (asked directly: build real login-based guardian accounts, a genuinely new auth surface this repo has never had, explicitly avoided once already near a deadline — vs. keep the existing 100% token-link `GuardianView.jsx`/`GuardianSession` system and make it read like a real portal; she chose the latter). Investigating the "known duplicate, not yet reconciled" flag from the Phase 7 section above turned up a correction, not a merge: `TravelerGuardian.jsx`/`ShareLiveLocation.jsx` were never guardian-facing at all — the first is the *patient's* screen for activating milestone check-ins, the second a *patient-consented* distress-triggered GPS beacon for the care team, with no guardian-facing surface in either to merge into `GuardianView.jsx`. The real problem was just a naming collision ("Traveler Guardian" reading as part of the same family as the real Guardian/Family Portal system). Fixed with copy-only changes — no route, entity, or function renamed, so every already-issued guardian link keeps working: `TravelerGuardian.jsx`'s user-facing text now says "M-Safe Check-Ins" instead of "Traveler Guardian"/"Guardian mode"; `GuardianView.jsx`'s heading, footer watermark, loading/expired-link states, and a new visible label above the notification/message feed now say "M-Care Family Portal" (a name `GuardianMessage`'s own entity description already used internally, just not shown consistently on the page); `GuardianAccess.jsx`'s eyebrow text matches. Left untouched, out of scope: `/traveler-guardian` still isn't in real navigation (only reachable by typing the URL — a real gap, but a UX placement call for Portia); a third, separate "Guardian Mode" naming collision in `ConsultationSuccess.jsx`/`Dashboard.jsx` (the solo-check-in opt-in toggle, yet another distinct concept, flagged not fixed).

**Verification**: lint clean; `npm run build` exit 0; 545/545 vitest; 222/222 redteam including the updated `MIGRATED` check. `npm run typecheck` still fails, but on the same pre-existing baseline as before this pass (5 errors, all in `src/components/mcare-agent/MCareVaultUpload.jsx`/`src/pages/MCareAgent.jsx` — unrelated to any of this session's changes; the baseline itself appears to have silently drifted from 4 to 5 at some point since Phase 21 was written, worth a dedicated look next time that surface is touched, not chased down here).

## M-Care super-agent, Phase 22 — real WhatsApp-style voice messages (2026-08-09)

A mic-button bug report ("not working on my laptop") turned into two separate findings and then a real feature build. First: the "Allow microphone?" dialog added the day before (`MicPermissionGate.jsx`, part of the same `base44-builder[bot]` batch that shipped Talk Mode's mic UX) opened but rendered behind M-Care's own full-viewport panel — a z-index collision (`Dialog`'s shared `z-50` vs. the panel's `zIndex: 9001`) that made the button appear to silently do nothing. Fixed with a single `z-[9999]` override at the call site (`MicPermissionGate.jsx`), not the shared `dialog.jsx` primitive (`db0a6eed`). The push that carried that fix also surfaced a second, unrelated regression from the same bot batch: real-time barge-in (Conversational Mode's core "stop M-Care mid-sentence" feature, Phase 19) had been silently replaced with half-duplex behavior (mic ignored entirely while M-Care speaks) — `createBargeInDetector` was imported but never called anymore, `handleBargeIn` was dead code, and this broke `npm run lint`, which `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs as a required step on every push to `main` — so CI had likely been red since the bot merge landed, not just from this session's own pushes. Removed the unused import to restore a clean CI run (`4c0c0ab0`); whether the half-duplex simplification itself was Portia's deliberate choice (a reasonable one — real barge-in risks false triggers from the mic picking up M-Care's own voice without headphones) or an accidental side effect was flagged to her directly rather than silently reverted or silently kept — **unresolved as of this entry, worth checking before assuming either way**.

Once the mic button actually worked, Portia asked for the real thing behind her original report: WhatsApp-style voice messages (record → live waveform → send as a playable audio note), not the transcribe-to-editable-text mic feature that's existed since Phase 2B. Researched WhatsApp's actual mechanics (WebSearch — live waveform during recording, pause/resume, preview before send, a static playback waveform with a progress fill) and the real codebase building blocks before designing anything: confirmed nothing resembling "attach raw recorded audio to a chat message" existed anywhere (repo-wide sweep — no waveform library installed, only `VoiceMode.jsx`'s cosmetic `Math.random()` bars); confirmed `base44.agents.addMessage` genuinely accepts `content` (text) + `file_urls` (flat array of URL strings) together, already exercised today by `MCareOrb.jsx`'s existing file-attachment flow; confirmed the agent itself never reads file bytes behind a `file_url`, only the `content` text — so a real transcript still has to travel to the agent even though the UI won't show it as text.
