Asked Portia directly on the two real design forks: replace the chat's mic button entirely with record-and-send (not keep both behaviors — she chose full replacement, matching real WhatsApp, which never offers an edit-before-send step either), and render the sent bubble audio-only like real WhatsApp rather than adding a bonus transcript caption underneath (she chose audio-only, the literal match).

**Shipped** (`4d95b85e`): `src/lib/voiceMessageAudio.js` — the first real audio-reactive waveform code in this repo, built on `AudioContext`/`AnalyserNode` directly (no library exists for this; confirmed absent from `package.json`). `startLevelMeter(stream, onLevel)` drives a live ~20fps RMS amplitude meter during recording (throttled deliberately — a UI meter doesn't need 60 computations/sec); `computeWaveformBars(samples, barCount)` (the pure, unit-tested part — `tests/voiceMessageAudio.test.js`) turns any amplitude array into a normalized, peak-per-segment bar envelope; `generateWaveformBars(audioBlob, barCount)` wraps `decodeAudioData` around it for a finished recording's static waveform shape, closing its `AudioContext` in a `finally` so repeated record/discard cycles can't leak contexts, and never rejects — any decode failure (corrupt blob, unsupported codec, no `AudioContext` at all) resolves to a flat neutral bar shape so a UI component always has something to render. `src/components/mcare/VoiceMessageRecorder.jsx` (new) is the chat's new mic control — idle mic icon → (after `MicPermissionGate`, reused unchanged) a WhatsApp-shaped recording toolbar (trash/discard, pulsing timer, the live waveform, pause/resume via `MediaRecorder.pause()/.resume()` where supported, gold send arrow instead of WhatsApp's green) → `onSend(audioBlob, durationMs)`, with a 2-minute cap (extended from `VoiceInputButton.jsx`'s 20-second transcription cap — a voice note is naturally longer than a dictation snippet) that correctly pauses/resumes with the recording rather than ticking down during a pause. `MessageBubble.jsx` gained `isAudioUrl(url)` (mirrors the existing `isImageUrl` pattern) and a `VoiceNotePlayer` sub-component — fetches the sent audio once, computes its waveform via the same `generateWaveformBars`, renders play/pause + bars + a real `timeupdate`-synced progress fill + duration; a message whose only attachment is that one audio file now suppresses its own `content` paragraph, so the human-visible bubble is audio-only exactly as chosen, while the transcript that traveled to the agent stays real and unseen. `MCareOrb.jsx`'s chat input row swapped `VoiceInputButton` for `VoiceMessageRecorder`; the send handler uploads via the same `Core.UploadFile` this file's existing file-attachment flow already uses, transcribes via the unchanged `transcribeVoiceInput` function (falling back to a generic `"[voice message]"` placeholder if transcription fails, so a transcription hiccup can never silently break the send), and calls the exact same `sendAgentMessage`/`addMessage` path every other message in this file already uses — no parallel send mechanism.

**Deliberately untouched, confirmed via git diff and grep**: `VoiceInputButton.jsx` itself and its two other call sites, `BookingIntentEntry.jsx`/`AvailabilityIntentEntry.jsx` — those are one-shot text-field mic buttons (fill a form field), not a chat thread, and still need the editable-transcript behavior the main chat just gave up. `base44/agents/m_care.jsonc` — zero `tool_configs`/instructions changes, zero Publish-order risk, since the agent already understood `content`+`file_urls` messages before this feature existed.

**Known, stated-not-hidden limitations**: `audio/webm` (this app's existing recording format in both `MediaRecorder` implementations) has inconsistent `<audio>` playback support in Safari — not a new gap, every voice feature in this app already documents a browser caveat somewhere, but worth Portia testing on more than one browser before the demo. Voice-message audio uploads through the same public `Core.UploadFile` every other chat attachment (images, documents) already uses, not the encrypted vault path — consistent with existing precedent, not a new privacy regression, but spoken messages can carry more incidental PHI than a typed one, worth knowing rather than assuming encrypted-by-default.

**Deliberately out of scope this pass**: `MCareAgent.jsx`'s full-page `/m-care` surface (same "`MCareOrb` first, parity later" scoping every prior voice feature in this file has used); WhatsApp's playback-speed control (1x/1.5x/2x) and outside-the-chat background playback; the slide-to-cancel gesture (an explicit trash button was used instead — matches Portia's own reference screenshot and suits a laptop trackpad better than a mobile swipe).

**Verification**: lint clean; `npm run build` exit 0; 552/552 vitest (7 new, covering `computeWaveformBars`'s pure logic only — the `AudioContext`-dependent parts are untested by the same jsdom-can't-do-real-audio precedent as `neuralSpeech.js`); 222/222 redteam; `npm run typecheck` still fails on the same pre-existing baseline (4 errors, `MCareVaultUpload.jsx`/`MCareAgent.jsx`, confirmed via git diff to be outside this feature's changed files). Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: real waveform feel/timing on an actual mic and browser, and Safari audio playback — Portia needs to test this on her own laptop (and ideally a second browser) before the demo, the same standing limitation as every prior voice feature in this file.

## Travel-agency quote requests — activated a dead feature, enriched it (2026-08-10)

Portia wanted M-Care to email a travel agency for a flight+hotel quote once a doctor confirms a case — reading human, not robotic, and including everything the agency actually needs (passport/visa readiness, traveler count, package inclusions/exclusions), on email and WhatsApp both. Real research before building anything found the feature already ~80% existed as `base44/functions/assignTravelAgency/entry.ts` but was dead code, for two compounding reasons: its portal link was an unsigned string token built from the wrong ID field (`CaseRecord.id` instead of the real FK `consultation_id`, which is what `getPortalData` — the actual token consumer — keys every lookup off), so the agency's browser threw immediately on click; and its `doctor_confirmation_status !== 'CONFIRMED'` precondition (all-caps) never matched the real value the doctor-confirm action actually writes (`'Confirmed'`, mixed-case) — the same bug sits in two other dormant "notify travel agency" implementations in this codebase (`pipelineOnDoctorConfirmed`, `requestPartnerQuotas`), very likely why travel-agency notification has silently never fired from any code path in this app, ever.

**Two explicit product decisions made with Portia before building** (both matching this app's standing discipline, not new exceptions): the outbound message stays a warm, human-*sounding* but fixed, reviewed template — this app has never let an LLM freely author unsupervised prose sent to a real external business, and Rule 3/`notify.ts`'s "no invented data, no unreviewed external content" discipline holds here too; and this pass targets agencies already verified in the Morales system only — cold outreach to agencies Morales has never worked with (no existing way to even identify one) is real, separate, future scope, not rushed in here.

**Fixed**: `signPortalToken(caseRecord.consultation_id, selectedAgency.id, 'travel')` (the canonical signer, not a local reimplementation) replaces the broken token, URL-encoded when building the link; the confirmation check accepts both casings; the auth gate moved from hardcoded admin-only to `internalOrAdminAuthorized` (needed since the real trigger has no forwardable user session, while a direct admin call still works). **Wired**: `respondToDoctorPortalCase`'s `decision === 'confirmed'` branch — the one place in the codebase directly traceable end-to-end from a doctor's real "Confirm" click — now calls `assignTravelAgency`, awaited and wrapped so a dispatch failure can never fail the doctor's own confirmation. **Enriched**: joins in `Consultation`'s real, already-captured `nationality`/`passport_expiry_date`/`visa_required_status`/`number_of_companions` fields (the visa-requirement computation itself, `src/lib/visaMatrix.js`, was already real and working — it just needed its inputs); phrases the stored `exempt|evisa|embassy|unknown` enum in plain language for a travel-agency reader. No passport *number* anywhere — that field doesn't exist as structured data in this app by a deliberate prior security decision (an OCR-extraction feature for it was removed after review; only a redacted display hint and expiry date survive in `PassportVault`). Dropped the patient's name from the message entirely — a travel agency doesn't need it to quote, and it's one less piece of identity leaving the platform pre-engagement. Added WhatsApp as a second channel (`TravelAgency.whatsapp_number`/`phone`, via the existing generic `shared/twilioWhatsApp.ts` sender — not a reimplementation), non-fatal, sent only after the email succeeds. Also closed a latent XSS-in-email gap while the file was already open (every interpolated value is now HTML-escaped; the previous version wasn't escaping anything).

**Flagged, not fixed, same pass**: `pipelineOnDoctorConfirmed`/`requestPartnerQuotas`'s identical case-mismatch bug — left a disclosure comment on the former noting `assignTravelAgency` is now the real trigger, so fixing their dormant bug later needs reconciling against this path first or a confirmed case would double-notify the agency. `assignChauffeurServices`/`matchTravelRequestPartners` — separate, different token bugs of their own, out of scope here. M-Care's own agent tool grant for `assignTravelAgency` likely still doesn't do anything from a live patient conversation (can't satisfy even the widened auth gate without a real forwarded session) — not touched, avoids `m_care.jsonc` Publish-order risk for no benefit.

**Explicitly deferred, Portia's own call**: generalizing this "M-Care orchestrates a partner for a service" pattern to doctors, drivers, companions, and security agencies. Each has a genuinely different real-world interaction shape today — doctors get matched-and-confirmed, travel agencies/taxis get quoted, companions get offered jobs, security gets dispatched in emergencies — with no single existing pattern to copy across all five. Decided to prove this out for travel agencies first and design the multi-partner version as its own focused piece once there's a real, working template to generalize from, rather than rushing a shallow version across five different shapes at once.

**A real, unrelated merge conflict surfaced and fixed along the way**: while this was in flight, a substantial `base44-builder[bot]` batch landed on `main` from Portia's own concurrent Base44 Builder session — a genuinely well-built "M-Care Voice & Privacy" layer (`src/lib/distressDetection.js`, `voiceCommands.js`, `spokenLanguageDetect.js`, `mcarePreferences.js` + `useMcarePreferences.js` hook), none of it touching the same files as this fix (confirmed via diff against the shared base before merging — zero real conflict, despite `git push` initially rejecting on a stale ref). Worth crediting: `distressDetection.js`'s design matches this app's own safety discipline closely without any prompting — word-boundary/whole-utterance matching to avoid false positives from ordinary chat, and a heard distress signal triggers only a calm, audit-logged welfare check, never an automatic emergency dispatch (real escalation still flows through the existing `runSilentSafetyEscalation` ladder). It also fixed a real bug in M-Care's own conversational-mode half-duplex guard — the old check only read the React `speaking` state, which lands one render behind reality and misses several audio paths, letting M-Care's own voice occasionally leak back in as a "user" message; the fix (`isNeuralSpeaking()`, a synchronous module-level flag set the instant any audio path starts) closes that gap for good. `m_care.jsonc`'s `tool_configs` (88 entries) were confirmed byte-identical before/after this batch — the whole diff was `description`/`instructions` prose, no new tool grants, no Publish-order risk. One real, structural gap this merge exposed and fixed: `useMcarePreferences.js` landed in a brand-new `src/hooks/` directory that `eslint.config.js`'s `files` glob never covered, so its `react-hooks/exhaustive-deps` disable comment referenced a rule ESLint had no definition for in that file's context — broke `npm run lint`, which `ci.yml` runs as a required step on every push. Fixed by adding `src/hooks/**/*.{js,mjs,cjs,jsx}` to the glob — the correct, permanent fix (any future file placed there gets linted too), not a one-off suppression.

## M-Care super-agent, Phase 23 — fixing Talk Mode's mid-speech cutoff, and making choice options actually spoken (2026-08-10)

Portia sent a full spec for TTS voice replies in M-Care's chat, saying she'd tried building it directly in Base44 Builder but kept hitting a bug: "M-Care would cut when talking and type the rest." Her real goal, stated directly: a blind user should be able to complete an entire booking, door-to-door, by voice alone. Investigated before building anything — everything in her spec already existed (Talk Mode, a real neural TTS pipeline via Base44's `Core.GenerateSpeech` with browser-TTS fallback, a per-message "Listen" button, UI + voice-command toggles), built across Phases 17-21. Asked her directly and she agreed: fix the real bug in the existing system rather than add a new paid TTS vendor (OpenAI/ElevenLabs, as her spec suggested) — the reported symptom turned out to be a state-management bug, not a TTS-quality problem a different vendor would have fixed.

**Root cause, fully diagnosed** (two research passes over the real code): `revealState` in `MCareOrb.jsx` — the single state driving which chat bubble's text is being word-by-word revealed in sync with its audio — is shared across the whole component, not scoped per message. Three call sites (a tool-call spoken acknowledgment, the distress-welfare prompt from the new Silent Guardian feature, and a voice-message "I'll reply out loud" cue) called `speakTextNeural` directly — which always kills whatever's currently playing (`stopNeuralSpeech()`) — but never told the bubble that WAS speaking that it had been cut off. That bubble froze mid-sentence, audio dead, until some unrelated later event happened to clear `revealState` — at which point its text jumped straight to fully visible with zero indication anything had gone wrong. That's the literal "cuts off mid-speech, rest just appears as text" mechanism. A second, related gap: the spoken/acknowledged dedup sets were keyed only by a message's server `id` — which silently no-ops while a message is still id-less (a tool call still running) and only gains a real id on a later tick, letting the same logical message occasionally get processed twice, triggering the same kind of interruption.

**Fixed** (`9438bd66`): new `speakAncillary()` wrapper in `MCareOrb.jsx` nulls `revealState` the instant an ancillary utterance takes over the audio channel, giving the interrupted bubble the same graceful "reply's over, show the rest" treatment a genuine new-message supersession already got — right when the interruption happens, not whenever an unrelated event later gets around to it. New `isClaimed`/`claim` helpers key the dedup sets by both a message's stable array index and its id once assigned, closing the double-processing gap. Separately, `neuralSpeech.js`'s `audio.onended` used to treat any browser playback finish as unconditional success — a dropped or truncated audio stream fires `ended` early (not `error`), so the fallback voice never engaged and a partial finish looked identical to a clean one. Now verifies `audio.currentTime` actually reached `audio.duration` (rate-independent — using the word-reveal count instead would false-negative on nearly every normal finish, since playback runs at `rate` 0.9x relative to the reveal timer's own pacing) before declaring success; short of that, it falls back. The fallback itself (`resumeFromInterruption`, moved to `talkMode.js` — the file's own pure/testable sibling, since importing anything from `neuralSpeech.js` pulls in a `base44Client`/`window.location` import-time dependency that breaks under vitest) now resumes from the unspoken remainder instead of re-speaking the entire reply from the top.

**A second, real gap surfaced in the same investigation, not a regression**: when M-Care offers tappable choices (e.g. payment plan options), only the visual chip labels were guaranteed to exist — `extractSpeakableText` stripped the `{{choices:...}}` token with no spoken trace at all, so a blind user relying on Talk Mode had no guaranteed way to hear what the options actually were, short of the AI happening to restate them in its own free-form prose. Fixed the same pass, confirmed with Portia: `extractSpeakableText` now captures the option labels before stripping the token and appends a deterministic, code-generated sentence ("Your options are: Pay in Full, 25% Deposit, or 50% Deposit.") — natural single/two-option phrasing for those cases, visual chip rendering completely untouched. Flagged, not fixed: `MessageBubble.jsx`'s separate per-message "Listen" button has the identical gap in its own local extraction path — a distinct call site, deliberately left as a disclosed follow-up rather than silently duplicated or silently left broken.

**Explicitly out of scope, Portia's own choice**: sign-in (phone + 6-digit OTP, a required, unvoiced, typing-only gate before any real booking action) and payment (Stripe's own hosted checkout page — genuinely outside this app's control to make voice-accessible) are both real, hard walls for true door-to-door voice booking, surfaced during the same investigation. Both bigger and separate from this session's scope — worth their own dedicated pass, not a quick add-on here.

**Verification**: lint clean, typecheck unchanged at the same pre-existing baseline (`MCareVaultUpload.jsx`/`MCareAgent.jsx`, unrelated), `npm run build` exit 0, 599/599 vitest (14 new/updated across `tests/talkMode.test.js` and the new `tests/neuralSpeech.test.js`, covering `resumeFromInterruption` and the choice-enumeration phrasing), 222/222 redteam — run and confirmed green after each piece landed, per Portia's own instruction, not just once at the end. Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: whether the fix actually eliminates the reported cutoff on a real device/network, and whether the spoken choice enumeration reads naturally in a live conversation — Portia should test Talk Mode through a real multi-message exchange, including a reply that offers choices, before the next demo.

## M-Care super-agent, Phase 24 — Conversational Mode's voice toggle no longer dies on a WiFi blip (2026-08-10)

Portia: "sometimes the talk button untoggle for it self i think its a bug," with a screenshot of a destructive "Voice input unavailable / You appear to be offline" toast. Asked her to narrow down the trigger; she diagnosed it herself before the clarifying question finished: "i got it when it goes offline the talk to toggle off."

That exact toast title exists in exactly one place in the repo — `MCareOrb.jsx`'s `onUnrecoverableError` handler for Conversational Mode's continuous `SpeechRecognition` session (`src/lib/conversationalMode.js`'s `startContinuousRecognition`). Root cause: Chrome's `SpeechRecognition` is cloud-backed and fires a `'network'` error on *any* brief connectivity blip — a WiFi hiccup, a cellular handoff — not just a genuine outage. `recognition.onerror` treated **any** error other than `'no-speech'`/`'aborted'` as instantly unrecoverable, with zero retry — so one transient blip permanently killed the always-listening voice toggle mid-conversation, every time, even though `'no-speech'`/`'aborted'` already got silent auto-retry treatment. `friendlyError.js`'s `isOffline()` regex matches the literal string `"network"`, which is why the toast read as a full "you're offline" message rather than something proportionate to a 1-2 second hiccup.

**Fixed**: `'network'` errors now get the same silent-retry treatment as `'no-speech'`/`'aborted'`, up to a bounded grace window. New `createNetworkErrorTracker(maxRetries)` (mirrors the existing `createBargeInDetector`'s shape) tracks consecutive `'network'` errors — `onError('network')` returns `true` (retry) while under the cap, `false` once spent; `onSuccess()` (called on any real recognized result — proof the connection is actually alive) resets the streak. New `NETWORK_ERROR_MAX_RETRIES` (6) and `NETWORK_RETRY_BACKOFF_MS` (1500ms, longer than the routine 400ms restart — a real WiFi drop commonly takes a few seconds) — starting guesses needing live tuning, same honesty precedent as this file's `SILENCE_NUDGE_MS`/`BARGE_IN_MIN_EVENTS`. Only once the network retry budget is genuinely exhausted does it fall through to the original `onUnrecoverableError` path — a sustained outage still gets the same honest toast and shutoff as before, just after a real grace period instead of on the first blip. The existing `restartCount`/`MAX_AUTO_RESTARTS` mechanism is untouched and the network tracker's streak is a separate counter, so a long conversation full of ordinary pauses can't cannibalize the network retry budget and vice versa.

**Also added**: once a network-caused shutoff *does* happen, Conversational Mode now resumes automatically when the connection actually comes back, instead of leaving the user to notice and manually re-tap the phone icon — mirrors how a phone call recovers from a signal drop rather than requiring a manual redial, and directly serves the "even a blind user should get a robust, door-to-door experience" goal from earlier phases. A new `autoResumeConversationalRef` in `MCareOrb.jsx` is set `true` only when `onUnrecoverableError`'s `reason === 'network'` (never for a blocked/revoked mic — those still require the user to act), and the existing `online` window-event listener calls `setConversationalMode(true)` when the flag is set. The flag is explicitly cleared on every path where the user intentionally turns Conversational Mode off themselves (the toggle button, the `"stop listening"` voice command, starting a new journey, closing the panel) — a later reconnect only ever resumes a state the user actually wanted, never overrides an explicit choice made while offline.

**Incidental bug found and fixed while investigating, unrelated to the reported symptom** (confirmed: `MCareOrb.jsx`'s own `talkMode` state only reads from `prefs` once at mount, so this couldn't have caused the visible toggle flip): `useMcarePreferences.js`'s `update()` is memoized on `[user]` only but read `prefs` directly from its closure — after the first render following a `user` change, `update` kept referencing that stale `prefs` snapshot for every later call, so each persisted patch merged onto an increasingly stale base and could silently revert other already-saved preferences (`privateMode`, `responseLanguage`) back toward defaults. Fixed with a `prefsRef` kept current via a small effect, read inside `update` instead of the closed-over variable — same latest-value-via-ref pattern already used elsewhere in `MCareOrb.jsx` (`agentMessagesRef`).

**Verification**: lint clean, typecheck unchanged at the same 4-error pre-existing baseline (`MCareVaultUpload.jsx`/`MCareAgent.jsx`, unrelated), `npm run build` exit 0, 604/604 vitest (5 new in `tests/conversationalMode.test.js` covering `createNetworkErrorTracker`), 222/222 redteam. Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout (no live Base44 session / real network to test against): whether `NETWORK_ERROR_MAX_RETRIES`/`NETWORK_RETRY_BACKOFF_MS` feel right against a real WiFi blip, and whether auto-resume-on-reconnect actually fires on a real device — Portia should test by toggling Conversational Mode on, then briefly disabling/re-enabling WiFi mid-conversation, before the next demo.

## M-Care super-agent, Phase 25 — a real, playable voice-note bubble in reply to one (2026-08-10)

Portia, sending voice notes while driving: "M-care should be able to send back voice notes like a human... once send voice note M-care listen and response back in voice note." Her screenshot showed M-Care replying in plain text, once literally saying "I can only respond in text. I don't have the ability to generate or send voice messages back to you" and offering to connect her to a human instead — Talk Mode's icon visibly off after two voice notes already sent.

**Two separate things, investigated before touching anything.** First: `base44/agents/m_care.jsonc` already has a `RULE 18 — VOICE REPLIES` ("You are NOT text-only... never tell them 'I can only respond in text'... because that is false") — added via a `base44-builder[bot]` sync at 00:52 UTC the same day, almost certainly Portia's own edit in Base44 Builder, landing right around when she hit this. Not touched this pass — no code fix belongs there, and re-editing that giant single-line `instructions` field for a change already made risks nothing but a collision. **Flagged to Portia directly**: confirm it's actually been Published (a separate step from saving in Builder — the exact bug class documented in Phase 10 above) if she's still seeing the denial after that edit.

Second, the real buildable gap: Talk Mode (Phases 17-24) already auto-enables and speaks a reply aloud the moment a voice message is sent (`handleVoiceMessage`'s `justEnabledVoice` check, Phase 22) — but the reply only ever played once through a transient `Audio()` object and rendered as a plain text bubble with a small "Listen" button, never as a real, replayable, WhatsApp-style bubble the way the *user's own* voice notes already render (`VoiceNotePlayer`, also Phase 22). That's the literal gap between "gets read aloud once, if Talk Mode happens to be on" and "voice note out." Traced every place `talkMode` can be set to false and found nothing that should have flipped it off between her two voice notes — rather than chase an unreproducible one-off, the fix makes the *reply* to a voice message unconditionally speak and render as a voice note regardless of the toggle's current state, which is the actual thing being asked for.

**Shipped, wiring together pieces that already existed**: `speakMcareText` (Phase 17, `Core.GenerateSpeech`) already returns a real, persistent MP3 URL; `neuralSpeech.js`'s `speakTextNeural` already fetched it, it just never handed the URL back — new optional `onAudioUrl(url)` callback, fired the instant the URL is confirmed (purely additive, never fires on the browser-TTS fallback path, which has no persistent file to hand back). `MCareOrb.jsx` gained a new ref, `forceSpeakNextReplyRef`, set unconditionally at the top of `handleVoiceMessage` every time a voice note is sent (not just the first) — the main speak effect's trigger widened from `talkMode && isSpeechSupported()` to `(talkMode || forceSpeakNextReplyRef.current) && isSpeechSupported()`, consuming the flag immediately so it only ever overrides the one reply that follows a voice note. That same speak call's real audio URL, once known, is written into a new sibling state, `voiceReplyAudioUrls` (keyed by message index) — deliberately **not** merged onto the message object itself, since `agentMessages` gets wholesale-replaced by `subscribeToConversation` on every server tick and would silently wipe anything written directly onto it; a separate map survives that. `MessageBubble.jsx` gained one new optional prop, `extraAudioUrl` (default `undefined` → byte-identical rendering for every existing caller), merged into a computed `allFileUrls` array reused everywhere `message.file_urls` was — so the reply's audio renders through the exact same `VoiceNotePlayer` the user's own voice notes already use, no new player component. Two guardrails while in there: `isPureVoiceNote` (hide the text, show only the waveform) is now restricted to `isUser &&` — an assistant reply must always keep its text visible, the audio bubble is an addition, never a replacement; and the "Listen" button hides itself when a real audio attachment already exists for that message, avoiding a redundant slower "generate it again" button next to a bubble that already plays the same audio instantly.

**Explicitly out of scope**: any `m_care.jsonc` edit (see above — a Publish/reliability question for Portia, not a code change); re-diagnosing the exact prior state that left Talk Mode visibly off — the fix makes the next reply speak regardless, which resolves the practical symptom without needing to reproduce it.

**Verification**: lint clean, typecheck unchanged at the same 4-error pre-existing baseline, `npm run build` exit 0, 604/604 vitest, 222/222 redteam — no entity/auth/`tool_configs` surface touched, frontend-only. Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout (no live Base44 session, no real mic/network): whether the reply audio actually plays back as a proper voice-note bubble on a real device, and whether RULE 18 is actually Published and live — Portia should send a real voice note and confirm both that M-Care never denies its voice capability and that a tappable, replayable audio bubble appears under its reply.

## M-Care super-agent, Phase 26 — same-day hardening after Phase 25 didn't show up live (2026-08-10)

Portia tested Phase 25 and got the identical old symptom — plain text reply, generic "Listen" button, no spoken audio, no bubble. Two things, not mutually exclusive, checked before touching code again:

**Confirmed the likely real cause first, by just asking.** Rather than assume a code bug, asked Portia directly whether Base44 showed an unpublished-changes/Publish button — it did. A `git push` only syncs code into Base44 Builder; the live app doesn't pick it up until a separate Publish click (this repo's own recurring bug class, see Phase 10 above). That alone fully explains her test showing the exact pre-Phase-25 behavior: she was very likely still on the old code. **Publishing was the most important next action, more than any further fix.**

**A second, real, independently-found gap, shipped as hardening regardless of the above.** Re-reading `MCareOrb.jsx`'s speak logic: every place deciding whether to *attempt* speaking a reply was gated by `isSpeechSupported()` (`talkMode.js` — checks only `window.speechSynthesis`/`SpeechSynthesisUtterance`, i.e. browser-native TTS). But `neuralSpeech.js`'s `speakTextNeural` is server-first (`Core.GenerateSpeech` + a real `<audio>` element) and needs none of that — the browser API is only ever needed by its own *fallback* (`talkMode.js`'s `speakText`), which already self-checks `isSpeechSupported()` and no-ops safely on its own. Gating the outer decision to even try on the same check meant the entire voice-note-reply feature could silently never run at all in any environment lacking `window.speechSynthesis` — a real possibility for Base44 Builder's own preview iframe or certain webviews — even with Phase 25 correctly published. Fixed: dropped `&& isSpeechSupported()` / `isSpeechSupported() &&` from the four places it gated an *attempt* to speak (the main reply-speak effect, `handleVoiceMessage`'s `justEnabledVoice`, the distress-welfare prompt, the voice-command confirmation) — every one of them already routes through `speakTextNeural`, whose own error handling was already safe, so this only widens *when* it tries, never removes a real guard. Deliberately left alone: the Talk Mode toggle button's own visibility gate (still `isSpeechSupported() &&`) and `isConversationalModeSupported()`/Conversational Mode's recognition gating (genuinely needs those browser APIs for voice *input*) — narrower scope under demo-prep time pressure, not silently dropped.

**Verification**: lint clean, typecheck unchanged at the same 4-error baseline, `npm run build` exit 0, 604/604 vitest, 222/222 redteam. Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: whether Publishing alone resolves it (most likely), or whether the `isSpeechSupported()` fix was also load-bearing — Portia should publish first, then retest.

## M-Care super-agent, Phase 27 — the real reason a tool-call reply never got a voice-note bubble (2026-08-10)

Portia, after Phase 26 shipped: "if this work this would be the world first super agent that can listen to voice message and relpy in voice message we have to make it happen" — not a new bug report, a demand that the round trip actually be bulletproof. Re-read `MCareOrb.jsx`'s speak effects end-to-end from scratch rather than re-diagnosing the old screenshots, and found a third, concrete, structural bug — independent of both Phase 26 candidates (Publish lag, `isSpeechSupported()`).

Base44's `subscribeToConversation` delivers the full messages array wholesale every tick (confirmed at the call site). A reply that involves a tool call arrives in two steps: an assistant message appended with empty content + a `pending`/`running` `tool_calls` entry (array growth), then the same message id updated in place with the real text once the tool finishes (no array growth — an id-replace). Phase 19 already built an effect to catch that id-replace transition, but scoped it to Conversational Mode only. Two compounding bugs meant a plain Talk-Mode voice-note reply could never reach it: (1) the main growth effect's `forceSpeakNextReplyRef.current = false` fired unconditionally the instant `talkMode || forceSpeakNextReplyRef.current` was true — *before* checking whether there was anything to actually say — so the flag got burned on the empty tool-call placeholder, with nothing left armed by the time real content showed up; (2) the id-replace catch-up effect itself was gated `if (!conversationalMode ...) return`, and `handleVoiceMessage` deliberately never turns Conversational Mode on (output-only, to avoid a mic feedback loop) — so even an armed flag had nothing listening for the in-place update. Net result: any voice-note reply touching so much as one of M-Care's ~88 tools — the common case — silently never spoke and never got its `onAudioUrl` bubble attached, reproducing the exact "plain text + generic Listen button, no bubble" symptom with zero dependency on Publish state. A reply needing no tool call (the earlier, simpler tests) worked fine, which is consistent with this being intermittent rather than total.

**Fixed**, all in `MCareOrb.jsx`: the growth effect now only consumes `forceSpeakNextReplyRef.current` once `extractSpeakableText` actually returns something, leaving it armed across a content-less placeholder; the id-replace effect's gate widened from `conversationalMode`-only to `conversationalMode || talkMode || forceSpeakNextReplyRef.current`, with the same `onAudioUrl` wiring the growth effect already had — the spoken "let me look that up" tool-running acknowledgment stays Conversational-Mode-only by deliberate, unwidened scope (that's a live-call behavior, not asked for here). Traced the shared `spokenAssistantIdsRef`/`isClaimed`/`claim` guard and `prevMsgCountRef` bookkeeping through both the plain-chat (no regression — gate still fully closed) and tool-call (now actually closes the gap) cases before shipping. Same one-line `onAudioUrl` addition made to `toggleTalkMode`'s output-only fallback (speaks the last reply when Talk Mode turns on with no `SpeechRecognition` support) for full spoken-reply-to-bubble consistency everywhere M-Care speaks — a fourth call site with the identical gap, found while already in this code.

**Verification**: lint clean, typecheck unchanged at the same 4-error baseline, `npm run build` exit 0, 604/604 vitest (no new tests — `MCareOrb.jsx` isn't unit-tested, consistent with its whole history; verified by structural re-read instead), 222/222 redteam. Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: Portia should test with a request likely to trigger a tool call specifically (e.g. "what's my hotel address," "show me my trips"), not just a conversational one, since that's the exact case this closes.

## M-Care super-agent, Phase 28 — the reply still read as a text wall with a Listen pill, plus a real multilingual bug (2026-08-10)

Portia, after Phase 27: "you missing the point i want M-care to reply in a voice mail like how a user would send a message in a voice mail." Not a mechanism complaint this time (Phase 27 fixed that) — a presentation one. She then pasted a long, detailed AI-generated spec for a full voice-in/voice-out pipeline, explicitly instructing to inspect the existing system and report what's already real before building anything, and explicitly forbidding browser `speechSynthesis`/a "Listen" button as the primary mechanism.

Did the inspection her spec asked for, file by file, rather than assuming either way. Verdict: the full pipeline she described already exists and is real — `VoiceMessageRecorder.jsx` (real `MediaRecorder`), `transcribeVoiceInput` (real `Core.TranscribeAudio`), the real 88-tool agent conversation, `speakMcareText`/`Core.GenerateSpeech` (real neural TTS — browser `speechSynthesis` is only ever the automatic fallback when that network call fails, not the primary path her spec correctly warns against), a real persistent audio URL, and `VoiceNotePlayer` (real audio-amplitude waveform, play/pause, progress, duration — already used for the user's own sent notes). Nothing here needed rebuilding, and nothing in this pass adds a parallel data model, a new "TTS provider abstraction," or any other duplicate infrastructure the spec itself says to avoid.

Two real, narrower gaps found and fixed instead:

1. **Presentation.** `MessageBubble.jsx`'s `isPureVoiceNote` (the flag that hides the text paragraph and shows only the tappable waveform pill) only ever applied to the user's own recorded notes — an assistant reply with a real `extraAudioUrl` attached still rendered its full text *plus* the audio pill underneath, i.e. a text message with an attachment, not a voice message. Widened to `(isUser && ...) || (!isUser && !!extraAudioUrl)` — one boolean, everything downstream (text-hiding, `SpeakButton` suppression, the choices/maps/qr chip rendering) already keyed off it or was already independent of it. Trade-off, flagged not silently made: this also hides Phase 18's word-by-word caption reveal for these messages, since the paragraph it animates is now hidden — deliberate, since a real voicemail never shows a live transcript either, and nothing is lost (the hidden text is exactly what was just spoken). A reply that never got a real audio URL is completely unaffected.
2. **A real multilingual bug, found while verifying her spec's own explicit requirement** ("if the user speaks Spanish, M-Care should respond with a Spanish voice"). `speakMcareText/entry.ts` genuinely honors a `language` param for voice selection — but every auto-speak call site in `MCareOrb.jsx` omitted it, silently defaulting to English every time, in any language. The per-message "Listen" button (`MessageBubble.jsx`'s `SpeakButton`) already did this correctly (`language={i18n.language}`); the automatic voice-note-reply path never picked up the same pattern. Fixed: `language: i18n.language` added to all 4 direct `speakTextNeural` calls plus folded into `speakAncillary`'s own definition (covering its 3 callers — the tool-running ack, the distress-welfare prompt, and the voice-message "I'll reply out loud" cue — for free).

**Verification**: lint clean, typecheck unchanged at the same 4-error baseline, `npm run build` exit 0, 604/604 vitest, 222/222 redteam. **Confirmed live 2026-08-10**: Portia sent a Spanish voice note and got a real, playable Spanish voice-note bubble back (not a text wall, not an English default) — the round-trip (transcribe → agent replies in the input language → TTS speaks it → renders as a tappable voice note) works end-to-end on a real device.

## M-Care super-agent, Phase 29 — cross-wiring the research brain into a procedure-knowledge miss (2026-08-10)

Portia pasted a large, detailed spec asking M-Care to become an "adaptive knowledge system" (DISCOVER → RESEARCH → VERIFY → STORE → RECALL → ACT instead of ASK → GUESS → ACT), with a screenshot of M-Care telling her "I don't have detailed safety information on spine surgery in my knowledge base yet... I can help you find a verified specialist." The spec itself explicitly demanded an audit first ("inspect the entire existing M-Care intelligence architecture... do NOT build a second parallel agent") — did exactly that via two Explore passes over `base44/agents/m_care.jsonc` and the real backend, not assumptions.

**Verdict: almost the entire spec already exists and is real**, built across Phases 7 and 14. `mcareResearchAndLearn/entry.ts` genuinely does recall-first-against-`McareKnowledge` → `Core.InvokeLLM({ model: 'gemini_3_flash', add_context_from_internet: true })` on a miss → persist only at `confidence_score >= 80` (confirmed exact, `ACCURACY_THRESHOLD = 80`) — a real DISCOVER→RESEARCH→VERIFY→STORE loop. `recallMcareKnowledge/entry.ts` is a real, separate recall-only lookup. Both are already granted in `m_care.jsonc`'s `tool_configs` (68 function grants, confirmed via full parse — no Publish-order risk this pass, neither tool needed newly granting). `McareKnowledge`'s schema already keeps general knowledge strictly separate from patient data (no patient-identity field exists on it, confirmed against both writer functions' payloads) — the spec's "three memory layers, never mixed" requirement is already true by construction, just not under that name. The screenshot wasn't the system lacking a knowledge loop — it was two already-built, already-instructed systems (`getProcedureKnowledge`'s curated, admin-only `ProcedureKnowledge` table — currently zero rows populated, Portia's own future curation task — and the research brain) never being chained together for this one case. Three places in the instructions (the early PROCEDURE INQUIRY protocol step A, RULE 15's restatement, and the tool's own returned `m_care_protocol.steps[2]` text) told the agent to stop and offer a human specialist on `found:false`, with zero mention of falling back to the research tools it already had full access to.

**Fixed, three small text-only edits, zero new functions/entities/tool grants** (`tool_configs` count confirmed unchanged at 88 via a full `JSON.parse` before and after): both `m_care.jsonc` instruction locations now tell the agent to try `recallMcareKnowledge` → `mcareResearchAndLearn` first on a procedure-knowledge miss, falling back to the specialist-referral line only if research also comes up empty or under 80% confident; `getProcedureKnowledge/entry.ts`'s own returned protocol text updated to match, so the in-context tool result never contradicts the system prompt. Real safety caveat built into the wording, not glossed over: a researched answer is a free-text blob with no `risk_factors`/`red_flag_combinations`/`smoker_warning` to cross-check against a patient's disclosed conditions — the new instructions explicitly tell the agent to label it as researched (not from the curated safety database) and still recommend a specialist confirm it applies to them, never let it quietly stand in for the structured safety check. Nothing about Safe-T4life or the Safety Gate changes.

**A suspected bug checked and ruled out, not silently fixed**: one Explore agent flagged `getProcedureKnowledge/entry.ts`'s "zero active records" branch as a possible `ReferenceError` (referencing the module-level `const m_care_protocol` before its textual declaration further down the file). Read the file directly to confirm: `Deno.serve`'s callback only executes per-request, long after the module's synchronous top-level evaluation (including that `const`) has already completed — no actual TDZ violation. Consistent with the screenshot showing a real, successful `getProcedureKnowledge` call. Not fixed because it isn't broken.

**Deliberately not built this pass, with reasons, not silently dropped**: multi-source corroboration with per-source URLs / a CONFLICTED cross-source state — not feasible on this platform today, since `mcareResearchAndLearn` calls a black-box `add_context_from_internet` grounding flag, not a controllable search API with inspectable individual sources to compare (re-confirms Phase 14's identical finding); a structured 20+ field knowledge record with formal versioning/audit trail — `McareKnowledge`'s real 13-field schema already carries what the pipeline needs, expanding it is a genuine schema migration, not a drop-in; a formal 7-state confidence taxonomy / explicit autonomy classes A–D as enforced code — today's single confidence score + 80% threshold + the existing consent/Safety-Gate architecture already produce the same behavior the taxonomy describes; a dedicated redteam suite for this specific loop — the existing 222-test suite already guards the adjacent real invariants, a knowledge-loop-specific suite is real, separate future work.

**Verification**: lint clean, typecheck unchanged at the same 4-error baseline, `npm run build` exit 0, 604/604 vitest, 222/222 redteam — no new invariant needed, confirmed by running the suite, since no entity/tool_configs permission surface changed. Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: whether the agent actually researches and answers instead of immediately deferring to a specialist on a real unmatched-procedure question after publish — Portia should re-ask about a procedure not in the (still-empty) `ProcedureKnowledge` table and see whether M-Care now researches first.

## M-Care super-agent, Phase 30 — audited the "journey execution engine" ask; nothing left to build (2026-08-10)

Portia pasted a second large spec, this one asking for a formal "journey execution engine" — a named state machine, per-tool-call execution logging, a package generator, payment/consent gates, failure recovery, mid-journey replanning, an observability timeline — so M-Care stops "describing what it could do" and starts really orchestrating. Same audit-first discipline as Phase 29, applied to a much bigger ask: read `base44/shared/bookingState.ts` and `base44/functions/logMcarePerformance/entry.ts` in full, and grep-verified the live `m_care.jsonc` instructions directly rather than trusting memory of prior phases.

**Verdict: there was nothing to build.** Nearly everything in the spec already exists, real, under different names, built incrementally across Phases 4/7/14/15/22 of this project: `bookingState.ts` is a real, incident-driven state machine (`Submitted → ... → Completed`, plus a sticky `Admin-Review` safety hold reachable from any state and a `Cancelled` terminal — exactly the spec's BLOCKED/REQUIRES_HUMAN/CANCELLED); `logMcarePerformance` writes into the same hash-chained `AuditLog` every safety event uses, and — confirmed by grep against the live instructions, not assumed — the agent is already told to call it on every stage advance and key outcome; the exact "never claim a booking/dispatch succeeded without a real result" rule already exists verbatim in the instructions (Phase 15's fix, still live: *"Never tell the traveler their flights are booked, a hotel is reserved, or a driver is dispatched/confirmed until that partner's quote actually comes back"*); "never re-ask what you already know, preserve state across the conversation" is also already verbatim in the instructions. Base44's own agent runtime already gives per-tool-call `pending/running/completed/failed` status natively (surfaced today via `MessageBubble.jsx`'s `StatusIcon`) — building a parallel execution-status system would duplicate the platform, not add capability. The agent structurally cannot call or claim a capability outside its 68 granted tools, which is a stronger "capability registry" guarantee than a manual pre-check would give.

**The one real, non-code gap**: live flight/hotel booking doesn't exist on this platform (re-confirms Phase 4's and Phase 15's identical finding, unchanged) — `assignTravelAgency`/`assignChauffeurServices` send a real quote *request* to a real human partner, never a live search-and-book. No code change closes this; it needs Portia to choose a vendor and get API credentials first.

**A known, already-documented gap, re-flagged not re-fixed**: `guardedStatusUpdate` (the one safe way to write `CaseRecord.status`) is used by only ~4 of the ~72 functions that write that field directly — a real gap this project already found and deliberately deferred twice (most recently in the WhatsApp/Crisis-Reroutes phase), since wiring it into dozens of already-shipped, safety-adjacent functions is genuine separate refactor work, not a side effect of an audit. Still not touched this pass, for the same reason — surfaced again so it isn't silently forgotten.

**No code changes shipped this pass** — manufacturing a change here just to have shipped something would be the opposite of Portia's own explicit instruction in that spec: "Do not create fake implementations merely to make the demo appear complete." Offered her the two real gaps above as their own separately-scoped pieces if she wants either pursued.

## M-Care super-agent, Phase 31 — audited an "autonomous open-web provider discovery" spec; shipped 3 real fixes it surfaced instead (2026-08-10)

Portia pasted a very large, detailed spec asking M-Care to become a full agentic execution engine: given "I need dental implants in Cancun," autonomously decompose the journey, discover real providers via live web/registry/business-directory search, resolve provider identity, run tiered verification, discover pricing, compare/rank, get authorization, contact providers via real business channels, and orchestrate booking. The spec's own sections were explicit and repeated: this is the live production system, no mockups, no fake providers, no simulated tool execution, audit the existing architecture first, don't duplicate or build a parallel agent system, and honestly report capability gaps rather than fake them.

Three parallel audits (M-Care's tool_configs/learning system; partner verification/matching; journey-state/consent/payment/audit/safety) found the platform is **structurally a closed, pre-vetted partner network, not an open-web discovery system** — zero code anywhere searches Google/a directory for an unknown business and cold-contacts it. Every "provider" enters only through gated self-onboarding (`mcareCreate*Pending`) or patient nomination of a doctor they already know (`submitDoctorNomination`, admin-gated, never creates/verifies a `Doctor` record on its own). The "LEARN-AND-RECALL BRAIN" (`mcareResearchAndLearn`) is `InvokeLLM({add_context_from_internet:true})` — a black-box grounding flag, not a structured/queryable search API with inspectable per-claim sources, which is what the spec's "government registry search," "professional registry search," "LinkedIn/company profile search" as separate real data sources would actually require. Building the full spec would mean genuinely new infrastructure (a real search API/connector, expanded per-country registry coverage beyond 3 countries, a new discovery/identity-resolution data model, a governed cold-outreach framework) needing vendor decisions this project has consistently deferred to Portia for (same shape as the still-open live flight/hotel booking gap). Asked her directly (AskUserQuestion) rather than either faking the big ask or silently doing nothing; she chose: ship the real, immediately-buildable fixes the audit surfaced, defer the full vision.

**Real bugs found and fixed, all in code the audit had to read in full anyway:**

- **`runDoctorVerificationScan`'s registry layer was pure LLM guesswork even for the 3 countries this platform has a REAL government registry API/scraper for.** `base44/shared/registryLookup.ts` (US via the real NPI Registry/CMS REST API, CO/RETHUS and MX/SEP via government-portal scrapers) had **zero callers anywhere in the repo** — real, working, completely unwired. Now wired into `runDoctorVerificationScan`'s Layer 2: a real registry hit is tried first and used when supported; the existing LLM-web-search estimate is kept as the fallback for the ~30 other countries this platform sees, but every credential result now carries an honest `source_type` (`registry_api`/`registry_scrape` vs. `llm_web_search_estimate`) so an LLM guess can never be mistaken for a real registry hit downstream.
- **`runDoctorVerificationScan` had a second, unguarded doctor auto-activation path.** On a clean scan it wrote `verification_status:'verified', status:'active'` directly onto the `Doctor` record — bypassing `activateVerifiedDoctor`, which its own header comment calls "THE SINGLE GATED FUNCTION that can set a Doctor to active" and which requires license + identity + **background** to each independently reach a passed state (`background_check_status` is only ever set by a human, per the existing redteam invariant `PARTNERS: nothing automated may mark a background check passed`). This is the same bug class Phase 8 fixed for `runDoctorVerificationScan`/`reviewTravelAgencyVerification`'s *ownership* gap — this time it was the *activation* gate itself being bypassed, on a different function that gap-closing pass didn't touch. Fixed: the scan now only ever records the two sub-checks it can legitimately attest to (`license_verification_status`, `identity_verification_status`) and never writes `status`/top-level `verification_status` — activation now only ever happens through an admin's own click or `initiatePartnerVerification`'s separate HMAC-proof-gated auto-clear path. Since a clean scan can no longer silently activate, the admin-notification email now fires on *every* outcome (previously only on a flagged result), not just to avoid a doctor sitting invisibly in `pending_verification` forever. New redteam invariant added (`PARTNERS: runDoctorVerificationScan never activates a doctor directly`) since the existing background-check invariant only covered `initiatePartnerVerification`/`activatePartner`, not this function.
- **`sendClientBookingProposal` (granted to M-Care) violated the link-only notification doctrine.** Its email/SMS/WhatsApp bodies embedded the client's name and the raw dollar amount directly — exactly what `shared/notify.ts`'s `assertLinkOnly`/`LEAK_PATTERNS` exist to catch, and the same class of gap already fixed elsewhere (`sendMilestoneWhatsAppCheckIns`, `sendWhatsAppCaseUpdate`) but missed here. Fixed: now routes through `linkOnlyEmail`/`linkOnlySms` with a generic message + the same real payment link — the link itself was always the right thing to send (this function's whole point is a genuine "click-and-pay" link, unlike a portal-login notification), only the inline PHI needed to go.

**Deliberately not fixed this pass, flagged instead**: `runRegistryLookup/entry.ts` — a second, redundant implementation of the same registry adapters (locally duplicated rather than importing `shared/registryLookup.ts`, the exact "local copy of a shared helper" anti-pattern this file's Edge Functions section already forbids), on a raw `Deno.serve` with no `createHandler`, and with **no self/admin ownership check** — any authenticated user could call it with an arbitrary `doctor_id` and write `license_verification_status:'passed'` onto someone else's record. Currently harmless (zero callers anywhere), so left alone rather than risk touching dead code under the same session as three live fixes — but it should not be wired up as-is; either delete it in favor of the now-integrated `shared/registryLookup.ts` path, or rebuild it on `createHandler` with the same self/admin gate `runDoctorVerificationScan` already has. Also flagged, not built: the full open-web discovery/identity-resolution/cold-outreach vision remains a real, scoped-but-undone capability gap — needs Portia to make the underlying vendor calls (a real search API, expanded registry coverage) before any of it can be built for real rather than faked.

**Verification**: lint clean, typecheck unchanged at the same pre-existing baseline (`MCareVaultUpload.jsx`/`MCareAgent.jsx`, unrelated — `base44/functions/**` isn't even in tsc's checked scope), `npm run build` exit 0, 607/607 vitest, 223/223 redteam (222 existing + 1 new). No `m_care.jsonc`/`tool_configs` changes — zero Publish-order risk, since all three fixes are inside functions the agent already had access to.

## M-Care super-agent, Phase 32 — a second, more detailed "provider intelligence" spec; a 10-point audit + an honest, real buildable slice (2026-08-10)

Same day as Phase 31, Portia pasted a second, far more detailed version of the same ask — this time specifying the exact data model (provider identity, provenance-per-fact, memory decay/TTL, ranking, governed outreach, negotiation limits, a 9-state onboarding machine, auditability, explicit machine-readable failure states) and demanding a structured 10-point audit *before* any code changes, per the spec's own repeated rule: "DO NOT build a mock... FIRST RETURN: 1. Current architecture... 10. What cannot be implemented until a real external dependency exists." Went through Plan Mode rather than building directly, since the architecture hadn't changed since Phase 31 except for that morning's 3 fixes — the plan updated Phase 31's audit for the post-fix state and answered the 10-point request directly (full text preserved in that session's plan file), then proposed a second, still-narrow slice of buildable work using only infrastructure that already exists — no new vendor decision required. Portia confirmed: ship it.

**Audit conclusion, updated from Phase 31**: still a closed, pre-vetted partner network. No structured/controllable web-search or business-directory API exists anywhere (only the black-box `InvokeLLM({add_context_from_internet:true})` grounding flag); registry coverage still stops at US/CO/MX; no identity-resolution model; no cold-outreach-to-unknowns framework. All of that stays a real, disclosed gap pending a vendor decision only Portia can make — building around it would violate the spec's own "no fake providers, no simulated tool execution" rule.

**What shipped instead — real infrastructure reused, nothing simulated:**

- **`base44/shared/capabilityGap.ts`** (new) — canonical, machine-readable "this isn't real yet" states (`DISCOVERY_UNAVAILABLE`, `VERIFICATION_UNAVAILABLE`, `OUTREACH_UNAVAILABLE`), the literal buildable version of the spec's own Section 13 ("Critical Rule: do not invent capability... surface the dependency clearly"). `providerDiscoveryStatus()` always returns unavailable today, honestly — there's no code path that could return anything else. `verificationCapabilityFor(countryISO)` derives its answer directly from `registryLookup.ts`'s own `REGISTRY_ADAPTERS` table (not a duplicated country list) so it can never drift out of sync with what's actually wired up. `outreachCapabilityFor(isKnownPartner)` is true only for an already-onboarded partner. One new sentence added to `m_care.jsonc`'s Rule 3 (NO INVENTED DATA — the same rule Phase 15 already extended to cover illustrative example answers) telling the agent to say plainly it can't search the open web or cold-contact a business, rather than improvise, when asked — text-only, zero `tool_configs` change, so zero Publish-order risk (confirmed via a JSON parse before/after: still 88 entries).
- **A real governed-outreach audit trail** — new `ContactAttempt` entity (admin-only RLS, matching `NotificationLog`'s precedent) + `base44/shared/logProviderContactAttempt.ts`, built on the exact never-throwing `logCrisisReroute.ts` pattern (logs a real send a caller already made; never sends anything itself, never blocks the real send on a logging failure). This is the honest, buildable version of the spec's Section 8 (outreach auditability) — it logs outreach that's real today, not a new autonomous-contact capability. Wired into the 4 real outbound-to-provider paths that exist: `assignTravelAgency` (email + WhatsApp quote request to a travel agency), `assignChauffeurServices` (email quote request to both origin and destination drivers), `assignDoctorToCase` (email case-assignment notice to the matched doctor), and `initiatePartnerVerification`'s 3 applicant-outcome emails (sanctions-blocked, activated, pending-manual-review). **One correction made mid-build, not glossed over**: the approved plan initially listed `sendClientBookingProposal` as a 4th call site — re-checking the function during implementation confirmed it's actually *client*-facing (sends the payment link to the patient, already fixed for its own separate link-only leak in Phase 31), not provider-facing outreach at all, so it doesn't belong in a provider-contact audit log. Swapped for `assignDoctorToCase`, a genuinely real "Morales contacts a provider partner on a traveler's behalf" path the plan had missed.
- **License-verification staleness** — the concrete, minimal version of the spec's Section 6 (Memory Decay). `runDoctorVerificationScan`'s response now includes `license_freshness` (`confirmed`/`stale`/`unverified`), computed via `freshness.ts`'s existing `isFresh`/`TTL_MS.doctor_license` (7 days — the same generic TTL framework already governing clinic/visa/regulatory data) against `license_last_checked_at` (a field Phase 31's fix started writing but nothing had read since). A scan that actually performed a license check this call reports `confirmed` immediately; a scan called without a license number/country to check (the existing "skipped" branch) falls back to the doctor's prior on-file timestamp instead of silently having no freshness signal — so a months-old check can never be presented as still current just because this particular call didn't happen to re-run it. Backend-only; no admin-UI surface was built to display it this pass.

**PHI safety, checked structurally**: `ContactAttempt`'s schema carries only provider-facing fields (`recipient`/`partner_name` are always the provider's own business contact info) — a new redteam invariant (`PARTNERS: ContactAttempt (provider-outreach audit log) is admin-only and carries no patient PHI field`) asserts all 4 RLS operations are admin-gated and that no patient-identity/clinical field key (`client_name`, `patient_email`, `medical_*`, `diagnosis`, `condition`, etc.) is ever declared on it — checked against declared JSON keys only, not the entity's own free-text description (which legitimately discusses patient PHI in prose while explaining why the schema must never carry it).

**Deliberately not built this pass** (named honestly in the audit, same as Phase 31's deferred list): open-web provider discovery, identity resolution across sources, ranking/comparison of undiscovered providers, autonomous negotiation, self-recruitment of a provider type Morales has never encountered, and any registry coverage beyond US/CO/MX. All blocked on the same real vendor/infrastructure decision Phase 31 already identified — not rushed around with a fake result.

**Verification**: lint clean, typecheck unchanged at the same 4-error pre-existing baseline, `npm run build` exit 0, 620/620 vitest (13 new, `tests/providerIntelligence.test.js`), 224/224 redteam (223 existing + 1 new). No `m_care.jsonc` `tool_configs` change.

## M-Care super-agent, Phase 33 — a third round of the same provider-network ask; explainable trust scores + a real driver score (2026-08-10)

Same day as Phase 31/32, a third version of the request arrived — more elaborate (explainable trust scores, price intelligence with currency normalization, a generalized `ProviderProfile` state machine, legitimate cold-outreach scripts, a "self-improving network loop") but resting on the same premise both prior audits already found unsupported. Rather than re-run a full 3-agent audit a third time to re-derive the same conclusion, this pass verified only the specific new claims: (1) the real trust-score system (`calculateDoctorTrustScore`/`calculateCompanionScore`) computes real numeric components but never turns them into the human-readable "why" the spec's example shows; (2) driver trust scoring was still genuinely unbuilt, exactly as this file already flagged; (3) no currency-conversion utility exists anywhere in the repo (confirmed via a repo-wide search — the spec assumes infrastructure that isn't there); (4) real Twilio Voice integration exists (`escalateSoloCheckIn`), but only for playing a safety message to a patient's phone — not remotely the same thing as a business-to-business outreach call, and not a capability that extends to "contact a clinic and ask if they take international patients." Asked Portia directly (AskUserQuestion) whether to keep shipping narrow real slices, pause for a real vendor conversation, or stop at the audit — she chose to keep building the real, narrow pieces.

**Shipped — both extend a pattern that already worked, zero new vendor:**

- **`base44/shared/trustScoreReasons.ts`** (new) — three small, pure, deterministic functions (`doctorTrustReasons`, `companionTrustReasons`, `driverTrustReasons`) that turn each scorer's already-computed `components` object into plain-language reasons (e.g. "Confirms new cases quickly (avg 2h)", "No safety incidents on record", "4.8/5 average patient rating (12 rated trips)") — the direct, honest answer to the spec's "every score must explain WHY" ask. Nothing invented: a reason is only included when the underlying count is real, and a partner with zero history gets one honest baseline reason, never a fabricated positive claim. Wired into `calculateDoctorTrustScore`/`calculateCompanionScore` as a new `trust_reasons`/`performance_reasons` field (matching each entity's own existing naming convention) alongside their existing `trust_components`/`performance_components` — a rendering upgrade of data that already existed, not a new data source.
- **`base44/functions/calculateDriverTrustScore/entry.ts`** (new) — the `TaxiService` equivalent of `calculateDoctorTrustScore`, closing the gap this file already flagged. Same `cronAuthorized`-gated, `createHandler` shape; scored 0-100 from on-time record (40 pts — cross-references each case's linked `TravelRequest.noshow_flagged_steps` against the specific steps *this driver's own leg* covers: origin = steps 1/9, destination = steps 3/5/7, not just "was any step on the case flagged"), safety record (30 pts, SOS events on the driver's cases), and patient rating (30 pts, the same `RecoverySession.feedback_rating` whole-trip-proxy the doctor/companion scorers already use, same honesty caveat). Correctly queries `CaseRecord.origin_driver_id`/`destination_driver_id` — the pre-existing `getTaxiDriverCases` bug (reads `assigned_driver_id`, a field nothing writes) was not touched, but was not repeated here either. `TaxiService.jsonc` gained the matching `trust_score`/`trust_components`/`trust_reasons`/`trust_case_count`/`trust_last_calculated` fields (Doctor/Companion never declared theirs in schema at all — added here anyway for a clean audit trail, since this is new capability rather than a pre-existing undocumented one). Scheduled in `freshness-cron.yml`'s daily slot, right after the two it mirrors.
- **A pre-existing gap, inherited not created**: `TaxiService.jsonc`'s `update` RLS already lets the owning driver self-update their *entire* record (`data.email === user.email`, no field-level restriction — the same shape `quality_score`/`license_verified`/`verification_status` already had before this pass). The new `trust_score` fields inherit that same systemic limitation, not a new one — Base44's RLS has no field-level granularity, a known, already-accepted characteristic of this app's RLS model repo-wide, not something this pass introduced or is scoped to fix.

**Confirmed via the existing repo-wide auto-discovery sweeps rather than a new dedicated invariant**: `AUTH: no edge function is reachable without SOME guard` and `BUNDLER: no function directory keeps a local copy of a shared helper` both scan every function directory automatically — `calculateDriverTrustScore` passes both without any new test, since it already contains `cronAuthorized` and imports only from `shared/`.

**Deliberately not built this round, same reasons as Phase 31/32**: open-web provider discovery, cold-outreach to a newly-discovered provider, price intelligence/currency normalization, a generalized `ProviderProfile` entity spanning all provider types, and the 8-value verification-status taxonomy — all still blocked on the same real vendor/infrastructure decisions (a controllable search or business-directory API, a currency-exchange API, a compliance-reviewed outreach channel) neither this pass nor the two before it could responsibly build around.

**Verification**: lint clean, typecheck unchanged at the same 4-error pre-existing baseline, `npm run build` exit 0, 630/630 vitest (10 new, `tests/trustScoreReasons.test.js`), 224/224 redteam (no new invariant needed — confirmed via the existing auto-discovery sweeps). No `m_care.jsonc` change — this pass never touched the agent's tools or instructions.

## M-Care super-agent, Phase 34 — two dormant adapters, checked pricing, and why "just wire it" isn't a code problem (2026-08-10)

Portia's direct follow-up to Phase 31-33's repeated "blocked on a vendor decision" answer: "why you can't wire the missing pieces?" A fair question worth a real answer rather than a fourth round of narrow code slices. The honest answer, given in full and confirmed with her: the blocker was never a missing capability on my end, it's that a real search/currency/registry API requires an account tied to Portia's own identity and billing (I cannot sign up for a third-party paid service on her behalf), and even once she has a key, only she can put it into Base44's own env-var dashboard, which this checkout has no session into — the same reason Twilio/Stripe/ComplyAdvantage all needed her or a contractor to actually configure, despite being fully coded already. Real 2026 pricing was checked live (WebSearch, not memory) rather than presenting stale numbers for what would be a real spend decision: Serper ($0.30-1/1K queries, cheapest, 2,500 free) beats Tavily (~$7.50-8/1K, agent-native but pricier) and Exa (~$7/1K, semantic search — weakest fit for local-business lookups specifically) for this use case; OpenCorporates' real pricing (£2,250/yr minimum for just 500 calls/month) isn't justified at current volume, so the honest recommendation was to NOT buy it yet and keep extending `registryLookup.ts`'s country-by-country pattern instead; ExchangeRate-API ($19/mo) beats Open Exchange Rates ($12/mo but locks the free tier to USD-only base, adding rounding-error risk).

Offered Portia a middle path rather than either building nothing or faking something: write the adapter *scaffolding* now, with zero live key, that goes live with no further code the moment she adds one. She confirmed.

**Shipped — two new, completely dormant files, modeled directly on `registryLookup.ts`'s existing `{ supported: boolean, ... }` shape rather than `capabilityGap.ts`'s constants** (those are documented as "no code path exists that could return anything else" — forcing a key-activatable adapter through that module would misrepresent both once a key exists):

- **`base44/shared/providerDiscovery.ts`** — `searchForProviders(query)`. Reads `Deno.env.get('SERPER_API_KEY')`; unset → an honest `{ supported: false, message }` (matching how every unsupported-country registry lookup already behaves), never a fabricated or silently-empty "success." Set → a real POST to Serper's search endpoint, mapping its `organic[]` results to `{title, url, snippet}`.
- **`base44/shared/currencyConvert.ts`** — `convertCurrency(amount, from, to)`. Same honest-unavailable-until-configured shape, reading `EXCHANGE_RATE_API_KEY`. Calls ExchangeRate-API's v6 pair-conversion endpoint when a key exists — its exact response field names are written from documentation, not verified against a live call, and the file says so in its own header comment; re-confirm before ever wiring this into a live function.

**Deliberately, structurally inert**: zero callers anywhere in the app, no `tool_configs` grant, no `m_care.jsonc` mention — identical to how `registryLookup.ts` itself sat completely unwired until Phase 31 connected it. No unit tests: both functions call `Deno.env.get` at runtime, which throws in this repo's vitest environment (no `Deno` global shim) — the same reason `neuralSpeech.js`/`conversationalMode.js`'s impure, platform-coupled logic already goes untested by design elsewhere in this codebase, not a gap unique to this pass.

**Verification**: lint clean, typecheck unchanged at the same 4-error baseline, `npm run build` exit 0, 630/630 vitest (no regression, no new tests per the reasoning above), 224/224 redteam (the two new files are `shared/`, not `functions/`, so the repo-wide function-directory sweeps correctly don't apply — confirmed by running the full suite, not assumed). No `m_care.jsonc` change.

## M-Care super-agent, Phase 35 — a real `?mcare=open` deep link, for judges (2026-08-10)

Portia needed a one-click link for buildathon judges that lands them directly inside a working M-Care chat — no login, no hunting for the orb. Two real constraints ruled out the obvious options: `/m-care` (`src/pages/MCareAgent.jsx`, the full-page agent surface) is registered in `clientRoutes.jsx`, which requires an authenticated session — a judge with no account would hit a login wall, not a chat window. The floating orb (`MCareOrb.jsx`) IS already public on every non-quiet page, but only ever opens on a manual click — there was no existing URL mechanism to open it automatically on load.

**Shipped**: a new `?mcare=open` query-param deep link on `MCareOrb.jsx`. A new effect (right after the existing `isQuietRoute` check, reusing that same list so a demo/checkout/emergency page still correctly suppresses the orb regardless of the param) reads `location.search` once per page load via a ref guard (`autoOpenedRef`) and, if `mcare=open` is present, calls the exact same `setOpen(true); setDismissed(true); setStruggleHint(null)` sequence the manual orb-button click already uses — so a deep-link open is indistinguishable from a real click, not a separate code path. Fires at most once per load, so navigating elsewhere in the app afterward never force-reopens the panel. `useLocation()`'s destructure widened from `{ pathname }` to `{ pathname, search }` — the only other change.

**The real link for judges**: `https://<the live domain>/?mcare=open` (homepage — any public, non-quiet page works identically). Confirmed with Portia which domain is actually live before handing this out, since a prior session found `moralesdentalandaesthetics.com` had no DNS at one point (see the Dead Domain Bug entry in memory).

**Verification**: lint clean, typecheck unchanged at the same 4-error baseline (`MCareOrb.jsx` carries its own pre-existing `@ts-nocheck`), `npm run build` exit 0, 630/630 vitest (no regression — no test file exists for `MCareOrb.jsx`, consistent with its whole history per Phase 27's note), 224/224 redteam. No `m_care.jsonc` change — purely a client-side presentation change, the agent itself is unaffected.

## M-Care super-agent, Phase 36 — real Tavily web-discovery, wired through the existing candidate → verification → confidence → memory discipline (2026-08-11)

Portia named a specific vendor this time (`TAVILY_API_KEY`, correcting her own earlier Brave recommendation after checking Brave's current pricing — free tier now needs a card and bills uncapped) and gave a precise spec: Tavily as a structured discovery tool, never treat a result as verified, preserve `mcareResearchAndLearn` as-is with the existing Base44 grounding kept as a fallback, audit every external search, and — explicitly — audit the exact file/function list before writing any code. Two Explore agents did that audit first (verification/identity-resolution architecture; audit-logging patterns) and found real, load-bearing facts that reshaped the build: `providerDiscovery.ts` already existed as a dormant Serper adapter from an earlier pass this same day and needed a full rewrite, not a new file; `capabilityGap.ts`'s `providerDiscoveryStatus()` was a static stub that always said unavailable regardless of any key; no general multi-field identity-resolution scorer existed anywhere (`procedureKnowledgeMatch.ts`/`fuzzyMatch.js` are both single-string-field matchers); `runDoctorVerificationScan` and `initiatePartnerVerification` each use exactly one of registry-lookup vs. research-brain, never both, so a new discovery step is new orchestration, not an insertion into either; and neither existing audit-log pattern (`AuditLog`'s fixed allowlist, `ContactAttempt`'s outreach-specific shape) fit "log every search call."

**The design has two paths, not one**, since forcing every kind of discovery through one pipeline would either cram provider-identity data into a Q&A cache or drag simple fact lookups through a verification pipeline they don't need:

- **Path A — general knowledge** (procedure info, destination intel, anything `mcareResearchAndLearn` gets asked): Tavily is tried first, its real, inspectable results (title/url/content) embedded directly in the research prompt with `add_context_from_internet` turned off; when Tavily is unavailable or returns nothing, the function falls straight through to the exact original `InvokeLLM({add_context_from_internet:true})` call, byte-identical to before — the literal "preserve the fallback" requirement. Request/response contract, recall logic, and the `McareKnowledge` ≥80-confidence persistence are all unchanged; a new `used_tavily` boolean was added to the response, additive only.
- **Path B — provider discovery** (the demo scenario: "a dental implant specialist in Cancun"): a new function, `discoverProviderCandidates`, runs Search result → candidate → verification → confidence → memory exactly as specified. It checks real `Doctor` records first (specialty + location match, so a web search never happens for someone Morales already works with), then calls Tavily, then filters out any result that's really just an existing partner (new `providerCandidateMatch.ts` — reuses `procedureKnowledgeMatch.ts`'s `tokenize()`, the same Deno-runtime primitive, rather than reaching across the runtime boundary to the frontend-only `fuzzyMatch.js`), then persists genuinely new leads to a new staging entity, `DiscoveredProviderCandidate`. **Verification is honestly deferred, not faked**: a raw search snippet essentially never contains a license number, so no live `registryLookup.ts` call happens at discovery time — each candidate is only tagged with whether a registry check would even be *possible* for its country, real verification stays a human reviewer's job once they have a license number in hand. **Confidence is structurally capped**: `computeCandidateConfidence` caps search-relevance-alone at 40/100 — nowhere near anything that could read as "verified" — and only a real registry confirmation (not built this pass) can push it higher. A candidate is never, anywhere in this code, promoted into a real `Doctor`/`TravelAgency`/etc. record — that stays exactly the existing human-gated `mcareCreate*Pending` → `initiatePartnerVerification` → admin-approval pipeline this project has protected since Phase 8/13.
