**New files**: `base44/entities/DiscoveredProviderCandidate.jsonc` (admin-only RLS, matches `ContactAttempt`'s precedent; its `status`/`verification_status`-equivalent enums deliberately contain no `verified`/`promoted`/`active` value at all — not even a state to accidentally write) and `base44/entities/ExternalSearchLog.jsonc` (admin-only, logs every search call — query, vendor, status, result count — success or not) + `base44/shared/logExternalSearch.ts` (never-throwing, same try/catch discipline as `logProviderContactAttempt.ts`) + `base44/shared/providerCandidateMatch.ts` (the new scorer) + `base44/functions/discoverProviderCandidates/entry.ts` (the Path B tool, on `createHandler` from day one, `requireAuth: false` matching the rest of M-Care's anonymous-visitor reach).

**Modified**: `providerDiscovery.ts` rewritten for Tavily (`TAVILY_API_KEY`, `https://api.tavily.com/search`, mapping `results[].title/url/content/score` into the existing `{title,url,snippet,source,relevance,timestamp}` shape — all six fields the spec asked for; response shape is written from documentation, not verified against a live call, same disclosed caveat as `currencyConvert.ts`); `capabilityGap.ts`'s `providerDiscoveryStatus()` now genuinely checks for the key instead of always returning unavailable; `mcareResearchAndLearn/entry.ts` gained the Path A precursor described above, additively; `m_care.jsonc` gained one new `tool_configs` grant (`discoverProviderCandidates`, tool count 88→89, JSON validity and count checked before and after via the established `python3 -c "json.load(...)"` method) and a rewritten Rule 3 passage — it used to flatly say M-Care "cannot search the open web for a provider"; it now says M-Care can, via the new tool, but a result is a lead, never a verified provider, and outreach to a newly-discovered candidate is still not possible (that capability gap is real and unchanged — only a Morales-onboarded partner can be contacted).

**Structural guardrails, not just prompt wording**: two new redteam invariants — `DiscoveredProviderCandidate`/`ExternalSearchLog` are admin-only with no patient-PHI-shaped field (mirrors the existing `ContactAttempt` check), and `discoverProviderCandidates` is asserted to never call `Doctor.create`/`TravelAgency.create`/any partner-entity create and never write a `verified` status — "never treat a Tavily result as verified" is enforced on the source, not just described in an instruction.

**Deliberately not built this pass**: a live registry check at discovery time (needs a real license number a search result won't reliably contain — stays a human's job); extending discovery beyond doctors to the other 4 partner types (the known-provider check is Doctor-only this pass, scoped to match the demo scenario); any UI change (Base44's own per-tool-call status display already gives the "M-Care is searching..." moment for free — `tool_configs` entries in this file have no `display_projection`/`active_label` field to customize, confirmed by inspecting an existing entry directly rather than assumed from an earlier, different session's note about a frontend-side field of the same name).

**Publish-order risk, flagged for Portia directly**: `discoverProviderCandidates` must be deployed/Published in Base44 before or atomically with this `tool_configs` change, and pinged directly (expect 400/401, never 404) before trusting the agent to call it — the exact failure mode that made M-Care go completely silent once already (Phase 10).

**Verification**: lint clean, typecheck unchanged at the pre-existing baseline (base44/functions and base44/shared aren't in tsc's checked scope), `npm run build` exit 0, 642/642 vitest (23 new — 13 in `providerCandidateMatch.test.js`, plus 2 rewritten in `providerIntelligence.test.js` to stub the minimal `Deno.env` surface `providerDiscoveryStatus` now touches, since a full Deno shim doesn't exist in this repo's vitest environment but a two-line env stub was worth the coverage), 226/226 redteam (2 new).

**Confirmed live 2026-08-11**: Portia added a real `TAVILY_API_KEY` and, walking her through a manual `curl`/`Invoke-RestMethod` test against Tavily's own endpoint directly, a real bug surfaced before it could bite the actual integration: this file's adapter sent the key as an `api_key` JSON-body field, but Tavily's real API authenticates via an `Authorization: Bearer <key>` header — confirmed against two independent current sources, fixed same-day (commit `69dc99cb`), and then Portia's own follow-up test against the real endpoint returned real dentist search results, proving both the fix and the response field names (`title`/`url`/`content`/`score`) correct. `discoverProviderCandidates` itself still needs Publishing in Base44 before it's live end-to-end in the agent (see the Publish-order risk note above) — Tavily's own side of the integration is the piece now confirmed working.

## M-Care super-agent, Phase 37 — visible trust tiers, so a search score can never read as verification (2026-08-11)

Immediately after confirming Tavily works live, Portia flagged a real risk: a Tavily relevance score (e.g. `0.87981987`) must never visually read as "Verified ✅" in the buildathon demo — it only means a search result looked relevant, nothing about licensing or legitimacy. She asked for three visibly distinct tiers on any provider M-Care names: 🔎 DISCOVERED, 🛡️ VERIFIED, ✓ APPROVED. An audit found this was a presentation gap, not a "fake it" problem — `Doctor.jsonc` already has real, separate fields for exactly these tiers (`license_verification_status`/`identity_verification_status` — written by `runDoctorVerificationScan`, independent of `background_check_status`, which only a human ever sets — and `status`/`verification_status`, only ever promoted to `active`/`verified` by `activateVerifiedDoctor`'s all-three-checks-passed gate). But `matchDoctorsForProcedure` — the tool M-Care already calls to recommend a known doctor — discards this data: its own pre-filter (`base44/functions/matchDoctorsForProcedure/entry.ts:20-30`) already guarantees every doctor it can return is fully approved, yet the returned object never included the fields that would let M-Care say so, and never once labeled a recommendation as approved. Worse, its own `m_care.jsonc` tool description already claimed it returns "verification status and license expiry" — it didn't return either, a real pre-existing overclaim found and fixed in this same pass.

Asked Portia directly before building: 🛡️ VERIFIED is real in the data (license + identity passed, background check still pending) but no traveler-facing tool ever surfaces a doctor in that in-between state — `matchDoctorsForProcedure` only ever returns fully-approved doctors, by design, since recommending someone mid-pipeline to a traveler would be wrong regardless of this feature. Her call: build DISCOVERED + APPROVED live now, since both fire for real in an actual conversation, and build VERIFIED as a real, correctly-defined tier for the badge component/legend without forcing a live path to it — the alternative (a live registry check attempted during web discovery whenever a license number happens to be extractable from a search snippet) would rarely fire, since a raw snippet essentially never contains one, and a badge that almost never appears is its own kind of misleading.

**Shipped**: `src/components/mcare-agent/ProviderStatusBadge.jsx` (new) — a small pill component for all three tiers, each with a one-line honest definition on hover/tap. Reuses this app's existing chat-rendering mechanism rather than inventing a new one: `MessageBubble.jsx` already extracts special `{{token:...}}` patterns from M-Care's own replies for rich rendering (`{{maps:...}}`, `{{qr:...}}`, `{{choices:...}}`) — a new `extractProviderStatus()` parses `{{providerstatus:TIER|Name}}` the identical way, chained into the same extraction sequence. `matchDoctorsForProcedure/entry.ts` now includes each doctor's real `status`/`license_verified`/`verification_status` in its response — data it already computed for its own filter and previously threw away. `m_care.jsonc` gained a new Rule 19 (PROVIDER TRUST TIERS, text-only, no `tool_configs` change, zero Publish-order risk): M-Care must emit exactly one `{{providerstatus:...}}` token whenever it names a specific provider — `discovered` for a `discoverProviderCandidates` result, `approved` for a `matchDoctorsForProcedure` result, and an explicit, direct instruction never to emit `verified` since no tool gives it a real signal to justify that yet. Both tool descriptions were updated to reference the correct token.

**A real self-inflicted bug caught before it shipped, not glossed over**: the first attempt at Rule 19 broke `m_care.jsonc`'s JSON validity — a multi-line edit accidentally inserted two literal raw newline bytes into the middle of the giant single-string `instructions` field instead of the escaped `\n\n` two-character sequence this file requires throughout, the same class of mistake this file's own established `python3 -c "json.load(...)"` verification step exists to catch. Caught immediately by that exact check before anything was committed, fixed with a precise, targeted byte-level replacement, and re-verified valid.

**Two new redteam invariants, not just prompt wording**: one structurally confirms `matchDoctorsForProcedure`'s approval filter still runs before the response is built and that the returned fields read from the real `doc` object rather than a hardcoded literal; the other confirms every mention of the `verified` provider-status token anywhere in `m_care.jsonc` sits inside an explicit prohibition, never a positive instruction to emit it — both failed on first write (an overly broad forbidden-literal check flagged the response filter's own legitimate `status: 'active'` query criterion; the prohibition-detection regex matched only one of the file's two differently-phrased "never emit this" sentences and needed broadening) and were corrected before the suite went green.

**Deliberately not built this pass, per Portia's own choice**: no live path to a 🛡️ VERIFIED badge — real in the data model and component, not reachable in a live traveler conversation today.

**Verification**: lint clean, typecheck unchanged at the pre-existing baseline, `npm run build` exit 0, 642/642 vitest (no regression — no new test file, `MessageBubble.jsx`/`matchDoctorsForProcedure` aren't unit-tested, consistent with this repo's established precedent for this class of file), 228/228 redteam (2 new).

**Real bug found live, same day, fixed**: Portia published all three pieces (Base44's own AI assistant re-saved the two functions plus the agent config to trigger deploy) and tested M-Care live — `discoverProviderCandidates` genuinely fired and returned real Mexico City veneer clinics, but the chat showed raw `{{providerstatus:discovered|Dentology Polanco}}` token text leaking straight into the reply instead of rendering as a badge. Root cause: `extractProviderStatus` was written to find only the FIRST `{{providerstatus:...}}` match per message, the same one-token assumption `{{maps:...}}`/`{{qr:...}}` correctly make — but unlike those two, a single M-Care reply routinely names several providers at once (a list of web-discovered candidates), so every token after the first was never stripped or rendered. Fixed: `extractProviderStatus` now finds every match via `matchAll`, strips all of them, and returns an array; the render side maps over it into a wrapped row of badges (same flex-wrap pattern the existing choices/maps chips already use) instead of a single badge. Re-verified: lint clean, 642/642 vitest, 228/228 redteam, build exit 0.

**Also observed, not a code bug**: M-Care didn't tag every candidate it listed — 2 of the 4 got a real `{{providerstatus:...}}` token, the other 2 (including the one it later referenced in a suggested-reply chip) didn't. That's LLM instruction-following consistency, not something the token-extraction fix addresses — worth watching in further live tests, and worth strengthening Rule 19's wording if it keeps happening, but not chased further this pass since the confirmed, reproducible bug (raw token leakage) is the one that actually broke the visible output.

## M-Care super-agent, Phase 38 — a real, live path to 🛡️ VERIFIED (2026-08-11)

Portia's next test plan: find a doctor, have M-Care verify them, save the result. Phase 37 deliberately left 🛡️ VERIFIED unreachable live — `discoverProviderCandidates` alone can never verify anything, since a raw Tavily snippet essentially never contains a license number, the one thing `registryLookup.ts`'s real US/CO/MX checks actually need. Worth flagging directly: Mexico is one of the three countries that adapter genuinely covers, so "find a doctor in Mexico, verify them" is a real, honestly-achievable target, not a stretch — asked Portia whether to keep tomorrow's test scoped to discover+save only or wire in a real live check; she chose the real check.

**Shipped**: `base44/functions/verifyDiscoveredCandidate/entry.ts` (new) — takes a `candidate_id` (from a row `discoverProviderCandidates` already saved) plus a human-supplied `license_number`, and runs the exact same `registryLookup.ts` `runLookup()` that `runDoctorVerificationScan` already uses for real doctors — no new verification logic, the existing real adapter. Updates the candidate's `registry_check_status` to `'checked'` and stores the raw `registry_result` (new fields on `DiscoveredProviderCandidate`, alongside `license_number_checked`/`registry_checked_at`), and recomputes `identity_confidence` via the existing `computeCandidateConfidence(relevanceScore, registryConfidence)` — now finally with a real registry confidence to combine, not just search relevance capped at 40. Returns `eligible_for_verified_badge` (`found && !route_to_manual`, the same "confident, real match" bar `registryLookup.ts` itself already enforces) — the one explicit signal M-Care's instructions are allowed to key `{{providerstatus:verified|Name}}` off of.

**The safety property that made this safe to build fast**: a passed check is real evidence for a human reviewer, never a self-sufficient approval. `verifyDiscoveredCandidate` never creates or updates a real `Doctor`/`TravelAgency`/etc. record, and never writes the candidate's own `status` field at all — it stays `'candidate'`, exactly as before. Promotion into a real Morales partner is still only the existing `mcareCreate*Pending` → `initiatePartnerVerification` → admin-approval pipeline, completely untouched by this function. VERIFIED and APPROVED stay honestly distinct: a candidate can now be genuinely license-checked without ever becoming a Morales-approved partner.

**`m_care.jsonc`** (text + one new `tool_configs` grant, 89→90, JSON validity and count re-checked before/after via the established `python3 -c "json.load(...)"` method): Rule 19 (PROVIDER TRUST TIERS) rewritten so `verified` is no longer an unconditional prohibition — it's now conditioned explicitly on `eligible_for_verified_badge:true`, with identity_confidence alone spelled out as never sufficient. `discoverProviderCandidates`'s own description updated to match (it previously said "never verified for a candidate," now correctly says "never unless verifyDiscoveredCandidate has actually run and returned eligible_for_verified_badge:true").

**Two redteam invariants rewritten/added**: the old "every mention of verified must be inside a prohibition" check no longer applies now that a real conditional path exists — replaced with a check that the emit decision is explicitly tied to `eligible_for_verified_badge:true` and that confidence-alone is explicitly ruled out in the instructions text. A new invariant on `verifyDiscoveredCandidate` itself: never creates/updates any real partner entity, never writes the candidate's `status` field to anything, and `eligible_for_verified_badge` is structurally required to be `found && !routeToManual` — not a looser truthy check that could pass on relevance alone.

**Deliberately out of scope this pass**: no audit-log entity for registry-check calls (the updated candidate row itself is already a durable, admin-visible record of what happened — same reasoning as skipping a separate log for a self-recording action elsewhere in this session); no UI for supplying a license number outside the chat itself (the traveler/reviewer just tells M-Care the number in conversation); extending this to non-doctor partner types or countries beyond US/CO/MX (registryLookup.ts's own real coverage, unchanged).

**Verification**: lint clean, typecheck unchanged at the pre-existing baseline, `npm run build` exit 0, 642/642 vitest (no regression, no new pure-logic module needing its own test file), 229/229 redteam (2 changed/new, both passed on first write). Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: whether a real Mexico SEP registry call actually returns a usable `found`/`confidence` result for a real license number tomorrow — Portia should test the full discover → supply a license number → verify chain end to end before trusting `eligible_for_verified_badge` at demo time.

## M-Care super-agent, Phase 39 — explicit next-step offers on every SAFE-T safety stop (2026-08-11)

Mentor/reviewer feedback on three areas: more granular age/procedure/country risk thresholds, deeper verification (license lookup, clinic accreditation, patient-review aggregation), and clearer handoff — offering something concrete ("Would you like me to prepare a clinical review request packet?") instead of just stating a safety outcome and going quiet. Assessed honestly rather than building all three: granular clinical/legal thresholds need a real clinical/legal source, not an invented one (same reason the `ProcedureKnowledge` risk table is still sitting empty, waiting on Portia's own judgment); deeper verification is already partly shipped (real license lookup, Phases 31-38) with the rest (clinic accreditation, review aggregation) blocked on a real data-source decision, same wall as the still-open search/currency vendor calls. Handoff clarity was the one buildable now — Portia confirmed: ship it.

Audited before writing anything, per this file's own standing discipline against invented capability (Rule 3): Rule 1 (SAFETY GATE) already defines all four SAFE-T outcome tiers, but only the CRITICAL/BLOCKED hard-stop message and the HIGH/WAIVER_REQUIRED waiver routing existed — nothing proactively offered a next action for CRITICAL/BLOCKED beyond "wait for a callback," and MEDIUM/review's "you may continue to finding providers" was an internal permission, never a spoken offer. `flagIntakeHandoff` (Rule 14, already granted) turned out to be the literal, already-built version of "a clinical review request packet" — it writes a high-priority `DoctorReviewTask` and emails the care team the full transcript so nothing has to be repeated. `matchDoctorsForProcedure` was checked too: it matches by procedure + AI synonym expansion only, with no condition-specific filter — so the MEDIUM/review offer is worded honestly as "keep searching for matched specialists," not the mentor's literal "surgeons experienced with this condition" phrasing, which isn't a real filter this tool supports.

**Shipped**: one sentence added to Rule 1, right after the four tiers are defined — whenever the outcome is anything other than LOW/PASSED, M-Care now offers one concrete next step in the same breath instead of just stating the outcome: for CRITICAL/BLOCKED or HIGH/WAIVER_REQUIRED, offer to send everything already discussed straight to the clinical review team right now via `flagIntakeHandoff`; for MEDIUM/review, offer to keep searching via `matchDoctorsForProcedure` while the care team reviews in parallel. Explicit closing clause, matching Rules 13/14/19's own pattern: never phrase any of this as something that could resolve, speed up, or bypass the safety decision itself — it only ever makes the human handoff faster and better-prepared, never softens the RED/SAFE-T block. Text-only edit — no `tool_configs` change (both tools already granted), zero Publish-order risk, 1-line git diff on the instructions field.

**Deliberately not built this pass, per the same assessment given to Portia before she picked this one**: granular age/procedure/country risk thresholds (needs a real clinical/legal source); clinic accreditation and patient-review aggregation (needs a real data vendor, same class of gap as Tavily/ExchangeRate-API before those were wired).

**Verification**: `python3 -c "json.load(...)"` before/after (valid, `tool_configs` count unchanged at 90), lint clean, typecheck unchanged at the pre-existing 4-error baseline, 642/642 vitest (no regression), 229/229 redteam (unchanged — no tool grant or write-permission surface touched).

## Homepage hero — Living M Orb (2026-08-12)

Portia sent a detailed "Living M Orb" spec (soft breathing glow, eyes that idle-drift and track the cursor, a wake-up moment, glancing toward a nearby card) to make the brand mark feel like a present agent rather than a static logo. Audited before building: the hero (`src/components/home/LuxuryHero.jsx`) had no persistent, prominent "M" element at all — the only mark present was an 18px icon that flickers in/out inside the mock chat bubbles (`HeroChatBubbles`, lines ~105-111), each on-screen for only ~1.6s, too fleeting for eyes to read. Confirmed with Portia via AskUserQuestion which placement she wanted (a new standalone orb vs. upgrading the flickering chat-bubble icon) rather than guessing on a brand-critical, first-thing-every-visitor-sees surface — she chose a new standalone orb near the headline.

**Shipped**: `src/components/home/LivingMOrb.jsx` (new) — a distinct component from M-Care's own `src/components/mcare/LivingOrb.jsx` on purpose (that one reflects real chat state — listening/thinking/speaking — via `/mcare-logo.png`; this one is the general brand mark, `/morales-m-mark.png`, with no chat state to reflect; two different contexts, not worth forcing into one shared abstraction yet). Reuses `LivingOrb.jsx`'s breathing-ring/glass-core visual language for brand consistency, with an embossed, semi-transparent M watermark and two small eye-dots layered on top. A single `requestAnimationFrame` loop owns eye position at all times — idle drift is a slow sine wave biased slightly rightward (an honest, cheap stand-in for "glancing toward" the live journey card that sits to its right, without needing per-element position math), and cursor-tracking smoothly takes over within a generous radius when the mouse has moved recently, handing back to idle drift ~2.6s after the mouse stops — one authority for eye position, so the two behaviors blend instead of fighting over the DOM. Wakes up ~300ms after mount (fade + scale in). Respects `prefers-reduced-motion` — same `matchMedia` pattern as `LivingOrb.jsx`/`JourneyStageTracker.jsx`/`SafetyGateCard.jsx` — falling back to a fully static mark with fixed eyes. Wired into `LuxuryHero.jsx` directly above `HeroChatBubbles`, 64px.

**Deliberately not built, not silently dropped**: a click-engagement pulse when "Start Your Journey" is clicked — that CTA is a React Router `Link` that navigates immediately, so a ~1s pulse would never actually be visible without adding an artificial navigation delay, which wasn't asked for. A status dot (not in Portia's own "quick win" fallback list). Mobile touch-follow — not needed as a separate build, since no `mousemove` ever fires on a touch device, so mobile automatically gets exactly the idle-drift-only behavior Portia's own spec asked for there.

**Verification**: lint clean, typecheck unchanged at the pre-existing 4-error baseline, `npm run build` exit 0, 642/642 vitest (no regression — this component has no pure extractable logic worth a unit test, consistent with this repo's precedent for DOM/rAF-coupled presentational components like `neuralSpeech.js`/`conversationalMode.js`'s impure pieces), 229/229 redteam (unchanged — frontend-only, no tool grant or write-permission surface touched). Live/visual confirmation (does the eye-tracking actually feel calm and "alive" rather than gimmicky, does the rightward idle bias read as a real glance) can't happen from this checkout — same standing limitation as every prior visual feature in this file; Portia verifies live.

**Follow-up, same day — M-Care's own orb brought into visual parity.** Portia sent two screenshots side by side (the new hero orb vs. `LivingOrb.jsx`, M-Care's chat icon) and asked for them to match. The mismatch was real: `LivingOrb.jsx` was still rendering `/mcare-logo.png` (a gold M inside a blue globe/network graphic, from Phase 6) — visually a completely different mark from the hero's `/morales-m-mark.png` glyph, confirmed by viewing both image assets directly rather than guessing from the screenshots alone. `src/lib/useLivingEyes.js` (new) extracts the eye-tracking `requestAnimationFrame` loop out of `LivingMOrb.jsx` into a shared hook (idle sine-drift + cursor-tracking + the smooth hand-off between them) so both "living M" surfaces move identically without duplicating the logic — `LivingMOrb.jsx` was refactored to consume it. `LivingOrb.jsx` now renders the same `/morales-m-mark.png` glyph at reduced opacity (an embossed watermark, matching the hero orb) plus the same two eye-dots, wired to the same hook — in both its reduced-motion fallback and its main animated render. Deliberately untouched: all four `STATE_CONFIG` ring/glow behaviors (idle/listening/thinking/speaking) and the `flashToken` barge-in pulse — those are real, wired to actual voice/tool state, not part of what looked wrong; only the inner mark and the missing eyes needed to change. Two call sites (`MCareOrb.jsx:1148` at 44px, `MCareOrb.jsx:1187` at 36px) needed no changes — same `state`/`size`/`flashToken` prop contract.

**Verification**: lint clean, typecheck unchanged at the pre-existing 4-error baseline, `npm run build` exit 0, 642/642 vitest, 229/229 redteam — unchanged, no entity/tool/permission surface touched, no new pure logic worth a unit test (same reasoning as the hook's own DOM/rAF coupling). Live confirmation that both orbs now read as visually identical, and that eye-tracking still looks right at the smaller 36px header size, can't happen from this checkout — Portia verifies live.

**Correction, same day — the eyes came out, and the real culprit turned out to be a third component.** Portia tested the eyes live and flagged them hard: they read as a face ("something is watching me") rather than an AI presence, with a detailed spec explicitly banning eyes/pupils/a face/humanoid features anywhere in M-Care's avatar. Her attached "current problem" screenshot (a pale circle with two dark marks) didn't actually match `LivingOrb.jsx`'s dark-background/gold-eyes design though — a real audit turned up the actual source: `src/components/mcare-agent/McareAvatar.jsx`, a completely separate, previously-untouched component using a real photo of a person in a Morales vest, circle-cropped (a `base44-builder[bot]` "mascot-style" addition, predating this session). It's also the far more *frequently seen* avatar — used on every single assistant chat message (`MessageBubble.jsx`), the panel header when not in Conversational Mode, and every typing/loading indicator in both `MCareOrb.jsx` and `MCareAgent.jsx` — while `LivingOrb.jsx` only ever appears in the floating closed button and the header during an active voice call. A cropped photo at 28-36px reading as an unsettling face is a well-known effect, and matches her description far better than gold eye-dots on a dark orb would.

**Fixed**: removed the eye-dots from both `LivingOrb.jsx` and `LivingMOrb.jsx` (homepage hero) — back to just the embossed `/morales-m-mark.png` glyph, no eyes, applied to both for consistency rather than leaving one orb with eyes and one without (the same consistency Portia asked for two messages earlier, now honored the other direction). `src/lib/useLivingEyes.js` deleted — both its callers were just removed, zero remaining references, no orphaned code left behind. `LivingOrb.jsx` gained a real 5th state, `error` — a deliberately muted ring/glow (fewer rings, lower opacity, dimmer core glow, same shape, never a different icon) — wired in `MCareOrb.jsx`'s `orbState` derivation to the app's own already-real `isOnline` (`navigator.onLine`) signal, not a fabricated status. `McareAvatar.jsx` rewritten as a thin wrapper around `LivingOrb` (`glow` prop maps to `state='thinking'`, matching its existing "typing/loading" usage) instead of the vest photo — every one of its ~6 call sites across `MessageBubble.jsx`/`MCareOrb.jsx`/`MCareAgent.jsx` needed zero changes, same `size`/`glow`/`className` prop contract. Net effect: there is now exactly one visual definition of "M-Care's presence" in this app (`LivingOrb.jsx`), reused everywhere instead of three different marks (the blue-globe `mcare-logo.png`, the vest photo, and the hero's own gold M) doing the same job inconsistently.

**Deliberately not touched, flagged not silently skipped**: `src/components/mcare/SpecialistCard.jsx:15` still renders the old blue-globe `/mcare-logo.png` at 28px — a doctor-recommendation card icon, not a chat avatar, and not what Portia's "creepy face" complaint was about; worth a follow-up if full mark consistency across the whole app is ever wanted. The now-unused `.mcare-glow` CSS keyframe/class in `src/index.css` (its only caller was the old `McareAvatar.jsx`) was left in place — dead CSS, not a functional risk, not worth the extra diff surface this pass.

**Verification**: lint clean, typecheck unchanged at the pre-existing 4-error baseline, `npm run build` exit 0, 642/642 vitest (no regression), 229/229 redteam (unchanged — no entity/tool/permission surface touched). Live confirmation that the fix actually lands on the surface Portia saw the problem on, and that the new `error` state reads as calm/restrained rather than alarming, can't happen from this checkout — Portia verifies live.

**Reversal, same day — the eyes are back, the M mark is gone.** Looking at the live, emblem-only orb next to her original reference image, Portia explicitly asked for the opposite: a friendly, playful face (eyes that blink and occasionally wink), no M glyph at all — "talking, breathing, winking, super friendly AI agentic agent... playful at sometimes." Deliberately not cursor-tracking again — that's the exact mechanic from earlier the same day that read as "something is watching me."

All of `LivingOrb.jsx`'s five `STATE_CONFIG` ring/glow behaviors (idle/listening/thinking/speaking/error) are untouched — only the inner content changed, for the second time today, without touching what was already real and working. `McareAvatar.jsx` needed no changes — it already just wraps `LivingOrb`.

## M-Care super-agent, Phase 41 — intake polish: name prominence + a concise-greeting nudge (2026-08-12)

Portia sent a low-pressure "small improvements to consider" list from a live intake screenshot: tighten the opening message, shorten option-chip labels for mobile, make the "M-Care"/"M-Safe" name more prominent, add idle animation. Checked each before touching anything, since two weren't what they looked like:

- **The opening greeting and its choice-chip labels are not fixed strings.** The screenshot's actual text ("Welcome to Morales, Theon. I'm M-Care, your journey coordinator...") doesn't match either hardcoded `GREETING` constant in the codebase (`MCareOrb.jsx`/`MCareAgent.jsx` — both pre-conversation placeholders only) — confirmed by grepping the exact screenshot phrasing against `m_care.jsonc`'s instructions and finding zero matches. Both are genuinely generated live by the agent per conversation, per Rule 11 (OFFER CHOICES) — no prescribed exact wording. So "tighten it" means nudging the agent's own instructions toward brevity, not editing a string, and the exact wording will keep varying conversation to conversation, just trending shorter.
- **Idle animation is already real** — `LivingOrb.jsx`'s `idle` state already has a continuous breathing pulse plus the blink/wink eyes from the last two phases. Confirmed working, nothing to build.

**Shipped**: one small, ASCII-only addition to Rule 11 in `m_care.jsonc` — keep each choice label to 2-4 words, and keep the opening greeting in a new conversation to 2 short sentences before any choices token. Hit a real, repeat-of-a-known-mistake snag while editing: the anchor text matched fine against the *parsed* `instructions` string (newlines as real `\n` bytes) but not against the *raw* file text before `json.loads` — this specific region of the file stores newlines as the literal two-character sequence backslash-n, and emoji/em-dashes as literal `\uXXXX` escape sequences (inconsistent with other regions of the same file edited in earlier phases, which use raw UTF-8 bytes) — confirmed via a byte-level probe before writing anything. Fixed by anchoring on the literal two-char sequence and writing the new sentence in plain ASCII (hyphens, not em-dashes) to sidestep the encoding ambiguity entirely rather than risk a third bad write to this file. 1-line git diff, `tool_configs` count unchanged at 90.

**Also shipped**: a small, real name-prominence bump — `MCareOrb.jsx`'s panel header ("M-Safe") from `fontSize:15` to `17` with tighter letter-spacing; `MCareAgent.jsx`'s header (`<h1>M-Care</h1>`) from `text-base` to `text-lg` with `tracking-tight`, matching. Both were already bold/semibold — the size was the only real lever.

**Verification**: lint clean, typecheck unchanged at the pre-existing 4-error baseline, `npm run build` exit 0, 642/642 vitest, 229/229 redteam — unchanged, no tool grant or write-permission surface touched. Whether the greeting/labels actually read shorter live (an instruction nudge, not a guarantee), and whether the header bump reads as more prominent without crowding the layout, can't be confirmed from this checkout — Portia verifies live.

## M-Care super-agent, Phase 42 — 10x reasoning transparency, narrating the real safety numbers only (2026-08-12)

Portia pasted a detailed proposed "Core Reasoning Protocol" (GATHER→RETRIEVE→ANALYZE→DECIDE→EXPLAIN) plus a set of "Hard Safety Rules" with specific numeric thresholds (cardiovascular event within 12 months → Critical Block; age > 75 + comorbidities → likely block; procedure stacking → block; solo traveler with no aftercare plan → require monitoring) and a structured response template, asking to increase M-Care's reasoning capability "10x." Audited both halves before touching anything, since this repo's whole M-Care history is full of specs that read well but don't match what the real system computes.

**The transparency gap itself is real**: neither Rule 1 (SAFETY GATE) nor Rule 13 (PROFESSIONAL JUDGMENT) nor anything else in the ~97KB instructions string prescribed a step-by-step reasoning order or a structured response template for safety answers — confirmed via full-text search (zero matches for "GATHER"/"Reasoning Trace"/"Patient Profile"). **The proposed numeric thresholds are not real**, checked directly against `base44/functions/_shared/safeTEngine.ts` (`computeSafeT`): the engine has no time-recency field at all (no "12 months since a cardiac event" check exists anywhere), and no `CRITICAL` tier exists — real levels are `low | moderate | elevated | review`, capping at "needs human review," never an automatic block. The real age cutoffs are ≥70/≥65, not >75, with no procedure-specific carve-out. "Procedure stacking → block" matches `getViolations`' real RED-block logic well, but M-Care has no tool grant for it at all (confirmed via grep of `tool_configs` — no compatibility-check function is exposed to the agent; that block is enforced deterministically, server-side, at booking time, never something M-Care itself calls). "Solo traveler, no aftercare plan → require monitoring" doesn't exist as a pre-booking gate — the real system (`scheduleSoloCheckIns`/`escalateSoloCheckIn`) is reactive, post-departure monitoring, a different shape entirely.

Per this repo's non-negotiable Safety-Decision Architecture invariant (deterministic decides, AI narrates — only deterministic engines write safety outcomes, any risk level an LLM returns is discarded), wiring the pasted numbers in literally would have M-Care assert tiers its own safety record never actually computed — a real mismatch between what's said and what's on file, not just a style problem, regardless of which direction (more or less cautious) the invented number pushes. Asked Portia directly (AskUserQuestion) rather than guessing; she confirmed **narrate the real numbers** — keep the full reasoning-structure and "show my work" transparency upgrade, but M-Care must only ever state the engine's actual output level and real flags, never invent a "Critical Block" or any threshold the tool didn't return — and supplied the exact corrected phrasing to use as the model example.

**Shipped**: one new paragraph appended to the end of Rule 1 (SAFETY GATE), text-only, no `tool_configs` change (stays at 90) — zero Publish-order risk. It tells M-Care to reason out loud in a fixed order whenever explaining any SAFE-T outcome beyond LOW/PASSED (real disclosed factors, confirmed from the tool result not memory, the actual level + real flags computeSafeTScreening/safeT4LifeScan returned, plain-language why, then the safest next step), explicitly forbids inventing a "Critical Block" or any tier/age-cutoff/time-threshold a tool didn't return, includes Portia's own corrected before/after example verbatim as the model, and permits (never requires) laying the explanation out as a few short labeled lines when it aids clarity — with every line's content still bound to real tool output only.

**Deliberately not built this pass, stated to Portia, not silently dropped**: no tool grant added for procedure-stacking checks (that's new capability, not a narration fix — the real block still fires deterministically at booking regardless of what M-Care says); no pre-booking "solo traveler needs an aftercare plan" gate (doesn't exist in code today, would be new safety-engine work); no change to `computeSafeT`'s real thresholds (age 65/70, no CRITICAL tier, no recency field) — Portia's third option (fix the engine first, needs real clinical sign-off) was not chosen this round.

**A real, repeat-of-a-known-pattern encoding check, this time clean on the first try**: probed the raw (pre-`json.loads`) file text around Rule 1's end before writing anything, per the Phase 41 lesson — confirmed this specific region stores `\n` as the literal two-character backslash-n sequence and em-dashes as literal `—` escapes (not raw UTF-8 bytes), anchored the substring replace on that exact byte pattern, and wrote the new paragraph in plain ASCII (hyphens, not em-dashes) to avoid needing to replicate the escape convention. 1-line git diff, JSON validity + `tool_configs` count (90) confirmed before and after.

**Verification**: lint clean, typecheck at the same 5-error pre-existing baseline (`MCareVaultUpload.jsx`/`MCareAgent.jsx`, unrelated — a `.jsonc` file isn't in tsc's checked scope), `npm run build` exit 0, 642/642 vitest, 229/229 redteam (unchanged — no tool grant or write-permission surface touched, confirmed by running the suite rather than assumed, given this edit sits directly inside Rule 1). Whether the new structured explanations actually read as clearer and more trustworthy live, without feeling bureaucratic, can't be confirmed from this checkout — Portia verifies live.

## M-Care super-agent, Phase 43 — surfacing the real stacking check, a solo-traveler soft rule, real age numbers (2026-08-12)

Direct follow-up to Phase 42's three "deliberately not built" items. Portia came back with concrete direction on all three: surface the existing procedure-stacking check even though it's enforced elsewhere, design a solo-traveler soft rule (her own spec: moderate-or-higher risk + solo → recommend verified recovery support before booking, never a hard block), and have M-Care reference the real 65/70 age thresholds instead of inventing one. Investigated all three (3 parallel Explore passes) before designing anything, same discipline as Phase 42:

- **Procedure stacking had a real, ready-made function to reuse — no new backend code needed.** `base44/functions/validateProcedureSafety/entry.ts` already exists, is already live in production (called today by `Booking.jsx`), `createHandler`-wrapped with `requireAuth: false` (public, matching M-Care's own anonymous-access model), and is a pure pass-through to the real `getViolations` engine — takes `{ items: [{name/title}], conditions: string[] }` (just name strings, nothing session-bound), returns `{ isBlocked, violations, reason }`. `checkRedViolations` (referenced by an existing redteam test name) is NOT independently callable — it's inline logic inside the M Recon agentic loop's tool dispatcher, not its own edge function; `validateProcedureSafety` was the right one to grant instead.
- **Solo-traveler had a real, already-live definition to reuse.** `CaseRecord` (already granted to M-Care, read+update) carries `requires_companion` (bool) and `companion_requirement_status` (enum). The real, currently-scheduled definition of "solo" lives in `base44/functions/activateSoloCheckIn/entry.ts:26`: `isSolo = !caseData.requires_companion || caseData.companion_requirement_status === 'companion_required_pending'` — reused verbatim rather than inventing a new one. `CompanionAssignment` itself isn't granted to M-Care, but `CaseRecord`'s two fields already mirror the same truth, so no new entity grant was needed. "Moderate or higher" maps directly onto the real SAFE-T levels Rule 1 already narrates (everything except `low`).
- **Age numbers**: confirmed no literal age threshold appeared anywhere in the instructions before this pass (only unrelated unicode escapes like `≥`) — safe, uncontested addition.

**Shipped, three edits to `base44/agents/m_care.jsonc`:**

1. **New `tool_configs` grant**: `validateProcedureSafety` (90→91), matching the existing simple `{function_name, description}` shape used by entries like `getProcedureKnowledge`/`flagIntakeHandoff`. Its description explicitly tells the agent when to call it (2+ procedures mentioned), never to invent a stacking concern itself, and to frame any real `isBlocked` result as an early heads-up — the actual deterministic check still runs again, independently, at real booking time regardless of what M-Care says. **This is the one part of this pass with real Publish-order risk** (a new tool grant, the Phase 10 lesson) — but narrower than usual, since the backing function is already deployed and live (Booking.jsx already calls it today); only the agent-side tool registration needs confirming after Publish.
2. **Rule 13 (PROFESSIONAL JUDGMENT) — two new triggers**, in the exact voice/shape of its existing three ("a real, tool-grounded concern, never a hunch"), with the intro's "Three concrete triggers" bumped to "Five concrete triggers": **PROCEDURE STACKING CONCERN** (call `validateProcedureSafety` once 2+ procedures are mentioned; state the real reason plainly if blocked, offer to help sequence instead of combine) and **SOLO TRAVELER + ELEVATED RISK** (once a moderate/elevated/review SAFE-T outcome exists and the CaseRecord's real fields show no companion assigned, recommend verified recovery support and offer `flagIntakeHandoff` so the care team can help arrange it — a recommendation, never a block, matching every other Rule 13 trigger's own closing discipline).
3. **Rule 1 — one short parenthetical** appended right after its existing "the only valid levels to say out loud are the real ones" sentence: the engine's real age-related scoring uses 65 and 70 as its thresholds, for the rare case M-Care needs to speak about age-risk in general terms rather than narrating one patient's specific tool result — never a different number.

**Deliberately not built, stated to Portia, not silently dropped**: no new capability to actually arrange a companion or "book recovery support" — that's `flagIntakeHandoff`'s existing human-handoff job, not a new automated action; no change to `computeSafeT`'s real thresholds and no new `CRITICAL` tier (Phase 42's finding stands — only real numbers get spoken); `CompanionAssignment` itself was not newly granted to M-Care, since `CaseRecord`'s two mirrored fields were sufficient.

**No new redteam invariant added** — considered, but `validateProcedureSafety` is read-only, writes nothing, changes no RLS/safety-authority surface, and was already public before this grant; confirmed by running the full suite rather than assuming, and it stayed green with zero changes needed.

**Verification**: lint clean, typecheck at the same 5-error pre-existing baseline, `npm run build` exit 0, 642/642 vitest, 229/229 redteam. Whether M-Care actually calls `validateProcedureSafety` live and narrates it well, and whether the solo-traveler recommendation reads as helpful rather than presumptuous, can't be confirmed from this checkout — Portia verifies live, and should specifically watch for the new tool being called at all post-Publish (the standard new-tool-grant caution).

## M-Care super-agent, Phase 44 — auditing an "autonomous journey agent" spec; the real platform ceiling, and 2 real bugs fixed (2026-08-13)

Portia sent a very large spec for M-Care to become a "persistent, event-driven autonomous journey agent" — 20 named events (departure_48_hours, recovery_day_7, provider_confirmation_missing, etc.), 5 formal autonomy levels, proactive messages the user never asked for appearing unprompted inside the M-Care chat itself ("Your trip is coming up in 48 hours. I'm reconfirming your clinic and transfer now"), self-directed NEXT_BEST_SAFE_ACTION reasoning, full auditability. The spec's own "IMPORTANT IMPLEMENTATION RULE" section demanded auditing the existing architecture first and reusing what's real rather than rebuilding — so, same discipline as every other big-spec pass this session, that's what happened before any design.

**The load-bearing finding: the spec's central mechanic doesn't exist on this platform.** Read `node_modules/@base44/sdk/dist/modules/agents.js` in full — it exports exactly 8 methods (`getConversations`, `getConversation`, `listConversations`, `createConversation`, `addMessage`, `subscribeToConversation`, `getWhatsAppConnectURL`, `getTelegramConnectURL`). There is no way to inject a message into an existing conversation except `addMessage`, which is always a user turn that triggers the agent to respond — no `role: 'assistant'` unprompted-message primitive, no schedule/webhook/trigger field anywhere on `m_care.jsonc`'s own top-level schema (enumerated directly: `description, instructions, tool_configs, context_files, ..., whatsapp_greeting, telegram_greeting, line_greeting, memory_config, voice_config, allow_anonymous_access, name` — nothing scheduling-shaped). A repo-wide grep for any backend function calling `base44.agents.*`/`base44.asServiceRole.agents.*` returned zero hits across ~300 functions — this has never been done for M-Care or any other agent. "M-Care speaks first inside your open chat, unprompted, because a cron job fired" has no supporting primitive today. The real substitute is exactly what every other proactive nudge in this app already does — email/SMS/WhatsApp/push via `notify.ts`'s link-only templates, never the chat conversation object; push notifications specifically already carry the M-Care logo as their icon (Phase 4C), so the felt experience is already real, just not literally inside the chat bubble.

**Audited all 20 named events against real existing code.** Most are already covered, several under different names: `provider_selected` (`selectDoctorQuote`), `booking_confirmed` (`onDoctorConfirmed`/`pipelineOnDoctorConfirmed`), `payment_confirmed` (`pipelineOnConsultationFeePaid`/`stripePaymentWebhook`), `provider_confirmation_missing` (`detectFallbackCrisis` + `checkPartnerSLABreaches`), `airport_transfer_unconfirmed` (`escalateMissedDriverHandshake`), `procedure_completed` (`logProcedureComplete`), `recovery_day_3/7/14/30` (`schedulePostOpCheckIns`, a literal `CHECK_IN_DAYS=[3,7,14,30]` array), `abnormal_patient_response` (`checkRecoveryAnomaly` + `submitRecoveryCheckin`'s Critical-priority path — deterministic rules, real escalation, the best-covered event of the 20). Genuine, confirmed-real gaps: `procedure_selected` (no dedicated handler — a passive field), `departure_48_hours` (existing reminders are day-granular only), `return_travel_approaching`/fit-to-fly-before-return-flight (nothing checks post-op clearance ahead of a *return* flight specifically — every existing timing check keys off the outbound `departure_date`), `recovery_day_1` (existing check-ins are interval-based from procedure time, not a calendar Day-1 milestone), and flight-cancellation detection (`pollActiveTripFlights` has no `cancelled` branch — the same gap this file already named as deliberately deferred, still true).

**The two real bugs, in this exact recurring bug class**: `sendTravelCountdownReminders` (real, correct, 14/7/3/1/0-day departure milestones — covers the spec's own `departure_7_days` example almost exactly) and `autoCompletePatientJourney` (real, correct, valid-transition state machine for `journey_completed`) were both fully built, `cronAuthorized()`-gated, idempotent (flag fields / terminal-status checks) — and simply never added to `safety-cron.yml`. Both read in full to confirm none of that was a stale self-description before trusting it. Presented the whole picture to Portia (premise failure + the real substitute + the event-coverage table) via AskUserQuestion; she chose the narrowest, safest option: fix these two now, nothing else this pass. **Shipped**: two lines added to `safety-cron.yml`'s existing DAILY block (`call sendTravelCountdownReminders` / `call autoCompletePatientJourney`, right after the block's other daily calls) — both functions only need day-granularity, so the existing 09:00 UTC tick is sufficient; no new cron expression, no code changes to either function. Noticed, not fixed this pass (would have expanded scope beyond "just wire them in"): both bypass the mandatory `createHandler` middleware (raw `Deno.serve` with a manually-reimplemented `cronAuthorized` guard) — functionally fine, a real guard exists, but a known deviation worth a future migration pass.

**Deliberately not built this pass, stated to Portia, not silently dropped**: the proactive-in-chat mechanism (doesn't exist on this platform); the 4 real remaining event gaps named above; the 5-level autonomy framework as formal code (much of its spirit already exists informally — deterministic-decides-AI-narrates, `logMcarePerformance` audit logging, fail-closed on uncertainty — Rules 1/13/19 already embody most of it); the unified "journey state" object (Phase 30 already found `bookingState.ts` real but adopted by only ~4/72 status-writing functions).

**Verification**: this is a GitHub Actions workflow change, not a Base44 app change — no Publish step, no `tool_configs`/`m_care.jsonc` touched, so the standing Base44 publish-verification prompt doesn't apply here. Ran the full frontend gate anyway to confirm nothing else was disturbed: lint clean, typecheck at the same 5-error pre-existing baseline, `npm run build` exit 0, 642/642 vitest, 229/229 redteam — all unchanged, as expected, since no application code was touched. Whether the two functions actually fire correctly on the next real 09:00 UTC tick can't be confirmed from this checkout — Portia can check the Actions tab after the next daily run, or trigger `workflow_dispatch` manually to test sooner.

## M-Care super-agent, Phase 45 — a real proactive-message layer via a new JourneyEvent entity (2026-08-13)

Direct follow-up to Phase 44, this time asking for the CORRECT alternative architecture Phase 44 already pointed at: since a cron job can't inject a message into the live agent conversation, write a persistent backend record instead, have the frontend notice it, and render it into the M-Care chat UI as a normal bubble. Portia's spec explicitly ruled out re-litigating the disproven premise and explicitly demanded auditing what this app's real infrastructure supports before building anything — two parallel Explore passes did that first.

**Realtime is technically possible, but unproven — polling is what shipped.** `node_modules/@base44/sdk/dist/modules/entities.js` genuinely exposes `entities.<Name>.subscribe(callback)` over the same WebSocket `subscribeToConversation` uses — a real capability, not agent-only as Phase 44 first assumed. But it has zero usage anywhere in this ~300-function codebase for any entity — no proof Base44's backend actually scopes a subscription to only the calling user's own records before shipping it on patient journey data. `src/components/quotes/CaseThread.jsx` already polls a message-style entity every 20 seconds in production — the closest real precedent to "poll and render as chat bubbles." Presented both to Portia via AskUserQuestion; she chose the proven 20s-polling path.

**No existing entity fit** — surveyed `AuditLog` (actor-only read), `NotificationLog`/`DispatchFailureLog` (admin-only), `CrisisReroute` (the one genuine patient-readable event-log entity, but crisis-specific schema), `CaseRecord.timeline_log` (patient-readable, already used by 2 of the relevant functions, but an untyped array with no room for the structured fields Portia's spec required). A new, minimal, purpose-built entity was the right call, confirmed by absence of a real fit rather than assumed.

**The frontend hazard, found by reading `MCareOrb.jsx`/`MessageBubble.jsx` in full**: `agentMessages` gets wholesale-replaced on every `subscribeToConversation` tick and every 12s safety-net re-fetch — the codebase already has a direct, commented precedent against writing synthetic data into that array (`voiceReplyAudioUrls`'s own comment explains exactly this hazard). A second, real risk found only by reading the actual render loop (not anticipated in the original plan, corrected mid-build): `agentMessages.map((m, i) => ...)` keys `revealState`/`voiceReplyAudioUrls` by array INDEX — so interleaving proactive bubbles chronologically into that same loop would have shifted indices for real messages and silently corrupted the word-reveal/audio-replay wiring for the live conversation. Fixed by keeping proactive events in a fully separate render block (their own `.map()`, their own `je-${id}` keys, appended after the real conversation rather than interleaved) — simpler than the originally-planned merge-and-sort, and zero risk to the existing machinery.

**Shipped**:
- **`base44/entities/JourneyEvent.jsonc`** (new) — `case_id`, `client_email` (RLS anchor, matches `CrisisReroute`'s pattern), `event_type`, `source`, `message_text` (the ONLY field ever rendered as a bubble), `action_taken`/`tool_result` (audit-only), `user_action_required`/`escalation_occurred`. Read RLS matches `CrisisReroute` (`data.client_email == user.email` OR admin) — but `create`/`update` were deliberately tightened to admin-only rather than copying `CrisisReroute`'s own `authenticated: true` create rule: since every real write goes through `asServiceRole` anyway (which bypasses RLS), an open create RLS would cost nothing functionally while leaving a real spoofing vector open — any authenticated user could otherwise forge a fake "M-Care" message directly into another patient's own feed by setting their `client_email`. Closed here rather than reproduced from the sibling entity; `CrisisReroute`'s own equivalent gap wasn't touched (out of scope, flagged, not this pass).
- **`base44/shared/logJourneyEvent.ts`** (new) — the single, never-throwing writer both functions call, same discipline as `logCrisisReroute.ts`/`logProviderContactAttempt.ts`.
- **Two additive function edits**, both riding on each function's already-idempotent gate from Phase 44 (no new dedup logic needed): `sendTravelCountdownReminders` logs a `JourneyEvent` right where it already flips its `travel_reminder_Xd_sent` flag; `autoCompletePatientJourney` logs one inside its existing `if (shouldComplete)` block. `message_text` is deterministic, reused-copy-style text, never a fresh LLM call — `autoCompletePatientJourney`'s existing `aiJourneySummary` (a Haiku-generated sentence) was deliberately NOT reused for the patient-facing bubble; it stays scoped to its original internal `timeline_log` use.
- **`src/hooks/useJourneyEvents.js`** (new) — copies `CaseThread.jsx`'s exact 20s `refetchInterval` shape.
- **`MCareOrb.jsx`** — additive only: resolves the active case via the existing `useActiveCaseRecord` hook, polls journey events, renders them in their own trailing block (each with a small "M-Care checked in" label), a new symmetric voice effect (separate from the existing fragile speak-on-growth effect, dedup'd by JourneyEvent's own real persistent id rather than array index) speaks a genuinely NEW event when Talk Mode is on — deliberately primes (marks-seen-without-speaking) whatever's already on record on first load, so turning Talk Mode on doesn't trigger a backlog read-aloud of old events — and a small gold dot appears on the closed floating orb button when an event is newer than the last time the panel was opened, the honest way to satisfy "the user shouldn't have to send a message first" without a literal push notification.
- **New redteam invariant** (`JOURNEY EVENTS: ...`) — asserts `JourneyEvent`'s read RLS is patient-scoped (not wide open, not admin-only-read), create/update are admin-only, both writer functions route through the shared helper rather than a raw create call, and `MCareOrb.jsx` only ever hands `message_text` to a rendered bubble, never `tool_result`/`action_taken`.

**Deliberately not built this pass, stated to Portia, not silently dropped**: realtime subscriptions (her own chosen scope for v1); wiring `JourneyEvent` into `detectFallbackCrisis`/`checkPartnerSLABreaches`/`escalateMissedDriverHandshake` (would let `message_text` honestly narrate real clinic/transfer confirmation checks, matching her original example prose more closely — real, separate follow-on); the true 48-hour departure granularity (Phase 44's already-named gap, not fabricated here); any LLM-authored proactive message text; `CrisisReroute`'s own open `create` RLS (a sibling instance of the same gap this pass closed for `JourneyEvent`, not touched).

**Verification**: entity JSON validity confirmed via `python3 -c "json.load(...)"`. Full gate: lint clean, typecheck at the same 5-error pre-existing baseline (`MCareOrb.jsx` carries its own `@ts-nocheck`, unaffected either way), `npm run build` exit 0, 642/642 vitest, 230/230 redteam (1 new, passed on first write). **This pass touches a real Base44 entity file, unlike Phase 44's pure-workflow change — it needs a Publish** before it's live; give Portia the standing publish-verification prompt plus an explicit note to Publish the new `JourneyEvent` entity itself, not just the two changed functions. Whether the 20s poll actually surfaces a new event promptly, whether the trailing proactive-message block reads naturally next to real conversation history, and whether the unread dot is noticeable without being annoying, can't be confirmed from this checkout — Portia verifies live after the next real milestone or journey completion fires.

## M-Care super-agent, Phase 46 — JourneyEvent made the standing proactive-communication layer; AFTER CARE Day 3 wired in (2026-08-13)

Portia issued a standing architecture rule, not tied to one feature: `JourneyEvent` (Phase 45) is now the **official, permanent proactive-communication layer** for M-Care — no future feature may build its own separate notification mechanism when JourneyEvent can represent the event. She attached a wishlist of future event types across BEFORE TRIP / DURING TRIP / AFTER CARE, plus an explicit process: for every new event, audit existing code first, reuse real functions, never fabricate autonomy, and report exactly what's real vs. missing vs. unsupported before writing anything.

**Full audit against that wishlist (two parallel Explore passes, source read directly, not summarized from memory):**

- **Already wired (Phase 45, live):** departure milestones (14/7/3/1/0-day) and journey completion.
- **Real, cron-scheduled, `CaseRecord`-scoped, just not yet emitting a `JourneyEvent` — pure wiring gaps:** doctor confirmation (`checkPartnerSLABreaches` + `detectFallbackCrisis`, both firing every 15 minutes); driver confirmation/airport pickup/driver status (`escalateMissedDriverHandshake`, same 15-min cron); AFTER CARE Day 3 (`schedulePostOpCheckIns` — wired this pass, see below); the AFTER CARE response confirmation (`submitPostOpCheckIn` — equally safe, deliberately left for its own pass).
- **Real but structurally event-triggered, not cron/autonomous** (a different kind of wiring than the cron jobs above): `completeHandshake` (arrival/clinic-arrival taps), `activateEmergencyBeacon` (patient-triggered SOS).
- **A real, confirmed bug in the same "never actually scheduled" family this file already documents**: `schedulePostOpCheckIns` only ever sends the Day 3 recovery check-in. Days 7/14/30 get a `PostOpCheckIn` row *created* but nothing ever sends the reminder — the function's own comment claims "others are sent by a daily cron," and no such cron, or sender function, exists anywhere in the repo. Not fixed this pass (a new function + a new cron entry, not a wiring change) — flagged plainly, not silently left for someone to rediscover.
- **Fully aspirational, zero real backing code, confirmed by repo-wide search:** required-documents scan, outstanding-consent scan, hotel-confirmation-as-its-own-check, missed connection. `flight status`/`travel disruption` (`pollActiveTripFlights`) exists but isn't scheduled anywhere in this repo (its own header claims a Base44-native cron, unverifiable from here), and its delayed/cancelled branches are effectively dead code — cancelled-detection needs a real FlightStats key that was never set, the same gap this file already names elsewhere. `destination safety change` (`checkWeatherAlerts`/`getDestinationSafetyIndex`) are real on-demand checks with zero scheduling, zero persistence, zero notification.

**Portia's choice, per her own "smallest safe change" rule**: wire AFTER CARE Day 3 — the lowest-risk of the ready candidates, since it requires no new scheduling at all, just mirrors a notification that's already being sent. Shipped: `schedulePostOpCheckIns/entry.ts` (the `day === 3 && patientEmail` branch, which is naturally idempotent — it only ever runs the one time that `PostOpCheckIn` record gets created) now calls `logJourneyEvent` right after its existing email+push+`notification_sent_at` sequence, with a new `recovery_checkin_day3` entry added to `JourneyEvent.event_type`'s enum. `message_text` is fixed, reviewed copy with no name interpolation (matching every other event in this entity): *"You're 3 days into recovery — I've sent you a quick check-in so your doctor can see how you're doing. Reply whenever you're ready."* `tool_result` deliberately excludes the check-in's one-time `response_token` — that credential's single source of truth stays the `PostOpCheckIn` record itself, no reason to duplicate a secret token into a second entity with a different RLS shape. The existing `JOURNEY EVENTS` redteam invariant was extended to cover this third writer (asserts it calls `logJourneyEvent(` and never a raw `entities.JourneyEvent.create`).

**Deliberately not built this pass, stated plainly, not silently dropped**: `submitPostOpCheckIn` (the patient's response confirmation) — an equally safe, equally idempotent follow-on found in the same audit, left for its own pass; the Day 7/14/30 sender (a real bug, needs a new function + cron entry, bigger than this pass's scope); doctor/driver confirmation wiring (real and ready, but a separate decision — multiple tiers, wording needs care so a "still waiting on your doctor" message reads as reassuring, not alarming); every item in the "fully aspirational" list above.

**Verification**: `python3 -c "json.load(...)"` confirms `JourneyEvent.jsonc`'s new enum value is valid. Full gate: lint clean, typecheck at the same 5-error pre-existing baseline (unrelated files), `npm run build` exit 0, 642/642 vitest, 230/230 redteam (1 extended, passed). **Touches a real Base44 entity file again** (`JourneyEvent.jsonc`'s enum) plus `schedulePostOpCheckIns/entry.ts` — needs a Publish naming both files explicitly, same standing prompt as every entity-touching pass. Whether the Day-3 bubble actually appears within one 20s poll after a real Handshake-9 completion, and whether it reads naturally alongside the email/push the patient also gets, can't be confirmed from this checkout — Portia verifies live after the next real Golden M moment.

## M-Care super-agent, Phase 47 — JourneyEvent as the "Journey Watcher": driver no-show wired in (2026-08-14)

Portia relayed Base44's own read of the Phase 46 audit back with explicit endorsement: JourneyEvent stays the one standing proactive layer, "every real check-in should generate its own JourneyEvent," and sketched a longer-term "M-Care Journey Watcher" loop (every 15 min → check → did something change? → act → verify → JourneyEvent → M-Care speaks) plus a further-out 48-hour pre-travel composite check (flight/doctor/clinic/driver/hotel/documents/destination, all in one proactive message). The composite check isn't buildable honestly yet — several of those inputs (flight status, destination safety, documents, hotel-as-its-own-check) are still unscheduled or fully aspirational per the Phase 46 audit — so this pass stayed disciplined to the same "smallest safe change" cadence: one more real slice toward the Watcher.

Read `checkPartnerSLABreaches`, `detectFallbackCrisis`, and `escalateMissedDriverHandshake` in full before proposing anything (all three are the real, already-15-min-cron-scheduled candidates the Phase 46 audit surfaced). The two doctor-related functions are real but span 6 total branches across 2 files (12h/24h in one, 15-min/24h/4h in the other) — each needs its own message-tone judgment call so "your doctor hasn't confirmed yet" reads as reassuring, not alarming; queued as its own future pass, Portia's own choice. `escalateMissedDriverHandshake` is a single function, single branch, single idempotency flag (`noshow_flagged_steps`) — and it already sends the patient a real, already-approved SMS the moment a driver no-show fires, so this pass needed zero new message-wording judgment calls, only a mirror of existing, already-approved copy.

**Shipped**: `escalateMissedDriverHandshake/entry.ts` now calls `logJourneyEvent` right after its existing `logCrisisReroute` audit call (itself right after the patient's already-real SMS and the `noshow_flagged_steps` idempotency write) — so the JourneyEvent only ever fires once the whole no-show detection is fully resolved (backup search done, patient already texted, audit logged), never mid-flight. New `driver_noshow_backup` enum value on `JourneyEvent.event_type` — one value covers both outcomes (backup found vs. still searching); the outcome itself lives in `escalation_occurred` + `tool_result.backup_dispatched`, not a second enum value, since this is one detection event with two possible resolutions, not two events. `message_text` is the existing SMS copy verbatim, only swapped from "we've"/"we're" to "I've"/"I'm" to match this entity's established first-person M-Care voice — not new copy. `JOURNEY EVENTS` redteam invariant extended to a 4th writer.

**Deliberately not built this pass, stated plainly**: doctor confirmation (`checkPartnerSLABreaches` + `detectFallbackCrisis`) — queued, bigger scope, needs its own wording pass. The driver-SLA branch inside `checkPartnerSLABreaches` (a driver failing to respond to a *quote request* — a different moment than a physical no-show) — not touched. The 48-hour composite situational-awareness check — needs flight status and destination-safety scheduling (neither exists today) plus document/hotel checks (fully aspirational) as real inputs before it can be built honestly rather than faked.

**Verification**: `python3 -c "json.load(...)"` confirms the new enum value is valid. Full gate: lint clean, typecheck at the same 5-error pre-existing baseline (unrelated files), `npm run build` exit 0, 642/642 vitest, 230/230 redteam (1 extended, passed). Touches `JourneyEvent.jsonc`'s enum again plus `escalateMissedDriverHandshake/entry.ts` — needs a Publish naming both explicitly. Whether a patient finds it reassuring or redundant to get the same message twice (once by SMS, once as a JourneyEvent chat bubble) can't be confirmed from this checkout — Portia verifies live after a real no-show detection fires.

## M-Care super-agent, Phase 48 — doctor confirmation → JourneyEvent, full audit before code (2026-08-14)

A detailed spec arrived demanding a complete audit of `checkPartnerSLABreaches`, `detectFallbackCrisis`, existing patient-facing wording, idempotency, and data-safety per outcome, with an explicit "DO NOT IMPLEMENT UNTIL THIS AUDIT IS SHOWN" gate and a closing instruction not to fabricate test coverage that doesn't actually run. Read `checkPartnerSLABreaches/entry.ts`, `detectFallbackCrisis/entry.ts`, `base44/shared/findDoctorBackup.ts`, and `respondToDoctorPortalCase/entry.ts` in full before writing anything, per that gate.

**8 distinct doctor-confirmation outcomes identified, each independently audited** (real behavior, whether the patient is already notified today, existing wording, idempotency, data-safety): (0) doctor confirms normally — `respondToDoctorPortalCase:32-39` sets `doctor_confirmation_status:'Confirmed'`/`status:'Vendor-Pending'`, which verifiably removes the case from both watchers' scan criteria, confirmed by reading the actual query conditions, not assumed; (1) `checkPartnerSLABreaches`'s 12h doctor-only reminder; (2) `checkPartnerSLABreaches`→`findDoctorBackup` 24h timeout, backup found; (3) same, no backup available; (4) `detectFallbackCrisis`'s 15-minute `DOCTOR_MISSED_CONFIRMATION_WINDOW`; (5) its 24h `DOCTOR_UNCONFIRMED_PROCEDURE_APPROACHING`; (6) its 4h `DOCTOR_UNCONFIRMED_PROCEDURE_CRITICAL`; (7) its LLM-driven `aiAdditionalRisks` scan (JSON-response-only, no write, no notification, no persistence anywhere — exactly the "never create an event merely because an LLM predicts something might happen" case the spec named explicitly).

**A significant, real finding**: the patient is notified by **none** of these paths today — `findDoctorBackup.ts` read in full notifies the new doctor, admin, and (if urgent) the travel agency/companion, but never the patient; `detectFallbackCrisis` only ever reminds the doctor or pages admin. So this pass isn't adding a second channel to something already communicated (like the Day-3 check-in or driver no-show) — it's the *first* time the patient hears about a doctor-confirmation problem from M-Care at all.

**A better design surfaced by reading `findDoctorBackup.ts`'s own doc comment** ("the single real implementation behind every 'this doctor dropped the case' path"): it's called from three real places — `checkPartnerSLABreaches`'s 24h cron timeout, and an explicit doctor **decline** or **withdraw** via `respondToDoctorPortalCase:75-79` (a live user action, not cron). Wiring `logJourneyEvent` *inside* `findDoctorBackup.ts` itself (not at each call site) covers all three triggers with one change — genuinely reusing the one existing decision point rather than duplicating logic. Idempotency for the cron path is inherited for free: the success branch sets `sla_breached_doctor: true` (already existed, line ~201, now also the very field that makes `checkPartnerSLABreaches`'s own skip-guard work), and the failure branch's `status: 'Admin-Review'` transition removes the case from `checkPartnerSLABreaches`'s `status: 'Doctor-Pending'` query entirely — both confirmed by reading the code, not assumed. The decline/withdraw path is a one-time UI action, not re-entrant.

**Scope, decided with confirmation**: wired outcomes #2, #3, #5, #6. Deliberately excluded #1 (12h doctor-only reminder — too routine/premature, nothing's actually wrong yet), #4 (15-minute window — far too early, triggers no notification to *anyone* today, not even the doctor), and #7 (the AI risk scan — the explicit "don't fabricate an event from a prediction" case).

**Shipped**: `base44/shared/findDoctorBackup.ts` — two `logJourneyEvent` calls, one per outcome branch, both after every existing real side effect and right before that branch's `return`. New `doctor_backup_dispatched` event_type covers both (success/failure distinguished by `escalation_occurred` + `tool_result.outcome`, same one-event-two-resolutions shape as `driver_noshow_backup`). Neither message names the new doctor or repeats `admin_notes` — the patient learns the doctor's name through the normal case-detail flow, matching the `driver_noshow_backup` precedent of not naming the driver either. `base44/functions/detectFallbackCrisis/entry.ts` — one `logJourneyEvent` call inside the existing `!c.fallback_state?.in_flux`-gated block, guarded by the same `reason === 'DOCTOR_UNCONFIRMED_PROCEDURE_APPROACHING' || reason === 'DOCTOR_UNCONFIRMED_PROCEDURE_CRITICAL'` condition the existing `sendDoctorReminder` call already uses — inherits that gate's exactly-once-per-flux-episode idempotency for free, the identical mechanism `logCrisisReroute` already relies on. New `doctor_confirmation_delay_flagged` event_type, message varies by reason (approaching vs. critical wording), `escalation_occurred` true only for the critical tier. No frontend changes at all — `MCareOrb.jsx`'s JourneyEvent polling/rendering/Talk-Mode-speaking effect is already generic over `event_type`, confirmed by re-reading it rather than assumed.

**Redteam invariants extended, not just described**: the `JOURNEY EVENTS` test now also covers `detectFallbackCrisis` (functions/ path) and `findDoctorBackup` (shared/ path, checked separately since it doesn't fit the functions/ loop) as real writers; a new assertion extracts `aiAdditionalRisks`' actual function body via regex and confirms `logJourneyEvent` never appears inside it — the "never on an LLM prediction alone" rule enforced structurally, not just by convention; a new assertion confirms the `logJourneyEvent` call sits within 200 characters of the two-reason gate condition (i.e., actually inside that guard, not floating free elsewhere in the function); new assertions confirm neither new `message_text` template interpolates the doctor's name, admin notes, or the raw `reason` variable.

**Tests — stated honestly, not fabricated**: this repo's Deno edge functions have no integration-test harness (no mocked `asServiceRole`, `Deno.serve`/`createClientFromRequest` don't exist in the Vitest/Node environment) — every `JOURNEY EVENTS` redteam test, including the new assertions this pass, is a structural check over file source, not a behavioral one. Claims like "doctor not confirmed → JourneyEvent created exactly once" and "repeated 15-min execution → no duplicates" are true by code inspection (the idempotency mechanisms are the same ones this repo's existing `sla_breached_doctor`/status-transition/`fallback_state.in_flux` logic already relies on, inherited rather than reinvented) but are **not** independently re-verified by an executable test in this pass — stated plainly rather than claiming a coverage guarantee that doesn't exist.

**Verification**: `python3 -c "json.load(...)"` confirms both new enum values are valid. Full gate: lint clean, typecheck at the same 5-error pre-existing baseline (unrelated files), `npm run build` exit 0, 642/642 vitest, 230/230 redteam (extended, all passed on first write). Touches `JourneyEvent.jsonc`'s enum plus `findDoctorBackup.ts` and `detectFallbackCrisis/entry.ts` — needs a Publish naming all three explicitly. Whether the wording lands as reassuring rather than alarming for a real patient watching their doctor situation unfold live can't be confirmed from this checkout — Portia verifies live after a real SLA timeout, decline, or procedure-proximity flag fires.

## M-Care super-agent, Phase 49 — full pipeline audit: dedup, authz, visibility, voice, SMS/email, read state, audit trail (2026-08-14)

A bare pipeline diagram arrived (JourneyEvent → deduplication → authorization → patient visibility → voice → SMS/email → read/unread state → audit trail) — treated as an audit request across all 8 stages of the system built in Phases 45-48, checked against the real current code rather than assumed still-correct.

**Deduplication — mostly solid, one real gap found, one flagged not fixed.** `logJourneyEvent.ts` has zero dedup logic of its own; every writer relies entirely on its own caller-side idempotency gate. The one structural risk that implies — two overlapping cron ticks double-firing the same gate-check-then-act sequence — turned out to already be closed: `.github/workflows/safety-cron.yml` declares `concurrency: { group: safety-cron, cancel-in-progress: false }`, serializing every run of the workflow regardless of trigger type, and Base44's native scheduler is confirmed (this file's own Deploying & Environment section) not actually used for these functions — GitHub Actions is the sole real trigger. **Flagged, not fixed**: `findDoctorBackup` is also reachable from `respondToDoctorPortalCase`'s doctor-decline/withdraw action — a live HTTP call with no concurrency-group protection. A doctor double-clicking "Decline" before the first request's `CaseRecord.update` lands could, in principle, fire two concurrent `findDoctorBackup` calls (and two JourneyEvents) — a pre-existing race in that action's own logic, not introduced by JourneyEvent wiring, and out of scope to fix in a wiring pass.

**Authorization, patient visibility, voice** — all re-confirmed unchanged and correct: RLS still patient-own-read/admin-write; `useJourneyEvents`'s 20s poll → `MCareOrb`'s trailing block still wired; Talk Mode's speaking effect is generic over `event_type` (already proven in practice — Phase 48's two new event types needed zero frontend changes). No gaps.

**SMS/email — a real, deliberate gap, not a bug.** None of the 4 Phase 48 doctor-confirmation JourneyEvents has any patient-facing SMS/email counterpart (confirmed in Phase 48's own audit — only the doctor and admin are notified today). A patient who never opens the M-Care panel would not learn about a doctor-confirmation delay through any channel. Surfaced directly rather than silently built or silently ignored — Portia's call: leave it documented, JourneyEvent stays chat/voice-only, matching its original Phase 45 scope ("an additional communication channel, not a replacement"). Adding new patient SMS/email would be a bigger, separate pass (new wording review), not a wiring change.

**Read/unread state — a real bug, found and fixed.** `MCareOrb.jsx`'s `lastSeenJourneyEventsAt` was pure in-memory `useState(0)`, reset to epoch 0 on every mount — meaning the gold "unread" dot would show after almost any page reload if the case ever had a JourneyEvent, regardless of whether the patient already read it. The signal was only ever meaningful within one uninterrupted session.
