**Audit trail — consistent with existing precedent, not a gap.** `JourneyEvent` isn't written into the hash-chained `AuditLog` (reserved for safety *decisions*, per this file's own Audit Log Hash Chain section) — checked whether that's a gap by comparing against this app's other operational/comms logs (`CrisisReroute`, `ContactAttempt`, `DispatchFailureLog`): all three are standalone entities too, never duplicated into `AuditLog`. `JourneyEvent`'s admin-only create/update RLS with no delete-in-practice already gives it the same append-only-in-practice shape a communications log needs, without claiming the tamper-evidence guarantee a safety decision requires.

**Shipped**: `src/components/mcare/MCareOrb.jsx` — a new effect hydrates `lastSeenJourneyEventsAt` from `localStorage.getItem('morales_journey_events_seen_' + caseId)` once the active case is known (runs once per case, never re-fires and clobbers a later update); a new `markJourneyEventsSeen()` function sets the state and writes the same case-scoped key; both existing call sites (the `?mcare=open` deep-link auto-open effect, and the floating button's `onClick`) now call it instead of the raw setter. Follows this app's existing plain `localStorage.getItem/setItem` `morales_`-prefixed convention (`AuthContext.jsx`, `ConciergeIntake.jsx`, `Booking.jsx`) rather than inventing a shared wrapper. Deliberately scoped **per case_id**, not global — a brand-new case's fresh events must never be masked by a stale "seen everything" timestamp left over from a patient's previous, already-completed journey.

**Verification**: pure frontend fix, no entity/schema touched — **no Base44 publish needed** for this pass. Full gate: lint clean, typecheck at the same 5-error pre-existing baseline, `npm run build` exit 0, 642/642 vitest, 230/230 redteam (unchanged — no entity/tool/permission surface touched, confirmed by running the suite rather than assumed). Whether the unread dot now correctly stays quiet across a page reload after the patient has actually opened the panel can't be confirmed from this checkout — Portia verifies live.

## M-Care super-agent, Phase 50 — JourneyEvent → push notification, with a real priority field (2026-08-14)

Portia's direct follow-up to Phase 49's SMS/email finding: "the next slice: JourneyEvent → push notification," paired with a LOW/MEDIUM/HIGH/CRITICAL priority hierarchy (app-only / app+push / push+SMS / push+SMS+emergency-escalation), explicitly instructing not to build the whole ladder in one pass, and to keep the Phase 49 doctor-decline concurrency race as its own separate task.

Two audits before any code: `sendPushNotification` is real and working end-to-end — `web-push`/VAPID, a real `UserPushSubscription` entity + frontend subscribe flow (`usePushNotifications.js`, wired in `App.jsx`, prod-only), and `sw.js`'s `push` listener is already icon-aware (Phase 4C) — but had zero wiring to JourneyEvent. Separately, `sendTravelCountdownReminders` and `escalateMissedDriverHandshake` already send their **own** SMS today, independent of JourneyEvent, via a small per-function `sms()` helper (a raw Twilio REST POST — confirmed as an accepted, ~15-times-repeated pattern in this codebase, not something to fix). `linkOnlySms`/`linkOnlyEmail` (`notify.ts`) are pure validate-and-compose string builders, not senders — each caller does its own Twilio call. A generic "CRITICAL → also SMS" rule inside JourneyEvent would have double-texted the patient specifically on driver no-shows. Asked Portia directly; she chose **push only this pass**, deferring all SMS to its own dedicated slice.

**Shipped**: `JourneyEvent.jsonc` gained a required `priority` field (`low`/`medium`/`high`/`critical`, added to the `required` array — no silent default, matching the entity's existing "message_text must be deterministic, reviewed copy" discipline). `logJourneyEvent.ts` — the one central integration point, keeping JourneyEvent the single proactive-communication layer per Portia's own standing rule — now takes a required `priority` param and, after a successful create, best-effort (try/catch, still never-throwing) invokes the existing `sendPushNotification` whenever `priority !== 'low'`: `body: message_text` (push isn't held to the SMS/email link-only rule — confirmed existing push call sites already send real content), `url` deep-links via the real Phase 35 `?mcare=open` link, `icon: '/mcare-logo.png'`, `urgent: priority === 'high' || priority === 'critical'` (maps to `sw.js`'s `requireInteraction`, so a high/critical push stays on screen instead of auto-dismissing), and `internal_secret: Deno.env.get('CRON_SECRET')` — required for `sendPushNotification`'s `internalOrAdminAuthorized` gate to pass from a service-role caller; two existing call sites (`sendProcedurePrepReminders`, `checkStaleLiveLocations`) were found to have forgotten this and always silently 403 — not repeated here.

Priority assigned at all 6 real writers, derived only from data each one already computes (no new judgment logic invented, matching Phase 48's mandate): `sendTravelCountdownReminders`'s 14d/7d milestones → `low` (app-only, matches its own "don't spam" ethos), 3d/1d/day-of → `medium` (matches its own existing "SMS at 1d/3d only" judgment about which milestones matter more); `autoCompletePatientJourney` → `low` (informational, terminal); `schedulePostOpCheckIns`'s Day-3 check-in → `medium` (`user_action_required:true` — push nudges an actual response); `escalateMissedDriverHandshake` → `high` if a backup was found, `critical` if not (still unresolved); `detectFallbackCrisis` → `medium` for the 24h "approaching" tier, `high` for the 4h tier (not `critical` — deferred medical-logistics urgency, not the driver's physical-stranding shape); `findDoctorBackup.ts` → `medium` if resolved, `critical` if not (the doctor-backup-dispatch-failure case — the one event type with zero existing patient SMS today, flagged as the natural first candidate whenever the SMS slice gets built).

**Deliberately not built this pass**: all SMS — Portia's own choice. When that slice is built, `doctor_backup_dispatched`'s failure branch is the one genuine gap (no existing patient SMS); `driver_noshow_backup` must not get a second, generic SMS layered on top of the one it already sends without first auditing/consolidating the two. The Phase 49 doctor-decline concurrency race — untouched, its own task per Portia's explicit instruction. Flight/destination monitoring, Day 7/14/30 recovery, and the 48-hour composite Journey Check — all named by Portia as later, bigger pieces, not started.

**Verification**: touches a real entity schema (`JourneyEvent.jsonc`) and 7 functions (5 direct call sites + `checkPartnerSLABreaches`/`respondToDoctorPortalCase`, both importing the changed `findDoctorBackup.ts`) — **needs a Publish** naming the entity and all 7 functions. Grep-confirmed all 6 real `logJourneyEvent(` call sites carry the new `priority` arg (base44/functions isn't in tsc's checked scope, so a missing param wouldn't be caught by typecheck). Lint clean, typecheck at the same 5-error pre-existing baseline, 642/642 vitest (unchanged — no new pure-logic module), 230/230 redteam (unchanged — no write-permission/RLS surface touched), `npm run build` exit 0. Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: whether a real push notification actually arrives on a device for a medium/high/critical event, and whether `urgent:true` visibly changes behavior (stays on screen vs. auto-dismisses) — Portia verifies live after Publishing and the next real milestone/event fires.

## M-Care super-agent, Phase 51 — Weather Guardian: daily destination briefing + automatic severe-alert, via the real engine and JourneyEvent (2026-08-14)

Portia's spec ("M-Care Weather Guardian") asked for proactive weather alerts across 5 trip stages with practical packing/safety advice, proposing its own new files (`weatherService.js`, `journeyWeatherScheduler.js`, `weatherNotification.js` — the last with its own voice+text+WhatsApp/SMS delivery) and a new `journeyState` object.

Two audits before any code found `base44/functions/checkWeatherAlerts/entry.ts` already a genuine, working weather engine — real Open-Meteo calls (no key), a 27-country coordinate lookup with a live geocoding fallback, real WMO severity classification, and an already-good 4-party (patient/companion/clinic/driver) action-plan generator tailored by heat/cold/rain/procedure-type — just never scheduled, only called on-demand from `WeatherAlertBanner.jsx` on the dashboard. Building `weatherService.js` from scratch would have re-derived all of this. A standalone `weatherNotification.js` would also have directly violated the standing rule reinforced in Phase 50: JourneyEvent is the one proactive-communication layer, and it already gives any new `event_type` chat-bubble rendering, Talk-Mode voice (generic since Phase 45), and automatic push-on-priority (Phase 50) for free.

**Two real, previously-undiscovered bugs found in `checkWeatherAlerts`**: it read `rec.primary_procedure` and `rec.destination_country` to derive a case's context — neither exists on `CaseRecord` (the real fields are `procedures`, a required string array, and `procedure_country`). Every case-driven scan silently fell back to `procedure_type: 'General'`, so the action plan's `isSurgical` branch (extra swelling/inflammation/wound-care advice) never actually fired for a real case — only when a caller passed `procedure_type` explicitly. Also confirmed: `CaseRecord` has no `return_date` field at all, so "is this patient still at their destination" can't be answered precisely.

Asked Portia which of the 5 spec'd triggers to build first, given her own "don't build the whole ladder in one pass" instruction from Phase 50. She chose **daily destination briefing + automatic severe-weather escalation** — one job, since severity (already computed by the real engine) naturally drives both message tone and JourneyEvent priority, collapsing "ongoing daily check" and "real-time severe alert" into a single implementation.

**Shipped**: `base44/shared/weatherEngine.ts` (new) — the real fetch/scoring/advice logic extracted verbatim from `checkWeatherAlerts/entry.ts` (`COUNTRY_COORDS`, `resolveCountry`, `fetchWeather`, `wmoSeverity`/`wmoLabel`, `tempSeverity`, `maxSeverity`, `buildActionPlan`, `buildAlert`, `mockAlert`), so the existing on-demand function and the new scheduled one share one implementation — matching this session's established "extract once, both callers use it" pattern. Added `uv_index_max` to the Open-Meteo `daily` query (one more field, no new dependency), a new `uvSeverity` classifier folded into `buildAlert`'s overall severity computation, and a UV-specific sunscreen/sun-avoidance line in `buildActionPlan` (gated the same way the existing `isSurgical` branch already is) — serves the spec's own UV advice row and this app's existing "sun protection is critical post-cosmetic" framing. New `resolveCaseWeatherContext(rec)` fixes both dead-field bugs in exactly one place (`rec.procedure_country`, `rec.procedures?.[0] || 'General'`), reused by both callers so the fix can't drift out of sync. `checkWeatherAlerts/entry.ts` is now a thin, behavior-preserving wrapper importing from the shared engine — `WeatherAlertBanner.jsx`'s existing response shape is unchanged, its advice quality improves as a side effect of the bug fix.

**New `base44/functions/checkJourneyWeather/entry.ts`** — `createHandler`-wrapped (not the raw-`Deno.serve` pattern its cron siblings still carry as a disclosed deviation), gated with `cronAuthorized(req, base44)` inline exactly like `calculateDriverTrustScore/entry.ts`. Loads cases in `sendTravelCountdownReminders`'s exact `ACTIVE_STATUSES`, reusing its real, proven filter. Scopes to cases whose `departure_date` has passed, within a bounded "still likely at destination" window — `procedure_date + 7 days` (matching the existing recovery-check-in Day-7 checkpoint) when set, else `departure_date + 14 days` as an outer ceiling. **Disclosed limitation, stated in the function's own header comment**: this is a real workaround for the missing `return_date` field, not precise trip tracking. Idempotency needs no new schema — before writing, checks whether a `weather_advisory` JourneyEvent already exists for this case with today's UTC date, fails open (proceeds) if the check itself errors, so a rare duplicate is preferred over silently never briefing. `logJourneyEvent(...)` uses one `event_type` (`weather_advisory`) for every severity — `priority` carries the distinction (`clear→low`, `advisory→medium`, `warning→high`, `critical→critical`), matching the existing `driver_noshow_backup`/`doctor_backup_dispatched` one-type-varying-priority pattern. `high`/`critical` already auto-push per Phase 50 with zero extra code, so the daily scan doubles as the real-time severe-weather alert. `message_text` is built only from real data — the temp/condition line plus the first 1-2 items of the already-computed `action_plan.patient` array; critical severity's `action_plan.patient[0]` already opens in M-Care's own first-person voice ("⚠ Severe weather alert. M is monitoring your situation...") and is reused verbatim rather than rewritten. `JourneyEvent.jsonc` gained `weather_advisory` in its `event_type` enum (additive, no migration). `.github/workflows/safety-cron.yml`'s existing daily 09:00 UTC block gained a `checkJourneyWeather` call alongside `sendTravelCountdownReminders`/`autoCompletePatientJourney` — the same cadence tier those two already use for date-milestone-driven checks, no new cron expression. **No frontend changes** — chat-bubble rendering, Talk Mode voice, and push-on-priority are all already generic over `event_type`/`priority`, proven twice already (Phase 48, Phase 50).

**Deliberately not built this pass, stated plainly**: the spec's other 3 triggers (48h-before packing briefing, departure-morning origin+destination comparison, day-before-procedure advice) — real, buildable follow-ons; the departure-morning trigger specifically needs the patient's *origin* location, unconfirmed to exist anywhere on `CaseRecord`, needing its own audit first. True per-timezone "7am local" delivery — today's cron is a single fixed UTC time; the daily slot is an honest once-a-day approximation, not a per-timezone promise. WhatsApp/SMS backup for critical alerts — consistent with Phase 50's SMS-deferral decision; critical severity does already reach the patient immediately via real push. `/demo/weather` (`WeatherHealthDemo.jsx`) stays a disconnected, disclaimer-labeled mock ("Representative showcase — live engine runs on real patient data") — not rewired to the real engine this pass.

**Verification**: touches a real entity (`JourneyEvent.jsonc`'s enum) and 2 real functions (`checkWeatherAlerts` refactored, `checkJourneyWeather` new) plus a new shared module — **needs a Publish** naming the entity and both functions. Lint clean, typecheck at the same 5-error pre-existing baseline, 642/642 vitest (unchanged — no new pure-logic module needing its own test file), 230/230 redteam (unchanged, including the `BUNDLER: no function directory keeps a local copy of a shared helper` invariant confirming `checkJourneyWeather` imports rather than duplicates), `npm run build` exit 0. Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: whether a real severe-weather day actually produces a `high`/`critical` JourneyEvent with a real push, and whether the bounded departure/procedure-date window correctly stops pinging a case that's genuinely gone home — Portia verifies live after Publishing and the next real daily cron tick.

**Real bug hit immediately after Publish, same day (2026-08-15)**: Portia tested M-Care live by asking about Mexico weather; the tool call showed a green "success" badge but M-Care's own reply said "my live weather service isn't responding right now." Worth being precise about what this was and wasn't: expanding the tool call's raw result showed `{"error":"An internal error occurred."}` — meaning M-Care was telling the truth about a real backend failure, not fabricating an excuse over a result it ignored; Base44's "success" badge apparently reflects the HTTP round-trip completing, not the business-logic outcome, which is what made this look contradictory at a glance. `createHandler.ts`'s catch-all logs the real error to a `ReliabilityIncident` record on every unhandled exception — looking that up gave the real cause: `Error: Open-Meteo 429` (`fetchWeather`'s own `throw new Error(\`Open-Meteo ${res.status}\`)` on a non-OK response) — a genuine Open-Meteo rate-limit response, most likely from the day's own repeated manual testing of `checkWeatherAlerts`/`checkJourneyWeather` during publish verification, not a code defect. `fetchWeather` had zero retry/resilience — a single 429 failed the whole call outright. This is exactly the gap Portia's original spec asked for ("cache results for 6 hours to avoid redundant API calls") that Phase 51 deliberately scoped out as unnecessary for the daily-cron slice — today's live testing proved it worth closing sooner, at a smaller/safer scope than a full persistent cache.

**Fixed**: `fetchWeather` now retries once, after a 1.5s delay, specifically on a 429 — a second consecutive failure still throws (an honest "couldn't fetch" narration over a genuine outage, never a silent infinite retry or fabricated data). New `createWeatherCache()` in `weatherEngine.ts` returns a per-invocation memoizing wrapper around `fetchWeather`, keyed by rounded lat/lng — wired into `checkJourneyWeather`'s daily scan loop and `checkWeatherAlerts`'s `scan_all` admin action, both of which can process many cases in one run that share the same destination country (`COUNTRY_COORDS` maps one country to one fixed coordinate pair), so those batch paths now call Open-Meteo once per *destination* scanned that run instead of once per *patient* — directly reducing both real API usage and 429 risk. Scoped to the caller's own local `Map`, cleared every fresh invocation — never a cross-request cache, so it can't ever serve stale weather. The single-case `scan` action (the one M-Care's chat and the dashboard banner actually call) is unchanged apart from the retry — a lone lookup doesn't benefit from in-run memoization.

**Deliberately not built this pass**: a persistent, cross-invocation cache with a real TTL (Portia's original "6 hours" ask) — `base44/shared/freshness.ts`'s existing TTL/review-queue pattern (clinic status, doctor license, visa data) is the natural model to extend if 429s keep recurring under real, non-testing traffic; needs its own entity/schema decision, not squeezed into a live-debugging fix. Not investigated: whether Open-Meteo's free-tier limit is genuinely being approached by Morales' own real volume, or whether Base44's functions share an egress IP pool with other tenants whose traffic also counts against it — unconfirmable from this checkout, worth asking Open-Meteo support directly if 429s recur without an obvious burst of manual testing to explain them.

**Verification**: touches the same shared module + 2 functions as the original Phase 51 build — **needs a Publish** naming `weatherEngine.ts`, `checkJourneyWeather/entry.ts`, and `checkWeatherAlerts/entry.ts` again. Lint clean, typecheck at the same 5-error pre-existing baseline, 642/642 vitest, 230/230 redteam (unchanged — no entity/permission surface touched), `npm run build` exit 0. Live-only: whether the retry actually resolves a transient 429 in practice, and whether the in-run cache measurably reduces repeat-country calls on a real multi-patient day — Portia verifies live after publishing.

## M-Care super-agent, Phase 52 — proactive destination check: weather + recent events, the moment a country is named, no one has to ask (2026-08-14)

Portia's ask, live in conversation: M-Care should do an automatic background check on weather and recent natural-disaster/safety events the moment a user names or chooses a country — "I want a medical procedure [there] — ok, let's check the weather first," without the traveler ever having to ask. Audited `base44/agents/m_care.jsonc` directly (raw file + parsed JSON) before touching anything, same discipline as every prior phase.

**Two things confirmed by the audit**: (1) `checkWeatherAlerts`, `getDestinationSafetyIndex`, `recallMcareKnowledge`, and `mcareResearchAndLearn` are all already granted in `tool_configs` (91 entries, unchanged) — exactly the live tool set Portia's own recent screenshots showed firing correctly (the Mexico weather/UV check, the Colombia earthquake research-and-learn). This needed zero new tool grants — a pure instructions change, near-zero Publish-order risk. (2) The real gap: everything today is reactive and late, and MEDICAL journeys have no weather/safety wiring at all. `checkWeatherAlerts`/`getDestinationSafetyIndex` were referenced in exactly two places in the whole instructions text — BYOJ flow step 7 ("WEATHER + INSURANCE") and LEISURE flow step 8 ("WEATHER + DESTINATION SAFETY") — both firing only after `BOOKING`/`CONFIRMED`, during travel-coordination, and only if the flow naturally reached that stage. The `PROCEDURE INQUIRY` protocol (the block that already fires "the moment a procedure is mentioned," by its own existing design) had zero mention of destination/weather/safety. The standard MEDICAL journey stage list never called either tool at any point — so Portia's own example scenario had no proactive check at all before this pass.

**Shipped, one new rule, flow-agnostic — not three separate edits to the MEDICAL/BYOJ/LEISURE stage lists.** `RULE 20 — PROACTIVE DESTINATION CHECK`, inserted right after Rule 19 (the prior highest-numbered rule), same cross-cutting-behavioral-rule shape as Rule 13 (PROFESSIONAL JUDGMENT) and Rule 19 itself — layers on top of whichever stage list is active rather than being wired into each flow's own stage enumeration, so one safely-anchored text insertion covers all three journey types instead of three riskier edits at different byte offsets. The rule: the first time in a conversation a candidate destination country is named or selected — a one-shot booking parse, an intake answer, a casual mention, or an explicit choice — say so plainly in one short sentence, then call `checkWeatherAlerts` and `getDestinationSafetyIndex` for that country right away (country alone is enough, no travel dates needed yet). Also call `recallMcareKnowledge` for recent notable safety events or natural disasters in that country, falling through to `mcareResearchAndLearn` on a miss — this is the piece that actually covers something specific and recent like an earthquake, which `getDestinationSafetyIndex`'s own static score doesn't reliably surface (confirmed by the live Colombia session: the safety-index call returned a clean 90/100 and it was the separate recall/research pair that surfaced the real August earthquake). Narrate the real results plainly, never invent a concern the tools didn't surface, and say so plainly if there's nothing notable rather than manufacturing one. Do this once per candidate country per conversation, not on every repeat mention. Explicit closing clause, matching every other rule's own pattern: this is added context, never a gate — never confused with the real SAFE-T/RED safety decision in Rule 1.

**Encoding gotcha, same as Phase 41/42**: raw-byte probe confirmed the region right after Rule 19 stores newlines as the literal two-character `\n` escape and em dashes as literal `—` escapes in the raw file bytes, not actual UTF-8 bytes. Anchored a Python raw-byte string-replace on the literal sequence `anything further.\n\nCLICK-AND-PAY PRINCIPLE:` (confirmed exactly one occurrence before writing), and wrote the new rule in plain ASCII (hyphens, no em dashes, no smart quotes) to sidestep the escape convention entirely — same workaround as those two phases. `python3 -c "json.load(...)"` confirmed valid before and after, `tool_configs` count confirmed unchanged at 91 before writing to disk.

**Deliberately not built this pass**: no change to the MEDICAL/BYOJ/LEISURE stage lists themselves — Rule 20 covers all three without touching them, so their existing step-7/step-8 wording stays as reactive backup, not removed. No JourneyEvent/backend change — this is a live, in-conversation, agent-initiated tool call inside the same turn the user is already looking at, unlike JourneyEvent's cron-driven proactive-messaging problem (Phase 45's finding was specifically that a cron job *can't* inject a message into a live conversation — that constraint doesn't apply here, since the agent is already mid-turn). No new caching/rate-limit change beyond what Phase 51 already shipped (per-run destination caching + 429 retry) — worth watching whether this new proactive trigger increases real Open-Meteo call volume enough to matter, not pre-emptively addressed here.

**Verification**: text-only edit to `m_care.jsonc`'s `instructions` field — needs a **Publish** naming `m_care.jsonc` explicitly. Lint clean, typecheck at the same 5-error pre-existing baseline, 642/642 vitest (unchanged), 230/230 redteam (unchanged — no entity/tool/permission surface touched), `npm run build` exit 0. Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: whether M-Care actually proactively fires this the moment a country comes up in a real conversation without being asked, and whether "once per country per conversation" actually prevents annoying repetition — Portia verifies live by naming a destination early in a fresh conversation.

## M-Care evolution toward "E.V." (Spider-Man: Brand New Day, 2026), Phase 1 — Silent Guardian's am/pm check-in was never wired to anything, client or server

Portia asked M-Care to evolve toward E.V. — Peter Parker's suit AI in *Spider-Man: Brand New Day* (real, sourced research: WebSearch, since this postdates training knowledge) — specifically its trait of noticing *him*, not just outside threats. Two parallel audits of this codebase's real state (not assumption) found the "Silent Guardian" 4-layer system (`useContextAwareSafety.js` + `useTimeOfDay`/`useActivityDetection`/`useGuardianMode`) already exists, already has real backend functions (`guardianCheckIn`, `guardianEscalation`), and already renders a "Guardian Mode active · Check-in due" banner on `Dashboard.jsx` — but going deeper than the first audit found something more foundational than "not scheduled server-side": **the backend was never called by anything, ever, client or server.** `Dashboard.jsx`'s "I'm Safe ✓" button only calls `acknowledgeCheckIn()` — a pure client-side state reset — never `triggerCheckIn()`, the function that would actually invoke `guardianCheckIn` and send a real SMS. Opting into Guardian Mode changed what a banner said and nothing else.

**Deliberately scoped down, and why.** `guardianEscalation`'s 3-strike GPS-silence cascade was NOT wired up this pass — reusing it server-side would duplicate a system that's already real and already scheduled on this same cron file's 15-minute tier: `scheduleSoloCheckIns` + `escalateSoloCheckIn`'s 2h/3h/5h/9h ladder already covers "this traveler has gone silent" for every active traveler, not just Guardian-opted-in ones. Building a second, parallel GPS-silence monitor would be two systems watching the same thing. Whether Guardian's escalation half should be retired in favor of the SoloCheckIn ladder, or reconciled with it, is a real, separate product decision — flagged, not resolved here. This pass covers only the genuinely distinct half: the routine 9am/9pm "still doing okay?" check-in.

**Shipped**: `base44/shared/sendGuardianCheckIn.ts` (new) — the real check-in send/gate logic, extracted verbatim from what used to be inline in `guardianCheckIn/entry.ts` (opt-in gate, group-travel skip, sleep-hour gate, Twilio SMS, audit log), so the client-facing endpoint and the new sweep below share exactly one implementation rather than risking two that quietly drift. `guardianCheckIn/entry.ts` now just fetches nothing itself — it delegates straight to the shared helper, same request/response contract, same `requireAuth: true`.

`base44/functions/runGuardianCheckInSweep/entry.ts` (new, `createHandler` + inline `cronAuthorized`, matching `calculateDriverTrustScore`'s established shape) — the real fix. Finds active CaseRecords (`sendTravelCountdownReminders`'s exact `ACTIVE_STATUSES` set) joined to their `Consultation` via the real `consultation_id` FK, filtered to `guardian_mode_opted_in: true`. **Timezone problem solved by reuse, not new infrastructure**: `Consultation` has no stored timezone field, so the sweep calls `weatherEngine.ts`'s already-live `resolveCountry`/`fetchWeather`/`createWeatherCache` (Open-Meteo's `timezone=auto` already returns a real `utc_offset_seconds` per destination, previously only consumed for weather severity) to get a genuine per-destination UTC offset — an honest approximation (assumes the traveler keeps local time at their destination), not invented geo-infrastructure, and unresolvable countries are skipped rather than guessed at. Fires at local hour 9 (am) or 21/9pm (pm, solo-only, matching `useGuardianMode.js`'s own `PM_CHECK_HOUR`), deduped per local calendar day via two new `Consultation` fields (`guardian_last_am_checkin_date`/`guardian_last_pm_checkin_date`) rather than a tight time window — cron's 15-minute granularity means whichever tick lands first in the target hour sends it, once. Every real send also logs a `JourneyEvent` (`guardian_checkin_sent`, new enum value, `priority: 'low'` — routine, not urgent) so the check-in is felt inside M-Care's own chat, not just a bare SMS — directly serving "notices the traveler" as a felt presence, not just a text message. Wired into `safety-cron.yml`'s 15-minute life-safety tier and the daily deployment-audit block, matching every other real-but-previously-unscheduled function this project has fixed the same way.

**Redteam**: `runGuardianCheckInSweep` added to the existing `JOURNEY EVENTS` invariant's writer list (must call `logJourneyEvent(`, never a raw `entities.JourneyEvent.create`) — no new invariant needed, the auto-discovery `AUTH`/`BUNDLER` sweeps already cover any new function directory automatically.

**Verification**: touches 2 real entities (`Consultation.jsonc`'s 2 new fields, `JourneyEvent.jsonc`'s new enum value) and 2 real functions (`guardianCheckIn` refactored, `runGuardianCheckInSweep` new) plus a new shared module — needs a **Publish** naming all four. Lint clean, typecheck at the same 5-error pre-existing baseline, 642/642 vitest, 230/230 redteam (1 test extended, not new), `npm run build` exit 0. Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: whether a real opted-in traveler actually receives the 9am/9pm SMS at the right local time, and whether the Open-Meteo-derived UTC offset holds up as an honest approximation across a full day — Portia verifies live after Publishing and the next real cron tick.

**Real bug caught by the Publish step itself, fixed same day**: `runGuardianCheckInSweep/entry.ts`'s own header JSDoc comment included a literal cron-schedule reference (`*/15 * * * *`) as descriptive prose — the `*/` inside it closed the JSDoc block early, turning the rest of the comment into bare code and failing to bundle. Base44's assistant caught this correctly during publish (published the other 4 files verbatim and successfully, flagged this one specifically rather than silently guessing at a fix). Reworded the line to describe the schedule in prose ("runs every 15 minutes via `.github/workflows/safety-cron.yml`'s life-safety tier") instead of embedding the literal cron syntax, avoiding the character sequence entirely rather than trying to escape it inside a comment. A concurrent `base44-builder[bot]` sync landed on `main` in the meantime (re-serializing `Consultation.jsonc` — em dashes to `—` escapes, JSONC comments stripped, arrays expanded — and stripping trailing newlines from both function files); merged cleanly, full gate re-confirmed green, pushed (`ceb421b4`). **Needs a re-Publish** naming `runGuardianCheckInSweep/entry.ts` specifically before this function is actually live.

## M-Care evolution toward "E.V.", Phase 2 — distress detection now scans typed chat, not just live voice

Second slice of the E.V. arc (see Phase 1 above). `src/lib/distressDetection.js`'s `detectDistressSignal()` was already real, pure, and unit-tested — but its only caller anywhere in the repo was the Conversational Mode continuous-recognition callback in `MCareOrb.jsx`, meaning it only ever saw text if the patient had voice mode on and spoke. The much larger population who type never got any distress check at all, regardless of what they typed.

**Shipped**: `sendAgentMessage` (`MCareOrb.jsx`, the single real send path for every typed message, transcribed voice note, and non-command voice-mode result) now runs the exact same `detectDistressSignal(q)` check the voice path already uses, placed before the deterministic voice-command layer and before the message ever reaches the real agent conversation — same discipline as the voice path's own comment: "scan BEFORE treating it as a normal message." A hit calls the same `handleDistressSignal` handler the voice path already uses (calm welfare prompt, 30s-debounced, audit-logged, speaks it if Talk Mode is on) and returns early — the distress text itself is never sent to the booking/coordination agent as a normal question, matching the voice path's own reasoning that sending a cry for help to the agent as a question would be the wrong response to it. `handleDistressSignal` needed no changes — it's already generic over any `(signal, text)` pair regardless of source.

**Deliberately not built this pass**: no change to `MCareAgent.jsx` (the separate, login-gated `/m-care` full-page surface) — same "`MCareOrb` first, parity later" scoping this project has used for every prior voice/safety feature (Talk Mode, Conversational Mode, voice notes). No sentiment/tone inference beyond the existing literal keyword/phrase matching — still exact-cry and word-boundary-phrase only, same as the voice path; true stress-without-explicit-words detection remains out of scope, flagged in Phase 1's own "deliberately not scoped for tomorrow" list.

**Verification**: pure frontend change, no entity/function/agent-config touched — **no Base44 publish needed**. Lint clean, typecheck at the same 5-error pre-existing baseline (`MCareOrb.jsx` carries its own `@ts-nocheck`), 642/642 vitest (unchanged — `detectDistressSignal` itself wasn't modified, so its existing unit tests still cover the detection logic; the new call site isn't independently unit-tested, consistent with `MCareOrb.jsx`'s established precedent of being verified by full-suite/structural checks rather than component tests), 230/230 redteam (unchanged — no permission/entity surface touched), `npm run build` exit 0. Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: whether a typed distress phrase actually triggers the welfare prompt in a real session, and whether the interception correctly stops the message from reaching the real agent — Portia verifies live by typing (not speaking) a phrase like "I'm scared" into M-Care's chat.

## M-Care evolution toward "E.V.", Phases 3 & 4 — Portia delegated the two remaining calls ("make it like E.V., do whatever you have to")

Two decisions from Phase 1/2's writeup were flagged as needing her input rather than being silently assumed: whether Talk Mode should go default-on, and how far to take real-time delivery. Her answer delegated both — treated as "use good judgment," not "skip it," so both are decided here with reasoning stated, not silently flipped.

**Decision A — Talk Mode default: not changed, and here's why.** Reversing Phase 17's opt-in-only choice for every user everywhere (including a first-time anonymous visitor on a public page) reopens exactly the risk that decision existed to prevent — unsolicited audio in a quiet or shared setting. Re-checked the code rather than assume: `MCareOrb.jsx`'s `toggleConversationalMode` already does `if (next) setTalkMode(true)` when live voice conversation turns on ("a silent phone call makes no sense") — the one moment default-on voice actually makes sense (the user just explicitly asked for voice input) was already handled correctly, confirmed unchanged. Ordinary text chat stays quiet-by-default. No code change here.

**Decision B — real-time scope: `JourneyEvent` only.** `SoloCheckIn` has its own UI surface and its own polling that hasn't been audited — extending scope there under a "do whatever it takes" instruction would be scope creep dressed up as thoroughness, not the judgment that instruction actually asked for. `JourneyEvent` is the concrete, already-identified gap: a real, unused-for-this-entity WebSocket path (`base44.entities.<Entity>.subscribe()`) already proven in production for `SOSEvent` (`AdminSosSyncMonitor.jsx`) and `CaseMessage`/`DigitalHandshake` (`HeartNotificationCenter.jsx`) — the same channel type M-Care's own chat-turn streaming already uses.

**Shipped — Slice 3, real-time `JourneyEvent` delivery**: `src/hooks/useJourneyEvents.js`'s 20-second poll (unchanged, kept as a safety net) is now paired with a `useEffect` that calls `base44.entities.JourneyEvent.subscribe(callback)`, matching `AdminSosSyncMonitor.jsx`'s exact pattern — on a `create`/`update` event whose `case_id` matches, merges it straight into the React Query cache (`queryClient.setQueryData`) instead of waiting for the next poll tick. Can only make delivery faster, never less reliable — a missed socket event still gets caught by the poll. Return shape unchanged, so `MCareOrb.jsx` needed zero changes. Pure frontend — no Base44 publish needed.

**Shipped — Slice 4, a real character/tone pass**: new **`RULE 21 — VOICE AND CHARACTER`** appended to `m_care.jsonc`'s instructions (same anchor-verified, plain-ASCII edit discipline as Rule 20 and every prior addition — raw-byte probe confirmed the region still stores `\n` and em dashes as literal escape sequences, so the new rule uses hyphens, not em dashes, to avoid needing to replicate that convention). Fills a real, previously-empty gap: Rules 8/17 govern identity and disclosure, but nothing governed how M-Care actually *talks* — the new rule asks for brief, warm, present-tense delivery when it's actually doing something real ("I'm watching the weather for you," never generic filler, and never said unless true, per Rule 3), calm rather than falsely cheerful under a real safety flag, and is explicit that this is delivery only — every other rule, especially Rule 3's no-invented-data discipline and Rule 8/17's identity rules, still fully applies. Zero `tool_configs` change (91 before and after, verified) — no new Publish-order risk beyond the standard instructions edit.

**Verification**: Slice 3 — lint clean, typecheck at the same 5-error baseline, 642/642 vitest, 230/230 redteam, `npm run build` exit 0, no publish needed. Slice 4 — `python3` JSON-validity + unchanged-`tool_configs`-count check before/after, same full gate green, needs a **Publish** naming `m_care.jsonc`. Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: whether JourneyEvent bubbles now genuinely arrive near-instantly instead of up to 20s late, and whether the new tone guidance actually reads as more alive without drifting into inventing anything — Portia verifies live.

**Real bug caught by Base44's own publish step, fixed same day**: `runGuardianCheckInSweep/entry.ts`'s own header JSDoc comment (Phase 1) included a literal cron-schedule reference (`*/15 * * * *`) as descriptive prose — the `*/` inside it closed the JSDoc block early, turning the rest of the comment into bare code and failing to bundle. Base44's assistant caught this correctly during publish (published the other 4 outstanding files verbatim and successfully, flagged only this one rather than silently guessing at a fix). Reworded the line to describe the schedule in prose instead of embedding the literal cron syntax, avoiding the character sequence entirely rather than trying to escape it inside a comment. A concurrent `base44-builder[bot]` sync landed on `main` in the meantime (re-serializing `Consultation.jsonc` — em dashes to unicode escapes, JSONC comments stripped, arrays expanded — and stripping trailing newlines from both function files); merged cleanly, full gate re-confirmed green. **The general lesson, worth remembering**: a literal `*/` inside prose text anywhere inside a `/** ... */` JSDoc block will always break the parser — never write a raw cron expression, a closing-tag-like sequence, or any other `*/`-containing string inside a doc comment; describe it in words instead.

## M-Care evolution toward "E.V." — auditing a pasted "protective agent" prompt (2026-08-15)

Portia pasted a full candidate system-prompt for M-Care ("You are M-Care... Protective agent with authority to slow, redirect, or stop the process...") and asked for an audit against the real, live `m_care.jsonc` before changing anything — what's missing, what's already covered, what's worth adding. Did the audit directly (grep + targeted reads against the real 102KB `instructions` field and the real 91-entry `tool_configs` list), not from memory.

**Finding: almost the entire pasted prompt already exists, under different rule names.** "Protective first, authority to stop" already matches the opening identity paragraph's "willing to say 'No, I can't proceed until this is safe/verified'"; its safety/consent/no-invented-data rules already match RULE 1 (SAFETY GATE) / RULE 2 (CONSENT GATE) / RULE 3 (NO INVENTED DATA); its "Patient Profile -> Safety Check Result -> Why -> Next Step -> Reasoning Trace" template already matches RULE 1's own reasoning-order paragraph from Phase 42; its "narrate tool actions in the present tense" already matches RULE 21 (Phase 4, this arc); its "refuse with care, warmth never softens a refusal" is already stated near RULE 13. **The one genuine, confirmed gap**: its EDUCATIONAL MEDIA section (offer a diagram/video, call a `fetch_educational_media` tool) has zero backing anywhere — grepped "educational" across the full instructions: 0 hits; no such tool exists in the real 91-entry `tool_configs`; no content source (image/video library, CMS, vendor) exists anywhere in this app. Asked Portia directly; she chose to defer it — same standing discipline as every other vendor-gated capability in this project (Tavily, ExchangeRate-API, biometric liveness SDK): don't build or reference a tool without a real content source behind it first.

**Decision, stated not silently made**: did not copy the pasted prompt's redundant sections (its HARD RULES 1-4, SAFETY REASONING STANDARD, most of TONE) into the instructions verbatim — they restate what RULE 1/2/3/13/21 already say, and the file is already 102KB single-line JSON that had already caused one real parse bug this same day (the JSDoc `*/` collision above) from a careless addition; duplicating settled content adds real edit-risk for zero functional gain.

**Shipped**: one small, additive, text-only preamble block inserted right after the opening identity paragraph and before `ABSOLUTE RULES (never violate, never soften):` — a **PRIORITY ORDER** statement (safety > tool-grounded accuracy > logged consent > moving the journey forward, higher priority always wins on conflict) formalizing what the existing rule ordering already implies, paired with a short "board-certified clinician would pause" calibration line for how much caution to show in narration — explicitly scoped as a narration calibration, not license for independent clinical judgment, and explicitly deferring to RULE 1/RULE 13 in all cases. Same anchor-verified, plain-ASCII, byte-level safe-edit discipline as every prior `m_care.jsonc` change (confirmed this exact region still stores `\n` as the literal two-character escape) — `tool_configs` count confirmed unchanged at 91 before and after.

**Verification**: lint clean, typecheck at the same pre-existing baseline (`.jsonc` isn't in tsc's scope), `npm run build` exit 0, 642/642 vitest, 230/230 redteam — unchanged, no entity/tool/permission surface touched. Needs a **Publish** naming `m_care.jsonc`. Educational Media stays a named, flagged gap — not built, not referenced in instructions, ready to revisit once Portia has a real content source to name.

## M-Care evolution toward "E.V." — location-aware and offline-prepared (2026-08-15)

Portia sent two real screenshots of a live M-Safe session: the user had to type "I am in trinidad close to the airport" before M-Care could look anything up, `searchNearbyPlaces` failed and it silently reached for a different search path, and only after the user explicitly said "I am going offline to create QR code" did M-Care produce one. Her verdict: "M-Care is not a chatbot" — it should already know where the traveler is, and should sense connectivity risk and prepare an offline-safe artifact before being asked. She then pasted a full "autonomous journey coordinator" doctrine (OBSERVE -> REASON -> CHECK AUTHORITY -> ACT -> VERIFY -> INFORM -> AUDIT, a 7-point action-authorization checklist, a formal audit-record schema) with an explicit instruction: audit the real repo first, do not rebuild existing systems, report before changing code, implement only the smallest safe vertical slice.

**Audit, before any code**: cross-checked the doctrine's example trigger list against real, currently-scheduled functions rather than assuming. Most of it already exists, just never named "OBSERVE/REASON/ACT" — travel-date reminders (`sendTravelCountdownReminders`), doctor-not-confirmed fallback (`checkPartnerSLABreaches`/`detectFallbackCrisis`), driver-no-show fallback (`escalateMissedDriverHandshake`), procedure-completed recovery check-ins (`schedulePostOpCheckIns`), provider discovery (`discoverProviderCandidates`, Phase 36), destination-safety proactive checks (`checkJourneyWeather` + RULE 20), and the whole `JourneyEvent` proactive-communication layer (Phases 45-51) are all real and already running. The requested audit-record schema (`action/reason/journey_id/timestamp/trigger/tools_used/result/authorization_level/patient_notified`) is close to but not identical to `logMcarePerformance`'s real hash-chained write (`case_id`/`stage_from`/`stage_to`/`outcome`/`notes`) — flagged as a future additive-fields option, not needed for this slice. The doctrine's 7-point authorization check is already enforced structurally per-tool throughout `m_care.jsonc` (Rule 1/2/13, the gated-creation-function split on every partner entity) — a prior session (Phase 30) already ran nearly this exact audit for a "journey execution engine" ask and found the same thing. Building a second, parallel "authorization_level" framework on top would be exactly the "rebuild existing systems" the doctrine itself says not to do.

**Two real, confirmed gaps, not fixed by more instructions text alone**: (1) M-Care has zero automatic location context — `src/hooks/useAutoLocation.js` already exists (real, privacy-conscious: IP location on every mount with no permission needed, GPS upgrade only if explicitly granted, never caches precise coordinates) but was used nowhere in `MCareOrb.jsx`. The obvious fix — grant `getGeolocationAndCurrency` directly to the agent as a tool — would not have worked: that function reads the IP from the calling *request's* own headers, and an agent-initiated tool call comes from Base44's own backend infrastructure, not the traveler's browser, so the agent would have resolved its own server's IP, not the patient's. Location has to be captured client-side, where `useAutoLocation` already does it correctly. (2) `MCareOrb.jsx` already tracks `navigator.onLine` (confirmed real, `isOnline` state + `online`/`offline` listeners) but only used it to disable the input box — nothing proactively prepared anything before a real disconnect, and RULE 16 (the QR rule) is explicitly reactive-only by design ("use it only when the traveler needs to hand navigation to someone else... not for every address you mention"). Also confirmed: the "web_search" fallback visible in Portia's screenshot isn't a tool this repo built or grants — grepped the whole instructions/tool_configs for the literal string, zero hits — it's a Base44 platform-native capability, same category as `voice_config`/`whatsapp_greeting`, not something to "fix" here.

**Shipped — two small, additive slices, zero new backend functions, zero new vendor:**

- **Location context, always confirmed before being trusted.** `src/lib/locationContext.js` (new) — `buildLocationContextBlock(loc)`, a pure, unit-tested function (`tests/locationContext.test.js`) that turns `useAutoLocation`'s real location object into a `[[LOCATION_CONTEXT: city=X, country=Y, lat=A, lng=B, source=ip_approximate|gps_precise]]` block, or `null` if there's nothing real to say — explicitly refuses to build a block from `useAutoLocation`'s own honest all-fields-null fallback (`country: 'Unknown'`, the shape `getGeolocationAndCurrency` returns when every geo provider failed) so a non-hit can never be presented as if it were real. `MCareOrb.jsx` now calls `useAutoLocation()` and prepends this block to the first real message sent per mount (tracked via a new `locationContextSentRef`, reset on `startNewJourney` too, since a fresh journey may start somewhere new) — same token-stripping convention this file already uses for `{{maps:...}}`/`{{qr:...}}`/`{{choices:...}}`: `MessageBubble.jsx` gained a matching `extractLocationContext()`, wired first in the existing extraction chain, so the block is never shown to the user as raw text, only their own typed words. A new instruction paragraph in `m_care.jsonc` (positioned right after RULE 12, the maps rule — the natural place to look for "how does M-Care get location") tells the agent this is an auto-detected *approximate* hint it may use to skip asking "where are you," but must always state back in plain language for a one-line confirmation before treating as exact for anything address- or emergency-sensitive — explicitly citing `getGeolocationAndCurrency`'s own documented past bug (misidentifying Trinidad and Tobago IPs as Venezuelan) as the reason this can never be silently trusted. GPS stays exactly as permission-gated as `useAutoLocation` already has it — only the IP-approximate reading became automatic.
- **Proactive offline-prep, two honest halves.** RULE 16 gained one new sentence: M-Care now proactively offers a QR code the moment the traveler signals real connectivity risk in their own words ("heading somewhere with no signal," "about to go offline," "going somewhere remote") *and* a real address is already on file for their case — same "real data only, never a placeholder" discipline the rule already had, just triggered earlier. Separately, and more honestly given a cloud agent can't react to an *actual* disconnect (no network, no tool call can succeed): `src/lib/offlinePrep.js` (new) — `findLastMapOrQrToken(messages)`, a pure, unit-tested function (`tests/offlinePrep.test.js`) that scans conversation history backward for the most recent `{{qr:...}}`/`{{maps:...}}` token M-Care already emitted. `MCareOrb.jsx`'s already-real `offline` event listener now derives `offlineArtifact` from it and renders a small banner the instant the browser actually goes offline, reusing `MessageBubble.jsx`'s own `InlineQrBlock` (newly exported, not duplicated) to re-render the last real QR/maps destination — a pure client-side re-render of data the app already has, never a new network call. If nothing was ever generated this session, the banner says so plainly rather than fabricating one.

**Verification**: touches `m_care.jsonc` (text-only, two additive paragraphs, `tool_configs` confirmed unchanged at 91) plus 4 frontend files (`MCareOrb.jsx`, `MessageBubble.jsx`, and the two new `src/lib/` modules) — needs a **Publish** naming `m_care.jsonc` only, the frontend pieces ship with the normal deploy. Lint clean, typecheck at the same pre-existing baseline, 655/655 vitest (13 new), 230/230 redteam (unchanged — no entity/tool/permission surface touched), `npm run build` exit 0. Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: whether the auto-detected location is accurate enough in practice to be useful before confirmation, and whether the offline banner actually appears the instant a real connection drops on a real device — Portia verifies live after publishing.

## M-Care evolution toward "E.V." — Educational Media, the real slice (images + text, no video) (2026-08-15)

Portia re-asked whether M-Care has the EDUCATIONAL MEDIA capability from her pasted "protective agent" prompt — the same E.V.-in-Spider-Man framing, an agent that brings up diagrams/video to educate the user on what they're trying to accomplish. The earlier priority-order audit had already found "zero backing anywhere" for this — correct for the backend (`m_care.jsonc`: zero hits for "educational" in the instructions, no such tool in `tool_configs`, and a fresh grep this pass for `Core.GenerateImage`/`Core.GenerateVideo` across the whole repo: zero hits, confirming no Base44 image/video-generation primitive is used anywhere) — but that audit only ever checked the backend agent config, never the frontend.

**New finding this pass**: `src/components/procedures/ProcedureData.jsx` already has a real, hosted illustrative image for every one of its ~69 procedures (`media.base44.com/images/public/.../generated_image.png`), each paired with real `desc`/`whatToExpect`/`benefits` text, already used today on `/procedures` and `ProcedureModal.jsx` — and it already exports `searchProcedures(query)`, a working fuzzy-matched, scored (`>= 55`), sorted lookup built on the same `fuzzyScore` this app uses everywhere else. Asked Portia directly whether to ship this real, partial slice (images + text, no video, no "more graphic" tier — there's exactly one image per procedure) or hold off for a full vendor-backed vision; she chose to ship the real slice now.

**Shipped**: a new `{{media:PROCEDURE_NAME}}` token, following the exact same pattern `{{maps:...}}`/`{{qr:...}}` already use — the agent names what it's already discussing, the client resolves it against real, already-existing data, never something AI-generated or invented on the fly. `MessageBubble.jsx` gained `extractMedia()` (same shape as `extractQr`/`extractMaps`, wired into the existing extraction chain) and a new `ProcedureMediaCard({ query })` component (styled like the existing `InlineQrBlock`) that calls `searchProcedures(query)` and renders the top real match's image + title + description, or an honest "I don't have a diagram for that yet" line if nothing clears the lookup's own threshold — never a broken or fabricated image. New **RULE 22 — EDUCATIONAL MEDIA** in `m_care.jsonc` (text-only, appended after the existing PAYMENT INTEGRITY paragraph at the true end of the instructions field — same byte-level safe-edit discipline as every prior addition, this region's raw bytes confirmed via probe before writing): explain in plain language first, media is additive and never a substitute for RULE 1's real reasoning trace, offer once, only emit the token after an explicit yes, and — stated plainly so the agent can never overclaim — there is no video capability and no separate "more graphic" tier, every image is the same mild, non-graphic illustration regardless of what's asked.

**What this does NOT do, stated plainly**: no video (nothing in this app can generate or fetch one); no graphic-content escalation tier (only one image per procedure exists, already mild by construction); no coverage beyond the ~69 procedures already in `ProcedureData.jsx`'s catalog — anything outside that gets the honest empty-state card, never an invented image.

**Verification**: `MessageBubble.jsx` change is frontend-only, no publish needed for that half; `m_care.jsonc`'s RULE 22 needs a **Publish** naming that file, `tool_configs` confirmed unchanged at 91. Lint clean, typecheck at the same pre-existing baseline, 655/655 vitest (unchanged — `searchProcedures`/`ProcedureData.jsx` are pre-existing, already-exercised code, and `extractMedia`'s own regex-stripping isn't independently unit-tested, consistent with `MessageBubble.jsx`'s established precedent of no dedicated test file), 230/230 redteam (unchanged — no entity/tool/permission surface touched), `npm run build` exit 0. Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: whether the rendered image/caption actually looks right inline in the chat panel, and whether M-Care reliably explains first and only offers once — Portia verifies live after publishing.

## M-Care evolution toward "E.V." — a real doctor's own media, and an explicit "want to do this?" ask (2026-08-15)

Direct follow-up to the Educational Media slice above. Portia's framing: "M-care should put a video and pictures how it would look... push them to make the right choices" — the E.V.-from-Spider-Man idea again, this time asking specifically for video and a personalized preview ("how it would look"), stated as "I know it's possible."

Two parallel audits (video infrastructure; before/after or simulation infrastructure) confirmed neither exists anywhere in this app, matching and extending RULE 22's own honest disclaimer: the only real video is one generic, watermarked Storyblocks placeholder clip on the homepage hero (`LuxuryHero.jsx`) — not procedure content, and no `Core.GenerateVideo`/`Core.GenerateImage` use anywhere. No before/after pairing, no virtual-try-on or plastic-surgery-simulation API, no personalized preview of any kind. `ProcedureData.jsx`'s images (wired into chat last session) stay the only real still image — one static, same-for-every-user illustration per procedure.

**One real, previously-unwired asset was found while chasing a promising lead**: `Doctor.portfolio` (`Doctor.jsonc`) is a real schema field — an array of `{url, type: 'image'|'video', title}` items a doctor can already upload via `DoctorPortfolio.jsx` (a real, working `<video controls autoPlay>` player already exists there) — and it's already shown publicly to patients today on `ProviderDetail.jsx`, so surfacing it in M-Care's chat is not a new privacy exposure, just a new place the same public data appears. `matchDoctorsForProcedure` (the tool M-Care already calls to recommend a doctor) never returned this field, so M-Care had no way to know it existed. Confirmed likely empty for most doctors today — real infrastructure, not fabricated, but honestly likely to render nothing until doctors actually use the upload widget.

Presented the full audit to Portia, including what's genuinely not buildable today (true personalized "how would I look" simulation, true procedure-explainer video — both need a real vendor decision, the same class of gap as Tavily/ExchangeRate-API elsewhere in this app). She chose to ship both real slices.

**Shipped**:
- **`base44/functions/matchDoctorsForProcedure/entry.ts`**: added `portfolio: Array.isArray(doc.portfolio) ? doc.portfolio : []` to the mapped response, alongside the existing `photo_url`/`rating`/verification fields. One-line, additive, defaults to `[]` so "nothing to show" is an explicit signal, not a missing field.
- **`src/components/mcare-agent/MessageBubble.jsx`**: new `extractDoctorMedia()` (same shape as `extractMedia`) parsing a new `{{doctormedia:DOCTOR_NAME|TYPE|URL}}` token — URL must be one the agent actually received from a real `matchDoctorsForProcedure` result this turn, never invented. New `DoctorMediaCard` renders a real `<img>` or `<video controls>` (matching `type`), captioned "From Dr. X's own portfolio" — deliberately never falls back to the generic procedure stock image if the doctor's own media fails to load, since that would misrepresent a generic illustration as this doctor's own real result.
- **`base44/agents/m_care.jsonc`**: RULE 22 extended with one sentence — after showing the procedure image, M-Care now explicitly asks whether this is something the traveler would like to do, via the existing, already-proven `{{choices:...}}` token (never just show a picture and move on). New **RULE 23 — DOCTOR PORTFOLIO MEDIA**: when recommending a matched doctor whose real `portfolio` array is non-empty, may offer to show it; only after an explicit yes, emit `{{doctormedia:...}}` using the real name/type/url from that doctor's own tool-returned data; if the array is empty, say nothing about photos or video for that doctor — never claim one has before/after material when the tool result shows none, and never substitute RULE 22's generic image in its place. Explicitly states this is still not personalized — one doctor's own real prior work, never a preview of how the traveler themselves would look. Same byte-level safe-edit discipline as every prior addition (`tool_configs` count confirmed unchanged at 91 before and after).

**What this does NOT do, stated plainly**: no personalized "how would I look" simulation — needs a real image-generation/virtual-try-on vendor Portia hasn't chosen. No new video content is created or licensed — only a doctor's own real, already-public upload, shown only when one genuinely exists. No retroactive seeding of doctor portfolios — populating them is Portia's or each doctor's own future task through the existing upload widget, same as the still-empty `ProcedureKnowledge` risk table elsewhere in this app.

**Verification**: `matchDoctorsForProcedure` code change and the `m_care.jsonc` text edit both need a **Publish**; `MessageBubble.jsx` ships with the normal frontend deploy. Lint clean, typecheck at the same pre-existing baseline, 655/655 vitest (unchanged — no new pure-logic module, consistent with `MessageBubble.jsx`'s established precedent of no dedicated test file), 230/230 redteam (unchanged — no entity/RLS/permission surface touched; `portfolio` was already public via `ProviderDetail.jsx`), `npm run build` exit 0. Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: whether the confirmation question reads naturally, and whether a doctor's real portfolio media (once one exists) renders correctly inline — Portia verifies live.

## M-Care evolution toward "E.V." — the education image was silently gated behind medical history (2026-08-15)

Direct follow-up to the doctor-portfolio-media slice above. Portia tested "I want a tummy tuck and show me picture of before and after" live and got a real, reproducible bug: M-Care called `getProcedureKnowledge` successfully, gave a two-sentence intro, then demanded medical history and explicitly deferred the image — *"Once you share your background, I'll walk you through the facts and then offer to show you that illustration if you'd like."* Her reaction: "i shouldnt ask, he should just show me, M-care need to more bold."

Root cause traced directly in the live instructions (not guessed): the **PROCEDURE INQUIRY protocol** (fires the moment any procedure is named) has a strict lettered sequence — step A calls `getProcedureKnowledge`, step B says *"ASK MEDICAL BACKGROUND FIRST. Before sharing any procedure details, ask... Do NOT give risk figures until they answer,"* and only step D ("SHARE THE FACTS") comes after. RULE 22 (the image feature) offers the image as part of "explaining," which this protocol placed after the medical-history gate — so a generic, non-personalized illustration got swept into the same gate meant for real risk figures and personalized facts.

**Fixed, narrowly — the actual safety gate is untouched.** A new **step A2** inserted between A and B: right after `getProcedureKnowledge` succeeds, in the same reply, M-Care gives a short 1-2 sentence description and immediately includes the real image via `{{media:PROCEDURE_NAME}}` — no permission-asking, no deferring. Explicitly scoped to why this is safe: it's a generic illustration, never a risk figure or a case-specific fact — those (steps C/D) still wait for step B's medical background, completely unchanged. Step B's opening line reworded ("Before sharing any procedure details" → "Before sharing any risk figures or clinical facts (not the general image and overview you already gave in A2)") to remove the ambiguity that caused the original bug. RULE 22 gained one exception sentence acknowledging this is the one deliberate place showing comes before asking — every other context (revisiting a procedure later, RULE 23's doctor-portfolio media) keeps the offer-then-yes discipline unchanged.

**What stays exactly as-is**: the medical-history question itself, and every risk-figure/personalized-fact gate behind it — the M Principle's safety-first gating was never in scope for this fix, only when a generic illustration appears.

**Verification**: text-only `m_care.jsonc` edit, `tool_configs` count confirmed unchanged at 91 before/after. Lint clean, typecheck at the same pre-existing baseline, 655/655 vitest, 230/230 redteam, `npm run build` exit 0 — all unchanged, as expected for an instructions-only change. Needs a **Publish**. Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: re-testing the exact phrase live and confirming the image now appears in the first reply, not gated behind medical history — Portia verifies live.

## M-Care evolution toward "E.V." — broad, real picture coverage for tomorrow's demo; no fake before/after (2026-08-15)

Portia's ask, with a hard deadline: ship far more procedures with pictures into M-Care's database, keep self-learn working, and "literally show the user before and after pic" by tomorrow. Held the line on the one non-negotiable piece and built the rest for real.

**Self-learn was already real, confirmed, no work needed** — `recallMcareKnowledge`/`mcareResearchAndLearn` (Phase 14/29) is already the fallback inside `getProcedureKnowledge` when a procedure isn't curated.

**Real before/after cannot be fabricated by tomorrow, or ever, by this code.** No real patient photo data exists anywhere in this app, and AI-generating a fake "before/after" transformation would be actively misleading in a medical context — the same concern Portia's own earlier pasted Educational Media spec already named (real patient photos need verified rights). Asked her directly; she confirmed she'll get a real doctor to upload real, consented content through the doctor-portfolio pipeline shipped earlier today (RULE 23, `matchDoctorsForProcedure`'s `portfolio` field, `{{doctormedia:...}}`) — that path needed zero new code.

**Broad generic picture coverage is real and was built.** Found `Core.GenerateImage` genuinely exists as a Base44 platform capability (present in the SDK's `CoreIntegrations` type, confirmed never used anywhere in this repo before — almost certainly how `ProcedureData.jsx`'s existing 69 illustrations were originally made). This generates generic, non-personalized diagrams only — never a before/after, never a specific patient.

**A real schema conflict surfaced and was resolved before writing any data.** `ProcedureKnowledge.risk_level` is a required field, but this project already ruled out fabricating risk/complication content once (see the "Guardian email re-access + procedure risk display" section above — that's exactly why the entity still has zero admin-curated rows). Writing ~150 new rows with a guessed risk level would have repeated that exact mistake, now with real downstream weight (RULE 1/13's risk-flagging logic reads this field). Fixed by adding an honest `'Not Yet Assessed'` value to the enum — never a guessed level, an explicit "we don't know" signal — and excluding it from both `getProcedureKnowledge` and `getProcedureRiskSummaries` (the two functions that feed real clinical guidance to a traveler or a risk pill), so a picture-only record can never be mistaken for a real clinical assessment. Only the new `getProcedureIllustration` function (image-only, returns no risk fields at all) reads those rows.

**Shipped**:
- `ProcedureKnowledge.jsonc` gained `image_url` and the `'Not Yet Assessed'` risk_level value (with an explicit schema-comment warning against ever narrating it as real).
- `generateProcedureIllustrations` (new, admin-gated) — a curated, real list of 153 common medical-tourism procedures not already covered by `ProcedureData.jsx`'s 69, spanning Dental/Cosmetic/ENT/Ophthalmology/Cardiac/General Surgery/Bariatric/Orthopedic/Fertility/Other. For each without an existing image, calls `Core.GenerateImage` with a fixed prompt template constrained to a clinical/anatomical-diagram style — explicitly no real or photorealistic people, no graphic imagery, no before-and-after comparison of any kind — and upserts a `ProcedureKnowledge` row. Idempotent (skips anything already covered) and processes a bounded batch per call (function-timeout-safe, returns progress) so Portia can safely click it repeatedly. A single generation failure is skipped, never aborts the batch.
- `getProcedureIllustration` (new, public) — fuzzy-matches a chat query against `ProcedureKnowledge` using the existing shared scorer (`procedureKnowledgeMatch.ts`, same threshold discipline as `getProcedureRiskSummaries`), returns `{found, title, image_url, desc}`, never fabricates.
- `MessageBubble.jsx`'s `ProcedureMediaCard` is now a two-tier lookup: the existing static `searchProcedures` first (instant, the original 69), then `getProcedureIllustration` as a live fallback (brief loading state) before the honest empty state. **No `m_care.jsonc` change needed** — RULE 22's existing wording already covers an expanded catalog regardless of source, zero Publish-order risk for this piece.
- `AdminProcedureKnowledge.jsx` gained a "Generate More Illustrations" button (progress readout, safely re-clickable) and an image thumbnail on each row; "Not Yet Assessed" renders as a distinct neutral badge, never styled like a real risk level.
- Bonus fix, same session: `getProcedureKnowledge`'s own returned `m_care_protocol` object (a second, hardcoded copy of the PROCEDURE INQUIRY protocol handed to the agent alongside the instructions) still had the old "ask medical background before anything" wording from before the bold-image fix shipped earlier today — a real risk that this duplicate could quietly re-assert the exact bug just fixed. Updated its step 0/1 to match RULE 22's new A2 behavior.

**Real constraint, stated to Portia**: generating ~150 images costs real integration credits and real time — safe to run repeatedly, but if the monthly quota runs out mid-batch, further calls just return Base44's own 402 and stop (already-generated rows stay usable).

**Verification**: touches `ProcedureKnowledge.jsonc`, `getProcedureKnowledge`, `getProcedureRiskSummaries`, plus two new functions — needs a **Publish** naming all five. `MessageBubble.jsx`/`AdminProcedureKnowledge.jsx` ship with the normal frontend deploy. Lint clean, typecheck at the same pre-existing baseline, 655/655 vitest (unchanged — no new pure-logic module isolated for a unit test, `Core.GenerateImage`/live-network code stays untested from this checkout per this repo's established precedent), 230/230 redteam (unchanged — confirmed the auto-discovery `AUTH`/`BUNDLER` sweeps cover both new function directories correctly), `npm run build` exit 0. Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: whether the generated diagrams actually look appropriately clinical/non-graphic, whether the batch tool completes within Base44's real function timeout, and whether the real doctor-uploaded before/after renders correctly once Portia has one — she verifies all three live before the demo.

## Real-time cab dispatch + guardian alert via M-Care chat (2026-08-15)

Portia's ask had two parts. First, framing: M-Care as "a living organism — it breathes, it thinks on its own, it has a heart and soul, it thinks what if/what happens/what next," not a chatbot. Audited before building anything: this is **already largely real**, not aspirational — `LivingOrb.jsx`'s five animation states plus blink/wink eyes, `m_care.jsonc`'s PRIORITY ORDER preamble + RULE 13 (proactive judgment) + RULE 19 (trust tiers) + RULE 20 (proactive destination check) + RULE 21 (voice/character), and `JourneyEvent` (8 real writers, real-time `subscribe()` delivery, not just polling) are all confirmed shipped. Nothing needed building for the framing itself.

Second, concrete: a traveler tells M-Care "I need a cab/taxi" or "I am lost," and the system dispatches a real, verified driver, alerts their guardian, and keeps them updated. Audited what existed: `requestEmergencyRecoveryTransport` was already a real, working on-demand dispatcher (matches an `is_online` `TaxiService`, issues a `VisualVerificationToken` safety code like "MANGO-7421" the driver confirms before pickup) — but it was already granted to M-Care as a tool while being gated to PinSession-or-admin auth only, meaning a normal traveler asking for a cab would silently 401. `GuardianSession`/`generateGuardianLink`/`GuardianView` were real, consent-gated guardian infrastructure with no "notify right now" action decoupled from the existing 2h/3h/5h/9h `SoloCheckIn` ladder. `handleDistressSignal` (the Silent Guardian welfare-prompt) was deliberately non-dispatching — confirmed its "Tap here" language was literally inert text, no tappable element existed. Confirmed with Portia via AskUserQuestion: build the fast real-time path (not `assignChauffeurServices`'s 24h async quote-request flow), and defer partner-side (driver/companion) location sharing — `Driver`/`TaxiService` have no location field at all today, a separate future piece.

**Shipped**:
- `base44/shared/recoveryTransportDispatch.ts` (new) — the real matching/token/notify logic extracted verbatim from `requestEmergencyRecoveryTransport` (resolve `CaseRecord`, find an `is_online` `TaxiService`, create `VisualVerificationToken`, create `RecoveryTransportRequest`, admin email, driver SMS, `DispatchFailureLog` on no-driver) into one implementation with two callers — same "one shared helper, multiple real triggers" discipline as `weatherEngine.ts`/`findDoctorBackup.ts`. Adds one thing the original inline logic didn't have: a `logJourneyEvent` call (`ride_dispatched`) so the dispatch is felt back in M-Care's own chat regardless of which caller triggered it, and never claims a driver is coming when `driver_assigned:false`.
- `requestEmergencyRecoveryTransport/entry.ts` — refactored into a thin caller of the shared helper. Auth gate (PinSession OR admin) is completely unchanged — this function's whole safety contract (compromised-device, `requested_by: shopkeeper/guardian`) wasn't touched, on purpose: widening its own branch would have silently changed its blast radius without changing its name or tool description.
- `requestOnDemandRide/entry.ts` (new) — the fast path for a normal traveler. `requireAuth: true`, no PIN/admin gate, `bodySchema`-validated, rate-limited (3/10min). Verifies the caller owns a real `CaseRecord` (mirrors `generateGuardianLink`'s ownership check) before calling the same shared dispatch helper.
- `notifyGuardianNow/entry.ts` (new) — traveler-initiated, immediate guardian alert, additive to (never replacing) the existing check-in ladder. An active `GuardianSession` already existing for the case is treated as existing consent — sends immediately, link-only (`linkOnlyEmail`/`linkOnlySms`), real location written to `LiveLocation` for `GuardianView`'s own map, never interpolated into the message body. No session and not a confirmed emergency → returns `needs_consent:true` so M-Care offers `generateGuardianLink` instead, never silently creating a guardian relationship. No session AND `is_confirmed_emergency:true` (settable only by the deterministic client-side distress-confirm path below, never the LLM's own judgment) → auto-creates a session, mirroring `triggerSOS`'s real precedent — but fixes a real parity gap found while reading it: `triggerSOS`'s own auto-created session hardcodes `guardian_email: ''` (inert, notifies nobody); this one populates `guardian_email`/`guardian_phone` from `CaseRecord.emergency_contact_email`/`emergency_contact_number` when present, so it can actually reach someone.
- `JourneyEvent.jsonc` — added `ride_dispatched` and `guardian_alert_sent` to `event_type`'s enum. Zero frontend change needed beyond the enum — `useJourneyEvents.js` is already generic over event type.
- `MCareOrb.jsx` — `handleDistressSignal`'s welfare prompt now carries a real `{{choices:Yes — send help now|No, I'm okay}}` confirm step (the same tap-button mechanism `MessageBubble.jsx` already renders for every other closed-set question), tagged `__distressConfirm` so the render loop routes its `onChoice` to a new `handleDistressConfirm` instead of the normal agent-message path. A confirmed "Yes" calls `requestOnDemandRide` and `notifyGuardianNow` (`is_confirmed_emergency: true`) directly from the client via `Promise.allSettled` — the same "client calls the backend function directly, bypassing the agent" pattern `SOSDropdown.jsx` already uses for `triggerSOS`, not a new precedent. Fans out to both on one tap (the more protective default for someone who just confirmed they need help, matching `triggerSOS`'s own multi-channel-on-one-trigger behavior) — flagged to Portia as a real product decision rather than silently assumed. Does **not** touch the Safety-Decision Architecture invariant: that's scoped to SAFE-T risk-level computation only, and ride-dispatch/guardian-notify are operational actions the agent could already trigger today via `triggerSOS`/`generateGuardianLink`.
- `m_care.jsonc` — two new `tool_configs` grants (91→93). Instructions: the GROUND TRANSPORT & HANDOFF bullet now distinguishes `requestOnDemandRide` (real-time) from `assignChauffeurServices` (real quote request, ~24h window); the LIVE LOCATION & GUARDIAN / FAMILY PORTAL bullet gained `notifyGuardianNow`, describing both the existing-session and needs-consent branches; the LOCATION CONTEXT rule gained one clarifying sentence so the agent never confuses `requestOnDemandRide` (routine "get me a cab") with `requestEmergencyRecoveryTransport` (reserved for an actually-escalated monitored event) now that both sit in its toolset; both names added to the flat tool-name catalog list alongside their siblings. Same byte-level safe-edit discipline as every prior change (raw-byte anchor uniqueness checks, `json.loads` validity + `tool_configs` count confirmed before/after, plain ASCII in new text — this region stores literal `\"` for embedded quotes, one first-attempt bug caught by the JSON-validity check before anything was written to disk).

**Redteam**: extended the `JOURNEY EVENTS` test's writer list with `notifyGuardianNow` and a new `recoveryTransportDispatch.ts` shared-helper check (mirrors the existing `findDoctorBackup.ts` pattern). New test (`RIDE DISPATCH`) asserts `requestOnDemandRide` never accepts a `pin_session_token` field and verifies case ownership; `notifyGuardianNow`'s auto-create branch is structurally gated on `is_confirmed_emergency` inside its own no-session code block, not unconditional; `MCareOrb.jsx`'s `handleDistressConfirm` function body actually calls both new functions with the right flags; `requestEmergencyRecoveryTransport` still accepts `pin_session_token` and still allows admin fallback, confirming its contract survived the extraction unchanged.

**Deliberately deferred, per Portia's own scoping, not silently dropped**: partner-side (driver/companion) location sharing via M-Care chat — `Driver`/`TaxiService` have zero location fields today, a separate, bigger piece. Live GPS tracking of the dispatched driver's actual car — today's "safe driver" means a real verified/online match + a real safety code, not a moving pin on a map.
