Any change to `assignChauffeurServices`'s existing async flow. Any change to RULE 1/RULE 2 — this feature is operational dispatch, not a SAFE-T decision.

**Verification**: touches `JourneyEvent.jsonc`, 4 functions (2 new, `requestEmergencyRecoveryTransport` refactored, plus the new `recoveryTransportDispatch.ts` shared helper), and `m_care.jsonc` — needs a **Publish** naming all of these, functions/entity first, `m_care.jsonc`'s tool grant last, each new function pinged directly afterward to confirm a real 400/401 (never 404) before trusting the agent to call it, per this repo's own documented Phase 10 incident. Lint clean, typecheck at the same pre-existing baseline, 656/656 vitest, 231/231 redteam (1 new), `npm run build` exit 0. Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: whether the agent reliably distinguishes a routine "get me a cab" from a real emergency now that both tools exist side-by-side; whether the confirm-buttons render/dispatch correctly on a real device for the distress-welfare flow; whether a seeded `is_online:true` `TaxiService` exists to actually drive-test a real match (a real, disclosed caveat — `is_online` is currently only ever written by demo seed data, no real driver-facing "go online" endpoint exists in production). Portia verifies all three live.

## Companion Network Alerts — cascading through ranked emergency contacts (2026-08-15)

Portia pasted a 3-idea buildathon pitch: smartwatch fall detection (Apple Watch/Garmin/Wear OS), Bluetooth pairing with "any device, e.g. a TV" so M-Care's chat could be brought up from anywhere, voice-activated "Hey M" via a smart speaker (Alexa/Google Home), and "Companion Network Alerts" (cascading through ranked emergency contacts as a check-in goes unanswered). Audited all four before proposing anything, including a repo-wide grep for existing wearable/Bluetooth/voice-assistant code rather than assuming: fall detection has no smartwatch/HealthKit/Garmin integration anywhere, but a real, already-shipped phone-accelerometer fall heuristic exists (`useActivityDetection.js`, wired to Dashboard's "Enable Fall Detection" toggle) — a genuinely different, honest story than the pitch's mock. `navigator.bluetooth` is real but only used for BLE emergency-radio SOS messaging (`useWebBluetooth.js`, explicitly labeled "Experimental · untested hardware"), nothing resembling TV/display pairing. Voice is real but browser-only (`SpeechRecognition`/`speechSynthesis`), no Alexa Skill/Google Action exists. All three need a real hardware/platform vendor account this repo can't set up blind — confirmed with Portia via AskUserQuestion, she chose to build the fourth idea instead, the one built on infrastructure that's already real.

Audited the real infrastructure: `escalateSoloCheckIn/entry.ts` (cron-driven via `.github/workflows/safety-cron.yml`, 2h/3h/5h/9h ladder) only ever notified **one** contact, read from `CaseRecord.emergency_contact_email`/`emergency_contact_number` (single scalar fields). `src/components/emergency/PersonalEmergencyContactsPanel.jsx` already let a patient add multiple ranked contacts with a "Primary" star, real add/edit/delete UI — but `handleSyncToCase()` only ever pushed the primary into those three `CaseRecord` fields; contacts #2/#3 stayed in `localStorage` and were never read by anything server-side. That's the real gap: the UI for a network already existed, the backend never learned about it.

**A real bug found and fixed while reading this exact code**: `escalateSoloCheckIn`'s local `ensureGuardianLink` helper created every case's `GuardianSession` with `guardian_email: caseRecord.emergency_contact` — but `emergency_contact` is a display label ("Jane Doe (Spouse)"), not an email; the real field is `emergency_contact_email` (a comment elsewhere in the same file already documented this exact label-vs-email distinction, but this one call site still had the bug). Didn't affect the direct notification emails (those already read the correct field), but poisoned the `GuardianSession` row itself — which `notifyGuardianNow` (built earlier this session) reads directly. Also never set `guardian_phone` at all, unlike `notifyGuardianNow`'s own auto-create branch. Fixed both, since it's the exact object literal already being touched for the cascade.

**Design decision, stated plainly, not silently assumed**: the pitch's literal "no response in 5 minutes → contact #2" cadence isn't buildable honestly — there's no mechanism anywhere for detecting a *guardian's* non-response (only the patient can tap "I Am Safe"; a guardian read-receipt system would be a real, separate, much bigger feature). Instead, the cascade widens as the existing hour-scale tiers progress: **2h stays exactly as today** (primary only — earliest, most-trusted signal, unchanged behavior). **3h is where the full ranked list gets notified** — not spread further into 5h/9h, since those tiers are already a different audience/register (security dispatch, police/embassy) and delaying contact #3 to 5h would mean they're never told at all if the case resolves at 4h.

**Shipped**:
- `base44/entities/EmergencyContact.jsonc` (new) — holds only contacts #2+; the primary stays untouched on `CaseRecord`. `case_id`, `patient_email` (RLS anchor, matches `GuardianSession`'s pattern), `name`, `relationship`, `phone`, `email`, `priority` (2, 3, 4… — never 1). RLS: patient owns their own case's contacts, including delete — deliberately more permissive than `GuardianSession`'s admin-only delete, since this is personal address-book data the panel already let a patient delete freely client-side, not a security-relevant revocable link.
- `base44/shared/emergencyContacts.ts` (new) — pure data-shaping, no Twilio/email calls (per `escalateSoloCheckIn`'s own documented convention of keeping notification primitives inline, not centralized). `getOrderedCaseContacts(base44, caseRecord)` returns one unified, ordered list: priority 1 = the primary (built from `CaseRecord`'s three fields, included only if a phone or email exists), priority 2+ = `EmergencyContact` rows sorted and re-numbered contiguously (a deleted middle contact never leaves a gap). Fails open — a lookup failure returns whatever it already has, never throws, since a contact-list read must never be able to block the escalation ladder. `parsePrimaryContactLabel` centralizes the "Jane Doe (Spouse)" label-splitting logic that used to be duplicated inline.
- `base44/functions/escalateSoloCheckIn/entry.ts` (modified) — the 2h and 3h tiers' near-duplicate inline "notify one contact" blocks became one local `notifyContact(base44, contact, ctx)` helper (kept local to this file, not `shared/`, matching its own stated convention), fanned out via `Promise.allSettled` so one bad contact address can never stop the rest of the cascade. 2h calls it with `[priority 1]` only (unchanged); 3h calls it with the full ordered list (cascade widens here, completes here). 5h/9h untouched.
- `src/components/emergency/PersonalEmergencyContactsPanel.jsx` (modified) — `handleSyncToCase` still writes the primary to `CaseRecord` exactly as before, and additionally replace-alls the case's `EmergencyContact` rows from the current non-primary contacts (delete existing, recreate in list order as priority 2, 3…). Replace-all over diff/upsert: lists are small (1-4 contacts), sync is a low-frequency manual button click, nothing else references a row's `id` that a replace would orphan. Stays a direct client entity write — the new entity's RLS already grants exactly the access this needs, same as the existing primary-sync call.
- Redteam (`tests/redteam/invariants.spec.js`, `COMPANION NETWORK`): `EmergencyContact`'s RLS is patient-scoped on all four operations, not wide open; a structural slice-based regression guard confirms the cascade widens exactly at the 3h tier (`getOrderedCaseContacts(` present) and never at 5h/9h; `notifyContact`'s email send is individually caught and its SMS/WhatsApp pair is fanned out via `Promise.allSettled`; the `ensureGuardianLink` bug fix is now a permanent regression test (asserts the fixed literal is present, the buggy one is absent); the shared helper never itself calls `SendEmail`/Twilio; the panel's sync writes `patient_email` from its own `userEmail` prop, never a caller-suppliable field. Plus `tests/emergencyContacts.test.js` (8 vitest cases) for `getOrderedCaseContacts`'s ordering/gap-handling/fail-open logic — caught a real off-by-one in the first draft (re-numbering used the sorted array's index instead of a count of contacts actually kept, so a skipped no-contact-info row shifted every priority after it) before it ever reached the redteam suite.

**Explicitly deferred, not silently dropped**: fall detection, TV/Bluetooth pairing, smart-speaker voice control — all need a real vendor/platform decision only Portia can make. `anonymizePatientRecords.ts`'s GDPR erasure doesn't yet cover `EmergencyContact.patient_email` — flagged as a real follow-up (same one-field-redaction shape as its existing `GuardianSession` handling), not bundled into this already-multi-file change. Migrating `escalateSoloCheckIn`'s emails onto the `notify.ts` link-only framework — separate, bigger, unrelated; this function already sits outside that framework today (its own header implies the "active-emergency dispatch" carve-out) and this pass doesn't change that.

**Verification**: `EmergencyContact.jsonc` and `escalateSoloCheckIn/entry.ts` (which bundles in the new `emergencyContacts.ts` import) need a **Publish** — the cron already calls this function every 15 minutes, so an unpublished edit means the old single-contact behavior keeps running silently. `PersonalEmergencyContactsPanel.jsx` ships with the normal frontend deploy. Lint clean, typecheck at the same pre-existing baseline, 664/664 vitest (8 new), 232/232 redteam (1 new), `npm run build` exit 0. Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: whether a real 2h/3h cron pass actually notifies the right contacts at the right tier, and whether the synced `EmergencyContact` rows appear correctly after a real "Sync to Journey" click — Portia verifies live after publishing.

## Driver live-tracking map in M-Care chat — audited and fixed a Base44-built feature (2026-08-15)

Portia pasted a PRD ("driver taps an SMS link, traveler watches them approach on an inline map inside M-Care chat") and asked whether it was a good plan. Mid-audit of the real `LiveLocationRequest`/`LiveLocation`/`RecoveryTransportRequest` code (which found a real landmine — `LiveLocation` upserts one row per `case_id` with nothing distinguishing a traveler's own GPS from anyone else's), Portia said Base44 had already built the whole thing directly in the Builder — 4 commits already on `origin/main`. This section is the review of that already-shipped code (read via `git show`/`git diff` before pulling, per this session's own plan-mode discipline), not a fresh design.

**The good news**: the collision was solved correctly. Base44's implementation adds a `role` field (`traveler`/`driver`) to both `LiveLocation` and `LiveLocationRequest`, and for a driver-role link, repurposes `case_id` to hold the `RecoveryTransportRequest.id` instead of a real `CaseRecord.id` — since those two ID spaces never collide, `storeLiveLocationUpdate`'s existing case_id-keyed upsert works unchanged for both roles. Applied consistently across `generateLiveLocationRequestLink`, `storeLiveLocationUpdate`, and the new `getDriverLocationStatus`. The matching/token/SMS-append logic, `DriverMapWidget.jsx` (polling, stale/arrived/ended state machine, geofence auto-arrival, safety-code fallback), and `MessageBubble.jsx`'s `{{drivermap:REQUEST_ID}}` extractor were all real and well-built in isolation.

**But the feature as shipped would never actually appear for a traveler, plus 6 more real issues** — found by reading every touched file in full, confirmed with Portia via AskUserQuestion (she chose "fix everything"), then fixed:

1. **The `{{drivermap:...}}` token was never emitted anywhere.** `base44/agents/m_care.jsonc` had zero diff in the whole push — the agent was never told the token exists — and `dispatchRecoveryTransport`'s own `logJourneyEvent` message wasn't updated either. Every other piece worked; the one trigger that would ever show the map to a traveler was missing. **Fixed** by appending `{{drivermap:${transportRequest.id}}}` to `recoveryTransportDispatch.ts`'s existing driver-matched `messageText` — confirmed via `MCareOrb.jsx`'s render loop that every `JourneyEvent.message_text` already flows through the same `MessageBubble` component real chat replies use, so this needed **zero `m_care.jsonc` change and zero Publish-order risk**, unlike granting the agent a new tool.
2. **`retryDriverLocationSms`** (the 3-min driver reminder / 5-min care-team alert) was real, correctly `cronAuthorized`-gated, and not wired into `.github/workflows/safety-cron.yml` at all — the exact recurring "real function, never scheduled" bug class this file's own Travel Safety Escalation section already names repeatedly. **Fixed**: added to both the 15-min life-safety tier and the daily deployment audit, with an honest comment that GitHub Actions' ~15-min granularity can miss the narrow 3-5-min reminder window on an unlucky dispatch time (the open-ended 5+-min care-team alert is unaffected).
3. **`RecoveryTransportRequest.update`'s RLS was widened** from admin-only to `data.user_email === user.email OR admin` — but every real write path in the whole push (`markTransportArrived`, `storeLiveLocationUpdate`'s en_route bump, `DriverMapWidget.jsx`'s two function calls) writes via `asServiceRole`, bypassing RLS regardless. Nothing needed the wider rule. Since Base44 RLS has no field-level granularity, this let any traveler directly rewrite `payment_status`/`driver_id`/`status`/etc. on their own row from the client. **Fixed**: reverted to admin/platform_admin-only, pinned with a new redteam invariant.
4. **The widget's "5 minutes, no share yet" fallback clock started on first successful poll** (whenever the traveler happened to open the chat message), not the real `dispatched_at` — the function's own comment admitted the shortcut. **Fixed**: `getDriverLocationStatus` now returns `dispatched_at` from the real transport record; `DriverMapWidget.jsx` uses that instead of a client-mount-time clock.
5. **A narrow, silent-failure race**: the driver's `LiveLocationRequest` is created with empty `case_id`/`transport_request_id` placeholders, then backfilled in a second best-effort call once the real transport ID exists — a failure there was caught and swallowed with no retry, permanently breaking that ride's tracking with zero visible signal. **Fixed** two ways: the backfill now retries once on failure, and `getDriverLocationStatus` self-heals by falling back to the caller's own verified `transport_request_id` if `driverReq.case_id` is still empty.
6. **`ShareLiveLocation.jsx`'s consent screen stayed patient-framed for a driver** ("we share it only with your care team so they can keep you safe" doesn't make sense to a driver). **Fixed**: header/privacy copy now branches on `ctx.role === 'driver'`.
7. **The battery-critical alert called the wrong function entirely.** `storeSafetySignal` requires an `internal_secret` the client will never have and an anonymized-aggregate body shape (`destination_country` etc.) with no `signal_type`/`context` fields — the call was guaranteed to always fail silently (wrapped in `.catch(() => {})`), so the "alert the care team at <10% battery" half of the PRD never actually did anything. **Fixed**: extended `storeLiveLocationUpdate` (already public, token-gated, already the endpoint this page calls on every GPS tick) to accept an optional `battery_level` and fire a real, deduped (`battery_critical_alerted_at`, new field) admin email at ≤10% — reusing the one endpoint this page can already reach rather than inventing a second, separately-authorized call. Also fixed the battery reading itself being frozen at share-start via closure (a `batteryLevelRef` now keeps `sendUpdate` reading the live value as it actually drains), and cast `navigator.getBattery()` (`/** @type {any} */`, matching `VoiceMode.jsx`'s existing pattern for `SpeechRecognition`) — the Battery Status API isn't in TS's DOM lib and this was a real, new typecheck regression Base44's push introduced.

**Verification**: touches `base44/entities/LiveLocationRequest.jsonc` (new `battery_critical_alerted_at` field), `base44/entities/RecoveryTransportRequest.jsonc` (RLS revert), `base44/shared/recoveryTransportDispatch.ts`, `base44/functions/getDriverLocationStatus/entry.ts`, `base44/functions/storeLiveLocationUpdate/entry.ts` — all need a **Publish**; `DriverMapWidget.jsx`/`ShareLiveLocation.jsx` ship with the normal frontend deploy. New redteam test `DRIVER LIVE MAP` (structural: the token is actually emitted in the driver-matched branch, `retryDriverLocationSms` is called exactly twice in the right two cron tiers, `RecoveryTransportRequest.update`'s RLS shape is pinned exactly, `getDriverLocationStatus` returns `dispatched_at` and self-heals the case_id fallback). Also fixed 2 pre-existing lint errors (unused `MapPin`/`Wifi` imports) in Base44's own `DriverMapWidget.jsx`. Lint clean, typecheck at the same 4-error pre-existing baseline, 664/664 vitest (unchanged — no new pure-logic module), 233/233 redteam (1 new), `npm run build` exit 0. Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: whether the map actually renders in a real dispatched-ride conversation now, whether the 3-min driver reminder fires usefully given the 15-min cron granularity, and whether the battery alert email actually lands — Portia verifies live after publishing.

**Same day, a concurrent Base44 Builder push (`f1052952`) tightened `RecoveryTransportRequest`'s `read` RLS further** (dropped the `data.user_email` clause too, leaving admin/platform_admin-only) — checked and merged, no conflict: the only frontend file that reads this entity directly is the already-admin-only `AdminSoloMonitor.jsx`, everything else goes through `asServiceRole`-backed functions. That same push also reworded `LiveLocationRequest.battery_critical_alerted_at`'s description to say "battery < 15%" — the actual threshold in `storeLiveLocationUpdate` is still `<= 10%`; the description text and the real code now disagree. Not fixed here (out of scope for this session, no function code changed in that push to indicate an intended threshold change) — flagged so it isn't mistaken for a real 15% behavior later.

## M-Care auto-location — a race that silently burned its one shot (2026-08-16)

Portia's screenshot: a brand-new M-Care conversation, first message "can you show my current location," and M-Care replied it had no location at all and asked her to state it — even though the 2026-08-15 location-auto-detect feature (`useAutoLocation`/`buildLocationContextBlock`, wired into `MCareOrb.jsx`) is supposed to silently attach an auto-detected `[[LOCATION_CONTEXT: ...]]` block to a session's first message with zero action from the traveler.

Read the real code rather than assuming a regression — it hadn't regressed, it had a real race present since that feature shipped, just never caught live before: the one-shot `locationContextSentRef` flag flipped to `true` the instant the "first message" branch ran, **regardless of whether `buildLocationContextBlock(bestLocation)` actually produced a block.** `bestLocation` (from `useAutoLocation`'s `ipLocation`/`gpsLocation` state) starts `null` and only populates once `getGeolocationAndCurrency`'s async IP lookup resolves. A brand-new session (no `sessionStorage` cache yet) with someone typing fast — exactly the screenshot — sends its first message before that resolves, gets `null` back from `buildLocationContextBlock`, attaches nothing, and burns the only chance: every later message in that same conversation was permanently excluded too, since the flag was already spent.

**Fixed, two ways, `src/components/mcare/MCareOrb.jsx` only** (no backend/entity/Publish involved — pure client-side timing bug): (1) the flag is now only consumed once a real, non-null block was actually attached, so a later message in the same session picks up what an earlier one raced past instead of the opportunity being lost forever; (2) on the very first attempt only, if location is still genuinely loading, a short bounded wait (150ms polling, capped at 1.5s) gives the in-flight IP lookup a chance to land before sending with nothing — closes the exact single-message case in the screenshot, where there's no second message to fall back on. The wait reads a `bestLocationRef`/`locationLoadingRef` pair (kept current via a small effect, the same "read the latest value via a ref inside a stable callback" pattern this file already uses for `liveRef.current.sendAgentMessage`) since `sendAgentMessage`'s `useCallback` closure can't otherwise see a location update that lands mid-wait. `setInput('')`/`setAgentSending(true)` were moved to fire before the wait, not after, so the input clears and the sending indicator shows immediately — the bounded wait is invisible latency before the optimistic bubble appears, never a delay the traveler perceives as "nothing happened when I hit send." In practice this rarely fires at all: `useAutoLocation`'s IP fetch starts when `MCareOrb` itself mounts (once per page load, persistent across the app), well before most travelers actually open the panel and type — the bad case is specifically a fresh session plus fast typing right after load.

No change to `src/lib/locationContext.js` (`buildLocationContextBlock` was already correct — pure, tested, and already correctly refuses to build a block from `useAutoLocation`'s own honest all-fields-null failure fallback) or to `m_care.jsonc`'s LOCATION CONTEXT rule (read directly, already correctly worded).

**Verification**: no entity/function to coordinate a Publish-order risk with, but the frontend bundle itself still needs a normal Publish in Base44 to go live like any other change — "pure frontend" is not "nothing to do." Lint clean, typecheck at the same 4-error pre-existing baseline, 664/664 vitest (unchanged — no new pure-logic module extractable from `sendAgentMessage`'s hook-coupled body; `MCareOrb.jsx` has no dedicated test file, consistent with its established precedent throughout this file), 233/233 redteam (unchanged — no entity/tool/permission surface touched), `npm run build` exit 0. Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: whether a real fresh session's very first message now actually carries location within the bounded wait — Portia verifies live by opening M-Care in a private/incognito window (no cached IP lookup) and immediately asking about her location.

**Same-day correction, real deeper bug found while re-testing live**: after publishing, Portia still saw "nothing has been shared with me yet." Live-tested the actual deployed `getGeolocationAndCurrency` directly (`curl` against the real Base44 function URL, both with and without a spoofed `X-Forwarded-For`) — it returns `DEFAULT_FALLBACK` (`country: 'Unknown'`) in under a second every time, not a real location. Confirmed the two free-tier providers it depends on (`ipapi.co`, `ipwho.is`) are themselves up and returning valid data when hit directly from outside Base44 — so this isn't "the providers are down," it's specific to how/whether Base44's own Deno runtime can reach them (the sub-1s response time rules out a timeout; `createKeyedMemoCache` (`base44/shared/memoCache.ts`) never caches a rejected promise, so this also isn't a stale cached failure — confirmed by reading the cache implementation directly). This means `bestLocation` legitimately has nothing real to attach today — the race-condition fix above is necessary but was never going to be sufficient on its own, since waiting longer for a lookup that always resolves to "Unknown" doesn't help. **Fixed, diagnostic only**: `resolveGeo`'s two catch paths in `base44/functions/getGeolocationAndCurrency/entry.ts` now `console.error` the real thrown reason from each provider before falling through — previously swallowed completely silently, with zero trace anywhere of *why* both failed. This function is used far beyond M-Care (11 call sites — signup currency detection, solo-check-in beacon components, `useGeoAutoAlign`) so this outage's real blast radius is bigger than the one symptom reported. Needs Portia to check Base44's own function invocation logs for `getGeolocationAndCurrency` after the next real call (the console.error output only appears there, not in anything this checkout can read) to find the actual root cause — a Deno-runtime outbound-network restriction, a DNS issue specific to that environment, or something else entirely.

**Same-day follow-up, real: `console.error` genuinely never surfaces for this function.** Portia ran the function through Base44's own testing tool. Its assistant confirmed the logs only show generic `[INFO] POST -> 200` lines, no diagnostic detail, and proposed two things — neither checked against the real code first. Verified both directly: (1) **correct symptom, wrong cause.** Read `createHandler.ts` in full — it has no success-path logger of any kind; its only `console.error` lives inside its own catch-all, which fires only on an unhandled throw. This function deliberately never throws out to it (always returns 200 + fallback), so that path never runs — the `[INFO]` lines are Base44's own platform-level access log, not anything this codebase emits, and there's no "framework logger" to switch to, because none exists. (2) **A specific, incorrect theory, disproven by the code**: the assistant read a 1460ms-vs-185ms timing gap between two test calls as evidence `createKeyedMemoCache` was caching the failed fallback "despite the comment saying it shouldn't." Re-read `memoCache.ts` directly (already read once diagnosing the original bug) — its failure path only ever removes the in-flight entry, never populates the success cache; a rejection is structurally never cached. The timing gap is ordinary Deno cold-start-vs-warm-isolate variance (this session's own direct `curl` tests against the same function both came back under a second either way) — no code changes there.

**Fixed with what already exists, not a new mechanism**: this codebase already has a proven answer for "a function that can't let an error propagate but still needs the real failure visible somewhere durable" — `createHandler.ts`'s own catch-all uses it. `base44/shared/incidentReporting.ts`'s `reportIncident()` writes a real, queryable `ReliabilityIncident` row and, at `severity:'high'`/`'critical'`, sends a real alert email if `INCIDENT_ALERT_EMAILS` is set. `resolveGeo`'s two-provider failure now combines both real error messages into one thrown `Error` (previously only the second provider's message would have survived); the handler now destructures `base44` (it didn't before) and calls `reportIncident` on failure, `severity:'high'`, `feature:'getGeolocationAndCurrency'`, the combined message — **awaited via the same 2s-timeout `Promise.race` `createHandler.ts`'s own catch-all uses**, not fire-and-forget, since a Deno isolate can tear down right after the response returns and would otherwise silently drop an un-awaited call more often than not. Gated by a module-level one-hour cooldown (`lastIncidentAt`) — this function fires on nearly every session's first location check, and without a cooldown a sustained outage (the exact situation that surfaced this) would create a fresh incident row and a fresh alert email on every single request. Response behavior is completely unchanged — still always 200 + the honest fallback.

**Verification**: needs a Base44 **Publish** of this one file. Lint clean, 664/664 vitest, 233/233 redteam (unchanged — no entity/permission surface touched by this specific function; typecheck/build unaffected, Deno function files aren't in tsc's scope). Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: whether the resulting `ReliabilityIncident` row's `error_message` field actually names the real underlying cause (a network/DNS/egress restriction specific to Base44's Deno runtime, most likely) — Portia checks Base44's Entities browser for `ReliabilityIncident` after the next real failing call, which given the outage is apparently 100% reproducible right now should be immediate.

## PBKDF2 iteration cap — three PIN systems were silently broken, not one (2026-08-16)

The `ReliabilityIncident` records the geo-diagnostic fix above started producing immediately surfaced a real, unrelated, higher-severity finding: 6 open `verifyVaultPIN` incidents (Aug 2-4), `Pbkdf2 failed: iteration counts above 100000 are not supported (requested 600000)`. Portia asked to fix that one first.

Read the real code before touching anything — the same hardcoded `iterations: 600000` PBKDF2 call existed in **three** separate server-side (Deno) files, not one, and two of them were completely invisible in the incident log because they catch their own errors internally before `createHandler.ts`'s automatic incident-reporting ever sees them (the exact same self-catching shape that hid the geolocation bug two sections above):

- **`verifyVaultPIN/entry.ts`** (`action:'set'`/`'verify'`) — lets errors propagate unhandled, so `createHandler`'s catch-all auto-reported it. The only one visible in the log.
- **`confirmPINReset/entry.ts`** (the "reset your PIN via email link" flow, for BOTH vault and emergency PINs) — has its own outer `try/catch`, swallows everything into a generic 500. Silently broken, zero incidents logged.
- **`verifyEmergencyPIN/entry.ts`** (`action:'setup'`/`'verify'`) — same self-catching shape, silently broken. This is the credential guarding a first-responder's access to a traveler's emergency medical manifest during an actual emergency — the most safety-critical of the three. Its own comment (pre-fix) read: *"This was 200k while the client, the docs and the security copy all stated 600k; the Emergency PIN is the credential guarding the emergency vault, so it gets the documented strength"* — real, confirmed evidence of a deliberate prior 200k→600k security-hardening change that broke verification without anyone checking the target runtime could actually compute 600k. CLAUDE.md's own "PIN Vault... Server-side: 200k iterations" line (Security-Critical Systems section, now corrected) was stale, describing the pre-that-change state.

**Ruled out, confirmed by reading the code**: `src/lib/vaultPINHashing.js` and `src/lib/vault/offlineVaultPIN.js` also hardcode 600,000 iterations, but run in the *browser* (imported by `EmergencyPINSetup.jsx`/`VaultPINGate.jsx`/`ResetPIN.jsx`) for a separate offline/local-verification path — browser WebCrypto doesn't carry this Deno-edge-runtime-specific cap, and none of the three broken server functions ever receives a pre-hashed value from the client (each does 100% of its own hashing server-side from a raw PIN). Not touched.

Presented the full scope to Portia (she'd only asked about `verifyVaultPIN`) via AskUserQuestion — she confirmed: fix all three, same root cause, don't leave the emergency-access one quietly broken.

**Shipped**: `base44/shared/pinHashing.ts` (new) — the two real hash "shapes" this app needs, extracted from their 4 previously-independent inline copies into one implementation each (same "one implementation, multiple callers" discipline as `weatherEngine.ts`/`recoveryTransportDispatch.ts` elsewhere in this repo): `hashVaultPIN` (base64 output, used by `verifyVaultPIN.ts` + `confirmPINReset.ts`'s vault branch) and `hashEmergencyPIN` (hex output, used by `verifyEmergencyPIN.ts` + `confirmPINReset.ts`'s emergency branch — the two already had to "stay identical" per a comment; now structurally guaranteed). `MAX_PBKDF2_ITERATIONS = 100000` is the confirmed platform ceiling, straight from the real error message.

**The self-describing fix, not just a smaller magic number**: a fresh hardcoded value would just repeat the exact failure mode that caused this bug — no way to tell what a given stored hash actually needs if the "right" number changes again. `VaultPIN.jsonc`/`EmergencyPIN.jsonc` both gained an `iterations` field; every write now stores the exact count used, and verification always hashes at *that* record's own stored value rather than a single global assumption — a future change only affects new PINs, never silently breaks existing ones. A record with no stored `iterations` (predates this fix, and per `verifyEmergencyPIN`'s own history was almost certainly hashed at 200k/600k — both still over the 100k cap, unrecomputable on this runtime, full stop) now fails with a clean `{valid:false, error:'Your PIN needs to be reset — it was created before a required security update. Use "Forgot PIN?" to set a new one.'}` (409) instead of an unhandled throw — and explicitly never counts as a failed attempt (it isn't a wrong guess, and shouldn't risk locking out a PIN that was already unusable). `VaultPIN`'s pre-PBKDF2-migration plain-SHA256 fallback path (no `pin_salt` at all) is untouched — genuinely unaffected by any of this.

**Verification**: `base44/shared/pinHashing.ts` (new), `base44/entities/VaultPIN.jsonc`, `base44/entities/EmergencyPIN.jsonc`, `base44/functions/verifyVaultPIN/entry.ts`, `base44/functions/confirmPINReset/entry.ts`, `base44/functions/verifyEmergencyPIN/entry.ts` — all need a **Publish**. A pre-existing redteam test (`PIN: emergency PIN hashing is 600k...`) had pinned the exact bug as an invariant — updated to check both files source their iteration count from the shared module instead of a literal, rather than deleted (the real intent — never silently weaken PIN hashing — still holds). New test (`PBKDF2 ITERATIONS`) pins: the shared constant never exceeds 100,000; all three functions import from the shared module with zero local `deriveBits`/`600000` remaining; both entities declare `iterations`; both verify-shaped functions gate on a missing `iterations` value before ever attempting a hash. Lint clean, 664/664 vitest, 234/234 redteam (1 rewritten, 1 new), typecheck/build unaffected (Deno function files aren't in tsc's scope). Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: whether any real `VaultPIN`/`EmergencyPIN` records exist today and would hit the legacy-reset path — Portia checks after publishing whether any real users need a heads-up.

## Base44's own audit tooling cites a stale doc as if it were live — a real hygiene gap, plus 3 real findings that surfaced while checking (2026-08-16)

Portia relayed a second, expanded "backend audit" from Base44's own assistant — the same 3 items as before (PBKDF2, chunk-loading, weather 429s) plus new ones: hardcoded secrets, N+1 query patterns, missing Stripe webhook signature verification. Checked every claim against the real code before acting on any of it, same discipline as every round this session — and found the audit tool itself has a real problem: `src/TECHNICAL_AUDIT_REPORT.md` is dated **June 1, 2026**, over two months stale, and something is feeding its "remaining risks" back as if they were live findings. Proved this directly: the doc lists "missing webhook signature verification" as unresolved, but `stripePaymentWebhook`/`stripeIdentityWebhook` both already verify via the shared `verifyStripeSignature.ts` (confirmed by reading the code, and by a redteam test that's been green the whole time). Same for "N+1 query pattern in workflow emails" — from the same stale doc, which *even at the time* already marked it fixed ("✅ Concurrent email dispatch"). `src/` also has a cluster of sibling stale-audit markdown files (`REFACTOR_SUMMARY.md`, `QUICK_REFERENCE.md`, `PRODUCTION_FIXES_SUMMARY.md`, `ARCHITECTURE_REFACTORING_SUMMARY.md`, `ARCHITECTURE_AUDIT.md`) — flagged to Portia as a likely repeat source of this same confusion, not touched (her call whether to archive/delete them).

The doc's "hardcoded emails" claim was mostly a false positive — `sendTestEmail`'s fixed recipient is a deliberate security allowlist (a redteam-guarded test-only address, the opposite of a vulnerability) — but checking it surfaced two real, current, previously-unknown findings in `stripeIdentityWebhook/entry.ts`:

1. **A genuinely hardcoded, wrong-domain admin address.** Both of its failure-alert emails hardcoded `to: 'admin@morales.com'` — a domain that doesn't match this app's real one — instead of the `ADMIN_EMAIL` env var every other function in this repo already uses. These alerts were very likely going nowhere. **Fixed**: reads `Deno.env.get('ADMIN_EMAIL')`, skips (with a `console.error`) if unset, matching the established pattern everywhere else.
2. **A latent gap in the "nothing automated may mark a background check passed" invariant** — this codebase's own hardest-guarded safety rule (see the existing `PARTNERS: nothing automated may mark a background check passed` redteam test, previously scoped to `initiatePartnerVerification`/`activatePartner` only). `syncVerificationStateToProvider` (same file) reads a `ProviderVerification` row's own `status` and writes it straight onto `Doctor`/`TravelAgency`/`TaxiService.background_check_status` with no guard. Traced whether this is live-exploitable: it isn't, today — `initiateCheckrScreening` is an admitted stub (real Checkr integration commented out, "In production, integrate with Checkr API here"), so no automated source can currently produce a real `status:'passed'` background-check row for this function to propagate. But the sync function itself trusts an upstream status verbatim regardless of provenance — if a real Checkr webhook is ever wired up later without full context, this file would silently reopen the exact hole `initiatePartnerVerification` was already fixed for. **Fixed while it's cheap and inert**: an untrusted automated `'passed'` is downgraded to `'pending'` (a valid, honest enum value — no trustworthy answer yet) unless the record's own `manual_override_status === 'approved_by_admin'`. Redteam invariant extended (same test, new assertions) to cover this third file.

**Also fixed, a genuinely new finding, not from the stale doc**: the "Failed to fetch dynamically imported module" `ReliabilityIncident`s Base44's assistant flagged (correctly, as *a* real symptom) got the wrong remedy — "tell users to hard refresh" is a support workaround, not a fix, and the underlying UX was worse than that framing suggested. Traced the actual path: `/providers` (and similar routes) are wrapped in `<ErrorBoundary>`, so a stale-chunk failure reached its generic "Something didn't work" crash screen — whose own two recovery buttons (*Continue Later*, *Return Home*) both navigate within the exact same stale bundle that just failed, risking the identical error again. `src/main.jsx` already had an established, exact precedent for this class of problem (a `pageshow`/bfcache listener forcing a reload when a frozen snapshot might be stale) but no handler for Vite's own `vite:preloadError` event — the standard, documented signal for exactly this. **Fixed**: `main.jsx` now reloads once on `vite:preloadError` (sessionStorage-guarded 15s cooldown against a reload loop — a persistent failure past that window isn't a stale chunk anymore, so it's allowed to surface normally instead of looping). `ErrorBoundary.jsx` gained a second-layer safety net for the (browser-timing-dependent) case a preload error still reaches React's render pass: a distinct, honest "A newer version of this app is available" screen with one "Refresh Now" button, instead of the alarming generic copy and its two dead-end buttons — and, since this isn't an application bug, it no longer files a fresh `'high'`-severity `ReliabilityIncident` on every occurrence, which is exactly what made it look like an ongoing "backend instability" in the first place.

**Verification**: `base44/functions/stripeIdentityWebhook/entry.ts` needs a **Publish**; `src/main.jsx`/`src/components/ErrorBoundary.jsx` ship with the normal frontend deploy. Redteam: extended the existing `PARTNERS: nothing automated may mark a background check passed` test to cover the third file and the hardcoded-email regression; new `STALE CHUNK RECOVERY` test pins the `vite:preloadError` listener, its reload-loop guard, `ErrorBoundary`'s pattern match, and that it no longer reports an incident for this class. Lint clean, typecheck at the same 4-error pre-existing baseline, 664/664 vitest, 235/235 redteam (1 extended, 1 new), `npm run build` exit 0. Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: whether `vite:preloadError` actually fires reliably across the real browsers Morales' users are on — Portia verifies after the next real deploy by watching whether these specific incidents stop recurring.

## `generateLiveLocationRequestLink` — live M-Care report, both of Base44's assistant's specific claims disproven (2026-08-16)

Portia showed a live M-Care screenshot: she confirmed the location-share link ("Yes, send the link"), the agent's own `read_LiveLocation`-labeled tool call showed a green "success" badge, and the very next message was M-Care being honest that the link service "isn't responding right now." Base44's assistant proposed two specific things to check — both checked directly and both wrong, the same pattern as the stale-audit-doc round above: (1) "ensure `m_care.jsonc` explicitly lists `generateLiveLocationRequestLink` in the tools array" — parsed the live `tool_configs` directly, it's already there (93 total grants). (2) "the agent's router logic (`base44/functions/routeMCareMessage/entry.ts`) might be failing to pass `case_id`" — `routeMCareMessage`/`mCareRouter.ts` have been confirmed dead code since Phase 7 (M-Care runs on a real native Base44 Agent now, not this old router); re-confirmed here by grepping every reference in `src/` — the only two hits are doc-comments in `BookingIntentEntry.jsx`/`AvailabilityIntentEntry.jsx` mentioning it by name, zero live imports or calls. Investigating that file would have been a dead end.

The real, matching pattern instead: this is the same "success badge reflects the HTTP round-trip, not the business-logic outcome" shape already documented once in this file (Phase 51, `discoverProviderCandidates`) — and, checked directly, `generateLiveLocationRequestLink/entry.ts` had exactly the diagnosability gap that made the earlier one hard to pin down too: every one of its 3 error paths (a transient case-ownership-lookup failure misreported as "access denied"; `LiveLocationRequest.create()` failing; and — found while reading it — an unset `APP_URL` silently producing a domain-less `/share-location/:token` relative link, which would be unusable from an external device the instant this ever happened, quietly failing an unsafe patient without saying so) swallowed the real error completely, no logging anywhere. Applied the same fix as `getGeolocationAndCurrency` earlier today: a small local `reportBestEffort()` wraps `reportIncident` (bounded 2s timeout race, matching `createHandler.ts`'s own catch-all — never fire-and-forget, since the isolate can tear down right after the response returns). The `APP_URL`-missing check was also moved to run *before* the `LiveLocationRequest` write, not after, so a config gap can't leave an orphaned, never-usable row behind.

**Verification**: needs a Base44 **Publish**. Lint clean, typecheck unaffected (Deno function files aren't in tsc's scope), 664/664 vitest, 235/235 redteam (unchanged — no entity/tool/permission surface touched, this is diagnostics-only). Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: what the *actual* root cause is — the fix makes it visible (a real `ReliabilityIncident` row with the true error message) rather than guessing at it blind. Portia re-triggers the same flow after publishing and checks the incident log for the real reason.

## `generateLiveLocationRequestLink` follow-up — the badge was for a different tool entirely (2026-08-16)

Base44 did the exact investigation asked of it and reported back real, useful data: a direct test of `generateLiveLocationRequestLink` succeeded (200, valid token + URL), and no `ReliabilityIncident` row exists for that feature at all — confirming the function was never the problem. Base44 couldn't pull the actual per-turn tool-call log (the live agent runtime's turn history isn't exposed as a queryable entity, only `base44/agents/m_care.jsonc`'s static tool grants are), but it corroborated the one fact that mattered: only one tool badge was visible in Portia's screenshot, `read_LiveLocation`, and both tools were confirmed granted, so nothing was blocking the agent from calling the real one.

That's the actual bug. `read_LiveLocation` (entity-level, `{entity_name:'LiveLocation', allowed_operations:['read']}`) and `generateLiveLocationRequestLink` (the function that actually creates the link and returns a URL) are two different, both-granted tools. The instructions' own numbered SAFETY flow spells out the correct order — step 2 calls `generateLiveLocationRequestLink` and sends the traveler the url; only steps 3-4 ("WAIT + CONFIRM" / "MONITOR") read `LiveLocation`, to check whether the traveler has actually tapped share yet. A read against a case with no link ever generated returns zero rows — which is a *correct, successful* read, not a failure — but the agent appears to have narrated that empty result as "the link service isn't responding" instead of either going back to step 2 or saying plainly that nothing's been shared yet. This is functionally the same class of bug RULE 3 (NO INVENTED DATA) already exists to prevent elsewhere: a claim ("the service is down") not actually grounded in any real tool failure.

**Fixed**: one clarifying sentence appended directly to step 4 of the LIVE LOCATION / SAFETY numbered flow in `m_care.jsonc` — same byte-level safe-edit discipline as every prior addition (raw-byte anchor-uniqueness check confirmed via Python before writing, `json.loads` validity + `tool_configs` count unchanged at 93 confirmed after, plain ASCII/no unicode in the new text). It states plainly that an empty `LiveLocation` read means no link has been generated yet, not a broken service, and instructs the agent to call `generateLiveLocationRequestLink` right then if it hasn't yet this conversation — never to describe a location or link service as down, unresponsive, or unavailable unless a real tool call actually failed.

**Verification**: text-only `m_care.jsonc` instructions edit, `tool_configs` count unchanged (93 before and after) — needs a **Publish** naming `m_care.jsonc`. Lint clean, typecheck at the same 4-error pre-existing baseline, 664/664 vitest, 235/235 redteam (unchanged — no entity/tool/permission surface touched), `npm run build` exit 0. Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: whether this was actually the full story (Base44 could only confirm circumstantial evidence — one visible badge, both tools grantable — not a definitive per-turn call log) and whether the new instruction actually stops the false "isn't responding" narration in a real conversation — Portia verifies live by re-triggering the same "share my location" flow after publishing.

## `base44.functions.invoke()` returns the raw axios response, not the JSON body — a footgun that had already bitten 4 real features (2026-08-16)

Portia asked for a full backend audit (bugs, wiring gaps, crash risks). One of the four parallel audit passes (M-Care real-time feature stability) surfaced a structural SDK footgun, verified directly against `node_modules/@base44/sdk`'s own source before trusting it: the axios client backing `base44.functions` is constructed with `interceptResponses: false` (`dist/client.js`), and `createAxiosClient` (`dist/utils/axios-client.js`) only installs the `response => response.data` unwrapping interceptor when that flag is true — so, unlike `base44.entities.*` calls, **`base44.functions.invoke(...)` always resolves to the full axios response object** (`{data, status, headers, ...}`), never the JSON body directly. The established, correct pattern throughout this codebase (confirmed present in the large majority of ~300 real call sites sampled) is to read `res?.data` — but it's an easy trap for freshly-written code to fall into, and it did, repeatedly, in some of this project's newest and most safety-relevant surfaces:

- **`DriverMapWidget.jsx`** (`poll()`) — `setData(res)` instead of `setData(res?.data)`. Every field the widget reads (`transport_status`, `driver_name`, `driver_location`, `dispatched_at`, etc.) was always `undefined` — meaning the driver live-tracking map shipped 2026-08-15 (and specifically re-audited and "fixed" the same day for a different bug) has **never actually worked**: permanently stuck on "No driver assigned yet," and since its terminal-state check (`arrived`/`completed`/`cancelled`) could never be true, it polled `getDriverLocationStatus` every 10 seconds forever, directly contradicting its own documented "never polls indefinitely" contract.
- **`ShareLiveLocation.jsx`** (`loadContext()`) — same bug, more consequential: `ctx` (the entire page's data source) was set from the raw response. `ctx.role`, `ctx.patient_name`, `ctx.reason`, `ctx.expires_at` were all always `undefined` — including `ctx.role === 'driver'`, the exact branch the 2026-08-15 "Driver live-tracking map" section above added specifically to stop showing a driver patient-framed consent copy. That fix was real and correctly written, but had no real data to ever branch on, so it never actually took effect. This is the live, public, token-gated page a driver or a distressed traveler opens straight from an SMS link — the least-recoverable place for this bug to hide, since neither audience would know anything was wrong versus the page just looking a little generic.
- **`MessageBubble.jsx`**'s `ProcedureMediaCard` (Tier-2 `getProcedureIllustration` fallback, shipped 2026-08-15 for the "broad picture coverage" pass) — `res?.found`/`res.image_url` instead of `res?.data?.found`. The newly-expanded illustration coverage silently never worked; any procedure outside the original static 69 always showed "I don't have a diagram for that yet" even when the server had a real image.
- **`ProximityWatcher.jsx`** — lower severity: `result?.relevant`/`result?.message` read off the raw response, so the AI-personalized upgrade to a proximity nudge silently never applied; the generic nudge always shows instead, never crashes.

All four fixed at the exact point the response is captured (`const body = res?.data`), so every downstream read in each file is correct without needing to touch each one individually. **Scope of this pass, stated honestly**: roughly 300 call sites use `base44.functions.invoke` across this repo. A full manual audit of all of them wasn't feasible in one pass — instead, every call using the `.then((res) => ...)` chained style was checked by hand (10 files, all already correct), and a small heuristic script (regex-matched the invoke-result variable, scanned a ~25-line window for property access not going through `.data`) swept the remaining `await`-assignment style call sites, flagging exactly the 4 above out of ~105 candidates and no others. That script is a reasonable first pass, not a guarantee — it can miss destructuring (`const {data} = res`) or usage beyond its line window. Worth a dedicated, more rigorous sweep (or a lint rule flagging any property access on a `functions.invoke()` result that isn't `.data`) as a follow-up, not assumed clean from this pass alone.

**Verification**: pure frontend fixes, no Base44 publish needed. Lint clean, typecheck at the same 4-error pre-existing baseline, 664/664 vitest (unchanged — none of the four fixed files has a dedicated test file), 235/235 redteam (unchanged — no entity/tool/permission surface touched), `npm run build` exit 0. Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: whether the driver map and share-location page actually render correctly now on a real dispatched ride — Portia verifies live on the next real ride dispatch or distress-location-share.

## Full backend audit (2026-08-16) — life-safety cron loops now isolate per-record failures

Portia asked for a full backend audit: bugs, wiring gaps, anything that could crash mid-use. Four parallel passes covered scheduled-job wiring, edge-function auth/crash-risk patterns, dead functions and entity field-name drift, and the newest M-Care real-time surfaces (the `res.data` sweep above came out of that fourth pass). Full findings from all four, including the ones not yet fixed, are in that session's own record — this section covers only what got fixed this pass, chosen because it's the same "one bad record aborts the whole batch" bug class this project has hit and fixed before, just newly found in two of the highest-consequence cron jobs in the repo.

**`escalateSoloCheckIn/entry.ts`** (the 2h/3h/5h/9h missed-check-in escalation ladder, every 15 min) processed every overdue traveler in one loop with no per-iteration try/catch — a single record throwing (a malformed row, a transient write conflict) aborted the entire loop, silently skipping every check-in *after* it in that run, including anyone overdue for the 5h/9h security/police tier. Fixed: the whole per-check-in body (all four tiers) is now wrapped in its own try/catch that logs and `continue`s to the next check-in on failure — a bad record can no longer starve the rest of the batch.

**`checkPartnerSLABreaches/entry.ts`** (partner-unresponsive auto-backup, same 15-min cron) had the same shape, worse blast radius: `makeToken()` throws by design (fail-closed) if `PORTAL_TOKEN_SECRET` is ever unset, called unguarded inside the per-case loop — one missing env var would have aborted not just the rest of the vendor-pending loop, but the entirely separate doctor-SLA loop that runs after it in the same outer try/catch, for every case in that run. Fixed the same way: both the vendor-pending loop and the doctor-pending loop now wrap each case's processing in its own try/catch.

Neither fix changes any escalation threshold, message, or the existing idempotency flags (`sla_breached_*`, `status` transitions) — purely adds failure isolation around logic that was already correct when it ran.

**Verification**: both files are outside tsc's checked scope (Deno edge functions), so syntax was verified directly via `npx tsc --noEmit --allowJs --skipLibCheck` against each file — zero new errors beyond the expected `Cannot find name 'Deno'`/import-resolution noise every Deno function shows under this repo's Node-configured tsc. Lint clean, typecheck at the same 4-error pre-existing baseline, 664/664 vitest, 235/235 redteam (unchanged — no entity/tool/permission surface touched), `npm run build` exit 0. Needs a **Publish** naming both files. Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: whether a real record failure during a real cron tick now correctly isolates instead of aborting the batch — this is genuinely hard to trigger on demand; Portia doesn't need to do anything special to verify it, just knows the protection is now there if it ever happens.

**Remaining findings from this audit, not touched that pass**: `processPaymentCascade` has no internal-secret gate and can create duplicate `EscrowHold` rows for the same payment; `findDoctorBackup`'s no-backup-available branch can leave a case silently orphaned if it throws after a doctor declines/withdraws; `storeLiveLocationUpdate` checks `caseRecord?.client_id`, a field `CaseRecord` doesn't have (real field is `client_email`), so its "your driver is on the way" push can never fire; `runRegistryLookup`'s auth gap (already known, already in this file, confirmed still live); ~74 functions bypass the shared `createHandler` middleware (most have their own auth checks, so not wide open, but none get its default rate limiting — a real structural drift worth a dedicated migration pass); and two lower-severity `MCareOrb.jsx` bugs (a distress-conversation exchange can silently vanish from the visible chat on the next real message; "New Journey" doesn't stop in-progress Talk Mode speech).

## Full backend audit (2026-08-16), part 2 — the 8 never-scheduled cron functions, wired

Direct follow-up to the audit above: Portia asked to fix item #1 next. Read all 8 functions in full before touching the workflow file, rather than trusting the earlier audit summary secondhand — confirmed each is real, correctly gated (`cronAuthorized`), and idempotent off a one-time flag before wiring it anywhere.

**Where they went**: `freshness-cron.yml`, not `safety-cron.yml` — none of these is a life-safety escalation, they're business/operational reminders, and `freshness-cron.yml`'s own header already exists specifically for "not life-safety" jobs with lighter FAILED-list reporting. Cadence assigned by re-deriving what's actually safe, not by copying each function's own header comment verbatim:

- **Folded into the existing hourly tier** (`autoPartnerPortalDelivery`, `autoTriggerDoctorVerification`, `checkStalledSignups` — each says "every 30 min" in its own header; `checkAbandonedBookings` — says "every 2 hours"): every branch in all four is gated on a one-time `*_sent_at`/`*_released_at` flag, so running them hourly instead of every-30-min/2h only means the flag gets set up to ~30–90 minutes later than the stated intent, never a duplicate send — the identical reasoning `safety-cron.yml`'s own header already uses for its 15-minute tier ("checking more often just means a threshold crossing is caught sooner, not that anything fires more often than intended"). Not worth a brand-new 30-minute schedule trigger (would double this workflow's daily run count) for a business nudge, not a safety escalation.
- **Added to the existing daily tier** (`sendPaymentReminders`, `sendProcedurePrepReminders`, `expireDoctorVerifications`, `sendQuoteReminders`): each says "run daily" and the first three are gated on exact-day-boundary one-time flags (`daysLeft === 7/3/1/0`), so they're actually safe at any cadence — but `sendQuoteReminders` has no per-send flag at all (its gate is a wall-clock stage window, `age < HR_48` etc.), so running it more often than daily would genuinely re-send the same stage's reminder to the same partner multiple times in one day. Kept all four together at daily rather than splitting `sendQuoteReminders` out alone, both for the safer default and because it matches the cadence its own header already claims.
- **All 8 also added to the `workflow_dispatch` manual-run catch-up list**, matching the existing pattern for every other freshness job — one click runs everything.

**`expireDoctorVerifications` needed a real code fix before it could be wired at all**: it was gated on `base44.auth.me()` + an admin-role check, not `cronAuthorized` — a scheduler has no user session, so it would 403 on every automated call regardless of workflow wiring. Fixed to `cronAuthorized` (cron secret OR admin session), matching every other function in this file — the admin-triggered path is unchanged, a real scheduler can now also drive it.

**A small, adjacent, already-known bug fixed in passing**: `sendProcedurePrepReminders`'s best-effort push-notification call was missing `internal_secret: Deno.env.get('CRON_SECRET')` — `sendPushNotification` requires `internalOrAdminAuthorized` for a service-role caller, so this push had been silently 403'ing on every single call since the function was written, with the email half masking it completely (this exact pattern — a forgotten `internal_secret` field, hidden by a sibling channel that still worked — had already been found and fixed at two other call sites; this was a third). About to wire this function into a live cron for the first time made it worth fixing rather than knowingly shipping a bug just spotted.

**Deliberately not touched, flagged not silently fixed**: `checkAbandonedBookings`' own per-case loop has the identical "no per-iteration try/catch" shape the two life-safety functions above were just fixed for — not fixed here, since it's a lower-severity operational job (not a life-safety escalation ladder) and this pass was scoped to wiring, not another isolation pass; worth folding into a future hardening round.

**Verification**: `.github/workflows/freshness-cron.yml`'s YAML validity confirmed via `js-yaml` (a transitive `node_modules` dependency, no new install needed), its embedded bash script's syntax confirmed separately via `bash -n` on the extracted `run:` block. Both modified `.ts` files (outside tsc's normal scope) verified the same way as the loop-isolation fix — `npx tsc --noEmit --allowJs --skipLibCheck` against each, zero new errors beyond the standard Deno-environment noise. Lint clean, typecheck at the same 4-error pre-existing baseline, 664/664 vitest, 235/235 redteam (unchanged — no entity/tool/permission surface touched), `npm run build` exit 0. Needs a **Publish** naming `sendProcedurePrepReminders/entry.ts` and `expireDoctorVerifications/entry.ts` — the workflow file itself needs no Base44 publish (it's a GitHub Actions file, picked up on merge to `main`). Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: whether a real run of any of these 8 actually fires correctly end-to-end — Portia can check the Actions tab after the next real hourly/daily tick, or trigger `workflow_dispatch` manually to test sooner.

## 13 functions — including OTP login — had no real Deno.serve() entrypoint at all (2026-08-16)

Right after the previous push, Base44's Builder started failing to sync entirely: "Couldn't sync your backend functions. Fix the function errors in GitHub, then sync again," with no per-function detail visible in that screen. Base44 support's own ticket response named the real, checkable candidates directly: a missing `Deno.serve()` entrypoint, or an unsupported bare Node built-in import.

Verified locally rather than guessed at, using tools closer to what a real bundler does than `tsc` alone: wrote a script that bundles every `base44/functions/*/entry.ts` with **esbuild** (the actual bundler, not just the type-checker) across all 365 real function directories, resolving real relative imports and only externalizing genuinely-external specifiers (`npm:*`, `node:*` — Deno's own Node-compat imports like `node:crypto` are legitimate and used correctly in 6 functions, `base44:runtime` — a real Base44 platform virtual module used in 2). Zero bundle errors anywhere. Also re-swept function-directory purity across all 365 (only the known-dead `_shared/` mirror has extra files) and re-validated all 147 entity `.jsonc` files plus the M-Care agent config with a real JSONC-tolerant parser (`json5`, already a transitive dependency — plain `json.loads` incorrectly flags legitimate comments/trailing commas as errors, a false-positive trap). All clean.

That ruled out syntax and resolution — which pointed straight at Base44 support's other named cause. Grepped every `entry.ts` for a literal `Deno.serve(` call and found **13 functions with none**: `checkStalledSignups`, `enrollExternalJourney`, `generateLiveLocationRequestLink`, `getDriverLocationStatus`, `getLiveLocationRequest`, `markTransportArrived`, `retryDriverLocationSms`, `sendOtp`, `storeLiveLocationUpdate`, `submitDoctorCorrection`, `trackSignupAbandon`, `validateGuardianRequirement`, `verifyOtp`. All 13 used `export default createHandler(fn, opts);` instead of the pattern `createHandler.ts`'s own doc comment has always specified: `Deno.serve(createHandler(fn, opts));`. Confirmed by reading `createHandler.ts` directly: the factory just returns a plain `(req: Request) => Promise<Response>` function — exporting that as the module's default never registers it with Deno's HTTP server. Base44's real sync/bundle step needs the literal call to exist; it does not auto-wrap a bare default export.

**Real severity, not cosmetic**: `sendOtp` and `verifyOtp` are the phone/OTP login functions. Six of the other eleven (`generateLiveLocationRequestLink`, `getDriverLocationStatus`, `getLiveLocationRequest`, `markTransportArrived`, `retryDriverLocationSms`, `storeLiveLocationUpdate`) are the entire driver-live-tracking-map feature this same file already documented being built and reviewed twice this week — every prior "it works, confirmed via a direct test call" note about that feature almost certainly reflects Base44's dashboard **test** button, which appears to invoke a function directly rather than through the same `Deno.serve()`-registered HTTP path real production traffic uses — explaining how testing could report success while the function was never actually deployable for real use.

**Fixed**: a small script rewrote all 13 files — `export default createHandler(` → `Deno.serve(createHandler(`, plus the matching extra `)` before the final `;` on each function's own closing options line (verified per-file via a uniqueness assertion before writing, not a blind global replace). Re-swept afterward: zero files missing `Deno.serve(`, all 365 still bundle cleanly with esbuild.

**New structural redteam invariant** (`BUNDLER: every function entry.ts actually calls Deno.serve...`), matching this file's existing sibling check for the directory-purity bug (same bug class, different mistake) — sweeps every real function directory and fails if any `entry.ts` doesn't contain a literal `Deno.serve(` call, so this exact regression can't silently reappear.

**Verification**: all 13 fixed files re-checked with the same esbuild-bundle method plus `npx tsc --noEmit --allowJs --skipLibCheck` (zero new errors beyond standard Deno-environment noise). Lint clean, typecheck at the same 4-error pre-existing baseline, 664/664 vitest, 236/236 redteam (1 new), `npm run build` exit 0. Needs a **Publish** naming all 13 files — this is very likely what actually unblocks Base44's "Couldn't fetch your code" sync failure, though that can only be confirmed live, by Portia retrying the sync after this lands. If the sync still fails after this, the remaining lead from Base44 support (unsupported bare, non-`node:`-prefixed Node built-in imports) is already ruled out — none exist anywhere in this codebase, confirmed by the same full sweep.

## MCP-exposure audit — 9 backend auth gaps closed, `User` RLS locked down (2026-08-17)

Portia asked what Base44's "App MCP" feature (exposes a published app's data/functions to external AI assistants like Claude/ChatGPT via `/api/mcp`, "Public: read-only" or "Sign in required: OAuth" access modes) would actually expose before deciding whether to enable it. Three parallel research passes found the exposure model itself is opt-in per entity/function/agent (three independent toggles, not automatic) — but surfaced real, live gaps that exist **independent of MCP entirely**, exploitable today via a direct API call. Portia's decision: don't enable MCP yet; fix everything real found regardless.

**What Base44's own docs confirm, and what they leave genuinely unconfirmed** (worth asking Base44 directly before ever enabling this): "Public" mode only ever grants read, never write, and only for entities individually toggled on; "Custom tools" (backend functions) are a separate, also per-function opt-in toggle; "OAuth" mode runs the assistant as the signed-in user under their normal RLS/role permissions. **Not confirmed**: whether an entity's own RLS still applies underneath the "Public" MCP toggle, and whether a function enabled as an MCP "Custom tool" could be called by an external AI assistant completely bypassing `m_care.jsonc`'s own 93-tool allowlist and its Rule 1-23 safety discipline — MCP's toggle system and the M-Care agent's `tool_configs` are architecturally separate, so nothing here rules that out.

**9 backend functions had `requireAuth: false` with no real gate, out of 155 checked (131 were already genuinely, deliberately self-gated).** Each got the fix its real callers actually needed, not a generic guess — designed from real caller evidence, which caught two wrong first-draft assumptions before any code was written:
- `notifySlackHighRisk` — added `internalOrAdminAuthorized` (same gate as sibling `notifySlackAssignment`); its real caller `createCaseFromConsultation.ts` (cron-triggered, no forwardable session) now passes `internal_secret: Deno.env.get('CRON_SECRET')`.
- `portalHubWorkflow` — **no auth gate added.** Real callers include an admin dashboard, a backend retry function, and an unverifiable-but-real Base44-native dashboard automation a redteam test already documents as a deliberately-open gap (`AUTH: known-open gaps left deliberately`) — an auth gate here would break real callers and contradict a pinned invariant. Fixed the actual exposure instead: added an idempotency guard (checks the existing `WorkflowEvent.stage`/`doctor_status` before re-running the LLM risk check and re-notifying doctors), so a repeated/duplicate invocation can no longer spam doctor emails or burn LLM calls.
- `recalculateTripTimings` — fixed the `user && !allowedRoles.includes(...)` short-circuit bug (same shape already found and fixed in `escalateMissedDriverHandshake`/`pollActiveTripFlights`, missed here) — but with the *correct* replacement for this function specifically: an ownership check (`isAdmin || trip.user_email === user.email || trip.user_id === user.id`) matching what its only real caller, `manageTripPause`, already runs on itself, not `cronAuthorized` (which would have 403'd the real, legitimate call path — a patient resuming their own paused trip).
- `sendPaymentReceipt` — added `internalOrAdminAuthorized`; its real caller `processPaymentCascade` already passed `internal_secret` to its other sibling dispatch calls, just not this one — added it there too.
- `activateEmergencyBeacon` — **no change.** A pinned redteam invariant explicitly forbids gating this on auth at all — it's an offline mesh-relay SOS device that may have no session established, and the existing default IP+user rate limit is the correct, deliberate mitigation. First-draft instinct here (add a PIN/ownership check) would have been wrong.
- `discoverProviderCandidates`, `verifyDiscoveredCandidate`, `mcareResearchAndLearn` — all three are real M-Care agent tools, intentionally anonymous-accessible by design (not an auth gap) — the fix was cost control, not identity: added `rateLimit: { max: 8, windowSeconds: 300 }` to each, matching the existing cost-conscious sibling pattern (`analyzeDestinationSafety`, `extractClinicalNote`).
- `trackSignupAbandon` — stacked a per-submitted-value rate limit (`checkRateLimit(base44, \`signup_abandon:${phone||email}\`, 3600, 5)`, same pattern as `requestGuardianAccess`'s per-address cap) on top of the existing default IP limit — previously an attacker could mass-create `SignupProgress` rows for arbitrary third-party contacts, each later triggering a real automated nudge via `checkStalledSignups`.

**`User` entity had zero RLS at all — fully open read+write to anyone, anonymous included.** Flagged once before (2026-07-25 RLS audit) and left deliberately untouched pending its own staged change, per this file's own standing caution about breaking login platform-wide. A dedicated research pass found it's now safe: **zero** client-side call sites anywhere in `src/` read another user's `User` record via the RLS-governed `entities` module — every identity read/write in this app goes through Base44's structurally separate `auth.me()`/`auth.updateMe()` module instead — and every backend cross-user read already uses `asServiceRole` (RLS-independent). Added `rls.read: self OR admin` (`data.email == {{user.email}}` OR `role: admin`). This closes real exposure: the entity carries `protection_type` (literally flags whether someone is a medical traveler), `account_deletion_requested_at`, and `profile_photo_url`. `create`/`update`/`delete` were deliberately left unspecified (not touched) — same reasoning, nothing real depends on them either way, and touching more than the confirmed-safe scope wasn't worth the risk this close to the RLS-lockdown caution already on record. **Smoke-test login/`auth.me()` after this specific change lands live** — the architectural inference that Base44's own auth machinery doesn't route through entity RLS is high-confidence but not something Base44's docs state explicitly.

**A second, previously-uncatalogued RLS bug found on 4 more entities**: `Clinic`, `CountryVerificationConfig`, `LocalizedGreeting`, `VisaRequirementSnapshot` all had `"read": {"user_condition": {"id": "{{user.id}}"}}` — a tautology comparing the requester's own id to itself, which always evaluates true for any logged-in user regardless of role (almost certainly a copy-paste mistake, since none of these 4 has an ownership field this could plausibly have been checking instead). Confirmed via a dedicated client-usage sweep that all 4 have zero direct client-side readers outside their own already-admin-gated pages, and none is granted to the M-Care agent — tightened all 4 to `role: admin`, matching each entity's own pre-existing `create`/`update`/`delete` gate.

**5 entities deliberately NOT touched this pass, flagged not silently left open**: `McareKnowledge`, `ProcedureKnowledge`, `ProcedureDietaryProtocol`, `BookingFallbackRule`, `VerifiedCredential`. All 5 have the same any-logged-in-user-readable gap (`McareKnowledge` and `VerifiedCredential` are genuinely sensitive — patient Q&A + email, and partner license/ID document URLs, respectively) but all 5 are also directly granted to the M-Care chat agent in `m_care.jsonc`'s `tool_configs`, and per the Base44 SDK's own documented behavior, an agent's entity-tool calls run under the *invoking user's own session* — meaning tightening these to admin-only would very likely silently break M-Care's own ability to answer procedure/knowledge questions for ordinary patients, the exact "agent goes mute with no clear error" failure mode this project has already been burned by once (see the JourneyEvent Phase 10 entry above). Real `asServiceRole`-wrapped functions already exist for 4 of the 5 (`recallMcareKnowledge`, `getProcedureKnowledge`, `getRecoveryNutritionPlan`, `handleCrisisReroute`) that could replace the raw entity grants in `tool_configs` — but rerouting an agent's tool grants is its own careful, separately-sequenced change (verify the wrapper function is Published and pingable *before* removing the entity grant, per this project's established Publish-order discipline), not something to bundle blind into a batch RLS pass.

**Verification**: touches `User.jsonc`, `Clinic.jsonc`, `CountryVerificationConfig.jsonc`, `LocalizedGreeting.jsonc`, `VisaRequirementSnapshot.jsonc`, and 9 function files (`notifySlackHighRisk`, `portalHubWorkflow`, `recalculateTripTimings`, `sendPaymentReceipt`, `discoverProviderCandidates`, `verifyDiscoveredCandidate`, `mcareResearchAndLearn`, `trackSignupAbandon`, plus the two caller-side fixes in `createCaseFromConsultation.ts`/`processPaymentCascade.ts`) — needs a **Publish** naming all of these. Lint clean, typecheck at the same 4-error pre-existing baseline, 664/664 vitest (unchanged — no new pure-logic module), 236/236 redteam (unchanged — including the `AUTH: known-open gaps left deliberately` test, confirming `activateEmergencyBeacon`/`portalHubWorkflow` weren't accidentally regressed), `npm run build` exit 0. Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: whether the real Base44-native automation calling `portalHubWorkflow` still works post-idempotency-guard, and whether `auth.me()`/login still work correctly after the `User` RLS change — Portia verifies both live after publishing. MCP itself remains disabled — this pass only closed the gaps that existed with or without it.

## M-Care Intelligent Scanner + Secure Document Vault — Phase 1, patient foundation (2026-08-17)

Portia asked for a native, in-chat document scanner — "I need to upload my passport" should open a real scanner (camera/upload, multi-page, quality checks, classification, verification routing), not a file-upload button — with an explicit audit-first requirement: inspect what already exists, name what's missing, flag any real vendor dependency instead of faking it, and wait for approval before writing code. Three parallel audits (upload/camera/OCR/vault infra; verification/security/audit-log infra; M-Care's chat-embedding mechanisms + offline architecture) found the real building blocks and two genuine forks, resolved with Portia before any code: **no client-side computer-vision library exists anywhere in this repo** for real Adobe-Scan-style live edge tracking — she chose a real camera preview with lightweight heuristic hints, with the actual quality verdict always coming from a server-side vision-LLM check after capture (reusing `analyzeDocumentIntelligence`'s proven technique, not the function itself — it's purpose-built for partner-fraud claims and the partner-specific `FraudIndicator` table, neither of which fits a patient's passport). And **v1 scope**: full pipeline, patient owner-type only, but a data model built generically across all 7 owner types from day one so extending to doctors/drivers/etc. is additive later, not a redesign.

**A real, previously-dead interactive-component mechanism, found and fixed as part of building this.** M-Care's chat already had a proven way to embed a full interactive component inline — when the agent calls a tool whose name `MessageBubble.jsx` matches (`computeSafeTScreening`/`safeT4LifeScan`), it renders `SafetyGateCard`, fed by the tool's real result, with an `onRespond` callback meant to resume the conversation. But `MCareOrb.jsx`'s actual `<MessageBubble>` call never passed `onRespond` at all — meaning `SafetyGateCard`'s own Proceed/Wait buttons (`{!responded && onRespond ? ...}`) have been silently dead since that card shipped, invisible because the card's loading/blocked/caution/cleared *display* states never depended on it. Fixed as a necessary part of this build (the new scanner card needs the exact same mechanism to report results back into the conversation) — `onRespond={sendAgentMessage}` now wired on the real call site, fixing both cards' interactivity, not just the new one.

**The old `BookingIntentEntry`/`AvailabilityIntentEntry`/`DoctorSignupChatFlow`/`TravelAgencySignupChatFlow` "swap a full component into the panel" pattern is confirmed dead code** (superseded by the Phase 7 native Base44 Agent architecture) — the scanner was deliberately NOT built on it. It renders exclusively through the `tool_calls`-name-match mechanism above.

**Shipped**:
- **`base44/entities/VaultDocument.jsonc`** (new) — the general, multi-owner (`patient`/`doctor`/`travel_agency`/`driver`/`companion`/`security_agency`/`other`), multi-page document record. Deliberately separate from `PassportVault`, which stays zero-knowledge and passport-only (the server never sees plaintext there — incompatible with OCR, which needs the server to see the image). `original_file_urls` is never overwritten by `processed_file_url`/`combined_pdf_url`. `verification_status` walks `unreviewed → scanned → ocr_complete → quality_passed → fields_extracted → verification_pending → verified | verification_failed | requires_human_review | not_applicable_no_authority` — the last state is the honest terminal "OCR succeeded, nothing real exists to check it against" case (passport/national-ID/visa/insurance authenticity — none of it has a real verification source anywhere in this app), distinct from `requires_human_review`. Read RLS is owner-or-admin; **create/update RLS is admin-only** — every real write goes through `asServiceRole` inside the two gated functions below, so a client can never directly fabricate a `verified` status via a raw entity write (the exact discipline this file's own RLS section already establishes for `JourneyEvent`).
