- **`base44/functions/openMcareScanner/entry.ts`** (new) — the real, tiny tool the agent calls to trigger the card. Resolves owner context from the caller's own session (never client-suppliable) and a best-guess `document_type_hint` via a fixed keyword map — deliberately LLM-free and file-free, so it's safe to grant from day one with minimal Publish-order risk.
- **`base44/functions/scanVaultDocument/entry.ts`** (new) — the real pipeline. One schema-driven `Core.InvokeLLM` vision call across all pages together (OCR + classify + extract + blur/glare/completeness/resolution quality signals), routed through a deterministic sequence: low classification confidence → ask the user what to save it as (never silently classify); quality failure → stop before verification (routing a document whose quality hasn't cleared would mean checking a registry against data not yet trusted); only once type and quality are both clean does real verification routing run — `registryLookup.ts`'s real US/CO/MX doctor-license check for the one case it covers, `not_applicable_no_authority` for informational types (flight docs, invoices, medical reports — no fraud/identity consequence), `requires_human_review` for identity/credential types with no authoritative source. Deterministic duplicate detection (same owner + type + extracted document number). Fires `logAuditEvent` (4 new allowlisted event types: `vault_document_scanned`, `vault_document_verification_result`, `vault_document_reviewed`, `vault_document_accessed`) and, for case-linked patients, `logJourneyEvent` (`document_vaulted`, a new `JourneyEvent.event_type`, `priority: 'low'`) so the outcome shows up as a real M-Care chat bubble, not just a silent database write.
- **`base44/functions/reviewVaultDocument/entry.ts`** (new) — the one admin-only Approve/Reject/Request-New-Scan action for the review queue, mirroring `DoctorVerificationAdmin.jsx`'s exact pattern. Approve is a real, human-attributed verification event (`verification_source: 'Manual admin review'`) — exactly what `requires_human_review` exists for, never the AI's own call.
- **`src/components/mcare/DocumentScannerCard.jsx`** (new) — the scanner itself: real `getUserMedia` camera preview (zero precedent existed for this anywhere in the app — every prior "camera" affordance was the OS's native `<input capture>` picker) with a live heuristic-hint overlay (`src/lib/scannerHeuristics.js`, new, pure/unit-tested — brightness, glare-ratio, a frame-fill proxy via inner/outer-region contrast, an edge-touch proxy for partial framing; deliberately not real contour detection, honestly documented as such), multi-page capture with retake/delete/reorder/rotate (real canvas re-encode), an "upload from device" fallback for camera-denied/PDF cases, and a results screen with the same 🔎/🛡️/✓-style honest verification badges this app already uses for provider trust tiers (never shows "Verified" without a real `verification_source`). Wired via `MessageBubble.jsx`'s tool-name-match (`toolCall.name === 'openMcareScanner'`).
- **`src/lib/pdfCombine.js`** (new) — the one new dependency this build introduces: `pdf-lib` (free, MIT-licensed, zero cost/account), for real client-side multi-page → single-PDF combination per the spec's "Save as PDF" requirement. A combined PDF is best-effort and never blocks the scan if it fails.
- **`src/components/dashboard/modules/VaultDocumentsPanel.jsx`** (new) — replaces `DocumentsModule.jsx` (deleted — a fully static mockup: a hardcoded `requiredDocs` array with fixed statuses, uploads that went to a public bucket and were never wired to any real entity). Reads live `VaultDocument` rows via a direct RLS-scoped client filter (matching `VaultDashboard.jsx`'s own established pattern — no dedicated listing function needed), shows a real "expiring soon" banner from actual `expiration_date` values.
- **`src/pages/AdminVaultDocumentReview.jsx`** (new, `/admin/vault-document-review`) — the human review queue, built directly on `DoctorVerificationAdmin.jsx`'s Approve/Reject-with-required-reason pattern.
- **`base44/agents/m_care.jsonc`** — two new `tool_configs` grants (`openMcareScanner`, function; `VaultDocument`, entity, **read-only** — never `create`/`update`, applying the Phase 8 lesson (`Doctor`/`TravelAgency`'s raw-write-grant mistake) from day one instead of relearning it for a new entity). New **RULE 24 — DOCUMENT SCANNER**: call `openMcareScanner` the moment someone indicates they need to provide/scan a document, narrate plainly, let the card do the actual work, and never say "verified" unless the card's own real `verification_status` says so — explicitly states this scanner is completely separate from the existing zero-knowledge passport vault (`uploadToVault`/`downloadFromVault`/`decryptPassportFile`/`verifyVaultPIN`), which stays exactly as off-limits to the agent as it always has been. Same byte-level safe-edit discipline as every prior `m_care.jsonc` change — a first-draft mistake (literal unescaped `"` characters around an example phrase in the new rule text) broke JSON validity and was caught immediately by this file's own established `json.loads`-equivalent verification step before anything was trusted, then fixed with the proper 2-char `\"` escape.
- New redteam invariant (`VAULT DOCUMENT`): `VaultDocument`'s RLS is owner-or-admin read / admin-only write, never wide-open; `openMcareScanner` never itself writes the entity; the agent's grant is structurally confirmed read-only; `verification_status = 'verified'` is structurally confirmed to only ever appear inside the real `runLookup()` result branch (never assignable from classification confidence alone) in `scanVaultDocument`, and always carries a real `verification_source` in `reviewVaultDocument`'s approve branch.

**Deliberately deferred, per the same discipline as every other large M-Care build this session, stated plainly, not silently dropped**: true interactive drag-crop and perspective correction (both genuinely CV-adjacent — rotate is real, crop/perspective-warp is not, alongside the already-agreed no-live-CV-library decision); offline encrypted capture-then-sync (no local-encrypted-queue mechanism exists anywhere in this repo yet — `offlineUploadQueue.js` is unencrypted and a different pattern; building this needs its own pass); proactive expiry-monitoring cron (the `expiration_date` field is captured from day one, ready for it — no scheduled job reads it yet); doctor/travel-agency/driver/companion/security-agency-facing dashboards and their own vault UIs (the data model already supports all 7 owner types — additive UI work later, not a redesign); wiring the scanner into `MCareAgent.jsx`'s separate full-page `/m-care` surface (same "`MCareOrb` first, parity later" scoping this project has used for every prior voice/interactive feature); a manual "📷 Scan a document" entry point in `AddImageMenu.jsx` (the agent-triggered path is the one the spec asked for; `MCareVaultUpload.jsx`'s existing simpler manual-attach dialog already covers "I just want to upload a file" without the scanner).

**Verification**: touches `VaultDocument.jsonc` (new), `JourneyEvent.jsonc`'s enum, `AuditLog.jsonc`'s enum, 3 new functions, and `m_care.jsonc` — needs a **Publish** naming all of these, with the usual extra caution on `openMcareScanner`'s tool grant (ping it directly after Publish, expect 400/401 not 404, before trusting the agent to call it). Lint clean, typecheck at the same 4-error pre-existing baseline (3 new TS errors surfaced and fixed during this build: `friendlyError`'s 2-arg contract called with only 1 arg in two new files, `Date` arithmetic needing explicit `.getTime()`, and a `pdf-lib`-returned `Uint8Array`'s stricter DOM buffer type needing the same `/** @type {any} */` cast convention `VoiceMode.jsx` already established), 682/682 vitest (18 new, `tests/scannerHeuristics.test.js`), 237/237 redteam (1 new). Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: whether the live camera preview and heuristic hints actually feel usable on a real device, whether the vision-LLM call correctly classifies/extracts across the full 19-type taxonomy in practice, and whether a real US/CO/MX doctor-license scan actually reaches `verified` end-to-end — Portia verifies all three live after publishing.

## 3-Way Care Gate — Portia's Care Room, built on the one real thread that already existed (2026-08-17)

Portia sent a detailed vision (with 4 reference screenshots of competitor patient/provider chat products) for a **secure "Care Room"** where a patient and their confirmed doctor communicate with M-Care sitting in as a third participant — translating both directions, confirming mutual understanding of instructions ("did both sides understand?"), catching logistics conflicts, flagging things that need clarification without diagnosing, gating contact behind provider-confirmation (a 3-tier gate: not confirmed → provider confirmed → care active), and surfacing a "Care Timeline" (a scannable shared-truth summary instead of a raw transcript).

Three parallel Explore passes + direct reads of the critical files found something significant before any design started: **the core of this already existed and worked in production, just scoped to the wrong moment.** `QuoteMessage` (entity) + `postCaseMessage` (backend) + `CaseThread.jsx` (frontend) was already a real, working, two-way patient↔doctor text thread — used only during quote-comparison (`MyQuotes.jsx`/`DoctorQuoteInbox.jsx`) — but `CaseThread` already worked with just a `case_id` (no `quote_id`), meaning it already behaved as an ongoing case-wide thread when called that way. It already had the malicious-action blocker, contact-scrubbing, and per-message audit logging built in, and — not yet wired up — already declared `body_translated`/`recipient_language` fields on the schema, unused. This changed the shape of the build from "invent a chat system" to "extend the one real thread that already works, and add M-Care as a real participant in it." Provider scope confirmed with Portia before building: doctor + patient + M-Care only for v1 — travel-agency/taxi/companion partners have zero existing free-text channel today (no RLS read-access, no portal UI), a real, separate follow-on.

**Gate — computed, not new stored state.** No 3-tier gate existed anywhere as stored state, but `CaseRecord.doctor_confirmation_status` (`PENDING`/`CONFIRMED`/`DECLINED`) was exactly the real signal to key off, so no new state machine was built. Gate 1 (not confirmed): the Care Room simply isn't rendered. Gate 2/3 (confirmed → care active): the same `CaseThread` component, mounted case-wide, becomes visible and stays available through the rest of the journey — no separate stored value distinguishing "just confirmed" from "care active," matching this app's own bias toward deriving state from real fields rather than inventing a parallel one.

**Data model — extended `QuoteMessage.jsonc`, no new entity.** `from_party` enum gained `'m_care'` (M-Care's own messages are now real, attributable rows in the same thread — not a separate channel). Three new fields turn any message into an optional confirm-request without a new message shape: `requires_confirmation`, `confirmed`, `confirmed_at`. RLS unchanged (`patient_email`/`doctor_email`/admin) — matches the confirmed-doctor-only v1 scope, a redteam invariant now pins this hasn't widened.

**Backend, three new/changed pieces, all best-effort and non-blocking:**
- **`base44/shared/translateText.ts`** (new) — plain text-to-text translation extracted from `walkieTalkieTranslate`'s own translate step (`Core.InvokeLLM` with a translate prompt) — the 3rd real use of that exact pattern in this codebase (`translateEmergencySOS`/`triggerSOS` each duplicate it inline), centralized here rather than a 4th copy. Falls back to the original text on any failure, matching every existing caller's own behavior.
- **`base44/functions/parseCareRoomMessage/entry.ts`** (new) — the Confirmation Engine's parse step, modeled directly on `parseAvailabilityIntent`: schema-driven `Core.InvokeLLM`, **zero entity writes**, deliberately conservative (most ordinary messages come back not-confirmable — a false negative just means M-Care stays quiet, never worse than nagging someone over something trivial). Extracts `is_confirmable`/`fact_type`/`summary`/`extracted_date`/`extracted_time`; never itself decides anything is confirmed.
- **`base44/functions/postCaseMessage/entry.ts`** (extended) — fixed a real gap the Care Room's own case-only calling shape exposed: `patient_email`/`doctor_email` on a new `QuoteMessage` row were previously sourced only from `quote`/`request` context, which is `null` once past the quote-comparison stage — now falls back to the real `CaseRecord.client_email`/`doctor_email` (and the notify-email/portal-link logic falls back the same way). After the human's own message is safely stored, a wrapped, swallowed reactive pipeline runs: (1) resolve each party's language (`Consultation.preferred_language` for the patient, `Doctor.language_preference` for the doctor) and translate via the new shared helper if they differ; (2) call `parseCareRoomMessage`, and on a confirmable fact, post a follow-up `m_care`-party message addressed to the recipient with `requires_confirmation: true`; (3) for an appointment-shaped fact with an extracted date, a best-effort check against the one clean source of a scheduled date this app has today, `CaseRecord.procedure_date` — flags a mismatch, never blocks, never diagnoses, and is honest in its own copy that fragmented transport-scheduling data elsewhere in this app means a real conflict could still be missed. Never runs on an `m_care`-authored message itself (no self-reactions).
- **`base44/functions/confirmCareRoomMessage/entry.ts`** (new) — the only place a confirmation is ever actually recorded. Only the addressed party (`to_party` match + their own email denormalized on that exact message) may confirm — not the sender, not an admin impersonating a party by accident, not an unrelated case member. Posts a small `m_care`-authored acknowledgment to the *other* party, so both sides end up with the same confirmation on record — the literal "did both sides understand?" audit trail Portia asked for.

**Frontend**: `CaseThread.jsx` extended (not replaced) — renders `body_translated` under the original with a "Translated" label when present, an `m_care`-badged message style (gold border, sparkle icon), and an "I understand" confirm button when a message needs the current viewer's own confirmation. `CareTimeline.jsx` (new) is deliberately built only from deterministic, already-real facts (doctor-confirmed flag, procedure, procedure date, each confirmation message's real `confirmed` state, each conflict-flag message) rather than re-summarizing free text — a scanned/guessed summary of a conversation is exactly the kind of unreliable shortcut this app avoids elsewhere; conflict flags are shown as open items with no inferred "resolved" state, since nothing in this pass tracks that reliably and guessing would be worse than not showing it. `CareRoomPanel.jsx` (new) is the patient-dashboard mount point — self-contained via the existing `useActiveCaseRecord` hook (the same one `TravelPassCard` already uses), renders nothing until `doctor_confirmation_status === 'CONFIRMED'`. The doctor-side equivalent is mounted directly in `DoctorDashboard.jsx`'s existing per-case card, right next to the date-confirm gate it already renders.

**Deliberately deferred, stated plainly**: travel-agency/taxi/companion parity (Portia's own choice this round); true real-time delivery (`.subscribe()` is proven elsewhere in this app — `HeartNotificationCenter.jsx`, `useJourneyEvents.js` — but no chat anywhere uses it yet; v1 keeps `CaseThread`'s proven 20s-polling pattern); passport sharing inside the room (stays in the separate, zero-knowledge `PassportVault` flow — `assignDoctorToCase` already hard-blocks doctor assignment without `medical_history_share_consent`, so a confirmed doctor has already-consented medical-summary access as a precondition, meaning v1 needed no new consent-gating logic for that half of "Provider Visibility"); a fully autonomous, continuously-reasoning M-Care participant (v1 is a deterministic, per-message-triggered reactive pipeline, not an open-ended agent loop — avoids a new agent-tool-grant/Publish-order risk class for no real benefit at this scope); retrofitting `translateEmergencySOS`/`triggerSOS` onto the new shared `translateText` helper.

**Verification**: touches `QuoteMessage.jsonc` (3 new fields + enum value) and 3 functions (`postCaseMessage` extended, `parseCareRoomMessage`/`confirmCareRoomMessage` new) — needs a **Publish** naming all four. New redteam invariant (`CARE ROOM`): `QuoteMessage`'s RLS is unchanged by the new fields; `confirmCareRoomMessage` structurally checks both `to_party` match and the caller's own denormalized email before allowing a confirm; `parseCareRoomMessage` never writes any entity; `postCaseMessage`'s reactive pipeline is wrapped in try/catch and never fires on an `m_care`-authored message. Lint clean, typecheck at the same 5-error pre-existing baseline (fixed one new, real error along the way: `CaseThread.jsx`'s `quoteId` prop had no default, so TS inferred it as required from its two pre-existing call sites, which would have broken the new no-`quoteId` Care Room usage — given an explicit `= null` default, the correct fix rather than patching around it at each new call site), 682/682 vitest (unchanged — no new pure-logic module; `parseCareRoomMessage`'s LLM-coupled logic isn't unit-tested from this checkout, consistent with this repo's established precedent for this class of function), 238/238 redteam (1 new), `npm run build` exit 0. Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: whether the Care Room actually renders for a real confirmed case, whether translation/confirmation/conflict-detection fire correctly on a real bilingual exchange, and whether the conflict-check's honest best-effort scope (procedure_date only) still feels useful in practice — Portia verifies live after publishing.

## M-Care super-agent — comprehensive procedure education via `explainProcedure` (2026-08-21)

Portia asked for M-Care to deliver a genuinely complete, clinician-quality explanation for any procedure question — overview, what happens before/during/after, real recovery realities, do's/don'ts, general risks/benefits/alternatives, and questions to ask the doctor — instead of the existing thin, safety-gated summary. Two parallel audits confirmed the real state first: there is no "PROCEDURE INQUIRY protocol" block in `m_care.jsonc` itself — it's a hardcoded `m_care_protocol` object *returned by* `getProcedureKnowledge/entry.ts` on every call (steps 0-5), and RULE 22 cross-referenced it by a stale step label ("step A2") dead since the 2026-08-15 renumbering — fixed to "step 0" while this file was already open for a related change. `ProcedureKnowledge`'s safety-curated fields (`risk_level`, `complication_rate`, `red_flag_combinations`, `smoker_warning`, `common_complications`) are admin-write-only by Portia's own standing decision never to fabricate clinical risk content — nothing in this build touches that boundary. The protocol's own existing step 3 already established the precedent this build extends: *"a confident (>=80%) researched answer can be shared as general procedure information, clearly labeled as researched rather than from the curated safety database"* — `mcareResearchAndLearn`'s confidence-gated, Tavily-first research brain is the already-approved mechanism for exactly this, just never applied to comprehensive procedure content specifically.

**Shipped**: `ProcedureKnowledge.jsonc` gained an additive `education_*`-prefixed field group covering all 7 mandated content blocks (overview, what-happens, recovery, dos-and-donts, benefits/alternatives) plus `education_source`/`education_confidence_score`/`education_updated_at`/`education_uncertainty_notes` metadata — every field optional, no migration, every existing curated row stays valid untouched. New `base44/functions/explainProcedure/entry.ts` mirrors `mcareResearchAndLearn`'s proven recall-first / Tavily-first / `add_context_from_internet`-fallback / confidence-gated-persist pattern (same `EDUCATION_CONFIDENCE_THRESHOLD = 80` convention), retargeted onto `ProcedureKnowledge` instead of the generic `McareKnowledge` Q&A cache: matches the procedure via the shared `procedureKnowledgeMatch.ts` scorer, returns a cached row directly when education content already exists and is confident, otherwise researches via a structured `response_json_schema` (no field anywhere resembling risk_level/approval/cleared/guaranteed — mirrors the `draftRecoveryGuidance` precedent) seeded with whatever curated safety fields already exist as anchor context the LLM must not contradict or restate as newly discovered. **The write payload structurally can never include `risk_level`/`complication_rate`/`red_flag_combinations`/`smoker_warning`/`common_complications`** — the one exception, `risk_level: 'Not Yet Assessed'` on a brand-new row, mirrors `generateProcedureIllustrations`'s own established honest-sentinel pattern and is redteam-pinned to never be any other value. `depth`/`focus` are presentation hints only, not separate fetch modes — the function always returns the full structured content in one call, so a follow-up question ("more on recovery," "what about alternatives") never needs a second round trip.

`getProcedureKnowledge`'s protocol object gained one additive step 6 (the existing steps 0-5 left byte-for-byte untouched, avoiding a repeat of the exact renumbering-drift bug just fixed) directing the agent to call `explainProcedure` right after step 0's image+overview, with clear headings, framed as safe to share immediately and boldly since it's general/population-level content — not a personalized risk assessment, which still requires step 1's medical-background question first. New **RULE 30 (COMPREHENSIVE PROCEDURE EDUCATION)** in `m_care.jsonc` states the quality bar and cross-references RULE 1/RULE 3/RULE 13 to confirm none of them are weakened. `tool_configs` grew 101→102 (`explainProcedure`, entity access to `ProcedureKnowledge` stays `["read"]`-only for the agent — every write routes through the new gated function via `asServiceRole`, same discipline as every other partner/procedure entity in this codebase). Standard byte-level safe-edit discipline applied throughout (this file is 100% ASCII, embedded newlines are literal two-char `\n` escapes) — hit one real transport-layer gotcha worth remembering: piping a Python heredoc through the Bash tool silently collapsed `\\n` (2 backslashes) down to a single backslash before Python ever saw it, turning an intended literal `\n` escape sequence into a real 0x0A byte and breaking JSON validity. Fixed by writing the edit as a real `.py` file via the Write tool instead of a Bash heredoc — sidesteps the transport-layer escaping ambiguity entirely; worth doing again for any future multi-edit `m_care.jsonc` script rather than debugging heredoc backslash-doubling a second time.

**New redteam coverage** (this whole system — `getProcedureKnowledge`, `ProcedureKnowledge`, the research brain — had zero coverage before this pass): 4 new tests modeled on the `RECOVERY GUIDANCE`/`PROVIDER TRUST TIERS` precedents — the write-boundary is checked structurally (scoped to the exact persist-block source range, not just described in prose), the research schema is checked for the absence of a risk/approval-shaped field, persistence is checked to gate on the literal threshold constant, and the tool's own `tool_configs` description is checked to state the safety boundary so a future prose edit can't silently drop it.

**Deliberately out of scope**: no admin UI changes to `AdminProcedureKnowledge.jsx` (no "regenerate education" button — the new fields are purely additive, existing page renders unaffected); no TTL/staleness system for the education fields (general procedure facts don't go stale like visa/clinic-status data; admin can hand-edit a row to force a refresh); no cost/pricing content (`ProcedurePricing` stays the one tool for that, per RULE 3's explicit "price" callout); no change to `getProcedureRiskSummaries`/`Procedures.jsx`/`ProcedureModal.jsx` (the public catalog page, not chat-agent scope). Zero frontend changes needed at all — both `MCareOrb.jsx` and `MCareAgent.jsx` share the same agent conversation and `MessageBubble.jsx` renderer, so this reaches both surfaces automatically.

**Verification**: touches `ProcedureKnowledge.jsonc`, `explainProcedure` (new), `getProcedureKnowledge`, and `m_care.jsonc` — needs a **Publish** naming all four, with the standard extra caution of pinging `explainProcedure` directly after Publish (expect 400/401 for a missing `procedure_name`, never 404) before trusting the agent to call it live. Lint clean, typecheck at the same 4-error pre-existing baseline (`MCareVaultUpload.jsx`/`MCareAgent.jsx`, unrelated), `npm run build` exit 0, 712/712 vitest (unchanged — no new pure-logic module extracted; `explainProcedure`'s LLM-coupled logic isn't unit-tested from this checkout, consistent with this repo's established precedent for this class of function), 251/251 redteam (4 new, all passed on first write). Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: whether the full 7-block explanation actually reads at the intended clinician-quality bar in a real conversation, and whether the confidence-gated research pass produces genuinely useful content for a real procedure name — Portia verifies live after publishing.

## M-Care precise-location — a broken IP-geo fallback, real reverse geocoding, actionable GPS failures (2026-08-21)

Portia reported M-Care gets the country right (Trinidad and Tobago) via IP but always narrates "near Port of Spain" regardless of where the user actually is, and asked for a full production-grade GPS pipeline. Two parallel audits found the premise partly wrong in the useful way: a real, comprehensive GPS pipeline already exists — `useAutoLocation.js`'s `requestGPS()` (real `getCurrentPosition`, `enableHighAccuracy: true`, a Permissions-API pre-check working around a real iframe-hang bug, a 10s browser timeout), two independent trigger points (`LocationPermissionGate.jsx`'s proactive dialog, and the reactive in-chat "Yes, use my exact location" path via `detectLocationConsentIntent` -> `triggerGpsUpgrade`), a 15s chat-side safety-net timeout, and four already-passing redteam invariants (`PROACTIVE LOCATION GATE`, `GPS REQUEST TIMEOUT`, `LOCATION PAUSE BYPASS`, `SEARCH NEARBY PLACES`) guaranteeing real structural properties. Not rebuilt — extended.

**Real gaps found and fixed**: (1) `src/hooks/useGeoAutoAlign.js` (currency/language auto-detection) still called the known-broken server-side `getGeolocationAndCurrency` directly with no fallback — `useAutoLocation.js` was already fixed for exactly this (commit `81c8d90f`, undocumented in this file until now — a real doc-hygiene gap) to try `fetchClientIpGeo()` (direct browser->`ipapi.co`/`ipwho.is`) first, since the server-side call has been confirmed to reliably fail from Base44's own Deno runtime (root cause never confirmed, only worked around). `useGeoAutoAlign.js` never got that fix — a real, previously-undiscovered instance of the same bug, now fixed the same way. (2) M-Care's "you're near X" narration had zero grounding — the LLM guessing a place name from raw lat/lng every time, even after a successful GPS fix. The only reverse-geocoding anywhere in the repo, `useCountryDetection.js` (Nominatim, `zoom=3`, country-level), was wired to `Dashboard.jsx`'s "new country" toast, never to M-Care. New `src/lib/reverseGeocode.js` (`reverseGeocodePrecise`, Nominatim at `zoom=14`/suburb-level, a `sessionStorage` bucket cache keyed to 3-decimal-place lat/lng — a differently-scoped sibling, not a refactor of `useCountryDetection.js`) is fired best-effort inside `useAutoLocation.js`'s `requestGPS` success handler and patches `gpsLocation.resolved_place` once it resolves (guarded by comparing `logged_at` so a slow reverse-geocode can't clobber a newer GPS reading). `buildLocationContextBlock` includes it in the GPS branch's `[[LOCATION_CONTEXT: ...]]` block when present (parens, not a comma, so it can't be misread as a second `key=value` pair); one additive sentence in `m_care.jsonc`'s LOCATION CONTEXT rule tells the agent to use `resolved_place` directly when present instead of guessing from the raw coordinates. (3) A denied GPS permission got one generic, non-actionable message — since a browser that reports `PERMISSION_DENIED` never re-prompts for that site, the only way back in is the user manually re-enabling it in their own browser settings, which nothing told them. New `gpsErrorCode` companion state on `useAutoLocation.js` (purely additive — `gpsStatus`'s own enum and every redteam-pinned transition untouched) lets `MCareOrb.jsx`'s retry effect give differentiated, actionable messages: denied (browser-settings guidance), timeout (worth retrying, especially with a clearer signal), position-unavailable/no-API (a device limitation, nothing to fix) — kept to exactly one `clearGpsRequestTimeout()` call in the merged branch, matching the redteam-pinned "exactly twice total in this effect" invariant.

**Deliberately not built**: no literal new agent tools named `get_precise_location`/`request_location_permission`/`get_location_status` — a server-side agent tool call can't trigger a client browser's permission dialog; the existing `{{choices:Yes, use my exact location|No, that's fine}}` token already correctly serves that role for this client-server split (`m_care.jsonc`'s own instruction already says so: *"You cannot request GPS yourself - only the tap does that"*). No paid IP-geolocation upgrade — `IPINFO_API_KEY` already exists in this Base44 project (used only by `runDoctorVerificationScan` for doctor-signup fraud checks today) and could give better city-level IP accuracy, but that's a real ongoing-cost decision for Portia to make, not built blind. IP-geolocation's inherent ISP-block-level resolution (which is most of "always Port of Spain" for a small country) isn't fixable by more code — `m_care.jsonc` and `searchNearbyPlaces/entry.ts` already say so honestly; the real fix is getting more users onto the GPS-precise path, which items 2-3 above both serve.

**Verification**: only `m_care.jsonc` needs a **Publish** (one additive prose sentence, `tool_configs` count unchanged) — every other touched file (`useGeoAutoAlign.js`, `useAutoLocation.js`, `locationContext.js`, `reverseGeocode.js` (new), `MCareOrb.jsx`) is pure frontend. Lint clean, typecheck at the same 4-error pre-existing baseline, `npm run build` exit 0, 721/721 vitest (9 new — `resolved_place` inclusion/omission in `locationContext.test.js`, and `reverseGeocode.test.js`'s pure `buildDisplayPlace` fallback-chain logic), 251/251 redteam (unchanged — all four existing GPS tests re-verified green with none of their pinned literals disturbed). Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: whether Nominatim's suburb-level reverse geocode actually reads naturally in a real GPS-granted conversation, and whether the denied-permission recovery message is clear enough on a real phone browser — Portia verifies live.

## M-Care chat attachment menu — a native "Location" option (2026-08-21)

Portia's screenshot showed M-Care's "+" attachment menu (Document, Photos & videos, Camera, Audio, Contact, Poll, Event) missing a Location option, next to reference screenshots of a phone's native share sheet and WhatsApp's "Send location" screen ("Share live location" / "Send your current location"). Investigation found both underlying capabilities already real and working — this was a UI-wiring gap, not new capability: `useAutoLocation.js`'s `bestLocationRef`/`requestGPS()` already give real GPS coordinates, and `MessageBubble.jsx`'s `{{maps:LABEL|lat,lng}}` token (confirmed role-agnostic — its extraction/render, lines 559-628, is never gated on `message.role`) already renders a real tappable "Open in Maps" button row for either a user or assistant message. `generateLiveLocationRequestLink` (a real, working, revocable `/share-location/:token` link generator — `ShareLiveLocation.jsx` -> `storeLiveLocationUpdate` -> `LiveLocation`) already existed fully built but was agent-tool-only, with zero frontend caller anywhere in the repo (confirmed via grep, and confirmed no redteam invariant restricted this).

**Shipped**: `src/components/mcare-agent/AddImageMenu.jsx` (the shared "+" menu component behind both `MCareOrb.jsx` and the full-page `MCareAgent.jsx`) gained a 4th item-dispatch branch, `location` (a `MapPin` icon, green, inserted after Camera), wired to a new optional `onLocationClick` prop — following the file's existing `unsupported`/`vault`/default dispatch shape exactly. In `MCareOrb.jsx`, tapping it posts a local, non-agent-routed choice prompt ("Send my current location" / "Share my live location"), tagged `__locationShareChoice` on the message object — the same marker-based routing pattern `handleDistressSignal`'s own `__distressConfirm` tag already established, so the two chip labels (generated by this component itself) are matched by that marker, never by literal text, in the render loop's `onChoice` router. "Send my current location" reuses `triggerGpsUpgrade`'s exact wait-for-GPS/15s-safety-net shape (a new `pendingLocationPinRef`, parallel to the existing `pendingGpsRetryQueryRef`, both now checked by the same `gpsStatus` effect) and, once a real GPS fix exists, sends a `{{maps:My Location|lat,lng}}`-bearing message — `sendAgentMessage`'s own existing auto-attach logic (it always prepends a fresh `[[LOCATION_CONTEXT: ...]]` block once GPS is granted, unrelated to this change) gives the agent grounded coordinates for free, with zero extra plumbing. "Share my live location" calls `generateLiveLocationRequestLink` directly from the client — a real, working precedent this exact file already established for `requestOnDemandRide`/`notifyGuardianNow` (`handleDistressConfirm`) and `triggerSOS` (`SOSDropdown.jsx`) — and posts the resulting real link back into the chat.

**One real gap found while finishing the live-location half**: a bare share-location URL sent as plain text would have rendered as inert, non-clickable text — `MessageBubble.jsx` has no generic URL auto-linker, and its only URL-recognizing extractor (`extractMapUrls`) matches only known map-app deep-link schemes, not an arbitrary Morales domain URL. Fixed with one more token in the same established family as `{{maps:...}}`/`{{qr:...}}`/`{{media:...}}`: `{{sharelink:LABEL|URL}}`, extracted and rendered as a single tappable "open in new tab" button — a small, scoped addition following the exact proven pattern already used ~6 times in this file, not a broader auto-linkify change.

**Deliberately out of scope**: `MCareAgent.jsx` parity (the separate, login-gated full-page `/m-care` surface doesn't get `onLocationClick` wired this pass — `AddImageMenu`'s new prop is optional so its existing call site there is unaffected, matching this project's established "`MCareOrb` first, parity later" scoping for every prior voice/interactive M-Care feature); a real embedded/draggable map preview (WhatsApp's actual "Send location" screen needs a mapping SDK — none exists in this repo, and adding one is a real vendor/cost decision this project has consistently deferred to Portia elsewhere, not built blind); a contact/recipient picker (no such UI exists anywhere in the app — both actions post into the M-Care conversation itself, where the agent's own already-correct RULE 12/dispatch-protocol instructions can act on a shared location exactly as they already do when a location is shared conversationally). No `m_care.jsonc` change of any kind — `generateLiveLocationRequestLink` was already a granted agent tool; this only adds a second, direct frontend caller for a real-time user-initiated tap, zero Publish-order risk.

**Verification**: all 4 touched files (`AddImageMenu.jsx`, `MCareOrb.jsx`, `MessageBubble.jsx`, plus a redteam test window widened to match the onChoice router's real, slightly-longer text) are pure frontend — no Base44 Publish needed. Lint clean, typecheck at the same 4-error pre-existing baseline, `npm run build` exit 0, 721/721 vitest (unchanged — no new pure-logic module), 251/251 redteam (unchanged — all four GPS-related invariants re-verified green; one test's fixed-length source-slice window widened from 2000 to 3000 chars to keep reaching the same real code it already checked, since the new branch pushed that code further from its anchor). Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: whether the two buttons feel natural in a real tap-through, and whether the live-location link actually opens and streams correctly end to end — Portia verifies live.

## Every shared location gets a real, offline-capable QR code (2026-08-21)

Direct follow-up to the "Location" attachment-menu feature above: Portia asked that any time a location is sent through M-Care — a traveler's own, or a physical place M-Care shares on a doctor's/partner's behalf — it must always come with a QR code, and that QR must keep working for someone with no data connection. Investigation found the real, bounded surface: the only place a location actually flows through a chat interface today is M-Care's own chat (`MCareOrb.jsx`/`MCareAgent.jsx` via `MessageBubble.jsx`'s tokens). A doctor's clinic location is a one-time form field (`PortalDoctor.jsx` -> `respondToDoctorPortalCase`), not a chat message, flowing into `JourneyMap.jsx`/`TravelPassCard.jsx`'s own QR codes. The Care Room (`CaseThread.jsx`, the doctor+patient+M-Care thread) is plain text only with zero token-rendering — confirmed out of scope, Portia's own choice, since building that from scratch is a materially bigger, separate task.

**The real, confirmed technical gap**: every map-link/QR path in this repo (`src/lib/mapLinks.js`'s `generateMapLink()`, `MessageBubble.jsx`'s `InlineQrBlock`, `TravelPassCard.jsx`'s own duplicate `buildMapsUrl()`) built only `https://` universal links (waze.com/maps.apple.com/google.com/maps) — confirmed via a repo-wide grep, zero use anywhere of the `geo:lat,lng` URI (RFC 5870), the standards-based scheme that routes through the OS's own native map-app handler rather than a specific website, which is what lets an already-installed offline map app (Google Maps with a downloaded area, or on Android specifically OsmAnd/Maps.me/HERE WeGo) show the pin without reaching any server first. Honest platform caveat, stated in code and to Portia rather than overclaimed: `geo:` is a first-class, guaranteed OS-level scheme on Android; iOS has no universally guaranteed system handler for it (Apple Maps handles it in many cases, but this is less rock-solid than Android's intent system) — real offline-first apps (Wikipedia's own mobile app, for one) use `geo:` for exactly this reason, but it's not a guarantee every scan on every device works with zero data.

**Shipped**: `src/lib/geoUri.js` (new) — a deliberately separate, zero-dependency module (not added to `mapLinks.js` itself, which imports `base44Client` for its preferred-app persistence — a transitive import that touches `window` at module-load time and would make an otherwise-pure function untestable under Vitest's Node environment for no real reason) holding `isCoords()` and `buildOfflineGeoUri(destination, label)` — only returns a real `geo:lat,lng?q=lat,lng(Label)` value for actual coordinates, `null` for an address string, since geocoding an address always needs a live service somewhere and can never be resolved offline by any client-side trick. `mapLinks.js` imports and re-exports it so its existing callers (`MessageBubble.jsx`, `TravelPassCard.jsx`) needed no import-path changes; `generateMapLink()`'s own 3-button-row behavior (Waze/Google Maps/Apple Maps) is completely untouched — this only changes what the QR itself encodes.

`MessageBubble.jsx`'s `InlineQrBlock` now tries `buildOfflineGeoUri(dest, label)` first, falling back to the existing Google Maps link only when no coordinates are available (unchanged behavior for that case) — with a visible "📡 Works without a data connection" caption when the offline URI is used, so the real distinction is honest and visible rather than silently implied. `MCareOrb.jsx`'s `sendLocationPin()` (the traveler's own "Send my current location" tap, shipped last pass) now always emits a `{{qr:...}}` token alongside its `{{maps:...}}` row. `TravelPassCard.jsx`'s hotel/clinic QR codes (already coordinate-aware via `clinic_coords`/`hotel_coords` — this is exactly where a doctor's clinic location and a partner-relevant hotel address already flow) get the same `buildOfflineGeoUri()`-first treatment, imported from `mapLinks.js` rather than a third re-implementation, with the same honest caption.

**`m_care.jsonc`**: RULE 12 gained one additive sentence — whenever the agent emits a `{{maps:...}}` token, it must immediately follow it on the next line with a matching `{{qr:...}}` token (same LABEL/DESTINATION), since some travelers won't have a data connection to tap the buttons. RULE 16's previous restrictive sentence ("Use it only when the traveler needs to hand navigation to someone else... not for every address you mention") — the exact opposite of the new default — was replaced with wording reflecting the new always-paired behavior, keeping its own standalone triggers (an explicit request, the proactive-connectivity-risk offer) as additional cases, not the only ones. Standard byte-level safe edit (written as a real `.py` file, not a Bash heredoc — this file's established encoding convention stores `\n` as a literal two-character escape in this region), `json.loads()` validity + unchanged `tool_configs` count (102) verified before and after.

A QR was also added for the `{{sharelink:...}}` token (the live-location streaming link, shipped last pass) — a new `ShareLinkQrBlock` in `MessageBubble.jsx`, encoding the URL directly rather than resolving it through `generateMapLink` (it isn't a maps destination). This is explicitly a convenience QR, not an offline one — its caption reads "Needs a data connection to open," never "works offline," since streaming live GPS to Morales' own backend can never work without a network round-trip by design; no QR/URI trick changes that.

**Deliberately out of scope**: the Care Room (`CaseThread.jsx`) — plain text only today, zero token-rendering pipeline, Portia's own choice; `GuardianView.jsx` — has map/share buttons already but no QR, a separate non-M-Care surface; `{{drivermap:...}}` — a live-telemetry widget (a moving driver dot), not a single fixed destination, no natural "QR for a live position" concept fits it without a bigger redesign.

**Verification**: touches `m_care.jsonc` (needs a **Publish**) plus 5 pure-frontend files (`geoUri.js` new, `mapLinks.js`, `MessageBubble.jsx`, `MCareOrb.jsx`, `TravelPassCard.jsx`). Lint clean, typecheck at the same 4-error pre-existing baseline (one new, real error surfaced and fixed along the way — writing the literal path-style reference `@/api/base44Client` inside a `/** */` JSDoc comment triggers the same `TS1003` JSDoc-tag-parser bug already documented once in this file for `neuralSpeech.js` — reworded to avoid an `@`-prefixed reference inside a doc comment, the same fix as before), `npm run build` exit 0, 726/726 vitest (5 new, `tests/geoUri.test.js`), 252/252 redteam (1 new — structurally checks RULE 12/16's real text, that `geoUri.js` is genuinely network-free and synchronous, that `InlineQrBlock` actually calls `buildOfflineGeoUri` rather than just importing it, and that `sendLocationPin` emits both tokens). Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: whether a `geo:` QR actually opens an offline-capable app on real Android/iOS hardware, and whether the paired maps+QR output reads naturally in a live conversation — Portia verifies live after publishing.

## Hardening the "Location" chat-attachment flow — 8 real failure modes found and fixed (2026-08-21)

Portia asked to make sure the newly-shipped Location attach-menu item "wired properly and work without fail." A full audit (every file in the chain read in full: `AddImageMenu.jsx`, `MCareOrb.jsx`'s whole location-share flow, `useAutoLocation.js`, `MessageBubble.jsx`'s token renderers, `generateLiveLocationRequestLink/entry.ts`) found 8 real, reproducible bugs — none hypothetical, each traced to exact lines. All 8 fixed:

- **`src/hooks/useAutoLocation.js`** gained a `gpsInFlightRef` reentrancy guard on `requestGPS` — three independent callers (`triggerGpsUpgrade`, `shareCurrentLocationNow`, `LocationPermissionGate`'s `onAllow`) could each start their own overlapping `permissions.query`/`getCurrentPosition` call; now a second call while one is already resolving is a clean no-op, cleared at the three true terminal points (both `getCurrentPosition` callbacks, the `permissions.query` `'denied'` branch) regardless of mount state.
- **The `locationPaused`-masking bug**: `bestLocation`'s selection ternary (`(!prefs.locationPaused && gpsLocation) ? gpsLocation : ipLocation`) still vetoed an explicitly-just-obtained GPS fix even though `requestGPS`'s own `force` parameter was already fixed (a prior session) to let the *acquisition* through — `locationPaused` means "don't passively track me in the background," and was never meant to hide a fix the traveler just explicitly asked for. Left `bestLocation` itself untouched (it's correct for its other, passive consumer, the `[[LOCATION_CONTEXT:...]]` auto-attach) and instead had `MCareOrb.jsx` add a `gpsLocationRef` (mirroring the existing `bestLocationRef` pattern exactly) reading the hook's already-exported raw `gpsLocation` — `sendLocationPin` and `shareCurrentLocationNow`'s fast-path check both now read that instead, so a user with background tracking paused no longer sees a false "I wasn't able to get your exact location" after GPS was actually, successfully granted.
- **A real race**: a typed question's pending GPS retry (`pendingGpsRetryQueryRef`) and a "Send my current location" tap's pending pin (`pendingLocationPinRef`) could both be set at once — the resolving effect consumed only one and, because it re-runs as `agentSending` cycles, resent the *other*, stale, unrelated message right after the location pin. Fixed with mutual exclusion at the two setting sites: whichever flow starts most recently clears the other's still-pending flag (newest tap wins) — this also closes the two flows' shared 15s safety-net timer canceling each other and stranding one flow with no way to ever resolve.
- **A silent no-op**: `shareCurrentLocationNow`'s "GPS already granted" fast path called straight into `sendAgentMessage`, which silently drops any send while `agentSending` is true — no toast, no retry, the tap just did nothing. Now checks `agentSending` first and, if busy, defers via the same pending-ref mechanism (with a short "One moment — I'll send your location as soon as I'm done here." message) instead of losing the tap.
- **No double-tap protection**: a new `locationChoiceInFlightRef`, set the instant either Location-menu choice is tapped, blocks a second tap on the same bubble from kicking off a second overlapping GPS request or a second live-location link — released at every real terminal point (`sendLocationPin`, `shareLiveLocationNow`'s `finally`, the denied/unavailable resolution, or a superseding `triggerGpsUpgrade` call).
- **Orphaned live-location links**: `shareLiveLocationNow` passed `activeCaseRecord?.id` straight through even when `undefined` (a pre-booking visitor, or the case query not yet resolved) — the backend call *succeeded* and showed a real, tappable link, but `generateLiveLocationRequestLink/entry.ts`'s own fallback (look up the caller's most recent `CaseRecord`) silently produced `case_id: ''` for a user with zero cases, creating a `LiveLocationRequest` attached to nothing any care-team surface could ever find. Fixed on both sides: the frontend now checks `activeCaseRecord?.id` before the round trip and shows an honest "You'll need an active journey for a live-location link" instead of a wasted network call; the backend now rejects with a clear 400 if `resolvedCaseId` still ends up empty, matching the function's own already-stated philosophy two blocks below for the missing-`APP_URL` case ("A patient who feels unsafe getting a link that quietly doesn't work is worse than an honest 'try again' here"). Confirmed the driver-role branch (used by the separate driver-live-tracking-map feature, which repurposes `case_id` to hold a `RecoveryTransportRequest.id`) is a completely separate `if` block, always requires an explicit `transport_request_id`, and is unaffected by this change.
- **Dead map buttons**: `MessageBubble.jsx`'s `{{maps:...}}` 3-button row rendered whenever a `maps` token was present with no check that `maps.dest` was actually usable — tapping a button with an empty `dest` silently did nothing (`generateMapLink` returns `null`, `openInMapsApp` short-circuits with zero feedback). Fixed with the same guard `InlineQrBlock` already effectively has: `{maps?.dest && (...)}` instead of `{maps && (...)}` — general robustness, not Location-specific, since any `{{maps:LABEL|}}` from any flow could hit this.

**Deliberately out of scope, named not silently dropped**: an app-wide "disable a choice chip / attach-menu item after it's tapped" pattern (a systemic UI change across every M-Care flow, not unique to Location — the narrower `locationChoiceInFlightRef` closes the actual functional risk without that redesign); `LocationPermissionGate`'s `onAllow` still doesn't participate in the pending-ref/timeout bookkeeping (a different, pre-existing proactive-gate feature — the reentrancy guard removes the specific race risk it could otherwise cause without needing to rewire that gate); the cosmetic duplicate "Getting your exact location now..." message if a reentrant call is superseded by the new guard (harmless, not a failure).

**Verification**: touches `generateLiveLocationRequestLink/entry.ts` — needs a **Publish** naming that file; every other touched file (`useAutoLocation.js`, `MCareOrb.jsx`, `MessageBubble.jsx`) is pure frontend. Lint clean, typecheck at the same 4-error pre-existing baseline, `npm run build` exit 0, 726/726 vitest (unchanged — no new pure-logic module; these are all React-hook/effect-timing fixes in files with no dedicated test file, consistent with this repo's established precedent for this class of change), 253/253 redteam (1 new — `LOCATION SHARE RACE`, structurally pins the mutual-exclusion fix, the `gpsLocationRef` read-source fix, and the reentrancy guard so none of the three can silently regress). Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: whether the `locationPaused` fix actually resolves the confusing error message on a real device with background tracking paused, and whether the reentrancy guard changes perceived responsiveness when multiple location actions are triggered in quick succession — Portia verifies live after publishing.

## Medication Reconciliation — a full, real system, undocumented until now (2026-08-21)

A large spec asked M-Care to evolve into "medication reconciliation, post-op monitoring & recovery coordination," with an explicit instruction to audit before building. Three parallel Explore audits found something significant: **a complete, production-wired medication reconciliation system already exists** — entity, 4 backend functions, 2 shared helper modules, an agent tool grant + explicit rule, and 2 UI panels (doctor + patient) — and none of it had ever been written up here. Documenting it now so it isn't rediscovered from scratch again.

**`base44/entities/Medication.jsonc`** — full provenance per record: `source_type` (`patient|doctor|document|prescription|hospital|system_observation` — only `patient`/`doctor` are ever actually written today; `document`/`prescription` are declared for a future scanner bridge that doesn't exist yet, see below), `status` (`reported|active|planned|completed|stopped|discontinued|unknown|requires_review`, default `reported`), `confidence` (0-100, "never a claim of clinical verification"), `patient_confirmed`/`doctor_confirmed` (booleans, both false until a real human acts), `flags[]`, an append-only `history[]`, and `rxcui` — always blank, documented in three separate places as a placeholder for a future RxNorm integration that doesn't exist (no drug-normalization API/library anywhere in this repo). Create/update RLS is admin-only — every write goes through `asServiceRole` inside the two functions below, never a direct client write, never the agent (see below).

**The pipeline**: `base44/shared/medicationNormalize.ts` (the one LLM call, self-reported `confidence`, explicitly instructed to never invent a detail not said) → `base44/shared/medicationReconciliation.ts` (fully deterministic, no LLM, no network — flags `duplicate_active_medication`, `possible_allergy_conflict` (a naive substring match against the patient's on-file allergy text, explicitly documented as **not** a real drug-interaction database), `missing_dosage_details`, `missing_frequency`, `ambiguous_extraction` if confidence `< 50` — any flag forces `status: 'requires_review'`) → `base44/shared/createMedicationFromText.ts` (orchestrates both, creates the row, fires a `JourneyEvent` nudge). Never sets `patient_confirmed`/`doctor_confirmed` true — a real human always has to.

**`base44/functions/reportMedication/entry.ts`** — the patient (or M-Care mid-chat, on the patient's behalf) reports a medication in free text; delegates to `createMedicationFromText`. **`base44/functions/confirmMedication/entry.ts`** — the *only* place a confirm/edit/reject/discontinue/request_info action is ever recorded, role-gated per action (`patient_confirm` is patient-only; `doctor_confirm`/`edit`/`reject`/`discontinue`/`request_info` are gated on `med.doctor_email === user.email`, never a caller-supplied field). A patient's own `patient_confirm` never clears a `requires_review` flag by itself — only a doctor's `doctor_confirm` resolves a real flag.

**Care Room already detects a doctor's medication instruction and links it end-to-end**: `postCaseMessage` calls `parseCareRoomMessage` (LLM parse-only, zero entity writes) and, on a detected doctor instruction, calls `createMedicationFromText` with `source_type: 'doctor'`, force-sets `status: 'planned'`, and posts a confirm-bubble `QuoteMessage` carrying `medication_id` + `requires_confirmation: true`. `confirmCareRoomMessage` flips `patient_confirmed`/`doctor_confirmed` when that bubble is confirmed by the addressed party. `parseCareRoomMessage` today is binary (medication instruction detected or not) — no granular event taxonomy (start/stop/change-dose/missed-dose/side-effect) exists yet.

**Doctor dashboard**: `src/components/doctor/MedicationSafetyPanel.jsx`, mounted in `DoctorDashboard.jsx`'s active-patients tab — Confirm/Request info/Discontinue, every action via `confirmMedication`, never a direct entity write. **Patient dashboard**: `src/components/dashboard/modules/MedicationsPanel.jsx`, mounted in `VaultDocumentsPanel.jsx` — Needs-review/Current/Past grouping, a patient-facing Confirm button.

**`base44/agents/m_care.jsonc`**: `reportMedication` is a granted tool, governed by **RULE 29 — MEDICATION REPORTING** ("never treat this call as confirming, prescribing, starting, stopping, or changing a medication yourself... those are real human actions... that happen outside anything you can do directly"). `Medication` is granted **read-only**. `confirmMedication` is **not granted to the agent at all** — confirmation is exclusively a human action, by design.

**Intake seeding**: `createCaseFromConsultation.ts` seeds the real `Medication` entity from `Consultation.medication_notes` (the one free-text intake field that can carry real drug names/doses — `medication_types` is just checkbox categories) at case creation.

**A separate, third, disconnected system exists too — don't conflate it with the real `Medication` entity**: `schedulePostOpMedReminders` (triggered by `logProcedureComplete`, not cron) builds a template-driven 7-day medication *schedule display* from a fixed `MED_SCHEDULES` map (or the doctor's own `CaseRecord.doctor_recommended_medications[]` if populated), writing `CaseRecord.medication_schedule`/`medication_schedule_set_at`/`medication_procedure_category` — fields `CaseRecord.jsonc` doesn't actually declare in its schema (a real, flagged-not-fixed gap). Displayed read-only in `src/components/patient/PostProcedureCarePanel.jsx`. This system never creates a `Medication` row and the real reconciliation pipeline never reads from it — two independent medication-adjacent systems that don't know about each other.

**Also don't conflate `runMedGuardAnalysis` ("MedGuard™") with medication** — despite the name, it's a behavioral/travel-safety risk-prediction engine (missed check-ins, GPS silence, night-alone, high-risk-country), unrelated to drugs.

**Deliberately not built in this pass** (real gaps, named not silently dropped): a scanned-prescription → `Medication` bridge (`scanVaultDocument` treats `prescription`/`medical_record`/`medical_report`/`lab_result` as fully generic document types today — no structured extraction, no call into `createMedicationFromText`); structured "doctor said follow up in 7 days" tracking (zero persistence exists anywhere — `readDischargePaper`/`extractClinicalNote` both already extract a `follow_up`-shaped object via LLM but throw it away after one-time display); RxNorm integration (a real, free, keyless NIH API exists for this, unlike this project's other paid-API gaps, but it's unwired); unifying this system with the separate `PostOpCheckIn`/`RecoverySession` recovery-monitoring tracks (see below).

## Post-Op Recovery Monitoring — two parallel systems, and a real timing bug fixed (2026-08-21)

Same audit as above surfaced the recovery-monitoring side: **two independent, never-cross-referenced systems**, sharing only a loose `case_id`/`patient_email` relationship.

**System A — `PostOpCheckIn`** (Day 3/7/14/30 milestone surveys, created by `schedulePostOpCheckIns` on Handshake 9 / home drop-off). `submitPostOpCheckIn` (public, token-gated) records the patient's response, emails the doctor directly if `pain_level >= 7 || concerns.length > 0` (raw PHI in the email body — not routed through `notify.ts`'s link-only discipline, a pre-existing, flagged-not-fixed inconsistency).

**System B — `RecoverySession`/`RecoveryLog`** (daily check-ins). `checkRecoveryAnomaly` is a deterministic rule engine (hardcoded thresholds — `pain_level >= 7`, `wound_status === 'bad'`, trend detection across consecutive days — plus a cosmetic-only Claude Haiku call that narrates but never decides severity), cascading to doctor/patient/emergency-contact notification on `warning`/`escalate` (also not link-only in `checkRecoveryAnomaly` itself, though `checkMissedRecoveryCheckins` does it correctly). `checkMissedRecoveryCheckins` is correctly cron-wired (hourly + daily in `safety-cron.yml`) and complete — this half of the system works.

**The real bug, found by reading `schedulePostOpCheckIns/entry.ts` directly rather than trusting a paraphrase**: `scheduled_at` was computed and stored correctly for all 4 days, but nothing ever read it back to decide *when* to send. The Day-3 send block ran synchronously inside the same create-loop that fires on Handshake 9 — meaning the "Day 3" email ("You've been home for 3 days...") actually went out **immediately at drop-off (day 0)**, not 3 days later, contradicting its own subject line and body copy. Days 7/14/30 never sent at all — no sender existed for them, despite their subject/question copy already being written and sitting unused in the `QUESTIONS` map. `schedulePostOpCheckIns` itself was never cron-scheduled (confirmed via grep of both workflow files) — it only ever fired once, synchronously, off Handshake 9.

**Fixed**: `base44/shared/sendPostOpCheckInNotification.ts` (new) is the one real sender for all 4 days now — extracted from the old Day-3-only inline block, generalized to take `day` as a parameter, moved the existing `QUESTIONS` map here (Day 7/14/30's copy needed zero new writing, it already existed). `schedulePostOpCheckIns/entry.ts` is now a pure record-creation function — the special-cased immediate send is gone entirely. `base44/functions/sendDuePostOpCheckIns/entry.ts` (new) is a real daily cron sweep (`createHandler` + inline `cronAuthorized`, the modern pattern — not the older raw-`Deno.serve` style `checkMissedRecoveryCheckins` still carries as a disclosed deviation): fetches `PostOpCheckIn.filter({status:'pending'})`, then compares `scheduled_at <= now` in memory (this SDK's filter has no `$lt`/`$lte` operator — matches `checkMissedRecoveryCheckins`'s own established pattern), sends via the shared helper with per-record `try/catch` isolation (one bad record can't abort the batch, the same fix already applied this session to `escalateSoloCheckIn`/`checkPartnerSLABreaches`). `JourneyEvent.jsonc` gained `recovery_checkin_day7`/`recovery_checkin_day14`/`recovery_checkin_day30` (matching the existing `recovery_checkin_day3` naming convention). Wired into `safety-cron.yml`'s existing daily 09:00 UTC tier (day-granularity is sufficient, matching this project's own established precedent for this exact class of fix) and the `workflow_dispatch` manual block.

**Deliberately out of scope, not silently dropped**: unifying `PostOpCheckIn` and `RecoverySession` into one system (a real, bigger architectural decision, not requested); fixing `submitPostOpCheckIn`/`checkRecoveryAnomaly`'s non-link-only PHI notifications (pre-existing, flagged not fixed); the scanned-prescription bridge and follow-up tracking from the same spec (real, separate gaps — see the Medication section above).

**Verification**: touches `JourneyEvent.jsonc` and 2 functions (`schedulePostOpCheckIns` simplified, `sendDuePostOpCheckIns` new) plus a new shared module — needs a **Publish** naming all three; the `safety-cron.yml` change needs no Base44 Publish. New redteam invariant (`POST-OP CHECK-IN`) pins the cron gate, the idempotency check, and that the old buggy inline send is really gone; two pre-existing tests (`JOURNEY EVENTS`, `M-CARE PUSH`) were updated to point at the new shared helper instead of the now-simplified `schedulePostOpCheckIns`. Lint clean, typecheck at the same 4-error pre-existing baseline, `npm run build` exit 0, 726/726 vitest (unchanged — no new pure-logic module), 254/254 redteam (1 new, 2 updated). Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: whether a real Day-3/7/14/30 check-in actually arrives at the correct elapsed time on the next real daily cron tick — Portia verifies via the Actions tab, or by triggering `workflow_dispatch` manually to test sooner.

## M-Care intelligence-stack audit — 10-point vision checked against real code, one real gap closed (2026-08-22)

Portia shared a detailed architectural vision for M-Care (understand → remember in layers → know when it doesn't know → real tools → a planner → proactive → context-aware → a permission system → explainable → learn from outcomes) — a framework to react to, not a specific bug report. Three parallel audits checked the real code against all 10 points before proposing anything, per this project's own repeatedly-applied discipline against big-bang-building a sweeping spec.

**Verdict, point by point**: (1) understand everything — real, covered by prior voice/scanner/location work. (2) memory in 5 layers — real only for knowledge memory (`McareKnowledge` + `ProcedureKnowledge`, both genuine recall→research→persist-at-≥80%-confidence loops); personal memory is thin (`mcare_prefs` on `User` holds exactly 3 keys — talkMode/privateMode/responseLanguage — and isn't agent-queryable at all, it's frontend-injected); journey memory is real data split across ~4-5 independently-evolved systems (recovery alone has 2 disconnected tracks, `PostOpCheckIn` vs. `RecoverySession`/`RecoveryLog`, plus a 3rd unrelated `medication_schedule` display field); provider intelligence (trust scores, `OutcomeRecord`, `DiscoveredProviderCandidate`) is real fragments explicitly, repeatedly documented elsewhere in this file as not unified; event history (`JourneyEvent`/`AuditLog`/`CrisisReroute`) is real and genuinely distinct, but M-Care had **zero read access to any of it** — this became the fix, see below. (3) know when it doesn't know — real for the specific gated tools (Rule 3, confidence-gated research), not a general 4-state taxonomy; no live violation found. (4) real tools — real, 102 `tool_configs` grants genuinely spanning medical/travel/safety/communication/operations, the read-only-entity-plus-gated-write pattern consistently applied except one already-documented deliberate gap (`ExternalJourney`). (5) a planner — confirmed missing: no code anywhere builds an explicit multi-step plan for M-Care, executes it, and replans on failure; `_shared/mRecon.ts`'s `runMReconLoop` is real but only powers the unrelated `/demo/m-recon` page, and Phase 7's own header comment documents a deliberate choice not to reuse that loop shape for M-Care, which now runs entirely on Base44's native Agent runtime — this repo has zero visibility into its internal step-ordering. (6) proactive — real (`JourneyEvent`, 24 event types, ~15 writers, real-time delivery) but the loop is detect→act→notify fused, not detect→assess→act→verify→notify. (7) context-aware — real but isolated to one function (`detectFallbackCrisis`'s 15min/24h/4h tiers scaling "doctor hasn't confirmed" by time-to-procedure); no general event-type×journey-stage×time framework exists elsewhere. (8) a permission system — missing as a unified taxonomy; the Auto/Needs-Consent/Human-Only boundary is real but scattered in prose across 6+ separate rules (1, 2, 13, 19, 29, 10), never named as one canonical scheme. (9) explainable — partial; Rule 1 has a genuine tool-grounded reasoning-trace requirement but scoped only to SAFE-T outcomes, and `JourneyEvent.message_text` already states real reasons — but with M-Care unable to read `JourneyEvent` at all, a patient asking "why did you do that" as a follow-up chat question had no grounded answer available, a structural fabrication risk. (10) learn from outcomes — mostly missing; `OutcomeRecord` is confirmed by grep to have zero references anywhere in `calculateDoctorTrustScore`/`calculateDriverTrustScore`/`calculateCompanionScore` — completely disconnected from trust scoring (which does use one narrow real signal, `RecoverySession.feedback_rating`, an "honest proxy, not precise attribution" per its own code comments); "was M-Care's own recommendation correct" is recorded nowhere — `logMcarePerformance` writes are one-way, never read back by anything.

**Portia's choice, presented via the same "pick one real slice" pattern this project uses for every sweeping spec**: close the explainability gap. Everything else above — the planner, unifying journey/provider memory, wiring `OutcomeRecord` into trust scores, generalizing `detectFallbackCrisis`'s timing pattern, naming the permission taxonomy — stays deliberately deferred, real and named, not silently dropped, each its own future session.

**Shipped**: `base44/agents/m_care.jsonc` gained one new read-only entity grant, `JourneyEvent` (`tool_configs` 102→103) — the same Phase-8 discipline as every other sensitive entity the agent touches (`Doctor`/`TravelAgency`/`VaultDocument`/`Medication`: read what the frontend already shows the patient, never write it directly; only trusted cron functions create a `JourneyEvent`). No RLS change needed — `JourneyEvent`'s existing RLS already scopes reads to `data.client_email == user.email` or admin, and an agent's entity-tool calls run under the invoking patient's own session, so this exposes nothing the patient's own dashboard doesn't already render to them; it only lets the conversational agent reference the same data. New **RULE 31 — GROUNDED EXPLANATIONS**: when asked why M-Care did something, or what it's done so far, look up the real `JourneyEvent` records for the case first and answer only from what's actually there — quote or paraphrase the real `message_text`/`action_taken`, never invent a reason or action not in a real record; if nothing relevant is on file, say so plainly rather than offer a plausible-sounding guess; explicitly cross-references Rule 3 (NO INVENTED DATA) as the same discipline applied to a new capability, not a new exception to it; states explicitly the grant is read-only. Same byte-level safe-edit discipline as every prior `m_care.jsonc` change (this file's end-of-instructions region stores `\n` as a literal two-character escape and is otherwise plain ASCII; anchor uniqueness and `json.loads()` validity + exact `tool_configs` count confirmed via a Python script before and after writing, executed as a real `.py` file rather than a Bash heredoc). New redteam invariant (`GROUNDED EXPLANATIONS`) pins the entity's RLS staying patient-or-admin scoped, the agent's grant staying read-only (never create/update/delete), and Rule 31's text requiring a real-record lookup, forbidding invention, and requiring an honest empty-result answer.

**Verification**: touches only `base44/agents/m_care.jsonc` — needs a **Publish** naming that file. No new function is being wired (an existing entity's read grant, not a new tool), so Publish-order risk is low; unlike a new-function grant, there's no separate endpoint to ping-test after publishing. Lint clean, typecheck at the same 4-error pre-existing baseline, `npm run build` exit 0, 726/726 vitest (unchanged — no pure-logic module involved), 255/255 redteam (1 new, all pre-existing unaffected — no entity RLS, no existing tool grant, no existing rule's text was touched). Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: whether M-Care actually looks up real `JourneyEvent` records when asked "why did you do that" in a live conversation rather than answering from plausibility — Portia verifies live after publishing, ideally by asking that exact kind of follow-up question right after a real `JourneyEvent` has fired (e.g. right after a departure-countdown reminder or a driver-backup dispatch).

## M-Care 7-phase roadmap, Phase A — JourneyPlan: the smallest real "planner" primitive (2026-08-22)

Portia prioritized 7 engineering phases for M-Care (A: Orchestration/planner, B: unified memory, C: proactive autonomous watcher, D: perception, E: recovery/replanning, F: learning from outcomes, G: human governance/permissions), building on the 10-point audit above. Investigated Phase A before proposing anything, and specifically chased down a lead the prior audit hadn't looked into: a redteam test named `DISPATCH ORCHESTRATOR` referencing an `mcare_orchestrator` agent. Found it's unrelated to a planner — `base44/agents/mcare_orchestrator.jsonc` (built 2026-08-18, undocumented here until now) is a narrow, read-only, single-turn backend recon assistant for dispatch failures across the 5 partner types, called from `base44/shared/askDispatchOrchestrator.ts` purely to add narrative color to an admin alert email after `partnerSearchWidening.ts`'s deterministic code has already made the real decision — no live frontend caller, no multi-step planning in its prompt, exactly two read-only tools. Also confirmed: no MCP (Model Context Protocol) code exists in this repo beyond the one already-declined Base44 App MCP feature (see the MCP-exposure audit section above); and no existing "plan"-shaped data structure exists to build on — `bookingState.ts`/`CaseRecord.status` is a fixed 19-value macro-stage enum, `BookingFallbackRule`/`handleCrisisReroute` is a static rule table, and `_shared/mRecon.ts`'s `runMReconLoop` (the closest mechanical analog — bounded loop, tool dispatch, fail-closed) decides one step at a time reactively rather than building an explicit plan array up front, and Phase 7's own header comment documents a deliberate decision not to reuse that loop shape for M-Care, which runs entirely on Base44's black-box native Agent runtime with zero visibility into its internal step-ordering.

**What this means**: a real planner is genuinely new architecture here, not a "find and wire up what already exists" fix like most of this project's prior work — and it can't be built by replacing or wrapping Base44's own agent reasoning loop (the exact "mode-picker" pattern Phase 7 deliberately moved away from). The only honest, buildable shape: give the agent a tool to externalize and track its own plan as a real, persistent, auditable artifact — not a parallel execution engine that decides for it. Presented this to Portia; she confirmed building the smallest real slice now rather than a full cross-phase design doc or a different starting phase.

**Shipped**: `base44/entities/JourneyPlan.jsonc` (new) — `case_id`, `client_email` (RLS anchor, same shape as `JourneyEvent`), `goal`, `steps` (array of `{step_number, description, tool_name, status, notes, updated_at}` — status one of `pending`/`in_progress`/`done`/`failed`/`skipped`), `status` (plan-level `active`/`completed`/`abandoned`). Read scoped to `client_email` or admin; create/update/delete admin-only — the same Phase-8 discipline as every sensitive agent-facing entity so far (`Doctor`/`TravelAgency`/`VaultDocument`/`Medication`/`JourneyEvent`): the agent gets **read-only** access, all real writes go through two new gated functions.

`base44/functions/createJourneyPlan/entry.ts` (new) — `bodySchema`-validated (`case_id`, `goal`, `steps: {description, tool_name?}[]`, capped at 20 steps), resolves `client_email` from the real `CaseRecord` (never caller-supplied), every step hardcoded to `status: 'pending'` server-side. Fires one `JourneyEvent` (`journey_plan_created`, `priority: 'low'`) via the existing `logJourneyEvent` helper — per Portia's own standing rule (Phase 46) that `JourneyEvent` is the one proactive-communication layer, no feature builds a separate notification path.

`base44/functions/updateJourneyPlanStep/entry.ts` (new) — the only place a step's status changes after creation; `pending` is excluded from the allowed target statuses (create-time-only). Real ownership check (`plan.client_email === user.email` or admin) before any write, mirroring `confirmMedication`'s established pattern. Deliberately does **not** fire a `JourneyEvent` on every single step transition (would be noisy — matches this project's existing "reactions should feel occasional, not a reflex on every message" ethos) — only fires one (`journey_plan_completed`, `priority: 'low'`) when an update causes every step to reach `done`. A `failed` step is recorded honestly with real notes; no auto-replan, no auto-notification — that's Phase E's job, not this slice's. Both new event types added to `logAuditEvent`'s `ALLOWED_EVENT_TYPES` allowlist and `AuditLog.jsonc`'s matching enum (plus a third, `journey_plan_step_updated`, for the ordinary non-completing case — the allowlist and the entity enum must match exactly, confirmed via direct read of both).

`base44/agents/m_care.jsonc` — three new `tool_configs` grants (`createJourneyPlan`, `updateJourneyPlanStep` as functions; `JourneyPlan` read-only as an entity — count 103→106). New **RULE 32 — JOURNEY PLANNING**: for a genuinely multi-step request (not a one-line question), call `createJourneyPlan` with a clear goal and ordered real steps; mark a step done via `updateJourneyPlanStep` only once it's actually happened, never pre-emptively; mark a genuine failure honestly with real notes, never silently skip or pretend success; cross-references RULE 3 (a status must reflect something real) and RULE 31 (if asked "what's your plan," reference the real `JourneyPlan`, never invent one). Explicit closing clause matching every prior rule's pattern: this is scaffolding for the agent's own reasoning and a persistent audit record, not a new execution engine — it still decides what to do turn by turn, and this changes nothing about the Safety Gate, Consent Gate, or any existing capability boundary. Same byte-level safe-edit discipline as every prior `m_care.jsonc` change.

New redteam invariant (`JOURNEY PLAN`): pins the entity's RLS, the agent's read-only grant, that `createJourneyPlan`'s `StepInput` schema structurally cannot accept a caller-supplied `status` or `client_email`, that `updateJourneyPlanStep`'s `STEP_STATUSES` structurally excludes `pending`, that it performs a real ownership check, and that RULE 32's text forbids marking a step done that hasn't happened.

**Deliberately not built this pass**: replanning-on-failure (Phase E); a proactive/cron watcher reading `JourneyPlan` to nudge on a stalled step (Phase C); memory unification (Phase B); the permission taxonomy (Phase G); pre-case (intake-time) planning — a plan always requires a real, already-existing `CaseRecord`, matching the same precondition already used by `requestOnDemandRide`/`schedulePostOpCheckIns`.

**Verification**: touches `JourneyPlan.jsonc` (new), `JourneyEvent.jsonc`'s enum, `AuditLog.jsonc`'s enum, `logAuditEvent/entry.ts`, 2 new functions, and `m_care.jsonc` — needs a **Publish** naming all six, then ping both new functions directly (expect 400/401, never 404) before trusting the agent to call them, per Phase 10's lesson that an undeployed-but-granted tool makes M-Care go completely silent. Lint clean, typecheck at the same 4-error pre-existing baseline, `npm run build` exit 0, 726/726 vitest (unchanged — no new pure-logic module), 256/256 redteam (1 new). Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: whether M-Care actually builds a sensible real plan for a genuinely multi-step request rather than over-planning trivial questions or under-planning complex ones — Portia verifies live after publishing, e.g. by asking for something genuinely multi-step ("I need dental implants in Cancun with travel coordination") and checking a real `JourneyPlan` row gets created with sensible steps.

## M-Care Phase 4 — agentic orchestration: the architecture verdict, and the smallest real slice (2026-08-22)

Portia sent a full spec for "agentic orchestration / MCP tool planner" — a much larger ask than the JourneyPlan primitive above, explicitly demanding an audit before code, warning against assuming MCP is right, and explicitly authorizing deviation from its own suggested architecture (a new/expanded orchestrator agent, possibly MCP-based) if something else is genuinely better, with a required existing-approach/problem/improved-approach/migration-risk explanation. Three parallel audits answered the load-bearing questions before any design, and the verdict is a real "no" to the spec's own suggested mechanism, backed by evidence rather than a hunch.

**Finding 1 — the native agent already chains multiple tool calls within one turn.** Confirmed, not assumed: the SDK's own type (`AgentMessage.tool_calls: AgentMessageToolCall[]`, `node_modules/@base44/sdk/dist/modules/agents.types.d.ts`) is a real array with independently-tracked per-call status, and `MessageBubble.jsx` already renders it as such. Real live evidence backs this too — Phase 52's Colombia session had one user message produce one reply synthesizing 3+ distinct tool calls (`checkWeatherAlerts`, `getDestinationSafetyIndex`, `recallMcareKnowledge` → `mcareResearchAndLearn` on a miss), surfacing a real earthquake the safety score alone missed.
