Whether this is *true* dependent step-by-step reasoning vs. parallel-dispatch-then-synthesize remains genuinely unconfirmable from static code (this project's own prior stated conclusion) — but for the spec's actual goal, the capability already exists today with zero code needed.

**Finding 2 — `mcare_orchestrator.jsonc` (built 2026-08-18) is the wrong foundation to grow into a planner.** Re-confirmed what the JourneyPlan session already concluded once: it's single-turn, read-only (2 tools), `memory_config.enabled: false`, called *from inside* `partnerSearchWidening.ts` purely to add narrative color to an admin email — its own instructions state it has "NO ability to take any action beyond calling the tools you have been granted." The real decision in that flow is 100% deterministic code; the orchestrator never drives anything. Growing its tool grants wouldn't turn it into a planner — it would need a fundamentally different (persistent, action-capable, conversation-driving) shape, which is exactly what `JourneyPlan` already is.

**Finding 3 — MCP adds no confirmed benefit and reopens an already-flagged risk.** The only MCP work in this repo is Base44's "App MCP" feature (external third-party AI assistant access, deliberately left disabled — see the MCP-exposure audit section above) — unrelated to internal orchestration. The literal string `app_mcp` doesn't exist anywhere in this codebase, and no local Base44 platform guide is readable from this checkout (the spec's own instruction to "read the provided platform guides" refers to something outside this repo). Tool discovery, chaining, and structured results are already native platform behavior (Finding 1); enabling MCP would add a second, parallel exposure/auth surface for no benefit, and the earlier MCP audit already names the real risk — an MCP-exposed function could bypass `m_care.jsonc`'s own Rule-based discipline entirely, since MCP's toggle system and `tool_configs` are architecturally separate.

**Finding 4 — tool authorization is already server-enforced, not just prompt-advisory.** There's no client wire path to invoke an arbitrary tool — `AgentMessage.tool_calls` is server-generated output, never client input; the only client action is posting `{role, content}` chat text. `tool_configs` is a real platform-level grant list (every phase in this file already treats it that way). Every real write-capable function additionally has its own independent ownership/role check regardless of agent intent — `confirmMedication`'s per-action role gate, `updateJourneyPlanStep`'s `plan.client_email !== user.email` check, `sendClientBookingProposal`'s ownership + price-readiness gate. A malicious prompt cannot make M-Care call an ungranted tool, and even a misused granted tool still has to pass the target function's own real check. The one honestly-flagged gap is MCP-specific (external exposure bypassing `tool_configs`) — exactly why MCP stays off, unrelated to prompt injection against the agent itself.

**Architecture verdict, presented to Portia and confirmed**: no new orchestrator agent, no MCP. Keep exactly one agent (M-Care itself) and give it what it's still missing — all textual, none touching the tool-chaining mechanism that already works.

**Shipped**: `base44/agents/m_care.jsonc` gained two new rules, zero new `tool_configs` grants (stays at 106 — pure instructions, reusing only already-granted tools). New **RULE 33 — REPLANNING**: when a real tool result indicates unavailability, no match, or genuine failure, don't stop and report failure — check whether a real, already-granted alternative exists and try it (e.g. `discoverProviderCandidates`→`verifyDiscoveredCandidate` after a `matchDoctorsForProcedure` miss); if a `JourneyPlan` step exists for the thing that failed, call `updateJourneyPlanStep` to mark it `failed` with real, honest notes — never leave a real failure untracked, never mark a step done to avoid recording it; once real alternatives are genuinely exhausted, offer `flagIntakeHandoff` (already granted, Rule 14) rather than keep guessing; explicitly forbids trying an unsafe or ungranted shortcut, and states Rule 1/Rule 2 still apply exactly as before. New **RULE 34 — TOOL AUTONOMY TIERS**: names the three tiers already enforced in scattered form across Rules 1/2/13/19/29/32/10 — AUTO (research, recall, comparison, status checks — no asking needed), NEEDS-CONSENT (sharing the traveler's info, reaching a new provider, changing their plan — Rule 2's gate), HUMAN-ONLY (a real safety/medical/verification decision — Rule 1's Safety Gate, Rule 29's never-confirm-medication carve-out) — cross-references each rule rather than restating them, so nothing drifts out of sync, and defaults to the more cautious tier when uncertain. Same byte-level safe-edit discipline as every prior `m_care.jsonc` change. New redteam invariant (`AGENTIC ORCHESTRATION`) pins `tool_configs` staying at 106 (proving this really is text-only) and both rules' real required properties.

**Deliberately not built this pass, named explicitly**: no new write-capable agent tool for autonomous doctor-reassignment/backup search (stays cron/human-action-only — `findDoctorBackup`, `respondToDoctorPortalCase` — a deliberately guarded, sensitive area; M-Care can narrate and hand off via `flagIntakeHandoff`, but doesn't get a button to reassign a doctor itself). No formal structured tool-contract rewrite across all 106 granted tools (too large, too risky for one pass). No memory-layer unification (Phase B, already deferred once). No `updateJourneyPlanStep` extension to insert a new step mid-plan — a revised plan is a fresh `createJourneyPlan` call for now. No literal end-to-end live test of the spec's 3 demo scenarios (doctor cancellation, driver no-show, a one-shot Cancun request) — this checkout has no live Base44 session, the same standing limitation noted throughout this project.

**Verification**: touches only `base44/agents/m_care.jsonc` — needs a **Publish** naming that file; no new function is wired, so Publish-order risk is low (nothing new to ping-test). Lint clean, typecheck at the same 4-error pre-existing baseline, `npm run build` exit 0, 726/726 vitest (unchanged — no pure-logic module involved), 257/257 redteam (1 new). Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: whether M-Care actually tries a real alternative instead of giving up on the spec's own 3 demo scenarios — Portia verifies live after publishing.

## M-Care Phase 4 continued — closing the LEARN node (2026-08-22)

Portia sent a diagram of the exact reasoning loop from her Phase 4 spec (GOAL → UNDERSTAND → LOAD MEMORY → CHECK SAFETY → PLAN → SELECT TOOL → EXECUTE → VERIFY → SUCCESS/FAILURE branch → UPDATE STATE / RETRY-ALTERNATIVE → NEXT SAFE ACTION / HUMAN ESCALATION → LEARN → JOURNEY EVENT → USER). Mapped every node against the real codebase using findings already gathered this session — no new agent spawn needed for most of it, since the diagram overlaps heavily with what RULE 32/33/34 and the original 10-point audit already covered. Every node came back real except one: **LEARN** — matching the original audit's own finding ("Learn from outcomes — MOSTLY MISSING... `OutcomeRecord` confirmed by grep to have zero references anywhere in `calculateDoctorTrustScore`/`calculateDriverTrustScore`/`calculateCompanionScore`... 'was M-Care's own recommendation/action correct' is recorded nowhere").

**A real complication, checked directly before proposing anything**: read `OutcomeRecord.jsonc` and `writeOutcomeMemory/entry.ts` in full. `OutcomeRecord` retains **zero provider identity of any kind** — no `doctor_email`/`doctor_id`/`driver_id`/`companion_id` field exists anywhere on the entity, and `writeOutcomeMemory` has `caseRecord.doctor_email` available at write time but deliberately never captures it — a real, intentional anonymization design, not an oversight. This meant "wire outcomes into trust scores" (the bigger half of LEARN) had two genuinely different-shaped options — add a provider-identity field to `OutcomeRecord` (a real anonymization-policy change) vs. a `case_id`-join at aggregation time that never denormalizes identity into the table — presented to Portia alongside the smaller, separate "M-Care learns from its own actions" reading (`logMcarePerformance`'s and `JourneyPlan`'s writes are never read back by anything). She chose the smaller slice: M-Care learning from its own actions, not the trust-score/privacy-policy question.

**Design, reusing existing infrastructure rather than building a new one**: the within-case half was already structurally in place — `JourneyPlan` is already read-granted to the agent (RULE 32) and RULE 33 already requires real, honest notes on a `failed` step. The genuinely missing piece was **cross-case** learning: a pattern like "when X fails, trying Y next actually works," observed across many journeys, generalized so a new case benefits from what happened on prior ones. Rather than a new entity or tool grant, this reuses `McareKnowledge` — M-Care's existing learn-and-recall brain, already granted `["read", "create"]` directly as an entity in `tool_configs` (confirmed by parsing the live file) — which until now only ever stored LLM-researched, externally-sourced Q&A. This adds a second, clearly-labeled source: deterministic, self-observed patterns computed from real execution history.

**Shipped**: `base44/functions/analyzeMcarePerformance/entry.ts` (new, `cronAuthorized`-gated, matching `calculateDriverTrustScore`'s modern pattern) reads the 500 most recent `JourneyPlan` records (a real, disclosed bound — this SDK's filter can't query inside a nested array field, so every plan in the batch is scanned in memory), and for each `failed` step looks at the first later step in the same plan that used a different `tool_name` — a real, disclosed simplification, not an exhaustive multi-hop analysis. Aggregates `(failed_tool_name, alternative_tool_name)` pairs across all plans; only writes a pattern with a real minimum sample size (`attempts >= 3`) and a success rate at or above chance (`>= 0.5`) — an honest floor against memorizing a single fluke. Writes/updates one `McareKnowledge` row per pattern, idempotently keyed on a deterministic `normalized_question` (`replanning:{failedTool}->{altTool}`) so a repeat run refreshes the same entry rather than duplicating it. `confidence_score` is computed by a simple, sample-size-scaled formula capped at 80 — deliberately never reaching the ≥80% floor `mcareResearchAndLearn` itself requires for a real researched fact, so a self-observed operational pattern can never read as more certain than a properly-researched one. `accuracy_estimation` and `created_by` both explicitly state this is "Computed from real JourneyPlan execution history, not researched" — never conflated with an LLM-sourced answer.

`.github/workflows/freshness-cron.yml` — added to the existing daily tier, right after `calculateDriverTrustScore` (aggregate-stats work, not a life-safety escalation, same precedent as the three trust-score functions it sits next to). `base44/agents/m_care.jsonc` — one sentence added to RULE 33 (a refinement of existing behavior, not a new rule): before choosing which alternative to try, a quick `recallMcareKnowledge` check may surface a real, self-observed pattern worth weighing — never a substitute for judgment, just one more real signal. Zero new `tool_configs` grants (confirmed unchanged at 106) — the whole feature reuses `McareKnowledge`'s and `recallMcareKnowledge`'s existing grants.

New redteam invariant (`LEARN`): pins `analyzeMcarePerformance`'s cron gate, the real `attempts >= 3`/`successRate >= 0.5` write thresholds (checked as an actual conditional, not just declared constants), that the written question/answer interpolate the real aggregated counts rather than a fabricated string, that the confidence formula structurally caps at 80, and that `tool_configs` stayed at exactly 106.

**Deliberately not built this pass**: wiring real-world `OutcomeRecord` data into doctor/driver/companion trust scores (Portia's own choice — the bigger, privacy-policy-adjacent slice, still open). Cross-case learning beyond the replanning-pattern shape (e.g. general "which procedures/countries need more replanning" analytics). No change to `logMcarePerformance`/`mcare_stage_transition` — this slice is scoped to `JourneyPlan`'s more precise per-step data, a separate, coarser audit trail untouched.

**Verification**: touches `base44/functions/analyzeMcarePerformance/entry.ts` (new) and `base44/agents/m_care.jsonc` — needs a **Publish** naming both; the `freshness-cron.yml` change needs no Base44 Publish. Lint clean, typecheck at the same 4-error pre-existing baseline, `npm run build` exit 0, 726/726 vitest (unchanged — no new pure-logic module), 258/258 redteam (1 new). Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: whether real replanning patterns actually accumulate enough sample size to produce a useful learned entry, and whether the agent's `recallMcareKnowledge` check meaningfully improves its alternative-selection in practice — Portia verifies over time as real journeys accumulate `JourneyPlan` history, or by triggering `workflow_dispatch` manually once enough real data exists.

## Hardening M-Care's learn-and-recall system — real freshness, provenance, and conflict handling (2026-08-22)

Portia sent a full, prescriptive hardening spec for `McareKnowledge` (recall → research → confidence-gate → learn): stop treating learned knowledge as permanently true, add real source provenance, give different fact categories different freshness windows, revalidate before trusting stale knowledge for anything operational, never silently overwrite a changed fact, distinguish confidence from verification, and handle a genuine source conflict by escalating rather than picking a side. Explicit constraints: don't replace the architecture, don't build a second memory system, audit first, implement the smallest safe change.

**Audit (3 parallel Explore passes, every real file read in full) found the loop itself is genuinely real** — `mcareResearchAndLearn`/`recallMcareKnowledge` backed by `McareKnowledge.jsonc`, `ACCURACY_THRESHOLD = 80` shared with `explainProcedure`, Tavily-first research with real inspectable URLs (confirmed already interpolated into the prompt at `mcareResearchAndLearn:109` and then simply thrown away, never persisted) — but **zero freshness of any kind**: no TTL, no `last_verified_at`, no source URL retained, no distinction between confidence and verification, no versioning, no conflict handling, and the token-overlap matcher was independently duplicated in both functions with three different, undocumented acceptance thresholds (0.6 / 0.15 / 0.4). Three genuinely reusable pieces of existing infrastructure were found and reused rather than duplicated: `base44/shared/freshness.ts`'s `TTL_MS`/`isFresh`/`freshnessState`/`flagForReview` (a real, generic-in-mechanism 4-key TTL map — extended, not replaced); `base44/shared/reviseAndUpdate.ts`/`PatientDataRevision` (snapshots the prior value before an overwrite — already used for SafeTProfile/CaseRecord/DietaryProfile, never for `McareKnowledge`); and `DiscoveredProviderCandidate.status`'s own precedent of an enum that structurally **excludes** `verified` since no authoritative source exists — applied identically here. No existing "two sources disagree" pattern existed anywhere in the codebase (grepped); the fix routes into the existing `DataFreshnessReview` human-review queue instead of inventing a parallel escalation mechanism. `explainProcedure`/`ProcedureKnowledge` was confirmed to have the **identical** staleness gap — deliberately left untouched this pass (named, not silently skipped) to avoid doubling the risk surface on two live knowledge systems in one change.

**Shipped**: `McareKnowledge.jsonc` gained 8 new optional fields — `source_url`/`source_type` (real provenance; a Tavily-backed answer's top result URL is now actually kept, not discarded), `verification_status` (enum `researched`/`corroborated`/`stale`/`conflicted`/`retracted` — **deliberately no `verified` value**, matching the `DiscoveredProviderCandidate` precedent exactly; `corroborated` is reachable only the honest way, via a second independent research pass over time agreeing with the first), `freshness_tier` (enum `volatile`/`regulatory`/`stable`, driving which `TTL_MS` window applies — a `PROVIDER` 4th tier from the spec was deliberately not added here, since provider-specific facts already belong on that provider's own entity, confirmed zero overlap), `last_verified_at`/`next_review_at`, and `jurisdiction`/`country`. Every existing row stays valid. `freshness.ts`'s `TTL_MS` gained three new keys (`knowledge_volatile` 24h, `knowledge_regulatory` 30d, `knowledge_stable` 180d — disclosed starting windows, not claimed-precise values) and `ReviewFlag['subject_type']` gained `mcare_knowledge_conflict`; `DataFreshnessReview.jsonc`'s matching enum too.

`base44/shared/mcareKnowledgeMatch.ts` (new) extracts the previously-duplicated tokenizer/scorer into one implementation both functions import, plus a shared `isActiveMcareKnowledgeRecord`/`knowledgeFreshnessKind` (defaults an untiered legacy record to the most lenient `stable` window — since it also has no `last_verified_at`, `freshnessState()`'s own "absent = not fresh" rule still correctly routes it through one real revalidation the next time it's actually recalled, an honest, self-throttling migration with no bulk backfill job needed).

`mcareResearchAndLearn/entry.ts` is the real behavior change: a recall match is now trusted immediately only if it's **both** fresh and `verification_status` isn't stale/conflicted/retracted — otherwise it's carried forward as `priorMatch` and the function proceeds to research anyway, with the prior answer embedded in the prompt asking the model (one extra schema field, no second round-trip) whether the fresh finding agrees or conflicts. Confirms → `reviseAndUpdate()`s the record in place (`corroborated`, prior value preserved in `PatientDataRevision`). Disagrees → neither is silently kept: the old record is marked `conflicted`, a new one is created also marked `conflicted`, and `flagForReview()` escalates into the existing `/admin/data-freshness` queue with zero new UI. A revalidation that still can't clear 80% confidence marks the prior record `stale` and returns **its own** old content, honestly labeled as unrevalidated background information — never the just-rejected fresh research, and never presented as current. `recallMcareKnowledge/entry.ts` stays strictly read-only (no revalidation triggered there, by design) but now returns `verification_status`/`is_fresh`/`source_url`/etc. on every match — including a `conflicted` one, which is still surfaced rather than hidden, so M-Care can present the uncertainty per the spec's own example rather than silently picking a side. `analyzeMcarePerformance` (the self-observed replanning-pattern writer) got the same provenance fields (`source_type: 'self_observed_pattern'`, `freshness_tier: 'volatile'`) and now goes through `reviseAndUpdate()` for its existing update-in-place path instead of a raw overwrite.

`m_care.jsonc`: the `recallMcareKnowledge`/`mcareResearchAndLearn` tool descriptions were reworded to drop "memorized brain"/"instant reuse" framing (zero new `tool_configs` grants — both already granted, so no Publish-order risk from a new-tool standpoint). New **RULE 35 — KNOWLEDGE FRESHNESS AND PROVENANCE**: never say "I permanently save this to my brain" (use "I've saved the researched information with its source so I can use it again and re-check it when it may change" instead); never say "I verified this" unless a real verification tool actually ran (cross-references RULE 19); on a `conflicted` result, present the uncertainty rather than pick a side, near-verbatim to the spec's own example phrasing; on a `stale` result for anything operational/consequential, say plainly it needs a fresh check rather than presenting it as current — general non-consequential chat may still share it, clearly labeled as unrevalidated background. Explicit closing clause: doesn't touch RULE 1's Safety Gate or RULE 19's provider-verification discipline, only how M-Care talks about its own general knowledge cache. `AdminMcareKnowledge.jsx` got small additive badges (verification_status, due-for-review, a real source link) and its flag toggle now syncs `verification_status: 'retracted'`/`'researched'` alongside the existing `flagged_for_review` boolean at their one shared write site.

**Deliberately not built this pass, stated plainly**: `explainProcedure`/`ProcedureKnowledge`'s identical staleness gap (same shape, same fix would apply — a separate, same-risk-class pass); extending provider-freshness (`TTL_MS.doctor_license`'s pattern) to TaxiService/TravelAgency/Companion/SecurityAgency (confirmed none of the 4 even has a `*_last_checked_at` field — a real, separate, larger gap unrelated to this knowledge-cache system); a dedicated `version` integer field, or duplicate `topic`/`category`/`knowledge_id` fields (all already satisfied by `question`/`journey_context`/the platform's own `id`); any path to `verification_status: 'verified'` for generic researched facts (no authoritative source exists for most of what this cache holds — fabricating one would repeat the exact "AI confidence ≠ verified truth" mistake the spec exists to prevent).

**New redteam invariant** (`KNOWLEDGE FRESHNESS`): pins the fresh-match-returns-before-any-research-call structural ordering; that a persisted/refreshed record's `baseFields` object actually carries every new provenance field; that `verification_status: 'conflicted'` is only ever set inside the explicit disagreement branch and never earlier; that `McareKnowledge`'s own schema enum structurally excludes `'verified'` and that none of the three writer functions ever sets it; that this cache never references a patient-identity field; a regression guard confirming `runDoctorVerificationScan`/`reVerifyDoctorCredentials`'s real `TTL_MS.doctor_license` usage is untouched; that every raw (non-`reviseAndUpdate`) `McareKnowledge.update()` call is metadata-only and never touches `answer`/`confidence_score`; that a failed revalidation's response is built from the prior record's own content, not the just-rejected fresh research; and that `recallMcareKnowledge` still surfaces a conflicted match rather than hiding it.

**Verification**: touches `McareKnowledge.jsonc`, `DataFreshnessReview.jsonc`, `freshness.ts`, the new `mcareKnowledgeMatch.ts`, `mcareResearchAndLearn`, `recallMcareKnowledge`, `analyzeMcarePerformance`, and `m_care.jsonc` — needs a **Publish** naming all of these. Because `mcareResearchAndLearn`/`recallMcareKnowledge` are already live and already granted (not a fresh tool-grant needing a Phase-10-style ping-test), the real risk here is a logic bug in an already-heavily-used live function, not an undeployed-tool silence — worth a few real test questions through M-Care right after publishing before trusting it in front of a patient. Lint clean, typecheck at the same 4-error pre-existing baseline, `npm run build` exit 0, 726/726 vitest (unchanged — no new pure-logic module extracted for a dedicated test file, consistent with this repo's precedent for this class of Deno logic), 259/259 redteam (1 new). Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: whether a genuine conflict/staleness scenario actually surfaces the new honest language correctly in a real conversation — Portia verifies live after publishing.

## M-Care exact device location + satellite Google Maps (2026-08-22)

Portia's report: asking M-Care "map my exact location and show me on Google Maps" got an approximate IP-based answer ("You're near Port of Spain...") with a follow-up asking whether to use exact GPS, instead of just acting deterministically. Audited before building (three parallel Explore passes): the GPS acquisition pipeline was already real and solid — `src/hooks/useAutoLocation.js`'s `requestGPS` already calls real `navigator.geolocation.getCurrentPosition` with `enableHighAccuracy: true`, captures real `accuracy_meters`, has a reentrancy guard, a `Permissions.query` pre-check for a fast honest "denied" (works around a real permission-prompt hang in a Permissions-Policy-restricted iframe), and `MCareOrb.jsx` already has a 15s timeout safety net, honest per-error-code denial messaging, and mutual exclusion between its two existing GPS-triggered flows (`LOCATION SHARE RACE` redteam test). The real bug was confirmed to be what happens *after* GPS resolves: `src/lib/locationConsentIntent.js`'s `detectLocationConsentIntent` already matches "exact location" as a substring, so the reported phrase already triggered `triggerGpsUpgrade` and a real GPS request today — but once granted, that flow just resends the same text to the LLM agent, which decides how to respond guided only by RULE 12's prose. There was no satellite/display-map capability anywhere in the codebase (confirmed via grep: zero hits for "satellite"/"basemap"/"map_action" across `mapLinks.js`/`geoUri.js`/`MessageBubble.jsx`/`m_care.jsonc`) — `generateMapLink()`'s Google Maps URL is a **directions** link (`/maps/dir/?api=1&destination=...`), not a display map centered on a point, and it only ever renders as one of three tappable buttons the user still has to tap. So "show me satellite view" was entirely up to unreliable LLM narration with no deterministic mechanism — that's the gap this closed, not a rebuild of the acquisition pipeline.

**Shipped**: `src/lib/geoUri.js` gained `buildGoogleMapsLocationUrl({latitude, longitude, zoom=19, basemap='satellite'})` — a real Google Maps **display**-map URL (`map_action=map`), deliberately distinct from `generateMapLink()`'s directions family; and `describeAccuracy(meters)` — honest, non-overclaiming accuracy language (`<=100m` → "Accuracy: approximately N meters," `>100m` → an explicit "lower-confidence location... treat this as approximate, not exact" caveat, never silently upgraded to sound precise). Both re-exported from `src/lib/mapLinks.js` (matching the existing `buildOfflineGeoUri` re-export precedent). New `src/lib/exactMapViewIntent.js`'s `detectExactMapViewIntent(text)` is a strict AND of the existing `detectLocationConsentIntent` against a narrow, additional "specifically wants it as Google Maps/satellite" signal — deliberately not a second, drifting phrase list; because it's a strict AND, checking it first in `MCareOrb.jsx`'s `sendAgentMessage` (before the existing `detectLocationConsentIntent` check) is safe, mutually exclusive routing that only ever intercepts the narrower case.

`src/hooks/useAutoLocation.js`'s `requestGPS(force = false)` gained an additive, backward-compatible second param, `options = {}` — every existing call site (`requestGPS()`, `requestGPS(true)`) keeps the established 60s `maximumAge` cache window unchanged; a new caller that must never silently reuse a stale fix passes `{ maximumAge: 0 }`.

`MCareOrb.jsx` gained a third, deterministic, client-only GPS-triggered flow mirroring the two existing ones: `pendingExactMapViewRef` (a new pending-flag alongside the existing two, with full 3-way mutual exclusion — each of the three trigger functions clears the other two's pending refs, matching the pattern the `LOCATION SHARE RACE` redteam test already pins), `triggerExactMapView()` (posts "I'll get your current device location and open it in Google Maps," always forces a brand-new fix via `requestGPS(true, { maximumAge: 0 })` — never the cached-fix fast path the other two flows use — and arms the same shared 15s timeout safety net), and `sendExactLocationMapMessage()` (reads the raw `gpsLocationRef` fix, never `bestLocationRef`/IP, builds a message with `describeAccuracy()` plus a `{{satmap:LABEL|lat,lng}}` token always paired with a `{{qr:...}}` token — matching RULE 12's own established "maps token always paired with a QR" convention, giving a real offline-capable artifact even when the live Google Maps page itself can't be reached — and branches its closing line honestly on the existing `isOnline` signal so it never claims Google Maps opened while offline). The existing `gpsStatus`-resolution effect gained a third branch for the new pending ref, placed so it does **not** add a second `clearGpsRequestTimeout()` call — the existing single call at the top of the granted branch already covers all three flows.

`MessageBubble.jsx` gained `extractSatMap` (same shape as `extractMaps`/`extractQr`) and `SatelliteMapBlock` — a `MapPin` icon (never an avatar/face), a real `<a href target=_blank>` "Open in Google Maps" button (reliable — not an async `window.open` that risks silent popup-blocking) via `buildGoogleMapsLocationUrl`, and a "Copy Coordinates" button (`navigator.clipboard.writeText`) — raw lat/lng stays out of the visible message text by default, available on demand.

**Concurrent work collision, reconciled, not silently overwritten.** While this was being built, Portia (via Base44 Builder) independently hit and fixed the exact same live reported bug — pushed as `f9839fcf` ("Prevent duplicate GPS request bubbles and infinite error loops," `MCareOrb.jsx`) and `44d97ebc` ("Apply exec_tool script file changes," `m_care.jsonc`/`MessageBubble.jsx`/`locationConsentIntent.js`/`mapLinks.js`). Investigated in full before merging (`git show --stat`/full diffs on both commits) rather than blindly force-pushing over real work or blindly auto-merging: their fix broadened `detectLocationConsentIntent`'s PATTERNS considerably (new phrasings: "map my location," "show me on the map," "where am i," "what are my coordinates," etc. — confirmed backward-compatible with every existing test case in `exactMapViewIntent.test.js`, since `detectExactMapViewIntent` is a strict AND against it) and added a genuinely different, complementary satellite mechanism: a new **SHOW MY OWN LOCATION** section in RULE 12 tells the agent that once GPS is already granted (a real `source=gps_precise` LOCATION_CONTEXT block already present) and the traveler asks to see their own location, it may emit the existing `{{maps:...}}` token directly with an optional `|satellite` third segment (`{{maps:My Location|lat,lng|satellite}}`) — `generateMapLink`/`openInMapsApp` gained a matching `satellite` param (per-app URL variants: Google Maps' legacy `/maps/@lat,lng,16z/data=!3m1!1e3` format, Apple Maps' `t=h` hybrid, Waze falls back to normal since it has no satellite toggle), and `extractMaps`'s regex gained an optional third `(?:\|(satellite))?` group.

This is a real, coherent complement rather than a duplicate: `detectExactMapViewIntent` still fires first and deterministically for the primary target phrase (forced-fresh GPS, honest accuracy, works even if the message never reaches the agent at all) — their mechanism is the natural secondary/fallback path for a later, in-conversation "show me on satellite" mention once GPS is already in hand, where re-requesting a brand-new fix would be excessive. Rather than keep two competing agent-facing tokens for the same role, this session's own `{{satmap:...}}` was made a genuine **client-only** capability — `m_care.jsonc` is never told the agent may emit it itself, closing the risk of an LLM reply claiming a "device fix" it never actually obtained that turn; their already-tested `{{maps:...|satellite}}` mechanism became the one real agent-facing satellite path, replacing this session's own now-redundant SATELLITE MAP FALLBACK paragraph, which was dropped.

**A genuine JSON-validity bug found and fixed as part of the merge, not part of either side's own work in isolation**: the bot's `44d97ebc` commit had pushed `m_care.jsonc` with 14 instances of `\'` (an invalid JSON escape sequence — only `\" \\ \/ \b \f \n \r \t \uXXXX` are legal; this file's own established convention, confirmed throughout this session and prior ones, uses plain un-escaped single quotes) inside its new SHOW MY OWN LOCATION prose (quoted example phrases like `'show me on the map'`). `origin/main`'s `m_care.jsonc` genuinely did not parse as valid JSON at the moment this merge began — confirmed directly (`json.loads()` raised `Invalid \escape`), not assumed. Fixed by replacing every `\'` with a plain `'` as part of resolving the merge conflict, re-verified `json.loads()`-valid and `tool_configs` count unchanged at 106 before writing.

**A second real bug, emergent only from combining both sides' changes, found and fixed**: the bot's new duplicate-bubble guard in `triggerGpsUpgrade` (an early return when `gpsStatus === 'requesting'`, avoiding a second "Getting your exact location now" bubble and a reset timeout on a repeated typed message) updated only `pendingGpsRetryQueryRef` on that early-return path — it never cleared `pendingLocationPinRef`/`pendingExactMapViewRef`, the two other pending-flow refs this session's own mutual-exclusion work depends on. A user whose GPS request was already in flight from an older, different flow (e.g. a "Send my current location" tap) and who then typed a fresh, more specific question would have had the OLDER, superseded flow still win when GPS resolved — silently dropping what they'd just asked for, the exact class of bug the "newest request wins" mutual-exclusion discipline exists to prevent elsewhere in this same file. Fixed by clearing both refs inside the early-return branch too, and the same "already requesting" duplicate-bubble guard was added to this session's own new `triggerExactMapView` for consistency with the newly-established pattern (with one disclosed, narrow, accepted edge case: if GPS was already in flight from a *different* flow using the default cached-fix window, the eventual fix this request receives may not be a freshly-forced one — a rare few-second-overlap scenario, not worth a second in-flight-request-priority system on top of the existing reentrancy guard).

**A real, pre-existing, unrelated redteam test bug found and flagged, not fixed** (out of scope for this pass — it doesn't affect anything this change touches, fixing it is a separate, deliberate decision Portia should make): the `GPS REQUEST TIMEOUT` test's `effectBlock` slice searches for the literal string `'}, [gpsStatus, sendAgentMessage, agentSending]);'`, but the real, current dependency array on that effect is `[gpsStatus, gpsErrorCode, sendAgentMessage, agentSending, sendLocationPin, sendExactLocationMapMessage]` — the search string was already stale before this pass. Since `String.prototype.slice`'s `indexOf`-not-found (`-1`) end argument is interpreted as "one before the end of the whole file," the test's `effectBlock` has actually been a giant slice spanning from the effect to near end-of-file, not the intended few-line block, for some time — it still happens to pass today only because the literal `clearGpsRequestTimeout();` call happens to occur exactly twice across that whole broad remaining span. This pass's own new `clearGpsRequestTimeout()` calls (inside `triggerExactMapView`) sit safely before `effectIdx` (declared earlier in the file), so they don't affect this count — but the underlying test bug remains real and worth a dedicated fix.

**Verification**: touches `m_care.jsonc` on the backend side (both the RULE 12 satellite mechanism and the JSON-validity fix) — needs a **Publish** naming that file (text-only content, no `tool_configs` change — count confirmed unchanged at 106 through the whole merge — near-zero Publish-order risk since nothing new is granted). Every other file is frontend-only. Lint clean, typecheck at the same 5-error pre-existing baseline (fixed one real, new error along the way — TS infers an incomplete parameter shape from a destructured-with-defaults JS function parameter with no type annotation, which surfaced as 3 errors across `geoUri.js`/`MessageBubble.jsx`; fixed by giving `buildGoogleMapsLocationUrl` an explicit JSDoc `@param` type and destructuring inside the function body instead of the parameter list), `npm run build` exit 0, 742/742 vitest (16 new across `tests/exactMapViewIntent.test.js`, an extension to `tests/geoUri.test.js`, and the bot's own extension to `tests/locationConsentIntent.test.js`), 260/260 redteam (1 new — `EXACT LOCATION SATELLITE MAP`, plus `LOCATION SHARE RACE` extended for the 3-way mutual exclusion — and two fixed-character-offset test slices widened twice over the course of this pass as added lines from both sides of the merge pushed their target text further from its anchor, the same class of maintenance this project has hit before). Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: whether a real device's `getCurrentPosition` and either satellite mechanism (the primary deterministic one, or the bot's own agent-narrated `|satellite` fallback) actually render correctly end-to-end on a real phone/desktop browser — Portia verifies live after publishing.

## Two real safety bugs in M-Care's distress-detection path (2026-08-23)

Portia showed a live screenshot: she told M-Care "I have chest pain," it replied with unrelated Trinidad tourism recommendations, and only a frustrated follow-up ("i think you missing the point i told i have a chest pain") triggered the real Silent Guardian welfare prompt. She then tapped "Yes — send help now" and got: "I'm having trouble reaching help right now. Please try again, or use the SOS option if this is urgent." — a confirmed medical distress signal produced no actual help. Three parallel Explore passes (distress phrase detection; the confirm/dispatch flow; the two backend functions it calls) found two distinct, real, safety-critical bugs, not one.

**Bug 1 — voice messages never ran distress detection at all.** `src/lib/distressDetection.js`'s `detectDistressSignal` was already correct and complete — `'chest pain'` is a real, word-boundary-matched phrase in its `medical` category, confirmed to match both "I have a chest pain" and the patient's actual follow-up text; there is no deny-list anywhere in that file that could suppress it (a same-named `DENY_PATTERNS` exists in the unrelated `mcareReactionHeuristic.js`, which only picks an emoji reaction and has nothing to do with this path). The bug was the call site: `MCareOrb.jsx`'s `sendAgentMessage` only ran the check inside `if (!fileUrls?.length) { ... }`. `handleVoiceMessage` (the record-a-voice-note flow) transcribes real spoken audio and calls `sendAgentMessage(transcript, [file_url])` — a **non-empty** `fileUrls` array — so the distress check was skipped entirely and the real transcript went straight to the LLM as ordinary text, in a voice-heavy app that has invested heavily in hands-free/accessibility flows. Fixed by removing that gate entirely — `detectDistressSignal(q)` now always runs on the outgoing text regardless of attachments. Safe for every existing caller: `handleFileSelect`'s own fixed "I've uploaded: X.pdf" string never matches any phrase (verified against the full list), so scanning it too is a harmless no-op; only the voice-message case actually changes behavior. On a hit, the function still returns before the turn ever reaches the agent, exactly matching the existing typed-text behavior — the voice recording itself is never forwarded that turn, the local welfare-check prompt takes its place.

**Bug 2 — a confirmed "Yes, send help" tap could fail completely for a patient with no `CaseRecord` yet, with no real fallback.** `handleDistressConfirm` fires two client-side calls in `Promise.allSettled`: `requestOnDemandRide` and (only if `activeCaseRecord?.id` exists) `notifyGuardianNow`. Both backend functions genuinely require an existing `CaseRecord` — `requestOnDemandRide` 400s with "No active case found for this account" if the patient has zero `CaseRecord` rows, and separately 400s if neither GPS/IP coordinates nor a manual `pickup_address` are available (the confirm handler never sent one, only raw lat/lng); `notifyGuardianNow` was skipped client-side outright whenever `activeCaseRecord?.id` was falsy. Because the SDK's functions client has `interceptResponses: false`, a non-2xx response **rejects** the promise rather than resolving with a `.data.error`, so both calls land as `rejected`, both `rideOk`/`guardianOk` end up `false`, and the generic "use the SOS option" fallback fired — a real, easily-reproduced dead end for exactly the kind of patient most likely to be describing an urgent symptom before finishing booking. Read `base44/functions/triggerSOS/entry.ts` in full to confirm the referenced SOS path actually is case-optional: `case_id` is `case_id || null`/`case_id || ''` everywhere, every case-dependent step (LiveLocation, GuardianSession auto-create, emergency-contact email) is an `if (case_id) {...}` enhancement, never a requirement — only `patient_email`/`trigger_type` are required, auth accepts either a real session or a PIN session token, and it always emails `ADMIN_EMAIL` (if configured) and SMS-confirms the patient (if phone + Twilio configured) regardless of case status. `src/components/emergency/SOSDropdown.jsx` is the established, working client-side call pattern this fix reuses rather than inventing a new one.

**Fixed, without touching `requestOnDemandRide`/`notifyGuardianNow`'s own case-required contracts** (those functions are legitimately designed around an active traveler+case; relaxing that broadly risked unrelated side effects) — instead `handleDistressConfirm` itself got more robust: (1) a `pickup_address` fallback (built from `bestLocation.city`/`.country`) is now sent to `requestOnDemandRide` whenever coordinates aren't available yet, satisfying that function's own "coordinates OR address" requirement instead of guaranteeing a 400 whenever GPS hasn't resolved; (2) when both soft channels still genuinely fail, the `else` branch now automatically calls `triggerSOS` — the same case-optional, already-proven mechanism `SOSDropdown.jsx` uses, with the same real payload shape (`patient_email`/`patient_name` from the real `user` object via `useAuth()`, `patient_phone`/`procedure_country` from `activeCaseRecord` when present, `trigger_type` mapped from the real `signal.category` — `'medical'` → `'ambulance'`, everything else → `'police'`, both real `SOS_ROUTES` values, never invented) — instead of just telling a possibly-distressed patient to go find and tap a different button themselves. The reply is honest about which real channel was actually reached, and — if even that throws — says plainly to call local emergency services directly rather than claiming a fourth attempt would help.

**Deliberately not built this pass**: no change to `requestOnDemandRide`/`notifyGuardianNow`'s own case-required design; no new `triggerSOS` schema field for "why this fired" context (its `.strict()` schema would need a backend change — `location_label` already carries a real, honest description without one); no change to `handleDistressSignal`'s existing 30-second de-dupe (unrelated to either bug).

**New redteam invariant** (`DISTRESS ESCALATION`): pins the old `fileUrls`-gated wrapper is gone from the distress check and that `handleVoiceMessage` still sends the real transcript through it; that a real `triggerSOS` call exists inside `handleDistressConfirm`'s failure branch, structurally only reachable after the real `confirmed` gate (never on a decline, never before a real traveler tap); that `trigger_type` is derived from the real signal category, never hardcoded; and that `requestOnDemandRide` gets the real `pickup_address` fallback.

**Verification**: both fixes are 100% frontend (`MCareOrb.jsx`) — no backend function changes, no `m_care.jsonc` change, no Base44 Publish needed; ships with the normal frontend deploy. Lint clean, typecheck at the same 5-error pre-existing baseline, `npm run build` exit 0, 742/742 vitest (unchanged — no new pure-logic module extracted), 261/261 redteam (1 new). Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: whether a real voice message containing a distress phrase now correctly triggers the welfare prompt, and whether a real confirmed "Yes" tap with no case on file now genuinely reaches the admin team via SOS — Portia verifies live after deploying, ideally by testing the exact scenario from her screenshots.

**Concurrent work reconciled, same push: a real GPS/sandbox-detection rewrite landed independently.** While the two fixes above were being built, 11 more `base44-builder[bot]` commits landed on `origin/main` — a genuine, well-reasoned root-cause fix for the earlier location loop bug (see the previous CLAUDE.md section), not a duplicate of anything this session built. New `src/lib/isSandboxed.js` detects a restricted preview/iframe context via the one reliable signal (`window.self === window.top`) — the actual root cause of the earlier "Getting your exact location now" loop: `getCurrentPosition` never fires either callback inside such a sandbox, so it hangs forever. `triggerGpsUpgrade` was rewritten onto a new pattern: `useAutoLocation.js`'s `requestGPS` now returns a Promise that **always** resolves (never rejects, never hangs) via its own internal hard timeout (25s live / 2s sandboxed, guarded by a `hardTimeoutFired` flag so the timeout and the real `getCurrentPosition` callbacks can never both resolve the same promise) — the timeout guarantee moved from each caller arming its own timer into the hook itself, so every caller gets it for free. `triggerGpsUpgrade` also gained a new synchronous `locationRequestPendingRef` cross-flow dedup gate (checked before any async work, not just after `gpsStatus` flips to `'requesting'`) and an upfront `isSandboxed()` short-circuit that shows a rich, honest, actionable approximate-location fallback (`src/lib/locationContext.js`'s new `buildSandboxLocationMessage`/`buildGpsFallbackMessage` — real city/country from `bestLocation`, a tappable `{{maps:...}}` button to see the approximate area, and real nearby-care choice chips) instead of ever attempting a request doomed to hang. `triggerExactMapView`/`shareCurrentLocationNow` also got the same `locationRequestPendingRef`/`isSandboxed()` upfront checks, but were **not** converted to the new inline-await pattern — they still use their own caller-side `gpsRequestTimeoutRef`/`setTimeout` safety net, a legitimate, deliberate hybrid state (not every GPS-triggering flow needed to move at once).

Reconciled by running the full test suite after merging (not by reading the whole 259-line `MCareOrb.jsx` diff blind) — the redteam suite's own precise structural pins made every real mismatch surface immediately: `GPS REQUEST TIMEOUT`, `LOCATION PAUSE BYPASS`, and `LOCATION SHARE RACE` all failed on the old, now-superseded single-line code shapes they were pinned to (`if (prefs.locationPaused && !force) return;` → now a multi-line block that also resolves the promise; `if (gpsInFlightRef.current) return;` → same; `triggerGpsUpgrade`'s own `gpsRequestTimeoutRef` → moved into `requestGPS` itself). All three rewritten to verify the same real safety properties against the actual new code — `GPS REQUEST TIMEOUT` now checks `requestGPS`'s own internal hard-timeout guarantee (3 `hardTimeoutFired` checks: one per resolution path) *and* confirms `triggerExactMapView`/`shareCurrentLocationNow` still carry their own unconverted timeout — rather than assuming the old single-timeout-in-`triggerGpsUpgrade` shape still holds. `EXACT LOCATION SATELLITE MAP` and `DISTRESS ESCALATION` (this session's own work) needed no changes — full gate re-confirmed green after reconciling: lint clean, typecheck at the same 5-error baseline, `npm run build` exit 0, 742/742 vitest, 261/261 redteam.

## `save_memory` — a hidden Base44 platform tool that can leak into the chat UI (2026-08-23)

Portia sent a screenshot showing a red `save_memory — error` badge inline in an assistant reply. `save_memory` is not anything this repo built or grants — zero references anywhere in `m_care.jsonc`'s `tool_configs` (106 grants, all explicit) or its instructions text. It's a Base44 platform-native built-in: the agent runtime automatically fires it because `memory_config.enabled: true` on `m_care.jsonc`'s own top-level schema (`scope: "both"`), and it shows up in `AgentMessage.tool_calls` exactly like any granted tool call (the SDK's own generic shape: `{name, status: "running"|"success"|"error"|"stopped"|"waiting_for_user_input", results}`). `src/components/mcare-agent/MessageBubble.jsx`'s generic `ToolCallDisplay`/`StatusIcon` renders ANY unrecognized tool_call name with a plain label + status icon — a red `XCircle` for `error`/`failed` — with no allowlist of "known" names, so this hidden platform call got the same raw badge treatment as a real granted tool failing.

Already fixed, same day, by a `base44-builder[bot]` commit (`2cb454a7`, "Hide save_memory tool calls from message bubbles") that landed on `origin/main` shortly before this was investigated: `message.tool_calls?.filter(tc => tc.name !== 'save_memory').map(...)` at `MessageBubble.jsx`'s one and only tool_calls render site. Verified, not just trusted: it's the only place `tool_calls` is mapped in the file; neither `MCareOrb.jsx` (whose own single `tool_calls` reference is a `.find()` for Talk Mode's speech-ack logic, never a render) nor `MCareAgent.jsx` (zero `tool_calls` references at all) has an independent rendering path that could bypass it; the screenshot's own label proves the real runtime name is the literal string `'save_memory'`, so the filter's `!==` comparison matches exactly with no casing/prefix drift to worry about. The screenshot itself almost certainly predates this fix landing/being Published — this repo's standing deploy model (a GitHub push only syncs into Base44 Builder, a separate manual Publish makes it live) fully explains it without the fix being broken.

**Confirmed cosmetic-only, not a functional bug**: the "sending" spinner clears purely on `role === 'assistant'`, never consulting tool_call status; the one place status *is* read (Talk Mode's speech-ack effect) only matches `pending`/`running`/`in_progress`, ignoring `error` entirely; `ToolCallDisplay`/`StatusIcon` degrade gracefully on any status with no crash risk. The assistant's real text reply was never affected by this badge either way.

**One real, separate, unresolved finding — flagged, not fixable from this repo**: the underlying `save_memory` call genuinely returns `status: 'error'` server-side. Hiding the badge doesn't fix that — it's Base44's own opaque, internal memory-write implementation, with no logs, error detail, or documentation reachable from this checkout. If M-Care's cross-conversation memory continuity matters, this is worth a direct question to Base44 support — nothing in this repo can diagnose or repair it.

**New redteam invariant** (`SAVE_MEMORY`): pins the filter staying in place in `MessageBubble.jsx`, confirms `tool_calls` is referenced exactly once in that file (so a second, unfiltered render path can't silently reopen the same leak), and confirms neither `MCareOrb.jsx` nor `MCareAgent.jsx` independently renders a tool_calls badge list.

**Verification**: test-only change (`tests/redteam/invariants.spec.js`) — no Base44 Publish needed. Lint clean, typecheck at the same 5-error pre-existing baseline, `npm run build` exit 0, 742/742 vitest (unchanged), 262/262 redteam (1 new). Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: whether the badge is actually gone in the live app now (needs a Publish, if not already done), and whether Base44 support can explain why `save_memory` errors server-side.

## Inline "ghost text" auto-fill for M-Care's chat input, Tab to accept (2026-08-23)

Portia wanted Gmail-Smart-Compose-style predictive typing in M-Care's chat input — gray suggested text appears inline right after the cursor as she types, with a small "Tab" hint to accept it. Investigation found the real prediction backend already existed and was already live (`base44/functions/smartMcareInput/entry.ts`, `mode: 'predict'`), but its only frontend consumer, `src/components/mcare-agent/SmartInputSuggestions.jsx`, rendered predictions as tappable chips below the input — a different interaction model from inline ghost text, and there was no overlay-positioning pattern anywhere else in the codebase to build on. Confirmed with Portia (AskUserQuestion): replace the chips with the new inline UI (both do the same job — showing both would be redundant) and leave the existing "Did you mean" grammar-correction bar (same file, `mode: 'correct'`) exactly as it already works.

**One honest calibration, not a silent mismatch**: the existing `predict` backend returns whole next-*phrase* completions meant to be appended after a space (confirmed by the old chip code's own `onPick={(p) => setInput(input + ' ' + p)}`), not raw mid-word character completion — so a literal "au" → "tomatically" merge isn't what ships; phrase-level continuation is (the same class of feature — real Gmail Smart Compose is phrase-level too). No backend prompt change — reusing the real, already-tuned endpoint as-is beat inventing a second LLM task for word-stub completion.

**Shipped**: `src/lib/ghostTextSuggestion.js` (new, pure, unit-tested) — `shouldQueryGhostSuggestion({text, caretAtEnd, disabled})` mirrors the exact thresholds the old chip predict effect used (`MIN_LEN=3`, skip if text ends in `.!?` or exceeds 140 chars), plus one new gate the chip version never needed: the caret must be at the very end of the text, or the ghost text would visually appear somewhere the blinking cursor isn't. `buildAcceptedText(currentText, suggestion)` appends with exactly one space, never double-spacing.

`src/hooks/useGhostTextSuggestion.js` (new) — the stateful hook: same 600ms debounce and dedup-by-last-query as the old chip effect, same `smartMcareInput` call, takes `predictions?.[0]` as the single ghost suggestion. Re-checks the caret position fresh at fetch time (not a stale closure value, since the user may have clicked elsewhere during the debounce window) and again right before setting the resolved suggestion. A monotonic request-token ref guards against a slower, superseded request overwriting a newer one's result — a real correctness risk with a debounced network call the old chip code's own `lastQueryRef` dedup didn't fully need to solve (chips never had a "wrong answer visually in the wrong place" failure mode; ghost text does). Clears the suggestion synchronously the instant the text changes, so nothing stale lingers on screen while the next fetch is in flight.

`src/components/mcare-agent/GhostTextOverlay.jsx` (new, presentational) — an absolutely-positioned, `pointer-events: none` (load-bearing — without it the overlay would silently swallow clicks meant for the real input beneath it), transparent-background layer stacked over the real input: an invisible span of the already-typed text (so it occupies the exact same width) followed by the gray suggestion and a small "Tab" `<kbd>` hint. Takes `matchStyle`/`matchClassName` (both defaulted, not required — an earlier draft that made them required-by-inference broke typecheck at whichever call site didn't pass one, since this repo's `--allowJs` prop-type inference unifies a component's shape across *all* its call sites) so each caller mirrors its own input's exact box model.

Wired into both real chat surfaces — `src/components/mcare/MCareOrb.jsx` (plain inline-styled `<input>`, previously had no `ref` at all — added one) and `src/pages/MCareAgent.jsx` (shadcn `Input`, styled via its `cn(...)`'d className string so the overlay's box model can't drift out of sync with Tailwind changes to the real one) — each wraps just the input element in a `position: relative` container, renders `<GhostTextOverlay>` as its sibling, and extends the existing `handleKeyDown` with a Tab branch checked *before* the existing Enter-to-send logic (Tab was completely unclaimed in both files before this — confirmed no accessibility regression, since the branch only fires while a real suggestion is showing).

`SmartInputSuggestions.jsx` had its `predict` effect, `predictions` state, `handlePick`, the chip JSX block, and the `onPick` prop removed entirely — the `correct` effect and "Did you mean" bar are untouched. Both call sites dropped the now-gone `onPick` prop.

**No backend changes** — reuses `smartMcareInput` exactly as it already runs in production. **No new redteam invariant** — this is a pure UX/convenience feature with no safety, PHI, or auth dimension, unlike what that suite exists to guard.

**Verification**: `tests/ghostTextSuggestion.test.js` (new, 13 cases). 100% frontend — no Base44 Publish needed. Lint clean, typecheck at the same 5-error pre-existing baseline (one new error surfaced and fixed mid-build — see the `matchStyle`/`matchClassName` defaults note above), `npm run build` exit 0, 755/755 vitest (13 new), 262/262 redteam (unchanged). Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: whether the overlay text actually lines up pixel-perfectly with the real caret on a real device/browser (font-rendering can vary), and whether the 600ms debounce feels responsive rather than laggy in a real typing session — Portia verifies live.

## M-Safe orb redesign — a pearl-shell, no-face identity wired to 8 real states (2026-08-23)

Portia's read on the shipped orb (`src/components/mcare/LivingOrb.jsx`, the M-Care/M-Safe chat orb — blink/wink gold eyes on a dark glass sphere, no M mark, per the 2026-08-12 "eyes are back, the M mark is gone" reversal documented earlier in this file): it looked passive/"depressed," like a loading indicator, not a premium AI-infrastructure presence. Full spec: pearl-metallic shell + dark inner core + gold M emblem + gold energy-ring halo, explicitly **no face, no eyes, no mouth**, and every visual state tied to real backend/tool/voice/safety signals — never simulated. This is a deliberate further reversal of the orb's own 2026-08-12 direction, not a contradiction to relitigate — the next honest iteration in a sequence Portia has already walked three times on this exact component, per this project's own "build on what's asked, don't front-load spec questions" pattern.

Investigation (3 parallel Explore passes + 2 direct file reads) found the state machinery mostly real and reusable, with two genuine gaps: **THINKING and TOOL_EXECUTING were conflated** — `agentSending` (driving the old `orbState`'s `'thinking'`) clears the instant the tool-call placeholder message lands in the socket payload, before the tool actually finishes; a real signal for "a tool is genuinely running right now" already existed (`runningTool`, using the tool's real `status` field and its own `display_projection.active_label` text like "Finding doctors...") but was only ever used to pick a spoken TTS phrase in Conversational Mode, never to drive the orb or show that label anywhere on screen. **ALERT (safety) had no orb-level signal at all** — `SafetyGateCard.jsx`'s tier classification (pending/failed/cleared/caution/blockRefusal) lived entirely inside each message bubble's own render; `MCareOrb.jsx` never scanned `agentMessages` for an active safety block. Every other state already had a real signal: SPEAKING (`speaking`/`isNeuralSpeaking()`), OFFLINE (`isOnline`, already the `error`-named config entry), PROACTIVE (`hasUnseenJourneyEvent`, already `acting`), LISTENING (real via Conversational Mode).

Two scope questions confirmed with Portia via AskUserQuestion before building: **parallax** — desktop pointer-tilt only this pass (no 3D-transform or device-motion code exists anywhere in the app today; mobile gyroscope parallax needs a real iOS permission-prompt UX untestable from this checkout, deferred as its own device-tested follow-up); **scope** — `LivingOrb.jsx` only, not `LivingMOrb.jsx` (the homepage hero orb, a deliberately separate component) — a disclosed, intentional visual inconsistency between the two surfaces for now, not a silent gap.

**Shipped**:

- **`LivingOrb.jsx` rewritten** — dropped the dark-glass gradient + `useBlinkState` blink/wink eyes entirely (no face, no eyes, no mouth, per the spec's repeated explicit instruction). New `Shell` sub-component: a pearl/white `radial-gradient` outer disc (metallic highlight bands, no new image asset) with a smaller inset dark `radial-gradient` circle layered on top as the "glass core," plus a small floating gold M badge — the existing `/morales-m-mark.png` asset (already used in ~30 other places, no new asset generated) sized to ~38% of the orb diameter with a pearl circular backing, positioned bottom-left overlapping the shell — deliberately a badge, not a face-filling watermark (avoiding a rehash of the approach already tried and reversed once on 2026-08-12). `STATE_CONFIG` grew from 6 entries to 8: `idle`/`listening`/`thinking`/`tool_executing`/`speaking`/`acting`/`alert`/`offline` (renamed from `error` — its only real use today). `thinking`'s old rotating conic-gradient sweep — which risked reading as a generic loading spinner, the one thing the spec explicitly rules out — is replaced by 3 small gold points orbiting the ring, each independently pulsing opacity (literalizes the spec's own "● ● ●" example); `tool_executing` reuses the same 3-point motif faster and brighter, so the two read as one family with `tool_executing` clearly more "active." `alert` gets its own amber ring color (`#D97706`) over the identical shell shape, moderate (2.0s) pulsing — never a different icon, never panicked. `acting` gained a small persistent gold notification dot on the orb itself (previously only on the outer floating button). Added a restrained desktop-only pointer-tilt: local `onMouseMove`/`onMouseLeave` on the orb's own bounding box compute a few degrees of `rotateX`/`rotateY` via framer-motion's `animate`, gated behind `matchMedia('(hover: hover) and (pointer: fine)')` so it's inert on touch, and behind `prefers-reduced-motion` (the same live-listening pattern the file already had) so it's fully off with the rest of the animation. `useBlinkState.js` itself is untouched — `LivingMOrb.jsx` still uses it.
- **`src/lib/safetyGateStatus.js`** (new) — `SafetyGateCard.jsx`'s own inline `parseResult`/tier-classification logic extracted into `classifySafetyToolCall(toolCall)` (behavior-identical, `SafetyGateCard.jsx` now imports and uses it, one implementation not two) plus `hasActiveSafetyAlert(messages)`, which scans a conversation's messages for a real, currently-blocking-or-cautionary safety tool_call (never pending, never cleared) — the function `MCareOrb.jsx` needed to derive its new `alert` state as a genuine reflection of what `SafetyGateCard` is already showing inline, never a second, independently-computed safety decision.
- **`MCareOrb.jsx`** — `orbState`'s priority chain rebuilt: `offline` → `alert` → `listening` → `tool_executing` → `thinking` → `speaking` → `acting` → `idle` (offline overrides everything; an active safety block is the most attention-critical live-conversation fact; proactive is lowest since it's background/idle-adjacent, not an active conversation). The panel header's `LivingOrb`/`McareAvatar` conditional (only showed the real orb during Conversational Mode, `McareAvatar` — idle/thinking-only — otherwise) was simplified to always render `<LivingOrb state={orbState} .../>`, so every real state (alert, tool_executing, etc.) is actually visible in the one most-prominent, always-open orb instance, not just when a call happens to be live — a natural extension of the redesign's own stated goal that the plan's own file list already implied. The `agentSending`-only typing indicator (Site 5) now also gates on the new `activeRunningTool` (so it stays visible through tool execution, not just the "still composing" phase before `agentSending` clears) and swaps its static "M-Safe is coordinating…" text for the tool's own real `display_projection.active_label` when one exists — reusing the exact text already spoken via TTS in Conversational Mode, never fabricated. Both typing indicators (Sites 4/5) swapped from the coarse `McareAvatar size glow` wrapper to `<LivingOrb state={orbState} .../>` directly, so they reflect the one real computed state instead of a hardcoded binary. The empty-state `GREETING` bubble now prefixes "Welcome back, {firstName}." when `user?.full_name` is available (real, already-loaded data — never fabricated), falling back to the unchanged generic opening line otherwise. The header status pill's live-state text renamed `"LIVE SESSION"` → `"LIVE AGENT"` to match the spec's literal wording — color/dot/PAUSED/OFFLINE variants unchanged. **Not** added: the second purple "AI SUPER AGENTIC" badge or the Analyze/Protect/Coordinate/Resolve icon row visible in the reference screenshot — the spec's own text says "Do not overdecorate the header," and neither element is described anywhere in the written instructions.

**Deliberately not built this pass**: mobile device-motion/gyroscope parallax (confirmed scope decision above); the homepage hero orb (`LivingMOrb.jsx`, confirmed scope decision above); any change to the actual SAFE-T/safety decision authority — the new `alert` orb state only ever narrates a classification `SafetyGateCard.jsx` already computes from a real tool result, it never scores or overrides anything.

**Verification**: touches only frontend files — no entity/function/`m_care.jsonc` change, no Base44 Publish needed for backend reasons, but the frontend bundle still needs a normal deploy to go live. Lint clean, typecheck at the same 5-error pre-existing baseline (unrelated `MCareVaultUpload.jsx`/`MCareAgent.jsx` errors, unaffected), `npm run build` exit 0, 755/755 vitest (unchanged — no pure-logic module in this specific change was already covered by a dedicated test file, and none needed one), 262/262 redteam (unchanged — no entity/tool/permission surface touched; the `alert` state only reflects an existing classification, it computes no new safety authority). Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: whether the pearl-shell/badge/halo actually reads as "premium AI infrastructure" rather than over- or under-designed, whether the pointer-tilt feels restrained rather than gimmicky on a real desktop, and whether the 3-orbiting-dots thinking/tool-executing states and the real tool-label text read clearly in a real conversation — Portia verifies live after this ships.

**Same-day correction — the first pass missed the mark, and screenshots showed exactly why.** Portia's reaction to the live result: "you give me nothing what i ask for," with a close-up of the live floating orb and the original reference image again. Comparing them found two concrete, diagnosable bugs, not a vague "needs more polish": (1) **`Shell`'s outer gradient was semi-transparent** (`rgba(255,255,255,...)` fading to `0.22` alpha at the edge) — over this app's dark backgrounds, a mostly-transparent white blends into gray rather than reading as an opaque pearl sphere, exactly matching the dim gray ring in the close-up screenshot. Fixed with opaque hex gradient stops (`#ffffff` → `#f5f1e8` → `#d9d3c4` → `#b8b2a0`) that can't blend with whatever's behind them, plus a separate specular-highlight blob and a thin bright rim-light ring layered on top for real glossiness (previously one smooth gradient, now genuinely layered). The dark core's inset was also reduced (68%/16% → 60%/20%) so the pearl rim is actually visible instead of a thin, easy-to-miss band, and gained one small, deliberately asymmetric gloss highlight (never a symmetric pair — that would read as eyes, the one thing this whole redesign is built around avoiding). (2) **The header orb was still tiny (36px)** next to a reference image where the orb is a dominant ~130px hero element — at 36px none of the shell work was perceptible, which is exactly the "loading indicator" impression Portia originally objected to in the first place. Fixed: the header orb grew to 104px and the header itself was restructured from a single thin row into a two-column layout (large orb on the left; title/subtitle/controls on top-right, then a badge row, then a capability row, all stacked on the right) — every existing header element (both mode-toggle buttons, the new-journey reset, expand/collapse, close, the status/private-mode/language pills) was repositioned, none dropped. Also reversed a call from the first pass: the second "✦ AI SUPER AGENTIC" badge (styled with this app's existing `PURPLE` constant) and the Analyze/Protect/Coordinate/Resolve capability row (gold-accented icon-in-ring + label, `Sparkles`/`Shield`/`Share2`/`CheckCircle2`) were added back in — the first pass's "reference-image styling, not literal spec" read of these two elements was wrong, confirmed by Portia re-showing the same reference image after seeing the result without them. Both are decorative (no new interactive behavior invented) and styled for this panel's actual light `#fff` chrome, not a literal dark reskin of the whole panel (which would mean re-theming every message bubble too — a much bigger, unrequested change). The floating launcher button and the 28-32px typing-indicator/greeting avatars were left at their existing sizes — they inherit the `Shell` opacity fix for free and don't need the same hero treatment as the one-time, always-visible header instance.

**Verification (correction)**: same files (`LivingOrb.jsx`, `MCareOrb.jsx`), no new files. Lint clean, typecheck unchanged at the same 5-error baseline, `npm run build` exit 0, 755/755 vitest (unchanged), 262/262 redteam (unchanged — no `orbState` derivation, entity, or tool-permission logic touched, purely visual/layout). Live-only, can't be confirmed from this checkout: whether the shell now genuinely reads as bright pearl (not gray) and whether the larger two-badge/capability-row header composition feels premium rather than crowded on a real device — Portia verifies live after this ships.

## Demo Pages — audit trail, not all are what they claim

`/demo/*` (24 routes, `src/routes/publicRoutes.jsx`) mixes three genuinely different things under one path prefix: features with a real, live engine behind a dramatized presentation (MedGuard, Siobhan, Weather, ArrivalIntel, IntelligenceScan, SituationRoom, EmailShowcase, RecoveryCascade, MemoryBank, MRecon, JamesVoice, MasterJourney, EmergencyScenario); honest vision-demos that say so (MeshBeacon, WaitingRoom, FamilyEye); and demos that claim real-time/automatic behavior the backing code doesn't actually do (EVN's street-level danger zones, Silent Mode / Tap Protocol's fake dispatch logs — a real gesture-SOS system, `useCovertSOS`→`triggerCovertSOS`, exists and is live but neither demo uses it, Language Bridge's "real-time" claim over browser TTS, Nightlife's wrong lockout numbers, and a Trust Score cluster — `calculateDoctorTrustScore`/`calculateCompanionScore` have zero callers anywhere despite claiming a "daily cron" that doesn't exist — fanning out into `AdminMissionControl`'s fabricated-but-undisclosed score column and `CoverageMatrix`'s "LIVE" mislabel). Audited 2026-08-04, full findings in memory (`project_demo_audit_20260804` if using the memory system) — don't assume a `/demo/*` page is either fully real or fully fake without checking; verify per-page.

**The one non-demo casualty found this way**: `PublicRecoveryTracker.jsx` (`/track/:token`, the real family-facing tracker link, not a demo route) was completely broken — fetched a REST endpoint that never existed, and its token generator (`generateShareToken`, real code) had zero UI callers so no valid link could ever be created. Fixed: `getCaseTrackerPublic` (new, public token-verified read, mirrors `getGuardianViewData`'s invoke-based pattern) + `ShareJourneyButton` (new, mounted in `Dashboard.jsx` next to `TripProgressStepper`) as the missing generate/share entry point.

**The 8 misleading demos were then fixed directly** (commit `4bd161c1`), each proportionate to what was actually wrong: `NightlifeRobberyDemo` had factual errors (5-attempt lockout not 3, IP-only logging not GPS/security-team dispatch) — corrected against `verifyEmergencyPIN`. `SilentModeDemo`/`TapProtocolDemo`/`EVNiQ400Demo`/`LanguageBridgeDemo`/`PartnerTrustDemo` got an honest illustrative-concept disclaimer (**never** reuse the "live engine runs on real patient data" badge wording on a page where no real engine exists — that phrase is reserved for demos like MedGuard/Siobhan/Weather where it's true). `CoverageMatrix`'s Trust Score row copy was softened to stop asserting active partner-blocking that doesn't exist; its `LIVE` pill was left alone since the page's own legend defines that tier as "has an interactive demo," which the row does satisfy — don't conflate "the pill is honest by its own definition" with "the response copy is honest," they're separate checks. `AdminMissionControl` got a real fix, not just copy: its MedGuard score column now queries each case's actual last `medguard_analysis` AuditLog entry (`base44.entities.AuditLog.filter({event_type, resource_id: {$in: [...]}})` — direct client read, admin RLS already permits it, no new edge function needed) instead of a fabricated placeholder; a case with no analysis yet shows "Not yet analyzed," never a fake number.

**Partner Trust Score made real for doctors + companions (commit `24e95c49`)**: `calculateDoctorTrustScore`/`calculateCompanionScore` were real but unwired (see the zero-reference follow-up above) and used a bespoke `x-morales-service`/`SERVICE_KEY` header no cron could send — rewritten to `createHandler` + `cronAuthorized()`, scheduled daily in `freshness-cron.yml`. Their "patient satisfaction" component read a `PostSurgeryFeedback` entity that doesn't exist anywhere in the schema (always threw, silently caught, permanently returned a hardcoded constant) — fixed to join through the real feedback data, `RecoverySession.feedback_rating` keyed by `case_id` (one overall trip rating, not split doctor-vs-companion — an honest proxy, not precise attribution). `PartnerTrustDemo.jsx`/`CoverageMatrix.jsx` now show real scored doctors/companions when viewed by an admin (real scores are admin-only by design, matching the scoring function's own "doctors never see this score" intent); everyone else still sees the same illustrative sample, honestly labeled. `matchLocalDoctor`'s existing `-trust_score` sort — previously inert since nothing ever wrote the field — now works for free. **Driver trust scoring** — built 2026-08-10 (M-Care Phase 33 below): `calculateDriverTrustScore` now covers `TaxiService`, using `origin_driver_id`/`destination_driver_id` correctly (not `assigned_driver_id`, the field nothing in the repo ever writes — the bug this section used to warn about).

**`/msafe` removed entirely (2026-08-08)**: found while investigating why voice input wasn't appearing on a screenshot Portia sent — the page wasn't the real M-Care at all. `MSafeChat.jsx` (mounted by `MSafeChatDemo.jsx` at the public route `/msafe`, outside the `/demo/*` prefix every other illustrative page in this app uses) was a fully disconnected mockup: a hardcoded seed conversation, three canned replies picked at random behind a fake 1.1s "typing" delay, zero calls to `base44` anywhere in the file — wearing a green pulsing **"LIVE SESSION"** badge the whole time. Worse than the 8 demos fixed in the original audit above, which at least lived under `/demo/*` as a signal to the visitor; this one sat at a clean, guessable production-looking URL with no such cue. Portia chose removal over relabeling, since the real M-Care (everything built across this entire session) already does everything this one only pretended to. Deleted `src/pages/MSafeChatDemo.jsx`, `src/components/msafe/MSafeChat.jsx` (and the now-empty `msafe/` directory), and the `/msafe` route + lazy import in `publicRoutes.jsx` — confirmed zero other references first. Incidental fix: `MSafeChatDemo.jsx` was also one of the 5 standing typecheck-baseline errors (`onMinimize` prop mismatch) — baseline is now 4, not 5.

## Commands

```bash
npm run dev          # Vite dev server (hot reload)
npm run build        # Production build
npm run lint         # ESLint — quiet mode (errors only)
npm run lint:fix     # Auto-fix lint errors
npm run typecheck    # tsc check on src/components + src/pages
npm run preview      # Serve production build locally
npm test             # Vitest unit tests (single run)
npm run test:watch   # Vitest in watch mode
npm run test:e2e     # Playwright — public/deployed-app suite
```

### Testing

- **Vitest** (`tests/*.test.js`) covers pure app logic. `tests/safety.test.js` is load-bearing — it asserts the RED hard block (`getViolations`) never regresses. Run one file/test: `npx vitest run tests/safety.test.js` or `npx vitest run -t "does NOT block a single procedure"`.
- **Playwright** (`playwright.config.js`): the `public` / `authenticated` E2E projects run against the **deployed** app (`E2E_BASE_URL`, default the live Base44 URL — local dev can't complete a Base44 session), and `redteam` is the deterministic safety red-team: `npx playwright test --project=redteam` (no browser/network/credits, must stay green). Single test: append `-g "pattern"`.
- Both safety suites are M-Principle guardrails — if either goes red, a dangerous combination or an AI-cleared risk can slip through. Keep them green.

## Deploying & Environment

- **Local env:** `.env.local` needs `VITE_BASE44_APP_ID` and `VITE_BASE44_APP_BASE_URL` (see `README.md`). This checkout has no Base44 session credentials, so sign-in can't complete on `localhost` — verify authenticated flows against the deployed app.
- **Deploy model:** pushing to the repo syncs code into the Base44 Builder; changes go live only after **Publish** in Base44. New edge functions and scheduled jobs are configured in the Base44 dashboard, not deployed from this repo — Claude cannot self-deploy.
- **Integration credits gate the backend.** When the account's monthly integration quota is exhausted, edge-function calls return **HTTP 402** at the platform layer — before the function body runs — so even credit-free functions fail. Deterministic app logic, Vitest, and the Playwright `redteam` suite all run without credits.
- **Scheduled work runs via GitHub Actions, not Base44 cron:** `.github/workflows/freshness-cron.yml` (data-freshness jobs) and `redteam.yml`. The freshness jobs share one `CRON_SECRET` set in both GitHub repo secrets and Base44 env; the guarded functions accept it via `_shared/cronAuth.ts` (cron secret **or** admin session).

## Architecture Overview

**What this is:** Medical tourism concierge platform. Clients book dental/aesthetic procedures with verified doctors abroad; the platform coordinates travel logistics, companions, security escorts, and post-operative recovery monitoring.

**Stack:** React 18 + Vite, React Router v6, TanStack React Query v5, Tailwind CSS, Radix UI/shadcn. Backend is Base44 BaaS — the frontend talks to it via `@base44/sdk`; ~270 Deno edge functions extend it.

### Base44 SDK

The SDK singleton is at `src/api/base44Client.js`:

```js
import { base44 } from '@/api/base44Client';
base44.entities.CaseRecord.filter({ client_email: user.email }, '-created_date', 20);
base44.entities.Doctor.get(id);
base44.functions.invoke('functionName', payload);
base44.auth.me();
```

In edge functions, use `base44.asServiceRole.entities.*` for admin-level access (bypasses user-scoped permissions).

### Auth Flow

`AuthContext` (`src/lib/AuthContext.jsx`) is the single auth provider wrapping the entire app. It:
- Calls `base44.auth.me()` on mount
- Falls back to `localStorage['morales_last_known_user']` when offline
- Restores the last known user as `isOfflineUser: true` so offline features remain accessible
